## 3.7. Nirile ninru

*nīrilē ninṟu ayarkkinṟōm nīdi allādana seydāyūragam sālavum sēyttāl ūḻi ellām uṇarvānēārvam unakkē udaiyōm ammanai mār kāṇil ottārpōravidāy engaḷ pattaip pūngurundu ēri irādē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ūḻi ellām – during the entire time of deluge; uṇarvānē – Oh, one who is fully aware (with the thought of protecting the worlds)!; nīrilē ninṟu – standing in water; ayarkkinṟōm – we are suffering; nīdi allādana – unjustifiable acts; seydāy – you are doing; Ur – our āyppādi (śrī gōkulam) too; agam – houses there; sālavum – to a great extent; sēyttu – are at a distance; Al – alas!; unakkē – only in matters concerning you; ārvam udaiyōm – we have affection; ammanaimār – our mothers; kāṇil – if they see us (uniting with you); ottār – will not agree; engaḷ pattai – our saris; pōra vidāy – mercifully give such that we could reach for them; pū kuruttu – on the blossomed kurundam tree; ēri irādē – do not climb and carry out mischievous acts

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is aware, during the entire time of deluge (thinking about how to protect the worlds)! We are suffering, standing in water, while you are indulging in wrongful acts. (Even if we wish to escape from your mischievous activities) Alas! Our town and our houses are a long way off. We are affectionate towards mattes related only to you; however, if our mothers see us uniting with you, they will not agree. Mercifully give us the dresses such that we could reach out towards them. Do not carry out mischievous acts, climbing the kurundam tree.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Despite telling him, in the previous pāsuram, that “The lotus stalks are biting us like a poisonous scorpion”, since he did not relent, they tell him “While we are suffering like this, do not indulge in unjustifiable acts and harass us further by creating calumny for us, who are affectionate towards you”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nīrilē ninṟu ayarkkinṟōm** – we had reached here before the rooster called and before you got up around dawn. Since we have been in this cold water for a long time, our minds have become bewildered; look at our situation.  Hearing this kaṇṇan said “You created difficulties for yourselves due to your ignorance. I too have been suffering on the tree for a long time, because of you. You are the ones who created difficulty for me too, like this”. Without giving them their dresses, he indulged in some lustful acts.

**nīdi allādana seydāy** – They ask “Could you indulge in such wrongful acts?” He responds “I have done what I had to. I am *svatantran* \[totally independent\]. I am wont to doing these. Who could stop me, a *svatantran* from doing what I wish? You can go to the town and appeal to the people about how you had suffered at my hands” They tell him ….

**Ur agam sālavum sēyttāl** – “Our town is far away from here. Even if we somehow reach the town, our houses are still farther off. Thus, we cannot appeal to anyone”. kaṇṇan says “I am indulging in wrongful acts. The town is at a faraway distance such that you could not appeal to anyone. Beyond this, what have you people thought of, as a remedial measure?” They tell him “You have to give the remedy”

**ūḻi ellām uṇarvānē** – When deluge was to submerge all the worlds, you had protected them by keeping them in your stomach. Oh, one who is aware all the time, thinking of ways to protect the worlds! Just as you remove the hurdles which happen due to others and the hurdles which a person brings on himself/herself, you should give the remedy for the troubles brought about by you. During the time of deluge, when all the entities, which you had created during the time of creation, had got destroyed, you had looked for ways to create them again. Only you have to find a way out for our troubles too. Just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-1 “*sadēva sōmyēdamagra āsīt ēkamēvādvitīyam*” (Oh child! This world (which is now seen with names and forms), was a single entity during the time of deluge, (since it was without names and forms) and was without a second entity (since there was none like itself); it was *sat*, as brahmam itself) when all the entities took protection under you, just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-3 “*tadaikśata bahusyām prajāyēyēti tattējo’śruta*” (that supreme entity took a *sankalpam* (vow) ‘Let me multiply manifold’; it created radiance), did you not find a way to create them all again?” Hearing this, kaṇṇan says “Since this world did not prevent me from doing good, I was able to do good to it; you people are preventing me from doing good to you”. They respond, saying ….

**ārvam unakkē udaiyōm** – “It is not only that we are not stopping you from doing good things to us; we have deep affection for you; you don’t seem to know it” kaṇṇan says “What you say is incomprehensible to me, just like compound words; is it apt to say that you have deep affection towards me while you are doing something which I do not desire? Is it enough to say that you have affection for me, through words? Should you not act in the same way?” They tell him…

**ammanaimār kāṇil ottār** – “We also like to unite with you, which manifests our affection for you; but our mothers will not allow that. We would like to get their approval and then unite with you”. kaṇṇan says “I too would like to play with you all in your houses, with you people donning your silk clothes and being happy in your minds. However, my heart is not agreeing to give you back your silk clothes. I will somehow make it to agree”. Hearing this, the covherd girls tell him “It is enough if you give our dresses, even when your heart is not in agreement”. He says “In that case, here take these”, tying  a few clothes to the *seṇḍukōl* \[stick which he used for playing with his friends\], and kept the stick on a branch which seemed near them but was actually a bit far away from them, turning his face towards flowers on the tree, as if he were admiring them. Looking at that, they tell him …

**pōra vidāy engaḷ pattai pūngurundam ēṟiyirādē** – mercifully lower the branch so that we could reach our clothes. They tell him not to torture them by keeping their clothes in such a way that they cannot reach for them, and pretending to admire the flowers.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of the this decad.

## 3.8. Mamimar makkale

*tollai irā – in earlier part of nightstū malar kaṇgaḷ vaḷara – suc tat te pious-flover-like eyes are under te control of sleeptuyilvānē – ōh, one vho sleeps!(tose vho are standing in te pond)māmimār makkaḷē allōm – ve are not only tose vho vould be your consortsmaṟṟum ellārum – your moters-in-lav, teir moters and oter suc relativesingu – at tis lacepōndār – have comeidu – tis miscievous act of yourssāla – to a great extentsēman anṟu – is not appropriateidu – tis vordnām – vecikkana – trutfullysonnōm – are sayingāyar – for te covherd peoplekōmaḷak koḻundē – oh, one vho is like a tender leaf!kurundidai kūṟai paṇiyāy – mercifully give te dresses vhic are on te kurundam tree*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who sleeps in earlier part of nights such that your pious-flower-like eyes are under the control of sleep! Those who have assembled here are not only those who would be your consorts, but also include your mothers-in-law, their mothers and such relatives. This mischievous act of yours is not at all appropriate. We are saying this word, truthfully. Oh, one who is like a tender leaf to the clan of covherd people! Mercifully give the dresses which are on the kurundam tree.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Despite telling kaṇṇan “Do not harass us by indulging in unjustifiable acts”, since he did not give them their dresses, they tell him in this pāsuram “There are relatives such as your mother-in-law and others too amongst us here; hence, do not indulge in mischievous acts in their presence and earn their displeasure” māmimār makkaḷē allōm maṟṟum ingu ellārum pōndārtūmalark kaṇgaḷ vaḷarat tollai irāt tuyilvānēsēmamēl anṟidu sālac cikkana nām idu sonnōmkōmaḷa āyar koḻundē! kurundidaik kūṟai paṇiyāy Wored-by-Word Meanings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**māmimār makkaḷē allōm maṟṟum ingu ellārum pōndār** – kaṇṇan started indulging in more and more mischievous activities. Knowing that the reason for his indulging in such activities is only because no one in the town will know of such activities, they frightened him saying “Those in the town will know of your mischievous activities”. kaṇṇan replied, saying “I will tell them ‘I engaged in these activities only with those who are of my age and who I am going to marry’” To that, they say ..

**māmimār makkaḷē allōm** – among those who have assembled here are not only those who have undertaken a religious ritual in order to marry you, but also their mothers, and other aged people. Thus, it is not only the covherd girls whom you thought had come here, but also your mothers-in-law in whose presence you would feel ashamed to carry out these mischievous acts. They frightened him further saying that they will go to town and reveal his activities here. kaṇṇan, hearing this, said “Even after knowing me, could you bring these people who will be a hindrance to our union in this secluded place?” They respond, saying ….

**tūmalark kaṇgaḷ vaḷarat tollai irāt tuyilvānē** – They said “By habit, you used to play with the girls in the early part of the night and sleep well in the later part of the night. We thought that you would be fast asleep at this time and that is the reason for bringing them here. We did not know that you will act just the opposite of your earlier habits”.  “What is the purpose in saying that now? āren’t you seeing that I had come ahead of you people?” Saying this, he started indulging in further impudent activities. They tell him, seeing that …

**sēmamēl anṟidu sāla** – “What you are doing is not good for us. We are saying that you and we, should be together, all the time. Your activities will not allow that to happen. They will even make us not to get together ever again”. Hearing their words, kaṇṇan asks “Who said like this?”

**nām idu sonnōm** – they said “We said this”. Hearing this, feigning that he was frightened, kaṇṇan said “How can I go against this *ācārya vacanam* (respectful master’s words\]? What you have said is very appropriate only. Haven’t you people said this!” They tell him “Don’t take this playfully”

**cikkanac connōm** – “We said this truthfully. If you do not consider this, many unfavourable results will follow your wrongful activities”. kaṇṇan said “Let me go to any hell for going against this *ācārya vacanam*” So saying, he donned a few soft dresses on his waist, left the remaining dresses on the *kurundam* tree and told them “See, how they fit my tender nature”. They tell him …

**kōmaḷa āyar koḻundē! kurundidaik kūṟai paṇiyāy** – “The dresses look beautiful on you. You are like the tender leaf of herd-people clan. We are not asking for the dresses which are apt for your tender divine form. Mercifully give us the dresses which are on the tree and which do not have the distinction of having a connection with your divine form”

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of the this decad.

## 3.9. Kanjan valaivaitta

*kanjan valaivaitta anṟu kāriruḷ ellil piḻaittunenju dukkam seyyap pōndāy! ninṟa ikkanniayarōmaianja urappāḷ asōdai āṇāda vittittirukkumvanjagap pēcci pāluṇḍa masimaiyilee kūṟai tārāy*

**Пословный перевод:**

kanjan – kamsan; valai vaitta anṟu – during the time when he wanted to destroy you; kār iruḷ – having pitch darkness; ellil – during the night; piḻaittu – escaping; ninṟa – those who remain; ik kanniayarōmai – for us, young women; nenju dukkam seyya – to create suffering in the mind; pōndāy – you incarnated; asōdai – yaśōdaip pirātti, on the other hand; anja – to make you frightened; urappāḷ – will not scold you; āṇāda vittittirukkum – she let you to grow, carrying out misciefs; vanjagam – having deceit; pēycci – the demon, pūtanai’s; pāl – milk from her bosom; uṇḍa – one who suckled (along with her life); masimai ilee – oh one, who is shameless!; kūṟai tārāy – mercifully give the saris

**Перевод:**

**You incarnated during the time when kamsan wanted to destroy you, by escaping in the darkness of the night, and to create suffering in our, the young girls’ minds. yaśōdaip pirātti will not scold you to frighten you, and let you grow up, carrying out mischievous acts. Oh one, who drank the bosom milk of pūtanai, the deceitful demon, along with her life! Oh, the shameless one! Mercifully give us our saris.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – kaṇṇan has two types of nature. In one, he will fulfil the wishes of those who praise him, such as pāṇḍavas. In the other, he will also do good to those who scold him, such as śiśupālan. Until now, the covherd girls had been praising him, without any success. Hence, now they decide to scold him,  to see whether by scolding him, their desire would be fulfilled.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kanjan valai vaitta anṟu** – on that day when kamsan attempted to destroy kaṇṇan and through him, to destroy both nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm) and leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm). On that day when kamsan tried to annihilate all the entities… if emperumān were present, even if both the vibhūtis are destroyed, he could re-create them; but if he himself were to be destroyed, all the entities, which take their existence from him, will get destroyed. Hence, instead of saying “casting the net for a particular person”, they say “casting the net” in a general way. In harivamśam viṣṇu parvam 6-39, the riśi too expressed this meaning when he said “*stanyam tad viśa sammiśram rasyamāsīt jagat gurō*:”  
(the poisoned milk from \[pūtanā’s\] bosom was like nectar for kriṣṇa, the lord of entire universe). He used the term *jagat guru* to identify kaṇṇan since he established himself as the lord of the universe, after taking her poisoned bosom milk which was the sustaining nectar for him. How did poison become the sustaining nectar for kaṇṇan? It was only emperumān who created the boundaries saying that poison is a destructive material and that nectar is life-sustaining material. When such is the case, how could poison destroy him? Just like nectar, even poison could sustain him, there is no shortcoming for that. This is the reason for nammāḻvār too to mercifully say in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-5-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-5-9-mayom-thiya/) “*tūya kuḻaviyāy vidappāl amudā amudu seyditta māyan*” (the child, without a blemish, drank poisoned milk as nectar; one who has amazing activities).

**kāriruḷ ellil piḻaittadu** – kamsan had devised a scheme to ensure that kaṇṇan would be destroyed, without any escape route. Despite that, a darkness had protected kaṇṇan! The implied meaning is – when vasudēvar carried kaṇṇan from maturā to tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam), since darkness was having the same complexion of kaṇṇan, who had a dark, divine form, and since it was the time when those, who were guarding dēvaki and vasudēvar in the prison, came under the control of sleep, due to darkness alone, kaṇṇan escaped from the net cast by kamsan. nammāḻvār too mercifully gave this meaning in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-4-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/01/thiruvaimozhi-6-4-5-vendith-thevar-irakka/) “*vīngiruḷvāy pūṇḍu anṟannai pulambappōy angōr āykkulam pukkadum*” (dēvaki lifted the child, embraced and started crying uncontrollably, as kaṇṇan \[left maturā and\] in total darkness, entered the clan of cow-herds in śrī gōkulam, which was a unique place\]. piḷḷai uṟangāvilli dāsar \[one of the distinguished disciples of rāmānujar\] used to say, with affection “kaṇṇan was an infant, not capable of protecting himself. There was none to protect him, with love. kamsan and his henchmen were waiting to somehow destroy the infant as soon as it was born. Was it not darkness which protected him at that time? We all should take refuge under that darkness”.

**ninṟa ikkanniyarōmai nenju dukkam seyyap pōndāy** – the covherd girls despise that darkness which is praised by those who are favourable to kaṇṇan, saying that darkness had protected him that day, such that he could later harass them. They say “Apart from tormenting dēvaki and vasudēvar, your parents, by separating from them, you had come to tiruvāyppādi only to harass women, other than dēvaki, too”. The implied meaning is that while they thought that the infant, escaping from the net cast by kamsan, would protect those who were born as girls, it has become the (unavoidable} reason for harassing them.

**anja urappāḷ asōdai** – they say that when they complained to yaśōdāp pirātti about the ways by which kaṇṇan was harassing them, they thought that she, being a woman like them, would reprimand him; however, she was not rebuking him such that he would mend his ways. Just as it has been said in periya tirumoḻi 10-7-11 “*anganam tīmaigaḷ seyvargaḷō nambī*! *āyar madamakkaḷai*” (Oh kaṇṇa! Would you indulge in such harassing acts towards the women folk in śrī gōkulam?), while the girls expected yaśōdāp pirātti to be strict with him and reprimand him, she would gently tell him not to trouble them. Hearing this, the covherd girls would say “How sad that we came to her to complain about him! It appears that kaṇṇan is better than yaśōdāp pirātti”

**anja urappāḷ asōdai** – in the presence of covherd girls who came to complain about kaṇṇan, yaśōdāp will say a word or two, merely as a lip-service, but would not rebuke him enough to frighten him.

**āṇāda vittirukkum** – kaṇṇan would think “They have gone to complain to mother yaśōdāp pirātti, to make her rebuke me. We have seen the result of their complaint too!” kaṇṇan, who has harassing girls as his nature, would now start harassing them hundred times more, with the support of yaśōdāp pirātti.

**irukkum** – making him behave mischievously towards them, yaśōdāp would feel happy, thinking “Even at this young age, he is indulging in such wrongful deeds!” Just as it has been said in [perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2023/08/perumal-thirumozhi-7-8/) “*tollai inbattu iṟudi kaṇḍāḷē*” (she saw the boundary of unbounded pleasures), feeling elated that she had done her duty properly. Hearing this, kaṇṇan said angrily “You people are complaining to others and trying to get things done through me”. They reply to him, saying …

**vanjagap pēycci pāḷuṇḍa masimaiyilee** – we do not think like that. It is you who vouldn’t know the difference between mother’s bosom and a demon’s bosom. Had it been the divine son of cakravarti (daśaradan), he would have felt ashamed, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 10-9 “*prasīdantu bhavantō mē hrīrēśō hi* mamātulā l  
*yadīdruśairaham viprair upastēyair upastita* ll” ((Oh sages!) May all be happy. You people are apt to be attained. However, you have come here to attain me. I feel more ashamed now), since he did not take the initiative to protect them. How would you, who would not know the sorrow of a woman, know about this? Are you not a shameless person? *masimai* – shame.

**kūṟai tārāy** – You be like whoever you wish to be. Give us our dresses first. We do not wish to have any connection with you, they say.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of the this decad.

## 3.10. Kanniyarodu engal

*kanniyarōdu engaḷ nambi kariya pirān viḷaiyāttaiponniyal mādangaḷ sūḻnda puduvaiyar kōn bhattan kōdaiinnisaiyāḷ sonna mālai īraindum vallavar tām pōymanniya mādavanōdu vaigundam pukkiruppārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

engaḷ nambi – as our lord; kariya – being dark complexioned; pirān – kaṇṇapirān; kanniyarōdu – carried out with covherd girls; viḷaiyāttai – regarding the divine playful deeds; pon iyal – beautiful, like gold; mādangaḷ sūḻnda – surrounded by mansions; puduvaiyar kōn – head of those who inhabit śrīvilliputtūr; bhattan kōdai – āndāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār; in isaiyāl – with sweet tunes; sonna – mercifully composed; mālai – garland of worlds; īr aindum – these ten pāsurams; vallavar tām – those who are capable of learning; pōy – going (through the path of arcis, light); vaigundam pukku – reaching paramapadam \[śrī vaikuṇṭam\]; manniya – one who is residing permanently; mādavanōdu – along with tirumāl (divine consort of śrī mahālakśmi); iruppar – will live, carrying out permanent kainkaryam

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇapirān, who is with a dark complexion, had indulged in playful deeds with young girls \[of tiruvāyppādi\]. āṇḍāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the head of those who inhabit śrīvilliputtūr, surrounded by beautiful, gold-like mansions, has mercifully composed with sweet tunes, as a garland of words, those playful deeds. Those who are capable of learning these ten pāsurams, along with their meanings, will reach paramapadam and will live there forever, carrying out permanent kainkaryam to tirumāl.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āndāḷ brings this decad to its end, with her desire having been fulfilled, and gives the benefit of reciting this decad. In the pravēśam (introduction) to this decad, it was said “This decad explains the ways in which they expressed their distress, the different ways in which they praised him, how he returned their dresses to them and united with them”. In this pāsuram \[3.10\], there is no mention about their union. However, in the next decad, since they are indulging in kūdal iḻaittal (drawing circles), asking for union with kaṇṇan, it is clear that union should have happened in this decad.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kanniyarōdu ….. kariya pirān viḷaiyāttai** – since the covherd girls have been identified as *kanniyar* (maids), it is hinted here that they are of the age at which they would believe the words mentioned by kaṇṇan, that they will always have appropriate age for kaṇṇan, always being fresh even if he keeps enjoying with them forever, and that they are children who would not tolerate his mischievous deeds.

**engaḷ nambi** – the term *nambi* refers to one who is complete; here, kaṇṇan is complete in harassing the five lakh girls in the covherd clans.

**kariya pirān** – even if he does not have mischievous disposition in his heart, which will make them to engage with him, he has beauty in his divine form which will make them to fall head over heels on him.

**viḷaiyāttai** – she hints that this is a playful deed indulged in by paramātmā \[supreme entity\] to unite with jīvātmā \[entity with knowledge\] and not the deed of a mischievous person

**ponniyal mādangaḷ sūḻnda puduvaiyar kōn bhattan kōdai** – the implied meaning is that āṇḍāḷ has such an engagement with kaṇṇan only because she is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the leader of those who live in śrīvilliputtūr, a place which is surrounded by beautiful mansions which are like gold.

**innisaiyāl sonna mālai** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 4.8 “*pātyē gēyē ca maduram pramāṇai tribhir anvitam*” (the epic śrī rāmāyaṇam is composed with three types of metrics, and is sweet whether it is read or sung), it is sweet to listen to, for everyone, whether it is recited with or without tunes, and is enjoyable like garland for emperumān.

**īraindum vallavar tām pōy manniya mādavanōdu vaigundam pukkirupparē** – those who recite these ten pāsurams, with their meanings, will reach the permanent śrī vaikuṇṭam, will permanently enjoy the union with śrīman nārāyaṇan, which āṇḍāḷ and the covherd girls experienced here, and will live happily.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the 4<sup>th</sup> decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 4.1. Telliyar palar

*teḷḷīyār palar kai toḻum dēvanārvaḷḷal mālirunjōlai maṇāḷanārpaḷḷī koḷḷum idattu adi kottidakkoḷḷumāgil nī kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kūdalē – Oh kūdal \[calling out to the circle drawn on the sand\]!; teḷḷiyār palar – many nityasūris \[permanent residents of śrī vaikuṇṭam\] who have clear; kai toḻum – being vorśipped to the fullest extent by their hands; dēvanār – as the lord; vaḷḷal – as being munificent; mālirunjōlai maṇāḷanār – the supreme entity who has mercifully taken residence at tirumāliunjōlai as the bridegroom; paḷḷi koḷḷum idattu – in the place where he mercifully takes rest; adi kottida – for me to gently stroke (his) divine feet; koḷḷum āgil – if (he) agrees in his divine mind; nī kūdidu – you should agree

**Перевод:**

**Oh kūdal! emperumān is being vorśipped by many nityasūris, who have clear knowledge, in paramapadam, through anjali \[cupping the palms together in salutation\]. He, who is highly munificent, has taken residence in tirumālirunjōlai as a bridegroom. If he thinks in his divine mind that I should carry out servitude to him at the place where he comes to take rest, you should agree \[by ending with even number of circles\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – śrīrangam is the abode where maṇavāḷan (bridegroom), who has taken residence in tirumālirunjōlai, comes to take rest. āṇḍāḷ desires to carry out kainkaryam \[servitude\] to that perumāḷ at śrīrangam.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**teḷḷiyār** – nityasūris, who always have clear knowledge \[about emperumān\]. While she attempted to be with him for some time, and took the help of *kūdal*, she thought of nityasūris, who enjoy him always. How fortunate are they! āren’t they fortunate that they enjoy him permanently without having to engage with *kūdal*, unlike her. They have the knowledge that if they separate from him, they too would have to engage with *kūdal* and hence they are not separating from him ever, enjoying him all the time. Are there just a few entities like them?

**palar** – just as puruśa sūktam 1-3 says “*pādōsya viśvā bhūtāni tripādasyāmrutam divi*” (all the entities living in samsāram (materialistic realm) form one fourth of his wealth; the remaining three fourths of his wealth, remain in paramapadam, indestructible), aren’t countless nityasūris enjoying him permanently in paramapadam, which is three times as large as samsāram? On the other hand, here, āṇḍāḷ is alone, engaging with *kūdal*. There is not a second person with whom she could converse about her earlier experience with him. There, since it is a permanent enjoyment, there is no time to speak to someone about the experience.

**kai toḻum** – mahābhāratam śanti parvam 344-45 says “*muktānām lakśaṇam hyētat yaccvēta dvīpavāsinām* l *nityānjaliputā hruśtā nama ityēva vādina*: ll”  
(the identity of *śvēta dvīpavāsis* (those who live in the land, śvētadvīpam) is also the identity of muktātmās \[those who have got liberated from samsāram and have attained śrī vaikuṇṭam\], viz. they will be always performing *anjali* \[cupping the palms together\]. Due to that, they will always be blissful; they will have the nature of repeatedly saying *nama*: \[I do not belong to anyone other than emperumān\]). nityasūris will have vorśipping emperumān as their nature.

**dēvanār** – just as these people have their existence by vorśipping him, he gets his radiance by having them worship him. The syllable *dēva* which comes from the etymology *divu -dyuti* indicates the meaning *dyuti*, which is radiance.

**vaḷḷal mālirunjōlai maṇāḷanār** – As mentioned earlier, even though he shines splendidly in paramapadam, even though there are countless nityasūris who have permanent experience with him there, that experience does not appear as a great one to him; he thinks about how the samsāris are losing out on that experience, becomes pale, like one who has not eaten for days, and with his divine quality of generosity that people in samsāram too should get that experience, he has come to tirumāḷirunjōlai, as a bridegroom, so that *cētanas* (those with knowledge), who are his consorts, could attain him. When he has unlimited experience there, why should he leave that and come here? Just like a rich person, even if he loses a very small part of his wealth, will feel sorry about that loss, emperumān too, despite countless *cētanas* enjoying him in paramapadam, not feeling satiated with that, thinking about the loss of the *cētanas* here, becomes pale and comes here to overcome that deficiency. mahōpaniśat says “*sa ēkāki na ramēta*” ((during the time of deluge) that nārāyaṇan, who is alone, does not attain bliss). During the time of deluge, even though emperumān is enjoying in paramapadam, when he looks at the destruction of leelā vibhūti (samsāram, the materialistic realm), he thinks that he is alone and gets dejected.

**vaḷḷal** – one who has the benevolence of offering himself and his wealth to his followers.

**maṇāḷanār** – the place which he comes to so that samsāris could attain him.

**maṇāḷanār** – unlike the covherd girls in *tiruvāyppādi* \[śrī gōkulam\] whom he could attain by hiding on the banks of river yamunā or in brindāvanam, with the help of darkness and night times, he has to get hold of the divine daughter of periyāḻvār. Since periyāḻvār is “*vēdappayan koḷḷa valla vittucittan*” (periyāḻvār who has given the meanings of vēdam \[sacred texts\] in his prabandams), to manifest his *gōtram* (clan) and *sūtram* (the divine treatise which he follows), he comes to tirumālirunjōlai, ties the auspicious thread around his wrist and waits as the bridegroom.

**maṇāḷanār paḷḷī koḷḷūmidattu** – in the divine place where that *maṇāḷanār* takes rest. Will the loving consort show her face in a public place? Her servitude is in her private quarters only. Here, the term *paḷḷi koḷḷum idam* refers to śrī rangam, the huge temple. periyavāccān piḷḷai, the commentator for this prabandam says that he has heard piḷḷai aḻagiya maṇavāḷar araiyar quoting parāśara bhattar “The place where he takes rest is kōyil \[śrī rangam\]”.

**adi kottida** – to massage his divine feet gently. She \[āṇḍāḷ\] thinks of carrying out servitude to emperumān, not only during the time when he is aware, but also when he is sleeping sweetly, by stroking his divine feet, gently. āren’t his divine feet the soft ones, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/06/thiruvaimozhi-9-2-10-koduvinaip-padaigal/) “*nilamagaḷ pidikkum melladi*” (tender, divine feet of emperumān, gently massaged by śrī bhūmip pirātti)? She is trying to carryout kainkaryam to the divine feet as a measure of her *śēśatvam* (being dependent on emperumān). īsn’t this the benefit of attaining him?

**koḷḷumāgil** – She manifests the nature of mumukśus \[those who desire to attain śrī vaikuṇṭam, after getting liberated from samsāram\], thinking “Only emperumān’s divine thought of getting servitude \[from his followers\] is the means; one’s activities such as engaging with *kūdal* etc are not the means”

**nī kūdidu kūdalē** – Even though she knows “He alone is the means”, since her urge in attaining him is such deep that she could not sustain herself without attaining him, she falls at the feet of *kūdal* knowing that it is not the proper means.

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.2. Kattil vengadam

*kāttil vēngadam kaṇṇapura nagarvāttaminṟi magiḻnduṟai vāmananōttarā vandu en kai paṟṟit tannodumkūttumāgil nī kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kūdalē – Oh, kūdal!; kāttil vēngadam – in tirumalai, which is in forest; nagar kaṇṇanpuram – and in tirukkaṇṇapuram, which is in a town; vāttam inṟi – without any grief; magiḻndu – with happiness in the divine mind; uṟai – one who is residing permanently; vāmanan – emperumān who incarnated as vāmana (dwarf); ōttarā vandu – running towards me; en kai paṟṟi – holding my hand; tannodum – with him; kūttum āgil – if he were to embrace; nī kūdidu – you should agree

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – “He \[emperumān\] appeared as if he was in sādānuśtānam (carrying out efforts on his own) at tiruvēngadam and at tirukkaṇṇapuram, in order to attain me \[āṇḍāḷ\]” She is beseeching kūdal “As a benefit of that, you \[addressing kūdal\] have not done anything to make him come hurriedly to me and to embrace me”*

**kāttil vēngadam** – this could be construed as – tirugvēngadam which is inside forest or as tiruvēngadam which has forest as its residence.

**kāttil vēngdam kaṇṇapura nagar** – emperumān is enjoying the taste of living in the forest in tirumalai, as tiruvēngadamudaiyān, just as he enjoyed it with sages in śrī dandakāraṇyam, when he incarnated as śrī rāma. Similarly, he enjoys as śaurirājap perumāḷ enjoying the taste of residing in a town, just as he enjoyed in ayōdyā, as śrī rāma. Alternatively, it could be said that he enjoyed in tiruvēngadam as tiruvēngadamudaiyān, similar to his enjoying the forests in brindāvanam during his incarnation as kaṇṇan; similarly, he enjoyed in tirukkaṇṇanpuram, similar to his enjoyment in the town of tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam).

**kāttil vēngadam** – isn’t the one, who is offering himself to nityasūris to enjoy in paramapadam, standing in tirumalai, manifesting himself to lowly entities, just as it has been said in [nānmugan tiruvandādi 47](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/08/nanmugan-thiruvandhadhi-47/) “*kānamum vānaramum vedum udai vēngadam*” (tiruvēngadam which has its inhabitants entities such as forest, monkeys and the hilly tribal people)?

**vāttaminṟi** – he is mercifully standing here, without any grief in his divine mind – “We, who have been manifesting ourselves to nityasūris in paramapadam, have to come down to this place and exist with these people”. Is it that he is only without any grief?

**magiḻndu** – he is supremely blissful, just as he is amidst nityasūris in paramapadam.

**uṟai** – he is residing permanently here, unlike *vibhavāvatāram* (incarnations such as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al), as if thinking “I will not leave this place until samsāram is rooted out”

**vāmanan** – he had transformed himself as a mendicant, in order to get back his wealth of these worlds, from mahābali. Is it necessary to define the limit of his bliss?

**ōttarā vandu** – running hurriedly. While he had come running in anxiety, to get a small piece of land \[from mahābali\], should we discuss about his haste to attain someone like āṇḍāḷ? If he had come in anxiety to get land from mahābali, who has the nature of a monster, would he be able to contain himself if he were to attain the divine daughter of periyāḻvār?

**ōttarā vandu en kai paṟṟi** – sitāp pirātti reminisces in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 119-16 “*na pramāṇīkruta*: *pāṇi*: *bālyē bālēna pīdita*: l” (that hand of mine, when I was young, which was held very tightly by you, when you too were young, is not being thought of \[today\]) āṇḍāḷ desires that emperumān should hold his hand, just as he had held sītāp pirātti’s hand. She wants him to hold her hand such that he cannot hold anyone else’s hand, just as nammāḻvār had said in [tiruvāimoḻi 9-6-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/08/thiruvaimozhi-9-6-6-engannan-kalvam/) “*angaṇṇan uṇḍa en āruyirk kōdidu*” (enjoyed by the most desirous \[emperumān\], my soul has become chaff; he interacted with the soul in such a way that it has become like chaff)

**tannodum kūttumāgil** – due to his grip on my hand, if I become slack, not knowing anything, and if he were to embrace me and make me as one with him …

**nī kūdidu kūdalē** – if you agree and give an indication accordingly, it is certain that he would embrace me. In *svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning), this means that even if emperumān, to get the *cētanan*, comes repeatedly in his presence, in order to get the benefit, the cētanan should agree in his mind \[to be a servitor to emperumān\]

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.3. Pu magan pugal

*pūmagan pugaḻ vānavar pōṟṟudaṟkāmagan aṇi vāṇudal dēvakimāmagan migu sīr vasudēvar tamkōmagan varil kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pū magan – brahmā, who was born in a flower, and; pugaḻ vānavar – eminent nityasūris; pōṟṟudaṟku – to be vorśipped and sung about; Am – apt; magan – as the best among all entities; aṇi vāḷ nudal dēvaki – dēvaki, who has a beautiful, radiant forehead, her; mā magan – distinguished son; migu sīr vasudēvar tam – vasudēvar who has many distinguished qualities, his; kō magan – kaṇṇapirān, his great son; varil – if he comes (to embrace me); kūdalē – Oh, kūdal!; kūdidu – you agree

**Перевод:**

**emperumān is the best among all entities, praised and vorśipped by brahmā, who was born in a flower, and nityasūris, who are eminent. kaṇṇapirān is the distinguished son of dēvaki who has a beautiful and radiant forehead, and vasudēvar who has many distinguished qualities. If that kaṇṇapirān were to come (to embrace me), you agree \[by ending up with even number of circles\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āṇḍāḷ tells kūdal to unite her with the lord of the two vibhūtis (nitya vibhūti, the spiritual realm and leelā vibhūti, the materialistic realm), who incarnated as kaṇṇan.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pū magan** – brahmā, who was born in the lotus flower, sprouting from emperumān’s navel. Here, mentioning brahmā is symbolic of referring to all the entities in leelā vibhūti who were created by him.

**pugaḻ vānavar** – lakśmaṇa was praised in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 18-28 as one who was rich with the wealth of kainkaryam (servitude) “*sarvapriyakarastasya rāmasyāpi śarīrata*: l *lakśmanō lakśmisampannō bahi*: *prāṇa ivāpara*: ll” (lakśmaṇa, who has the wealth of servitude, was one who carried out all the affectionate deeds for śrī rāma, more than śrī rāma’s divine form, and was another *prāṇan* (soul) for śrī rāma, outside his physical form). nityasūris are eminent as those having a wealth of servitude, like lakśmaṇa. Through this verse, the pāsuram refers to those in nitya vibhūti.

**pōṟṟudaṟku** – taittirīya samhitai 1-6-12-3 says “*nāvēva yāntam ubhayē havantē*” (just as people on both banks \[of a river\] call out to the boatman who is going in the middle of the river, entities in both the vibhūtis call out to the supreme being). In the same way, all the entities in both the vibhūtis call out and praise the most supreme entity.

**aṇi vāṇudal dēvaki** – dēvaki who has a forehead, which is beautiful and is radiant, and which does not require a separate ornament \[to make it appear beautiful\].

**māmagan** – since she had indulged in kaṇṇan a lot, he had grown up with plenty of pride. Those who see him would say that he is not her son, but her brother, since he had such an appearance and power of dominating others. Is it that he had the eminence only through his mother? pāsuram says…

**migusīr vasudēvar tam kōmagan** – \[*kōmagan* – lord\] he became the lord of vasudēvar who was full of qualities appropriate for a doting father. kaṇṇapirān was unlike śrī rāmapirān who was controlled by daśarata cakravarti (emperor), viz. kaṇṇan controlled vasudēvar. How did he control vasudēvar? Since vasudēvar had qualities of a doting father due to his affection for kaṇṇan, he was fearful of what harm would befall kaṇṇan, when he was born, from kamsan. kaṇṇan told him then “Oh father! Why do you fear? I will take care of kamsan. Have confidence in what I am saying and remain comfortable here”, thus telling vasudēvar right after his birth as to what he should do.

**varil** – coming with a gait, typical of both vasudēvar and nandagōpan, since he was the son to both of them.

**kūdidu kūdalē** – you agree \[through your positive indication\] and unite me with him.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.4. Ayccimargalum

*āyccimārgaḷum āyarum anjidappūtta neeḷ kadambēṟip pugap pāynduvāytta kāḷiyan mēl nadamādiyakūttanār varil kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āyccimārgaḷum – covherd women; āyarum­ – covherd men; anjida – to be frightened; pūtta neeḷ kadambēṟi – climbing a tall kadamba (Indian oak) tree which had just blossomed; puga pāyndu – to jump such that \[his divine feet and snake kāḷiyan’s head) immersed in water; vāytta kāḷiyan mēl nadam ādiya – one who danced atop the fortunate kāḷiyan; kūttanār varil – if kaṇṇapirān, who is an expert on the treatise on dancing, were to come; kūdalē – Oh, kūdal!; kūdidu – you indicate positively

**Перевод:**

**Even as the covherd women and men were frightened, kaṇṇapirān climbed a tall kadamba \[Indian oak\] tree, which had just then blossomed and jumped on the head of snake kāḷiyan such that both his divine feet and kāḷiyan’s head immersed in water. He danced on top of that fortunate kāḷiyan. If that expert on dance treatise were to come here, oh kūdal, you should indicate in a positive manner.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – There is no avatārikai for this pāsuram*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**āyccimārgaḷum āyarum anjida** – the womenfolk of covherd clan are by nature always frightened. The menfolk, on the other hand, are never fearful of anything, just as it has been said in [tiruppāvai 18](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-18-undhu-madhakalirran/) “*undu madakaḷiṟṟan ōdāda tōḷ valiyan*” (\[nandagōpan, father of kaṇṇan\] is a strong shouldered person who never runs away from a battlefield and is like an exulting elephant), the menfolk, like nandagōpan, were without any fear. However, both the womenfolk and menfolk were frightened. As mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 12-5 “*pōrkkaḷamāga niruttam seyda poygaikkarai*” (the bank of that river on which he danced such that it appeared like a battlefield), as soon as they head that kaṇṇan jumped on the head of *kāḷiyan*, both the women and men of the clans of covherds swooned down, wherever they were, such that both the bank of the river and āyppādi \[śrī gōkulam\] appeared like a graveyard.

**pūtta neeḷ kadambu** – the *kadamba* tree which had got burnt and dried due to the effect of *kāḷiyan*’s poison, blossomed as soon as the divine feet of kaṇṇan, which shower a rain of nectar, touched it. Would a burnt-out tree blossom as soon as the divine feet touched it? When a mango kernel, which has been applied with a type of oil, is planted, it will immediately sprout into a fully grown tree, yielding mango fruits. The implied meaning is that, if a seed, when planted, immediately starts yielding fruits, a burnt-out tree could also blossom, when touched by the divine feed of emperumān. If a stone could turn into a lady on being touched by the divine feet of śrī rāmapirān \[this narration is related to *ahalyā śāpavimōcanam*, wherein ahalyā, cursed by her husband, sage gautamar, regains her form, on being touched by the divine feet of śrī rāma\], why can’t a burnt-out tree blossom when touched by the divine feet of kaṇṇan?

**neeḷ kadambu** – in order to make it convenient to jump on top of *kāḷiyan*’s head, kaṇṇan climbed to the top of the k*adamba* tree and jumped from there.

**pugap pāyndu**– since kaṇṇan jumped from a height, as he jumped on to the head of *kāḷiyan*, both *kāḷi*yan’s head and kaṇṇan’s divine feet went into the water.

**vāytta kāḷiyan** – kāḷiyan was very fortunate since emperumān repeatedly jumped on his head with his divine feet. āṇḍāḷ’s desire that emperumān should touch her bosoms did not fructify, but *kāḷiyan*, who had not desired to be touched by emperumān, had emperumān’s divine feet dancing on him, several times. īsn’t he fortunate? āṇḍāḷ felt that instead of having been born as a lady and desiring this way, she could have been born as *kāḷiyan*, and even without such a desire, emperumān would have touched her. The head, which stored poison, received distinction of having been touched by emperumān while the bosom, which gives milk of knowledge, did not get that favour.

**kāḷiyan mēl nadamādiya** – did not sages such as bharata et al write down the treatise on the dance form *bharatanātyam* \[a type of classical dance performed in the southern parts of the country\], after they witnessed kaṇṇapirān dance on *kāḷiyan*!

**kūttanār varil** – she desires that kaṇṇan, after dancing atop *kāḷiyan*, should walk with his tender form and embrace her.

**kūdidu kūdalē** – āndāḷ says “I, who am looking up to him, am now looking up to your agreement” \[so that kaṇṇan would embrace me\].

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.5. Mada maligai

*māda māḷigai sūḻ maduraip padinādi nam teruvin naduvē vandittuōdai māmada yānai udaittavankūdumāgil nī kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ōdai – having caparison, the decorative piece on its forehead; māmadam – being in a highly exulted state; yānai – elephant (called as kuvalayāpeedam); udaittavan – kaṇṇapirān, who kicked it and destroyed it; mādam māḷigai sūḻ madurai padi – the city of maturā, which was surrounded by; nādi – searching (our house); nam teruvin naduvē vandittu – coming to the centre of our street; kūdum āgil – if he were to unite (with us); kūdalē – Oh, kūdal; nī kūdidu – you give positive indication

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇapirān kicked and destroyed the elephant, kuvalayāpeedam, which was in a high state of exultation and which was caparisoned. If he were to come looking for our house and were to come to the middle of our street in the town of maturā, which is surrounded by huge mansions and palaces, in order to unite with us, oh kūdal, you should give a positive indication.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells kūdal to indicate positively such that one, who has annihilation of enemies as his nature, comes and unites with her.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**māda māḷigai sūḻ maduraip padi** – keeping the festival of bows as the reason, kamsan \[the king of maturā\] had expanded his city by building many mansions and palaces around it. āṇḍāḷ imagines that kamsan did that so that kaṇṇan would search and look for her, in that place.

**nādi** – searching for her. If he comes to her directly, there will be no interest in it. He should go to every street in the town and search “Where is her house?” such that the entire town will know that “She is dear to kaṇṇan”, search her out so that the town will celebrate it, manifesting the tiredness of having searched her out. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-19-17 says “*kriṣṇarāmau mudā yuktau mālākāragruham gatau* l …… *prasāda paramau nātau mama gēham upāgatau* l *danyō’ham arcayiśyāmītyāha mālyōpajīvana*: ll” (balarāma and kriṣṇa went to the house of *mālākārar* (one who strings and sells garlands), with lot of happiness. *mālākārar,* upon seeing them said “The two of them, with excess of affection, and as two lords, mercifully came to my house. Let me, thankful to them, worship them”) Did not kaṇṇan search out for *mālākārar*, after going to all corners of the town? Since it has been said “They came with lot of happiness” it is evident that they had searched with happiness, through the big streets of the town and since they could not find him, they came to the narrow lanes, found his house there and reached it. Since it has been said “With excess of affection”, though they had both the qualities of *paratvam* and *saulabhyam* (eminence and easy approacability), it is clear that more than *paratvam* it was *saulabhyam* which was the dominant quality, since they came looking for him. Since *mālākārar* says that they came “as two lords”, the quality of *paratvam* was also evident in their nature. How did he know that *saulabhyam* was more dominant than *paratvam* and that *paratvam* was also seen in their nature? This is clear from his statement “They mercifully came to my house”. If *paratvam* had been the more dominant of the two qualities, they would have sent word for him to come to their place. Since he says “thankful to them” it is clear that he considers himself to be fortunate as the treasure \[kriṣṇa and balarāma\], which even eminent people, who search for it, are unable to find it, came looking for him, to his house. Since he says “Let me worship” appropriate to his nature \[of being a servitor\], he would enjoy the treasure which came looking for him, by means such as *arcanam, vandanam* (offering worship, salutation) etc. Through the word *iti* \[as in a*rcayiśyāmi iti …*\], parāśara maharishi \[the sage who composed śrī viṣṇu purāṇam\] indicates as to who had said this – *mālyōpajīvana*: (*mālākārar* who sells garlands and earns a livelihood). Just as those, who are to enjoy their wives, sell them in order to sustain themselves, this *mālākārar* sells garlands which he has to enjoy. The implied meaning here is that the eminence of emperumān’s quality made him to suffer like this. Those who string garlands, will have a desire to enjoy a portion of it. Is it not well known that in order to avoid that, even as they are stringing, they will look in some other direction? Since parāśarar says “*mālākārar* said this” he establishes that kaṇṇan’s coming to see him made the *mālākārar* say these words. Since she \[āṇḍāḷ\] too is the daughter of a *mālākārar*, periyāḻvār \[who was stringing garlands to offer to emperumān\], she thinks that just as kaṇṇan had gone in search of *mālākārar* \[through the streets of maturā\], he will come searching for her too. Did not emperumān desire the garlands which she had donned!

**nam teru** – just as āṇḍāḷ had considered tiruvāyppādi as her place, she considers that she has a divine mansion in maturā too. śrī viṣṇū purāṇam 1-9-145 says “*dēvatvē dēvadēhēyam manuśyatvē ca mānuśī* l *viṣṇōr dēhānurūpām vai karōtyēśātmanastanum* ll” (When \[bhagavān\] incarnates as a celestial entity, this pirātti \[śrī mahāḷakśmi\] takes the form of a celestial entity. When he comes in the form of a *manuśya* \[human\] she comes as a *manuśyai*. She makes her forms appropriate to viṣṇu’s forms). When she takes forms appropriate to his, there is nothing amazing when she desires to have a mansion in the places he comes to, with happiness.

**naduvē vandittu** – she desires that when he comes searching for her, he should come to the street where she resides, without anyone knowing about it, and then he should enter her mansion, with everyone wondering about it.

**ōdai** – this refers to the decorative caparison, which is placed on the face of an elephant.

**māmada yānai** – it is indicated that kamsan had given the elephant certain materials which will make it to exult excessively.

**māmada yānai udaittavan** – the elephant was arrogant due to its state of exultation. kaṇṇan kicked it such that its arrogance and brashness went out, along with its life.

**kūdumāgil nī kūdidu kūdalē** – emperumān has as his nature, annihilation of his enemies. He has destroyed the elephant which was a hurdle between her and him. She tells *kūdal* “You should show positive indication and unite him with me”

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.6. Arravan marudam

*aṟṟavan marudam muṟiya nadaikaṟṟavan kanjanai vanjanaiyilseṟṟavan tigaḻum maduraippadikoṟṟavan varil kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aṟṟavan – one who is there for me (since time immemorial); marudam muṟiya – such that the two maruda trees \[Indian laurel; also known as yamalārjuna trees\] snapped and fell down; nadai kaṟṟavan – one who learnt to crawl; kanjanai – kamsan; vanjanaiyil – through deceit; seṟṟavan – one who destroyed; tigaḻum madurai padi koṟṟavan – kaṇṇapirān, who is the king of the great town of; varil – if he were to come; kūdalē – Oh, kūdal!; kūdidu – you agree

**Перевод:**

**He has been there for me, all the time. He learnt to crawl by moving between two maruda \[yamalārjuna\] trees, making them to snap and fall down. He destroyed the demon kamsan, through deceit. He is the king of the great town of maturā, which is radiant. Oh kūdal! If he were to come here, you indicate your agreement.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – She tells kūdal “Oh kūdal! You should indicate positively such that the one, who incarnated only for me, unites with me”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**aṟṟavan** – he has been always there for me. Did he not incarnate only for her sake? Has he \[kaṇṇan\] himself not mercifully said in bhagavat gītā 4-8 “*paritrāṇāya sādūnām viṇāśāya ca dukrutām* l *dharma samstāpanārtāya sambhavāmi yugē yugē* ll” (I keep incarnating in every yugam (epoch) in order to protect the good entities, to destroy the wicked and to establish the path of righteousness)?

**marudam muṟiya nadai kaṟṟavan** – even at a young age, when he did not know about himself, he had crawled between two *maruda* trees which two monsters had pervaded, killing them. Hence, there is no need to fear about enemies. It has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-6-16 “*vyagrāyāmata tasyām sa karśamāṇa ulūkhalam* l *yamalārjunayōr madyē jagāma kamalēkśaṇa*: ll *karśatā vrukśayōr madyē tiryaggatamulūkhalam* l *bhagnāvuttungaśākhāgrau tēna tau yamalārjunau* ll” (When that yaśōdāp was engaged with some other work, kaṇṇan, who has lotus-like eyes, dragged the mortar \[to which he had been tied\] between the two yamalārjuna trees. When he went between the trees, the mortar rolled between them. The two trees, which had tall branches, were snapped by that mortar).

**kanjanai vanjanaiyil seṟṟavan** – since kamsan had a wicked mind, kaṇṇan destroyed him with deceit. If he had been like *cakravartit tirumagan* (śrī rāma, the divine of emperor daśarata) trying to kill him through the righteous means, that would not have fructified in kamsan’s case; hence, he killed him in a deceitful manner. He made the deceit, which kamsan tried on him, to return to kamsan and destroy him.

**tigaḻum maduraip padik koṟṟavan** – king of radiant maturā which is surrounded with mansions and palaces. Wasn’t ugrasēnan \[grandfather of kaṇṇan\], the king of maturā? Due to the curse on the clan of yādavas, kaṇṇan had crowned ugrasēnan as the king of maturā. However, in āṇḍāḷ’s opinion, since ugrasēnan was crowned by kaṇṇan and the kingdom was obtained with the help of kaṇṇan, she considers kaṇṇan as the king of maturā.

**varil kūdidu kūdalē** – Oh *kūdal*! You should agree such that he comes. There is normally no need to implore kings. Even if there is a necessity, you should implore him to come here.

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.7. Anru innadana

*anṟu innādana sey sisupālanumninṟa neeḷ marudum erudum puḷḷumvenṟi vēl viṟaṟ kanjanum vīḻa munkonṟavan varil kūdidu kūdlē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – at an earlier time; innādana sey sisupālanum – śiśupālan who was engaging with abominable activities; ninṟa neeḷ marudum – the two huge maruda trees which stood in the way; erudum – the seven oxen; puḷḷum – bakāsuran \[another monster\]; venṟivēl viral kanjanum – kamsan who had a spear which always brought victory and had strength; vīḻa – to perish and fall down; mun – in front of everyone; konṟavan – kaṇṇapirān, who killed \[all of them\]; varil – if he were to come; kūdalē – Oh kūdal!; kūdidu – you agree

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇapirān, in earlier times, had destroyed śiśupālan, who was indulging in abominable activities, the two maruda trees, which stood in his way, seven oxen, monster bakāsuran \[who came in the form of a stork\] and kamsan, who had a victorious spear and great strength. Oh, kūdal! If that kaṇṇan were to come here, you agree.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells kūdal to agree such that kaṇṇan, who annihilated all his enemies, comes.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**anṟu innādana sey sisupālanum** – the implied meaning is that just as hurdles which came in the way of *svayamvaram* \[an event where a lady chooses a husband of her choice from among assembled kings\] of rukmiṇi\[ pirātti \[śrī mahālakśmi\] by the abominable activities indulged in by śiśupālan were removed, now also, the hurdles which prevent our \[āṇḍāḷ with kaṇṇan\] union should disappear.

**ninṟa neeḷ marudum** – not being satisfied with sending several monsters individually, to kill kaṇṇan, kamsan had ordained two monsters to stand in the way of kaṇṇan when he would come crawling, so that they could kill him. āṇḍāḷ is frightened as to what would happen to kaṇṇan if they had harmed him together. Even though there is a *purāṇic* \[as told in old treatises\] story that two persons, naḷa and kūbara had been turned into two trees due to a curse, hasn’t periyāḻvār mercifully mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-1-3 “*poymmāya marudāna asurarai*” that two monsters had pervaded the two trees \[in order to kill kaṇṇan\]?

**neeḷ marudum** – they were so tall that if they fell on someone, there would have been no escape.

**erudum** – seven oxen which were kept as the wager \[for marrying nappinnaip pirātti\]. While the *maruda* trees were only two in number, here there were seven monsters who had pervaded the seven oxen, who were waiting to trouble kaṇṇan.

**puḷḷum** – bakāsuran, the monster, who came in the form of a stork. Mentioning a few such monstrous people here is representative of all the monsters whom kamsan had sent, continuously, in order to kill kaṇṇan.

**venṟi vēl viral kanjanum** – one who had a spear which had known only victory, until then. kamsan who had that spear, and who had sufficient strength in himself such that there was no need for that spear too.

**kanjanum** – kamsan, who had sent all the monsters mentioned previously, to kill kaṇṇan and who was their head.

**vīḻa mun konṟavan** – the way that kaṇṇan killed all these entities, one after another, with such speed, that it appeared that he had killed them all in one stroke.

**varil kūdidi kūdalē** – destroying all these entities, who were hurdles for her \[āṇḍāḷ\], it appeared that kaṇṇan was ready to attain āṇḍāḷ. She tells *kūdal* that if it agrees, then they could unite.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.8. Aval anbudaiyar

*āval anbudaiyār tam manattanṟimēvalan, viraiyār sūḻ tuvarāpadikkāvalan, kanṟu mēyttu viḷaiyādumkōvalan varil kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āval anbu udaiyār tam – those who have eagerness and affection; manattanṟi mēvalan – one who will not fit with any place other than their hearts; virai sūḻ tuvarāpadi kāvalan – one who is the protector of the fragrant dvārakā; kanṟu mēyttu viḷaiyādum kōvalan – kaṇṇapariān who is the protector of cattle, who plays with calves and grazes the cattle; varil – if he were to come; kūdidu – you should agree

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇan does not fit well with any place other than the hearts of those who have eagerness and affection towards him. He is the protector of dvārakā which is fragrant. He is the protector of cattle and calves, who enjoys playing with them. If that kaṇṇan were to come here, you have to agree.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āndāḷ says “There is no deficiency in me for eagerness and affection. There is no deficiency in him for protection and easy approacability. Oh kūdal!  Your disagreement appears to be the hurdle”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**āval udaiyār** – who are the people having eagerness? sītāp pirātti had said “He is complete with all the auspicious qualities. Hence, he will certainly accept us. It is only proper for us to wait till he comes” Though one may have complete faith in \[emperumān as\] the means, due to excessive affection that one should attain him, one can engage with rituals such as observing fast, taking refuge under *kāman* \[nācciyār tirumoḻi 1<sup>st</sup> decad\], building toy-houses \[nācciyār tirumoḻi 2<sup>nd</sup> decad\], taking bath in cold water before break of dawn \[nācciyār tirumoḻi 3<sup>rd</sup> decad\], drawing circles \[this decad\] etc, which are against one’s *svarūpam* \[basic nature of being totally dependent on emperumān\]. Those who engage with such rituals are called as *āval udaiyār*.

**anbudaiyār** – these are people who engage with singing “*mallāṇḍa tiṇḍōḷ maṇivaṇṇā*” \[Oh one with the complexion of gemstone! Your firm shoulders are strong, having defeated wrestlers) caring for the well-being of emperumān, due to misplaced sense of fear; or singing as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-2-9 “*uṟagal uṟagal*”, praying to nityasūris, who never sleep, to protect emperumān, without sleeping. Thus, these are people who have overflowing affection towards emperumān.

**tam manattanṟi mēlavan** – emperumān will not fit with anyone other than those who are in the clan of āḻvārs, who come under the categories of *āval udaiyār* and *anbu udaiyār*. Since she too comes in that clan, āṇḍāḷ points out that there is no shortcoming in her with regard to eagerness and affection. Now, she says that there is no shortcoming in him \[emperumān\] too, with regard to his qualities of protection and easy approacability.

**virai sūḻ tuvarāpadik kāvalan** – one who married sixteen thousand noble ladies and protected them too, in dvārakā. Has it not been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 31-17 & 18 “*tā*: *kanyā narakēṇāsan sarvatō yās samāhrutā*: l *ēkasminnēva gōvinda*: *kālē tāsām mahāmunē* ll *jagrāha vidivat pāṇīn prutag gēhēśu darmata*: l *śōdaśastrī sahasrāṇi śatamēkam tatō’dikam* ll” (Oh sage! kriṣṇa, at the same time, married all the sixteen thousand one hundred women, who were imprisoned by the monster *naraka,* individually, at their respective house (by taking as many forms)). Even if they are noble ladies, with high degree of fragrance, if he could unite with them who do not have the eagerness and overflowing affection which I \[āṇḍāḷ\] have, should I even mention about his desire to unite with me?

**virai sūḻ tuvarāpadi** – apart from the original fragrance of that place, didn’t kaṇṇan bring the fragrant *pārijātam* (night-flowering jasmine) tree from heaven? Has it not been said in periya tirumoḻi 6-8-7 “*mattēṟu kaṟpagattai mādarkkāy vaṇ tuvarai nattān*” (He \[kaṇṇan\] planted the *kaṟpaka* (wish-fulfilling) tree, which was full of honey, in the beautiful dvārakā))? The term *tuvarāpadi* could be construed as the protector \[*padi*\] of *tuvarai*, \[shortened\] name for dvārakā. The term *kāvalan* \[which follows this verse\] could be construed as one who creates and destroys the worlds, without any effort, and protects them too.

**kāvalan kanṟu mēyttu viḷaiyādum kōvalan** – śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-6-35 says “*kālēna gaccatā tau tu saptavarśa mahāvrajē* l *sarvasya jagata*: *pālau vatsapālau babhūvatu* ll” (With passing of time in āyppādi, balarāma and kriṣṇa, who were seven years of age, despite being the protectors of entire worlds, became protectors of cows). As said in this, instead of protecting those who are capable of protecting themselves, kaṇṇan was very happy protecting calves which did not have the capability to protect themselves. Just as nammāḻvār had said in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-3-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/11/thiruvaimozhi-10-3-10-avaththangal-vilaiyum/) “*tivattilum pasu nirai mēyppu uvatti*”, he was happier, protecting cows and calves, than protecting nityasūris. Other āḻvārs too, such as tirumangai āḻvār, as in [tirunedundāṇḍagam 16](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/08/thirunedunthandakam-16/) “*kanṟu mēyttu iniduganda kāḷāy*” (One who was happy grazing calves) said that he was joyous, grazing calves.

**kōvalan** – instead of having to put up with the heavy task of ruling dvārakā, he preferred coming to tiruvāyppādi in order to play with calves.

**varil kūdidu kūdalē** – thus, there is no deficiency in me, for eagerness and overflowing affection; there is no deficiency in him for protection and easy approacability. Hence, oh *kūdal*! You agree and unite us.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 4.9. Konda kola

*koṇḍa kōlak kuṟaḷ uruvāyc cenṟupaṇḍu māvali tan peru vēḷviyilaṇḍamum nilanum adi onṟinālkoṇḍavan varil kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇḍu – in an earlier time; kōlam koṇḍa kuṟaḷ uruvāy – decorating (with sacred thread, deer skin, waist reed, finger reed and stick) in the form of vāmana (a brāhmaṇa bachelor); māvali than peru vēḷviyil senṟu – mercifully reaching the grand arena where mahābali was conducting a ritual; aṇḍamum – upper worlds; nilanum – lower worlds; adi onṟināl – each with a step; koṇḍavan – one who measured; varil – if he were to come; kūdalē – Oh kūdal!; kūdidu – you should agree

**Перевод:**

**Simple ṭranslationi**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – Oh kūdal! You must agree so that the one, who measured everything as his possession, should accept me also, as his possession.*

Oh *kūdal*! In an earlier time, emperumān had decorated himself in the form of vāmana and reached the grand arena where mahābali was conducting a ritual. He had measured all the upper worlds and the lower worlds, each with one step. If that supreme entity were to come here, you should agree.

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**koṇḍa kōlak kuṟaḷuruvāy** – the way he had decorated himself as a bachelor vāmana, with the three stringed sacred thread, deerskin \[attached to that thread\], reed on his waist, reed on his finger and the stick \[*palāsa daṇḍam* carried by bachelors), he appeared to be more beautiful than parama pada nātan, who has decorated himself with divine weapons and divine ornaments.

**senṟu** – reaching that place, with those who were passing by that way, engaging with the beauty of his gait.

**paṇḍu** – āndāḷ is praising that time when “All the people in the world were fortunate to be touched by his divine feet”

**māvali than peru vēḷviyil** – unlike her present situation where, despite engaging with *kūdal*, she was unable to have *darśan* \[seeing\] of emperumān, amidst the ritual, as a benefit of the ritual, everyone had the fortune to see him.

**aṇḍamum nilanum adi onṟināl koṇḍavan** – he measured all the upper worlds with one step and all the lower worlds, with another step, thus measuring the entire leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) since they were all his possessions, with two steps.

**varil kūdidu kūdalē** – since he is one who will not let go of his possession, there is no hurdle in his recognising me \[as his possession\]. The only deficiency is in your not agreeing \[by coming up with even number of circles\].

The *vyākyātā* (commentator\], periyavāccān piḷḷai, has analysed extensively about emperumān measuring the worlds, here – were they two steps or was there a third step, and if there had been a third step, what did that measure?

yajur aśtakam 2-4-5 says “*trīṇi padā vicakramē*” (trivikraman, with three steps, occupied \[the worlds\]); periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-10 says “*mūnṟadi nimirttu*” (taking three steps). nanjīyar, one of our preceptors, asked his ācāryan (teacher), bhattar “When vēdam and āḻvārs have said that emperumān had measured the worlds with three steps, how is it correct here when this pāsuram says that he measured with two steps?” bhattar responded to him saying yajur aśtakam 2-8-10 says “*parō mātrayā tanuvā vrudāna* l *na tē mahitvamanvaśnuvati* l *ubhē tē vidma rajasīprutivyā*: l *viṣṇō dēvatvam paramasya vitsē* ll” (Oh viṣṇu! You, being very eminent, had expanded yourself with a huge form. (At that time) There was none who could measure your greatness and who could enjoy you. We know the two steps which you had taken in the sky and on the earth \[earth, here, refers to all the worlds below the sky\]. Perhaps, you alone would know where you had kept the third step!). *vēda puruśan* (vēhdam itself) had said “All entities knew that you had measured the upper and lower worlds with two steps; only you knew as to what you had measured with the third step, none else would know it”. However, since all the authentic texts say that he measured the worlds with three steps, it is true that he had measured with the third step too. Only he knew as to what he had measured.

It is pertinent to recall this conversation \[between bhattar and nanjīyar\] here.

Next, we will consider the 10<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.10. Palagu nanmarai

*paḻagu nānmaṟaiyin poruḷāy madamoḻugu vāraṇam uyya aḷitta emaḻaganār aṇi āycciyar sindaiyuḷ                                                                                                          kuḻaganār varil kūdidu kūdalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

paḻagu nān maṟayin porul āy – being the inner meaning of the four vēdams which have been in existence since time immemorial; madam oḻugu vāraṇam uyya aḷitta – one who showered mercy on gajēndrāḻvān who was secreting water of exultation excessively, to live (after ridding him of his suffering); em aḻaganār – one who has the beauty to make me engage with him; aṇi āycciyar sindaiyuḷ kuḻaganār – kaṇṇapirān, who has melted inside the hearts of the beautiful covherd women; varil – if he were to come; kūdalē – Oh kūdal!; kūdidu – you agree

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇapirān is the inner meaning of the four vēdams \[sacred texts\] which have been in existence since time immemorial. He showered his grace on the elephant gajēndrāḻvān, who was secreting water of exultation excessively, such that the elephant could get rid of his suffering and live. He has the beauty to make me engage with him. He remains inside the hearts of covherd womenfolk, in a melted state. If he were to come here, oh kūdal, you should agree.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – There is no shortcoming in emperumān protecting us too, when he had protected even gajēndrāḻvān, an elephant, who had no control over his mind and sensory organs. She tells kūdal to agree so that he would come.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**paḻagu nānmaāṟayin poruḷāy** – since time immemorial, the four vēdams have been taught with the ācāryan (teacher) reciting the verse once and the disciple repeating it twice, not having been written and passed from one generation to the next, through word of mouth. īsn’t emperumān, the meaning of all the words in the four vēdams? Hasn’t emperumān himself declared in bhagavat gītā 15-15 “*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya*” (It is only I, who is known through all the vēdams)? One may ask – how are cētana entities \[souls with knowledge\] and acētana entities \[souls without knowledge\] being spoken of in vēdams, if emperumān alone is the meaning of vēdams? Since both cētana and acētana entities cannot have existence without him, being his *śarīram* (physical form), it could be said that if one were to know him, these two entities will become known through him. cāndōjña upaniśat 6-1-4 says “*yadā sōmyaikēna mrut piṇḍēna sarvam mruṇmayam vijñātam syāt*” (Oh child! If one were to know about mud, all articles made from mud such as pot, lid etc will also be known. In the same way, if brahmam (supreme entity) is known, all the worlds created from brahmam will also be considered as known). Just as upaniśat has said, if brahmam were known, all the worlds, which never separate from it and which are his physical body, will also be known. Thus, there is no deficiency in saying that emperumān is the inner meaning of all the vēdams.

**madam oḻugu vāraṇam** – did emperumān protect an entity which had qualities of control over its mind and sensory organs? Did he not uplift a male elephant which had no control over its sensory organs, was in exultation and was uniting with female elephants? The underlying meaning here is – what a simplicity does emperumān have!

**uyya** – to get uplifted from the sorrow of having been caught in the mouth of a crocodile. Alternatively, from the sorrow of having been unable to proffer the lotus at the divine feet of emperumān.

**aḷitta em aḻaganār** – apart from ridding him of his sorrow, emperumān also manifested his beauty and protected the elephant. parāśara bhattar says in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-59 “*śrīrangēśaya*! *śaraṇam mamāsi vādyāvyālōlat kamala tatāka tāṇḍavēna* l *srag bhūśāmbara mayatāyatam dadāna*: *dingmāmiti anugajagarjamājaganta* ll” (Oh, śrī ranganāta! As soon as you heard the cry of gajēndaran, you blamed yourself saying “My bad! What did I do?” donning your divine garlands, divine ornaments and divine dress in a haphazard manner, and mercifully came, beautifully, just like a lotus pond agitates in furious wind. Such an entity like you alone would be this servitor’s \[bhattar, referring to himself\] refuge). As mentioned in this, despite donning his divine garland, divine ornaments and divine dresses in a haphazard manner, he showed his beauty and protected the elephant.

**aṇi āycciyar sindaiyuḷ kuḻaganār** – one who melts the hearts of beautiful covherd women.

**aṇi āycciyar** – covherd women folk who are beautiful. What is their beauty? Just as āṇḍāḷ herself has said in nācciyār tirumoḻi 8-7 “*kongaimēl kungamattin kuḻambaḻiyap pugundu, oru nāḷ tangumēl ennāvi tangumenṟu uraiyīrē*” (Tell that emperumān that I can sustain myself only if he unites with me for a day in such a manner that the vermillion paste applied on my bosoms gets destroyed), they can sustain only if embraced by him and if he doesn’t help them, they will engage with extreme activities such as carrying out *madal* (complaining publicly about the beloved, beseeching the town-folk to unite them) etc and being firm that they will not show their faces to him even if he comes \[later\].

**sindaiyuḷ kuḻaganār** – one who is capable of being humble, uniting with those who had separated after uniting, touching their heads with his beautiful, divine feet and lotus-like hands, saying words like *adiyēn, kudiyēn* (words which indicate being obedient).

**kuḻaganār** – we could consider this to mean one who melts their hearts.

**varil kūdidu kūdalē** – if he were to remove my sorrow too, just as he removed the sorrows of covherd womenfolk, you indicate through your agreement.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 4.11. Udal kudal

*ūdal kūdal uṇardal puṇardalainīdu ninṟa niṟai pugaḻ āycciyarkūdalaik kuḻaṟ kōdai mun kūṟiyapādal pattum vallārkku illai pāvamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ūdal kūdal – being together with ūdal (love-quarrel between beloveds); uṇardal – pointing out the deficiencies; puṇardal – (later) engaging with union; mun – for a long time; nīdu ninṟa – lasting, fitting well; niṟai pugaḻ āycciyar kūdalai – about the kūdal engaged with by the covherd girls, who have complete eminence; kuḻal kōdai – āṇḍāḷ, who has beautiful locks of hair; kūṟiya – composed mercifully; pādal pattum – the ten pāsurams; vallārkku – those who are capable of reciting; pāvam illai – there is no sin (of suffering due to separation from emperumān)

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ has sung about kūdal, engaged with by the women folk of covherd clan, who have been having eminence for a long time, who are together with love-quarrel with emperumān, point out his deficiencies, then unite with him. Those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams, composed by āṇḍāḷ who has beautiful locks of hair, will not suffer from the sin of separating from emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āṇḍāḷ brings the decad to an end by mentioning the benefits which will accrue to those who are capable of reciting this decad.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ūdal kūdal** – being together with *ūdal*, which is a love-quarrel between the beloveds.

**uṇardal** – pointing out his mistake saying “You harassed us, by separating from us”. The word *uṇardal* is a transformation of the word *uṇarttal*, which indicates hinting at or pointing out to.

**puṇardalai** – \[kaṇṇan is\] pacifying them saying “I separated from you all, only to nurture your love towards me”. He offers his apology and unites with them.

**mun nīdu ninṟa niṟai pugaḻ āycciyar kūdalai** – the pāsurams which talk about the *kūdal* engaged with by the covherd women folk, who have total eminence of engaging in love-quarrel and subsequently uniting with kaṇṇan as their daily chore. What is total eminence for these women? embār, one of our preceptors and one of the prime disciples of rāmānujar, would explain it as follows: one covherd girl did not show her face to kaṇṇan for four days and made him to undertake fasting for four days; another girl did the same, for ten days. This is their eminence.

**kuḻaṟkōdai kūṟiya** – while she is capable of making him to engage with *kūdal* in order to attain her, just by the beauty of her locks of hair, she had to compose these pāsurams as an engagement with *kūdal*.

**pādal pattum vallārkku illai pāvamē** – for those who are capable of learning these ten pāsurams, there will be no suffering such as engaging with *kūdal*, which āṇḍāḷ had to suffer. By merely learning this decad, they will attain the benefit of uniting with kriṣṇan.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of fifth decad.
