## 5.11. Vinnura nindu

*viṇṇuṟa nīṇḍu adi tāviya maindanai vēṟkaṇ madandai virumbikaṇṇuṟa en kadal vaṇṇanaik kūvu karum kuyilē enṟa māṟṟampaṇṇuṟu nānmaṟaiyōr puduvai mannan bhattarpiṟan kōdai sonnanaṇṇuṟu vāsaga mālai vallār namō nārāyaṇāya enbārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēl kaṇ – having eyes which are like spear; madandai – having qualities which are appropriate for women; maṇṇuṟu nānmaṟaiyōr puduvai mannan bhattar pirān kōdai – āndāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the head of śrīvilliputtūr, which is inhabited by śrīvaiṣṇavas who recite vēdams which are composed with tunes; adi viṇ uṟa nīṇḍu tāviya maindanai – emperumān who has strength such that he pervaded all the worlds when his divine foot extended up to paramākāśam (supreme worlds); virumbi – desiring; “karum kuyilē! en kadal vaṇṇanai kaṇ uṟa kūvu” enṟa māṟṟam – pāsuram as ordained by āṇḍāḷ saying “Oh dark cuckoo! You call out to my beloved who has the divine complexion of ocean such that I could worship him”; sonna – mercifully said; naṇṇuṟu vāsaga mālai – this śrī sūktimālai (divine garland of pāsurams) which spoke well (about emperumān); vallār – those who are capable of reciting; namō nārāyaṇāya enbār – they will attain emperumān as his confidential servitors

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the head of śrīvilliputtūr, inhabited by śrīvaiṣṇavas who recite the four vēdams, which are based on music. She has eyes which are like spear and is complete with feminine qualities. She desired emperumān who had the strength to grow such that his divine foot extended up to the supreme skies and who filled up the worlds. Those who are capable of reciting these well-spoken pāsurams which she mercifully composed, addressing a cuckoo to call out to that emperumān such that she could worship him, will get to be the confidential servitors to that emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āṇḍāḷ brings the decad to an end by saying that “Those who learn this tirumoḻi (divine verses) with their meanings, will get to carry out kainkaryam appropriate to their svarūpam (basic nature)”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**viṇṇuṟa nīṇḍu adi tāviya maindanai** – emperumān who had the strength to grow as trivikraman, measuring the three worlds with his two steps. *maindu* – strength. Once emperumān decides to take something from someone, he will ensure that they will not desire anything in that material, so that he could possess it fully.

**vēl kaṇ madandai virumbi** – she, who had eyes which were like spear, with which she could fell him by merely looking at him, and who was a noble lady, lost out to him and desired him. She says that even as I have eyes which are like spears stacked together, I lost out to him

**kaṇṇuṟa** – such that I could see him to the satisfaction of my eyes. She is trying to make him a target to her eyes.

**kaṇṇuṟa en kadal vaṇṇanai** – he has a divine form which has the qualities of an ocean and which removes the ennui of those who look at him. My eyes desire to see such emperumān.

**kūvu karunguyilē enṟa māṟṟam** – “By displaying your \[dark\] form to me, you reminded me of his divine form. You should call out such that he and I unite”. This decad deals with such pāsurams which relate to this theme.

**paṇṇuṟu nānmaṟaiyōr** – inhabited by experts in the four vēdams which contain musical notes through which the vēdams are recited by the teacher once and twice by the students learning from the teacher.

**puduvai mannan bhattar pirān kōdai sonna** – spoken mercifully by āṇḍāḷ, who is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, the head of śrīvilliputtūr.

**naṇṇuṟu vāsaga mālai vallār** – those who learn this garland of words, with meaning, which speak well about emperumān. taittirīya upaniśat ānadavalli 9-1 says “*yatō vācō nivartantē* l *aprāpya manasā saha* l *ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān* l *na bibhēti kutaścanēti* ll” (words, along with mind \[of vēhdam\], went to estimate emperumān’s bliss, but returned being unable to measure it. One who knows the happiness of that emperumān will not fear anything or anyone). Unlike vēdam which returned, being unable to speak about that emperumān, these ten pāsurams have the eminence of having spoken about him completely.

**namō nārāyaṇāya enbarē** – In the latter sentence of *dvaya mahāmantram* \[one of the three esoteric verses\], the greatest servitude to the supreme entity, which is carried out for the happiness of the supreme entity has been mentioned. Those who recite these ten pāsurams will get to attain that servitude in parama padam. Did not periyāḻvār too mention the praising of emperumān in paramapadam, as mentioned in the [12](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-12-pallandu-enru/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-12-pallandu-enru/) [pāsuram of tiruppallāṇḍu](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-12-pallandu-enru/) “*namō nārāyaṇāya enṟu paḷḷāṇḍum paramātmanaic cūḻndirundēttuvar pallāṇḍē*” as the benefit of reciting tiruppallāṇḍu? īsn’t this the benefit desired by all the āḻvārs who were born in the clan of *anjukudi* (those who worry about the safety of emperumān, when there is no need)? āṇḍāḷ, who also incarnated in this clan, states this as the benefit for those who learn this decad. cāndōjña upaniśat 3-1-14 says “*yatākraturasmin lōkē puruśō bhavati, tatēta*: *prētya bhavati*” (an *upāsakan* (one who has undertaken the path of devotion to attain emperumān) will attain the benefit in the upper world, in the same way in which he worships here). Those who recite *pallāṇḍu* to emperumān with affection in this word, will attain the same benefit of reciting *pallāṇḍu* in parama padam too. In this world which is affected by sleep, disease, hunger, old age etc, *pallāṇḍu* could be recited only on a few occasions. However, in parama padam which is not afflicted with any of these, *pallāṇḍu* could be sung all the time. The underlying meaning here is that those who recite this decad will reach parama padam which is such an exalted place to attain, and sing *namō nārāyaṇāya*, enjoying emperumān, as the benefit.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the sixth decad.

## 6.1. Varanam ayiram

*vāraṇam āyiram sūḻa valam seydunāraṇan nambi nadakkinṟān enṟu edirpūraṇa poṟkudam vaittup puram engumtōraṇam nāttak kaṇak kaṇḍēn tōḻī nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻī – Oh, my dear friend!; nambi – one who is complete in all the auspicious qualities; nāraṇan – śrīman nārāyaṇan; āyiram vāraṇam sūḻa – surrounded by a thousand elephants; valam seydu nadakkinṟān enṟu – is mercifully coming in the clockwise direction (affirmed by the music from nādasvaram \[a type of wind instrument, similar to flute\]); edir – opposite \[to his direction of arrival\]; pon pūraṇa kudam vaittu – keeping golden pots filled with water \[as a mark of welcome\]; puram engum – throughout the town; tōraṇam nātta – welcome arches being erected; kanāk kaṇḍēn – I enjoyed in my dream

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my dear friend! I saw in my dream that śrīman nārāyaṇan, complete in all the auspicious qualities was coming in a clockwise direction, surrounded by a thousand elephants. He was received with due respect, by being offered water in golden pots. Throughout the town, welcome arches have been erected \[to celebrate his arrival\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – Since she will not be satisfied if she just enjoys him after he comes \[to the place where she is staying\], she wants to enjoy from the time when kaṇṇan comes, in her dream, to the place where she is staying, with the people of the town telling one another that kaṇṇan has come to marry her, and the way the streets are decorated when he comes walking.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vāraṇam āyiram sūḻa valam seydu** – in tiruvāyppādi \[śrī gōkulam\], only cows and calves are roaming. Where did elephants come from? Since āṇḍāḷ herself has mercifully said in tiruppāvai pāsuram 18 “*undu madakaḷiṟṟan*”, with nandagōpar \[head of covherds and father of kriṣṇa\] coming on elephants to oppose his enemies, there is no issue in his son coming, surrounded by a thousand elephants. It is also well known that śrī nandagōpar often roams around the place, riding an elephant. How did nandagōpar get an army of elephants? Just as śrī rāma had accepted sugrīva as a dear friend, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 5-18 “*tvam vayasyō’si mē hrudyō hyēkam du:kkam sukhanca nau* l *sugrīvam rāghavō vākyam ityuvāca* *prahruśtavat* ll” (śrī rāma told sugrīva, with lot of happiness “You have become a very dear friend to me. For the two of us, happiness and sorrow will be the same, henceforth”) since vasudēvar and nandagōpar are close friends, the wealth of nandagōpar’s cows was also the wealth of vasudēvar’s and the wealth of vasudēvar’s army of elephants and horses was also the wealth of nandagōpar’s. During the time when vasudēvar was imprisoned by kamsan, it is possible that his elephants were left with nandagōpar. Moreover, for children taken in adoption, there is a right on the wealth of both parents \[the one who had sired and the one who nourished\]. Hence, by this manner also, there is no issue with elephants coming with kaṇṇan, during his marriage. What is the reason for saying “*a thousand elephants*”? Just as it has been said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-1-1 “*tannēr āyiram piḷḷaigaḷōdu taḷar nadai ittu varuvān*” (kaṇṇan will come together with his thousand friends who are like him, after grazing the cows), since kaṇṇan would not bear any \[even unintentional\] slighting of his friends, he would have organised for a thousand elephants such that each of those thousand friends too could sit atop an elephant. tirumangai āḻvār had mercifully said in periya tirumoḻi 11-3-5 ”*tammiaiyē nāḷum vaṇangit toḻuvārkkut tammaiyē okka aruḷ seyvar*” (emperumān will mercifully make those who worship him every day, to be like him). īsn’t emperumān one who grants eminence to those who worship him constantly, matching his?

**nāraṇa nambi** – when kaṇṇan, who is the leader of tiruvāyppādi and maturā, came, it appeared that it was śrīman nārāyaṇan, the lord of both nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm) and leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) who was coming.

**nambi** – one who is complete with all auspicious qualities. In his supreme state, being the lord of both nitya vibhūti and leelā vibhūti, we have heard from śāstrams \[sacred texts\] that he is full of auspicious qualities. But we have not enjoyed those qualities. It is only during *vibhavāvatāram* (incarnations such as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al) that we could enjoy those qualities. Moreover, in paramapadam, where he lets nityasūris and muktātmās (those who had got liberated from samsāram and had reached parama padam) to enjoy his auspicious qualities, his qualities such as *saulabhyam* (easy approacability), *sauśeelyam* (simplicity, where a superior entity becomes friendly with a lowly entity) etc do not get manifested. These qualities shine explicitly only when he offers himself to the deficient samsāris, just like a bright lamp lights up a dark room. His qualities such as honesty etc can be seen only in samsāram, with those who do not have honesty, since in paramapadam, there is no dishonest person. Thus, the word *nambi* indicates the completeness of qualities in vibhavāvatāram.

**nadakkiṟān enṟu** – Just as it has been said in mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 73-1 “*kuśastalē nivasati sa ca prātarihēśyati*” (bhagavān kriṣṇan has mercifully reached kuśastalam (an intermediate place) today. He will mercifully come here tomorrow morning), the news about his arrival to those who are favourable towards him, will be an aid in their sustaining themselves. Unlike viśvāmitrar, who was capable of carrying out any task as mentioned in the verse ”*śāpādapi śarādapi*” ((I am capable of completing a task) through curse or arrows), had to bring śrī rāma towards sītāp pirātti, without the need for anyone like that, kaṇṇan came on his own towards āṇḍāḷ.

**edir pūraṇa poṟkudam vaittu** – keeping pots with water, opposite to him \[to welcome him\]

**puram engum tōraṇam nātta** – just as it has been mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-9-2-naranan-thamarai/) “*tōraṇam niṟaittu engum toḻudanar*” (people erected welcome arches everywhere and vorśipped), when muktātmās ascend to paramapadam, those who are already in paramapadam will erect arches and celebrate their arrival. In the same way, when the head of paramapadam comes here, people here will erect welcome arches. *puram* – town. If the text is considered as *puṟam*, it would refer to orchards at the periphery of the town.

**kanāk kaṇḍēn** – during the time when we are awake, there are many matters which keep coming to us through our five sensory perceptions for us to enjoy, just like window in the house which allows rays of sun to come inside the house. However, since there are many matters to be dealt with simultaneously by the sensory organs, it is difficult to enjoy them properly. However, in a dream, only one matter comes to us and hence it is possible to enjoy it in a focussed manner. āṇḍāḷ says that she saw this \[kaṇṇan coming to the town in a grand manner\] happening in her dream and enjoyed it.

**kaṇḍēn** – instead of hearing from affectionate mothers or from you, my dear friend, I saw it myself such that I could reveal it to you.

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.2. Nalai vaduvai

*nāḷai vaduvai maṇamenṟu nāḷittupālai kamugu parisudaip pandaṟkīḻkōḷari mādavan gōvindan enbān ōrkāḷai pugudak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻī! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻī! – Oh, my friend!; nāḷai – tomorrow; vaduvai maṇam enṟu nāḷ ittu – establishing the muhūrtam (auspicious time) for the grand wedding celebration; pāḷai kamugu parisu udai pandal kīḻ – under the maṇappandal \[decorated dais for conducting the marriage\] which has decorations of areca plants with spates in them; kōḷari mādavan gōvindan enbān ōr kāḷai – a youth having divine names such as narasimhan, mādavan and gōvindan; puguda – to enter; nān kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my friend! The auspicious time for the wedding has been fixed for tomorrow. In my dream, I saw a youth, known by divine names such as narasimhan, mādavan and gōvindan, entering the maṇappandal, decorated with areca plants, which have spates in them.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells her friend in this pāsuram “I saw in my dream kaṇṇan entering the maṇappandal (decorated dais on which marriage is held)”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāḷai vaduvai maṇam enṟū nāḷittu** – fixing the grand wedding function for tomorrow. What is the underlying opinion for not fixing the marriage either today or after four \[few\] days, but fixing it for tomorrow? If it is after a long time \[in āṇḍāḷ’s opinion, four \[few\] days is a long time\], she would not be able to tolerate the delay. If it is said that if it is held today, due to excessive ecstasy, she may not be able to sustain herself, just like a thirsty man gulps down water in a hurry and dies due to choking. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116 -15 & 16 “*praharśēṇāvaruddā sā vyājahāra na kincana* l *abravīcca hariśrēśta*: *sītāmapratijalpatīm* ll *kim tvam cintayasē dēvi kinnu mām nābhibhāśasē* ll” ((after hearing about śrī rāma’s victory from hanuman), sītāp pirātti, overcome by excessive ecstasy, did not utter a word. Looking at her, hanuman said “Oh dēvi \[divine consort of śrī rāma\]! What are you thinking? Why are you not saying anything to me?” Thinking that only if he reduced her happiness to a certain extent, would she be able to sustain herself, hanuman further said “Did my telling you that śrī rāma has become victorious, not to your liking?”) As soon as she heard hanuman’s words, her flood of happiness receded by half. Sustaining herself, did she not say “Since my happiness choked my throat, I could not utter a word”

**nāḷittu** – all the activities for the marriage could be conducted only if the day fixed for the marriage is an auspicious one. The opinion here is that after comparing their \[āṇḍāḷ’s and kaṇṇa pirān’s\] birth stars, the date for the marriage was finalised.

**pāḷai kamugu parisudaip pandal kīḻ** – under the decorated shed \[where the wedding ceremony was to be held\] which was further decorated by planting areca tree with spates in them.

**parisudaip pandal** – the term *parisu* would refer both to eminence and beauty. The areca plant appeared as if it had grown there, from the time it sprouted and not as if it had been transplanted from elsewhere. It was very beautiful.

**kōḷari** – this speaks about the arrival of a mighty lion. mārīca tells rāvaṇa, in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 37-38 \[when rāvaṇa seeks his help in abducting sītāp pirātti\] “*apramēyam hi tattējō yasya sā janakātmajā* l *na tvam samartas tām hartum rāmacāpāśrayam vanē* ll” (The effulgence of that śrī rāma, with whom sītāp pirātti, the daughter of janaka, is together, is beyond one’s imagination. You are not capable of abducting her, who has taken refuge under śrī rāma’s bow, in the forest). Just as it has been said here about the effulgence which śrī rāma had, kaṇṇan entered that *pandal* \[decorated dais\] with a swagger, displaying with pride that he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi.

**mādavan** – the divine lord of periya pirātti \[śrī mahālakśmi\]. The opinion here is that since he had been together with her always, he came manifesting his quality of being a connoisseur.

**gōvindan** – one who makes himself amenable to the lowly cows and covherd people. The opinion here is that since no one would know as to when or from whom any hurdle would come during the wedding event, he moved with all in a respectful manner.

**enbān**– the opinion here is that these divine names are known throughout the world \[and that he is that famous\].

**ōr kāḷai** – even if he did not have any of the eminences mentioned until now, he came with the unique stature of a bull, such that people fell head over heels, on seeing him. None of the friends, mothers or relatives of the bride could say “We cannot say that the bridegroom is no match for the bride”

**kāḷai pugudak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻi nān** – \[addressing her friend, āṇḍāḷ says\] earlier, did you not take efforts to make me realise that he is of appropriate age for me and is qualified for me to deepen my love for him? Now, I am taking the effort to make you realise that he is the most apt person for me.

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.3. Indiran ullitta

*indiran uḷḷitta dēvar kuḻām ellāmvandirundu ennai magaḷ pēsi mandirittumandirak kōdi udutti maṇamālaiandari sūttak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻī! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

indiran uḷḷitta dēvar kuḻām ellām – all the assemblies of celestial entities, including indiran; vandu irundu – coming (to this earth itself); ennai magaḷ pēsi – conversing, treating me as the bride; mandirittu – (beyond that, in terms of arrangements which are to be made between the two sides, the bride’s and the bridegroom’s) consulting and finalising; andari – sister-in-law, ‘durgai’; mandirak kōdi udutti – making me wear the wedding sari; maṇam mālai sūtta – decorating \[me\] with fragrant flowers; tōḻī! – Oh, dear friend!; kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, dear friend! In my dream, I saw all the assemblies of celestial entities, including indran, coming to our place, conversing \[with my relatives here\], treating me as the bride. They consulted with the elders on both the bride’s side and the bridegroom’s side, finalising various wedding arrangements. I saw my sister-in-law, durgai, making me wear the wedding sari and decorating me with fragrant flowers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that she enjoyed the experience of donning kūṟaip pudavai \[wedding sari\] and wedding garlands.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**indiran uḷḷitta dēvar kuḻām ellām vandirundu** – In śrī rāmāyaṇam, śrī rāma identified himself as a human being and as the son of emperor daśarata, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-11 “*abravīt tridaśaśrēśtān rāmō darmabhrutām vara*: l *ātmānam mānuśam manyē rāmam dśaratātmajam* ll” (śrī rāma, the most supreme among noble people, addressing the distinguished celestial entities said “I consider myself as rāman, a human entity, the son of daśaratan”). Unlike that incarnation, for the divine wedding between kaṇṇan and āṇḍāḷ, all the celestial entities including indran, had come. The same indran, at the hermitage of sage śarabhanga had said \[of śrī rāma\] “It will not be apt for me to show myself to this person who is manifesting himself as a human entity” and left the hermitage, without even standing on the earth, saying “We shall meet after rāvaṇa is annihilated”. Unlike that incarnation, in kriṣṇāvatāram, emperumān manifested his qualities of both human and īśvara forms; hence celestial entities such as indran et al, came to earth and were taking care of the wedding arrangements, running hither and thither.

**dēvar kuḻām ellām vandu irundu** – the assemblies of celestial entities came in large strength, firmly resting their feet on earth, going beyond their norms. They were firm in their belief “We will not leave until the wedding activities are completed”

**ennai magaḷ pēsi** – speaking with the understanding that I am the bride. Speaking about the eminence of the clan, starting with brahmā and ending with periyāḻvār \[āṇḍāḷ’s divine father\].

**mandirittu** – discussing about all the activities to be undertaken and finalising them. All the elders left the wedding hall, discussed about the activities to be undertaken for the bride and the bridegroom and reached a consensus.

**mandirakkōdi udutti** – Discussing various facets of the marriage, including what will happen if the wedding were delayed by a few days. In line with the saying “*śrēyāmsi bahuvignāni bhavanti*” (for eminent events, many hurdles will occur) fearful that postponing the marriage will create hurdles later, deciding that the wedding should be completed before hurdles come in, it is evident that everyone had come prepared with the wedding sari, garlands etc.

**mandirak kōdi udutti** – making me, the bride, to wear the wedding sari which has been purified by reciting vēdams

**maṇamālai andari sūtta** – being born as the daughter of yaśōdāp pirātti \[and exchanging places with kriṣṇa when vasudēvar brought kriṣṇa in the middle of night from maturā to śrī gōkulam\], in kriṣṇāvatāram, durgā became \[kriṣṇa’s sister and\] my sister-in-law. Thinking that she should carry out the tasks during her brother’s wedding, she decorated āṇḍāḷ with a garland which was strung with the most fragrant flowers. Since she was kaṇṇan’s sister, when she touched āṇḍāḷ, she \[āṇḍāḷ\] felt her touch to be second only to kaṇṇan’s touch. She tells her friend “I was decorated with garland by his sister!” The word *antari* refers to durgai, who wanders in the *ākāśam* (sky) which is also called as *antaram*. kauśīdakī upaniśat 1-34 says of the way muktātmās are decorated when they reach parama padam “*tam pancaśata anyaprasarasām prati dāvanti śatam mālāhastā*:*, śatam anjanahastā*:*, śatam cūrṇahastā*:, *śatam vāsōhastā*:, *śatam paṇahastās tam brahmālankārēṇālankurvanti* ll” (Five hundred women with moon-like faces go towards that muktātmā \[one who has got liberated from samsāram and has reached parama padam\]. One hundred women with garlands in their hands, one hundred women with black pigment \[for the eyes\], one hundred women with fragrant powder, one hundred women with dress and one hundred women with ornaments decorate him like the supreme entity). Just like muktātmā is decorated by the women with moon-like faces, who are nityasūris, here āṇḍāḷ was decorated by durgai, at the time of her marriage.

**kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻi! nān** – she tells her friend that she enjoyed all the events of the wedding, without any deficiency.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 6.4. Narrisait tirtam

*nāṟṟisait tīrtam koṇarndu nani nalgipārppanac cittargaḷ pallār eduttēttippūppunai kaṇṇip punidanōdu enṟannaikkāppu nāṇ katta kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻi! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻi – Oh, dear friend; pārppanac cittargaḷ – many eminent brāhmaṇas; nāl tisai – from the four directions; tīrtam – sacred waters; koṇarndu – bringing them; nani nalgi – sprinkling well; eduttu ētti – reciting in a high pitch, carrying out mangaḷāśāsanam (blessing the thread); pū punai kaṇṇi punidanōdu – with kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa) who is the most sacred entity, donned with garlands which have been strung with (many types of) flowers; en tannai – me; kāppu nāṇ katta – to tie the sacred thread; nān kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, dear friend! Many eminent brāhmaṇas brought sacred waters from the four directions and sprinkled the water around the wedding arena. They recited hymns in a high pitch and blessed the sacred thread which is to be tied on the wrist. I dreamt that they tied the thread, uniting me with kaṇṇapirān, who is the most sacred and who had donned garlands strung with many types of flowers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that she experienced tying of kangaṇam (sacred, yellow coloured thread, tied at the wrist) at the beginning of the wedding events.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāṟṟisait tīrtam koṇarndu** – just as the army of monkeys brought water from oceans in all four directions during the coronation ceremony of śrī rāma pirān, bringing sacred water from the four directions.

**nani nalgi** – sprinkling well. Since they \[eminent brāhmaṇas\] firmly believe in the saying of śāstras (sacred texts) that sprinkling sacred water around the wedding arena forebodes long life and other such benefits, they sprinkled the sacred water all around.

**pārppanac cittargaḷ pallār eduttētti** – not only are they brāhmaṇas due to their clan of birth, but they are also celebrated by everyone as having learnt to recite vēdam and having good conduct and qualities. These brāhmaṇas, who are experts in conducting marriage events, bless the bride and bridegroom in high pitch. Instead of *pallār eduttētti*, there is a variation in the text as *pallāṇḍu eduttētti* \[meaning is the same as in the earlier one\].

**pūppunai kaṇṇip punidanōdu** – unlike āṇḍāḷ, the bride, who is decorated extensively during the time of wedding, for kaṇṇa pirān, the bridegroom, only one garland was used. After taking a bath, he kept telling everyone who came near him “Don’t come too close to me”, ensuring that a distance was maintained between him and the others, coming to the wedding arena with purity and *ācāram* \[observing good conduct\]. Does kaṇṇan have such *ācāram*? \[being a covherd, he normally moves with plenty of his friends through the day, and is not known to observe such *ācāram*\] It appears that since he was going to marry the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, unless he showed that he followed all the tenets of *ācāram*, he will not be allowed to marry āṇḍāḷ; hence, he voluntarily brought in these *ācāram*.

**en tannai kāppu nāṇ katta** – though I did not get to touch him, the way I desired, I had the fortune to touch the *purōhit* (the priest who conducts the wedding ceremony) who, in turn, had touched kaṇṇan. Even as the *kangaṇam* was tied to my wrist, I went in the proximity of emperumān, through his thoughts.

**tōḻī nān** – there is no benefit greater than being thought of by him. Once I went in his thoughts, oh my friend, what I had to attain was readily available. In other words, the meaning conveyed is that both the means and the benefit are through his thoughts only.

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.5. Kadir oli dipam

*kadir oḷi dīpam kalasam udan ēndisadir iḷa mangaiyar tām vandu edir koḷḷamaduraiyār mannan adi nilai tottu engumadirap pugudak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻi! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻī! – Oh, dear friend!; sadir iḷa mangaiyar tām – beautiful, young girls; kadir oḷi dīpam – auspicious lamps which were having the effulgence of sūrya (sun); kalasam – golden pots; udan ēndi – holding in their hands; vandu edir koḷḷa – coming to receive him; maduraiyār mannan – kaṇṇa pirān, the lord of those who live in maturā; adi nilai tottu – wearing his wooden sandals; engum adira – with the entire earth quivering; puguda – to enter mercifully; nān kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, dear friend! Even as beautiful, young girls, holding the auspicious lamps which were as effulgent as sūrya, and golden pots containing sacred water, came to receive him, kaṇṇa pirān, who is the king of maturā, wearing his sandals, entered the wedding dais, with the earth quivering. I saw this, in my dream.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells in this pāsuram that she enjoyed emperumān entering the wedding dais, received by auspicious lamps and pūrṇa kumbham (sacred water contained in a pot, covered with coconut\]*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kadir oḷi dīpam kalasam udan ēndi** – considering that if there is any deficiency in the auspicious lamps or in the *pūrṇa kumbham*, it would be deficiency in the marriage arrangements, they came with auspicious lamps which were as effulgent as sūrya, along with equally splendorous golden pots containing sacred water. Who came with these?

**sadir iḷa mangaiyar** – just as it has been mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-9-10-vidhi-vagai/) *“niṟai kuda viḷakkamum madimugamadandaiyar ēndinar vandē*” (ladies with faces shining like moon, came holding *pūrṇa kumbham* and auspicious lamps), women came to receive kaṇṇan, similar to young, beautiful women coming to receive muktātmā in paramapadam.

**tām vandu edir koḷḷa** – they were of such age and beauty that everyone would look at them; they came forward to receive kaṇṇan, bowing their heads and looking at the floor, as they walked.

**maduraiyār mannan** –isn’t being called as the king of maturā, which he chose to come to desirously after leaving paramapadam, more eminent than being called as parama pada nātan (lord of paramapadam)? Even āṇḍāḷ, in her [tiruppāvai 5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-05-mayanai/) had desired to address him as “*mannu vada madurai maindanai*” (king of maturā, in the north, which has ever present connection with matters related to bhagavān). That emperumān much desired this divine name is evident in the following narration: a śrīvaiṣṇava, consecrated the idol of a kriṣṇa, dancing for butter. He was wondering as to which divine name he could give for this vigraham. emperumān came in his dream and told him “Instead of calling me in any other manner, call me as *madurai mannan*”.

**adi nilai tottu** – donning the divine sandals.

**engum adirap puguda** – mercifully entering the wedding dais such that all the places in the dais trembled. viṣṇu darmam 43-31 says “*yasmin prayātē’sura bhūbhrutō’dvaram nanāma kēdādavanis sasāgarā* l *sa vāmanas sarvajaganmayas sadā mamāstu māngaḷya vivruddayē hari*: ll” (Let that vāmanan, incarnation of emperumān hari, who made the earth which is together with ocean to tremble when he walked to the *yajña* \[rituals\] arena of mahābali, the king of monsters, multiply my auspiciousness always). Some people, who read this, asked some wise men as to what is the meaning of the term “Earth trembled”. The wise men responded, saying “Since he is the lord of all, when he walked, should we wonder why the earth trembled?” nanjīyar, who had heard of this, asked his ācāryan, bhattar whether this meaning was apt. bhattar replied saying “emperumān is one who is used to giving everything to his followers. Since he came to take alms from someone, due to his anxiety, he came in such a manner that the earth trembled”. For the sake of three steps of land, if he could come with such anxiety, is it any wonder that he came with anxiety to marry āṇḍāḷ!

**kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻi nān** – the opinion here is that even though it was I who had dreamt of these, aren’t you the one who is happier than I, in this matter?

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.6. Mattalam kotta

*mattaḷam kotta varisangam ninṟu ūdamuttudait tāmam nirai tāḻnda pandaṟkīḻmaittunan nambi madusūdan vandennaikaittalam paṟṟa kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻī! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻī! – Oh, dear friend!; mattaḷam kotta – even as the percussion instruments were being played; vari sangam ninṟu ūda – even as the conces, with lines, were being blown for a long time; maittunan nambi madusūdan – kaṇṇa pirān, who is complete with all the auspicious qualities and who has the relationship with āṇḍāḷ to marry her; muttu udai tāmam nirai tāḻnda pandal kīḻ vandu – coming under the wedding; ennai kaittalam paṟṟa – to mercifully carry out pāṇigrahaṇam (holding my hand); nān kanāk kaṇḍēṇ – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, dear friend! I dreamt that even as various drums were being played and the conces with lines on them were being blown for a long time, kaṇṇa pirān, who is complete with all the auspicious qualities and who has the relationship to marry me, came under the wedding dais, which was decorated with garlands having pearls, to mercifully hold my hand.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that she also enjoyed madusūdanan (emperumān) holding her hand.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mattaḷam kotta varisangam ninṟu ūda** – sounding auspicious musical instruments such as drums and conch, which are usually played during weddings of people belonging to regal clans. Instead of saying “He held my hand with the recital of *vēda mantrams* (incantations from sacred texts)” is it proper to say “He held my hand with the sounding of auspicious musical instruments”? periyavāccān piḷḷai, the *vyākyātā* (commentator) for this prabandam, says that it is possible that kaṇṇan had married her before he went to sage sāndīpini’s hermitage for learning. According to historical facts, kaṇṇan had grown up in tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam) for a long time and then only went to sāndīpani to learn vēdam. Thus, it is only apt that he had married āṇḍāḷ, who is imagining herself as a covherd girl, before he learnt vēdam.

**muttudait tāmam nirai tāḻnda pandaṟkīḻ** – under the wedding dais where rows of garlands with pearls have been hung such that they were touching the heads \[of people coming under the dais\].

**maittunan nambi** – kaṇṇan, who is the emperumān, has the required relationship, in order to marry her. Hasn’t āṇḍāḷ transformed herself into a covherd girl so that she could be termed as a relative of kaṇṇa pirān, eligible to marry him, just like nappinnaip pirātti!

**madusūdan** – one who annihilated enemies such as madu et al. The meaning hinted here is that if someone else were to come to marry āṇḍāḷ saying that he is also related to āṇḍāḷ, he \[kaṇṇan\] has the capability to destroy him and to hold her hand. īsn’t he one who will destroy those who come to take control of any of his possessions, falsely claiming ownership, just as he had destroyed madu?

**vandennai kaittalam paṟṟa** – isn’t she the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who had *upāya adyavasāyam* \[the firm belief in the means\] that only he \[emperumān\], who is the owner \[of the soul\], is apt to come and take charge of the owned? Thus, she says that emperumān himself came and held her hand.

**kaittalam paṟṟa** – did not sitāp pirātti reminisce in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 119-16 “*na pramāṇīkruta*: *pāṇi*: *bālyē bālēna pīdita*: l” (that hand of mine, when I was young, which was held very tightly by you, when you too were young, is not being thought of \[today\]) reminding an incident to hanuman “Even if he forgets me, could he forget himself? Did he forget that he had held my hand \[in marriage\] when he was twelve years of age?” Some disciples asked embār, their ācāryan “Instead of using the term *pāṇigrahaṇam* why was the term *pīdita* used by pirātti in the ślōKam stated above?” embār replied, saying “Just as it has been said in [tiruvāimoḻi 9-6-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/08/thiruvaimozhi-9-6-6-engannan-kalvam/) ‘*angaṇṇanuṇḍa ennāruyikkōdidu*‘ (with desire, he \[emperumān) consumed my soul which is faulty), with his firm grip on her hand, he indicated that the firm grip is only for his sake. To emphasise this, the term *pīdita* has been used”. Here, the vyākyātā, periyavāccān piḷḷai, explains in detail the term *pāṇigrahaṇam* taken from śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam ślōkam 73-26 “*iyam sītā mama sutā saha darmacarī tava* l *pratīcca cainām bhadram tē pāṇim gruhṇīśva pāṇinā* ll“ (This sītai is my daughter; she will accompany you in all the righteous deeds; accept her. May you have all auspicious bodings. Hold her hand with your hand). *iyam sītā* – you were in your mother’s womb for 12 months. Unlike that, she did not come out of a mother’s womb. Just as it has been mentioned in amaram 2-9-14 and in vaijayantī bhūmi-vaiśya 30, “*sītā lāngala paddadi*:” (the path of the plough is known as sītai), \[when king janaka\] ploughed the field, since she appeared as *ayōnija* \[one not born from a womb\], she was called as sītai. īsn’t it eminent for you to attain her? *mama sutā saha darmacarī tava* – Since she is my daughter, when I had been treating protection of citizens as the path of righteousness, if you take a vow to protect those who have been tortured, she is capable of going to lankā ahead of you and being imprisoned to free celestial ladies \[who had been imprisoned there\]. *pratīcca cainām* – just as people are given wages in order to eat sugarcane, am I not falling at your feet, asking you to accept her hand! *bhadram tē* – just as it has been mentioned in [tiruppallāṇḍu 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-2-adiyomodum/) “*vadivāy nin valamārbinil vāḻginṟa mangaiyum pallāṇḍu*” (*mangaḷāśāsanam* (praises) to the divine beautiful pirāttis who are dwelling on the right side of your divine chest), janaka sings pallāṇḍu to the togetherness of śrī rāma and sītā. When the bow was broken and king janaka offered his daughter to śrī rāma, perumāḷ said “When my father and the people of the town have not come, how could I hold her hand?” manifesting the eminence of his auspicious qualities, declining to hold her hand. janaka said “It was appropriate to decline her hand then. Now that your father and others have come, hold her hand with your hand”. After saying “*pratīcca*” (accept her) he said “*pāṇim gruhṇīśva*” (hold her hand). The meaning for this could only be that unlike the earlier occasion when you had declined to hold her hand, please hold her hand.

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.7. Vay nallar

*vāy nallār nalla maṟaiyōdi mandirattālpāsilai nāṇal paduttup paridivaittukkāysina mākaḷiṟannān en kaip paṟṟittīvalam seyya kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻi! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻī! – Oh, my friend!; vāy nallār – vaidikas (those who follow vēdams) who recite \[vēdams\] well; nalla maṟai ōdi – reciting eminent vēda vākyams \[text from vēdams\]; mandirattāl – with hymns (appropriate for various events); pāsu ilai nāṇal paduttu – spreading reed grass which has green leaves; paridi vaittu – placing pieces of wood; kāy sinam mā kaḷiṟu annān – kaṇṇapirān, who was arrogant like an angry elephant, in exult; en kai paṟṟi – holding my hand; tī valam seyya – circumambulating the ritualistic fire; kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, dear friend! Those who are capable of reciting vēdam were reciting them, after spreading reed grass which had green leaves, around the ritualistic fire. They stacked pieces of wood on the fire. I dreamt that kaṇṇa pirān, who was arrogant like an angry elephant which was in exultation, held my hand and circumambulated the ritualistic fire.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – She says that she enjoyed going around agni (ritual fire)*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vāy nallār** – those who are capable of intonating hymns from vēdas

**nalla maṟai ōdi** – to qualify as good vēdam, are there two types of vēdams – a good one and not a good one? In the first part of the vēdams, it will mention about *yajñams* (rituals) which are conducted for emperumān who is the *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) for celestial entities such as indra et al. In the second part of vēdams which contain puruśa sūktam \[eloquently describing emperumān\] etc, the *svarūpa* (basic nature)*, rūpa* (divine form), qualities of emperumān will be mentioned. Considering the distinction of the latter over the former, āṇḍāḷ calls this as the eminent part of vēdam.

**mandirattāl** – while the greatness of emperumān has been vividly described in puruśa sūktam itself, reciting hymns appropriate for various events such as spreading the reed grass around ritualistic fire, applying the wooden sticks, circumambulating ritualistic fire etc.

**pāsilai nāṇal paduttu** – spreading reed grass, containing green leaves, around the ritualistic fire.

**paridi vaittu** – applying the wooden sticks

**kāysina mākaḷiṟannān en kai paṟṟi** – kaṇṇan, who came walking like an arrogant elephant, which was in exultation, held my hand

**tī valam seyya …. kaḷiṟannān** – when he walked in circumambulation around the fire, he walked slowly, like an elephant. yajur aśtakam 3, praśna-7, panjāti-89 says “*ēkamiśē viṣṇus tvā’nvētu* l *dvē ūrjē viṣṇus tvā’nvētu* l …… *sakhya: saptapadā abhūma* ll” (this is the hymn which the bridegroom will say, holding on to the bride’s hand ‘may viṣṇu lead you by one step…….after taking seven steps, may you be my companion”) āṇḍāḷ refers to this event, when kaṇṇan goes around the ritualistic fire, holding her hand

**kaippaṟṟi** – when he came as an elephant in exultation, and held her hand, it appeared that the female elephant was imprisoned by the male.

**tī valam seyya** – since they had held hands with *agni* (lord of fire) as the witness, he cannot let go of her hand and she too cannot let go his hand.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.8. Immaikkum elel piravikkum

*tōḻī! – oh, dear friend!immaikkum – for tis birtēḻ ēḻ piṟavikkum – for all te birts to be taken in futurepaṟṟu āvān – he remains as our refugenammai udaiyavan – as our śēśi (being our lord)nambi – being complete vit all te auspicious qualitiesnārāyaṇan – kaṇṇa pirān, vho is śrīman nārāyaṇansemmai udaiya tirukkaiyāl – vit his reddiś, divine handstāḷ paṟṟi – holding (my) footammi midikka – to keep atop te grinding stonenān kanāk kaṇḍēn – ī dreamt*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my dear friend! kaṇṇapirān, who is śrīman nārāyaṇan, is our refuge for this birth as well as for all the births which we may take in the future. He is our lord, and is complete with all the auspicious qualities. I dreamt that such kaṇṇa pirān, with his reddish, divine hands, held my foot and placed it on top of the grinding stone.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – after the event of circumambulating the ritual fire, āṇḍāḷ says that she experienced the event of ammi midittal (stepping on to a grinding stone) immaikkum ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum paṟṟāvānnammai udaiyavan nārāyaṇan nambisemmai udaiya tirukkaiyāl tāḷ paṟṟiammi midikkak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻī! nān Word-by-Word Highlights*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**immaikkum ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum paṟṟāvān** – In the previous pāsuram, āṇḍāḷ had said that since the two of them held their hands with *agni*, the lord of fire as the witness, each cannot let the other go. In this pāsuram, she says that even if that *agni* himself attempts to separate them, kaṇṇan is one who will not let go of us. Not only in this birth, irrespective of the number of births which we take, isn’t he the one who is our refuge!

**ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum** – the meaning implied here is that if we are able to attain him and enjoy him without any break, there will be no deficiency in our taking births repeatedly. ḍidn’t she pray to him, as in [tiruppāvai 29](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/) “*ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum unṟannōdu uṟṟōmēyāvōm*” (for all the births in the future, we will be related to you)?

**ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum paṟṟāvān** – if a ritual called as *kārīri* is observed in order to get benefits such as rains etc, there will be heavy rains, once. śrī rāma *carama ślōkam* (ultimate hymn) says “*sakrut Eva prapannāya*” (take refuge under me once), where he says that if he is taken refuge of once, the experience with the supreme entity will last for eternity. āṇḍāḷ refers this to *ēḻēḻ piṟavikkum*. In his [tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram 9-1-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/06/thiruvaimozhi-9-1-7-marronrillai/) “*kuṟṟamil sīr kaṟṟu vaigal vāḻdal kaṇḍīr guṇamē*” (His auspicious qualities are such that if we meditate upon his auspicious qualities which are opposite to all defects, we will live happily forever), nammāḻvār implied this meaning for the word *vaigal* (forever).

**nammai udaiyavan** – one who remains as our *svāmy* (lord). Even if we do not get the benefit of enjoying him for eternity, just as mentioned a little earlier, where could we go, leaving him, our *svāmy*? We cannot even think “If we do not have any refuge, we are there, for ourselves”. mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 42-35 says “*yō’nyatā santamātmānam anyatā pratipadyatē* l *kim tēna na krutam pāpam cōrēṇa ātma apahāriṇā* ll (When a person considers ātmā \[soul\], which is the possession of bhagavān, as his own, is there any sin which has not been committed by that thief?) Thus, isn’t considering ātmā, which is emperumān’s property, as one’s own property, a huge theft?

**nārāyaṇan** – isn’t this the divine name which refers to the qualities which are denoted by the verse “*nammai udaiyavan*” (he is our lord) and to the qualities which are denoted by the verse “*paṟṟāvān*” (he is our refuge)?

**nambi** – He is complete with auspicious qualities which do not have any boundary.

**semmai udaiya tirukkaiyāl tāḷ paṟṟi** – when the bride steps on to *ammi* (flat, grinding stone), he holds the foot of the bride with his reddish, divine hand, which indicates his quality of *āśrita pāratantryam* (he is dependent on his followers for his sustenance). While we did not show any interest since time immemorial, in holding on to his divine foot when he is our ordained lord, his *āśrita pāratantryam*, by which he shows great zeal in holding the foot of his follower, explains the meaning for the term *semmai* in this verse. During the event of marriage, the relatives of the bridegroom will tell him “Do not hold on to her foot” while the relatives of the bride will tell him “You have to hold on to her foot”. While the two sides keep arguing about this \[in jest\], āṇḍāḷ shows that he displayed great zeal in holding her foot, through the term *semmai*. While instructing the person who wishes to take refuge under him, emperumān says “*mām ēkam śaraṇam vraja*” (Take refuge under me alone); however, once he attains the follower and enjoys him, he holds on to the foot of the follower. That is the affection which he shows towards his follower.

**ammi midikkak kaṇāk kaṇḍēn** – I saw in my dream that he made me step on to the *ammi*, by reciting the hymn “*aśmēva tvam stirā bhava*” (Be firm like this stone, while being my servitor), through which he instructed *acitvat pāratantryam* (being dependent like an acit, an entity which has no knowledge).

**ammi midikka** – it appears that she was afraid as to what will happen if his foot touches the stone while he was keeping her foot on top of it. What will happen if his foot touches the stone? She was afraid that this stone too would get up, just like ahalyā did in śrī rāmāyaṇam!

**tōḻi! nān** – Oh, dear friend! Haven’t you realised how soft is my foot, when you had touched it? By holding my foot, he too realised that! Alternatively, āṇḍāḷ tells her friend – I enjoyed his *āśrita pāratantryam* which you had told me about.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.9. Varisilai vanmugattu

*varisilai vāṇmugattu ennaimār tām vanditt(u)erimugam pārittu ennai munnē niṟuttiarimugan accutan kaimmēl en kai vaittupporimugandu attak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻī! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōḻī! – Oh, dear friend!; vari silai vāḷ mugam – having eyebrows which were like a beautiful bow and having radiant faces; en aimār tām – my brothers; vandittu – coming there; eri mugam pārittu – making agni (fire) to glow well; munnē ennai niṟutti – making me stand in front of that agni; ari mugan – one who had the face of a lion; accutan – kaṇṇapirān’s; kai mēl – on top of his hand; en kai vaittu – keeping my hand; pori – puffed paddy grains; mugandu atta – to serve fully; nān kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my dear friend! I dreamt that my brothers, having their eyebrows like beautiful bows and with radiant faces, came \[to the wedding dais\]. They made the fire to glow resplendently and made me stand in front of that fire. They kept my hand on top of the hand of kaṇṇa pirān, who had the divine face of a lion. They filled my hand with puffed paddy grains.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that she saw in her dream, the activity described as lājahōmam in treatises on marriage.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**varisilai vāḷ mugattu ennaimār tām** – the implied meaning is that my brothers came to the wedding dais with the proclamation “We have the right to conduct this *lāja hōmam*” written on their brows which appeared like bows and on their radiant faces such that everyone could see.

**vandittu erimugam pārittu** – coming to the dais where the marriage is held and making the *agni* (ritual fire) to glow well.

**enai munnē niṟutti** – since I was overcome by shyness, a typical feminine trait, and refused to come forward, they forced me and made me to stand in front of *agni*.

**arimugan** – one who had the face like a lion’s. If my brothers, who are his brothers-in-law, tried to indulge in playful activities to tease him and make him laugh, he made himself such eminent that they could not get close to him.

**accutan** – one who does not let go of. Even after they \[brothers\] had dropped the puffed paddy grains in *agni*, he did not let go of my hand. Through this, it is said that he would never let go of her.

**kaimmēl en kai vaittu** – keeping my hand on top of his hand. The sequence *kaimmēl en kai vaittu* indicates that when it comes to giving protection to the followers, her hand is superior to emperumān’s hand.

**pori mugandu attak kanāk kaṇḍēn** – I saw in my dream that my brothers filled my hand with puffed paddy grains and offered that to *agni*.

**tōḻī! nān** – Don’t you know the tenderness of my hand? I became fortunate that now he also realises that.

Next, we will consider the 10<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.10. Kungumam appi

*kungumam appik kuḷir sāndam mattittumangalavīdi valam seydu maṇa nīrangavanōdum udan senṟu angu ānai mēlmanjanam āttak kanāk kaṇḍēn tōḻī! nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

angu – in that place; tōḻī! – Oh, dear friend!; kungumam – vermillion paste; appi – applying all over the physical form; kuḷir sāndam – cool sandalwood paste; mattittu – applying heavily \[in large measure\]; angu ānai mēl – on top of an elephant, there; avanōdum udan senṟu – being together with that kaṇṇa pirān; mangalam vīdi – (for the marriage) in the streets shining with decorations; valam seydu – going around the town; maṇam nīr – with fragrant water; manjanam ātta – being given divine bath (for the two of us); nān kanāk kaṇḍēn – I dreamt

**Перевод:**

**Oh, dear friend! I dreamt that in that place, we were applied with vermillion paste all over our physical forms, along with a heavy layer of sandalwood paste. We were taken on an elephant, in that place, around the well decorated streets. We were given divine bath with fragrant water.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that she dreamt that she went on an elephant along with him, around the town and that they were given a divine bath.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kungumam appik kuḷir sāndam mattittu** – In the fourth pāsuram \[of this decad\] *“pūppunai kaṇṇip punidanōdu en tannaik kāppu nāṇ katta*” it was mentioned that kaṇṇa pirān had just a single garland to decorate himself with, when he was to tie the sacred thread around her wrist. Since in the pāsurams which were recited subsequently, other events of the marriage were described, he would have had several other garlands and decorations. With such exhaustive decorations, would it not have been unbearable to sit in front of *agni* \[ritual fire\], with his tender form? Hence, removing all those grand decorations, cool and comfortable vermillion paste was applied all over the divine physical form, as a relief from the warmth of *agni*. Further, sandalwood paste, which was kept under the ground for maturing, was also applied heavily on the divine physical form.

**angu maṇa nīr mangala vīdi valam seydu** – Taking all the materials for giving a divine bath, going in a procession around the streets which had been decorated for the marriage.

**angu ānai mēl avanōdum udan senṟu** – In the first pāsuram, “*vāraṇam āyiram sūḻa valam seydu*”, it was mentioned that before the wedding commenced, he came with his friends atop elephant, around the streets. This verse now says that after the wedding, she and he sat atop an elephant to go around the streets, in a procession.

**manjanamāttak kanāk kaṇḍēn** – I dreamt that in order to rid ourselves of all the ennui that we had experienced during various events of the wedding, we were seated together and were given a divine bath.

**tōḻī! nān** – Since it was in my dream, I saw all these events. The meaning hinted here is that the friend did not get to see them.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 6.11. Ayanukkagat tan kanda

*āyanukkāgat tān kaṇḍa kanāvinaivēyar pugaḻ villiputtūrk kōn kōdai soltūya tamiḻ māḷai īraindum vallavarvāyu nanmakkaḷaip peṟṟu magiḻvarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēyar pugaḻ – being praised by those who came in the clan of brāhmaṇas; villiputtūrk kōn – the head of śrīvilliputtūr, periyāḻvār, his; kōdai – āndāḷ; tān āyanukkāga kaṇḍa kanāvinai – regarding the manner in which she became kriṣṇa’s consort, in her dream; sol – mercifully composed; tūya – sacred; tamiḻ mālai īraindum – these ten pāsurams which are garlands in tamiḻ; vallavar – those who are capable of reciting; vāyum – having distinguished qualities; nal makkaḷaip peṟṟu – siring eminent children; magiḻvar – they will be joyous

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the head of śrīvilliputtūr and who is praised by those who came in the clan of brāhmaṇas. She had mercifully composed ten pāsurams, which are garlands in tamiḻ, which describe the events which she had dreamt of, conducted during her marriage with kriṣṇa. Those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams will get eminent children with distinguished qualities and will be happy.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – In the last pāsuram of this decad, she tells the benefits which will accrue to those who recite the decad and brings it to an end.*

**vyākhyānam (Commenary)**

**āyanukkāat tāṇ kaṇḍa kanāvinai** – the dream in which she became the ordained consort of covherd kaṇṇa pirān. Alternatively, just as she had mercifully said in [tiruppāvai 29](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/) “*unakkē nām ātseyvōm*” (We shall be subservient only to you), this could be considered as her dream in which she became instrumental to be the benefit for him. just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10 “*ciram jīvati vaidēhi yadi māsam dariśyati* l *na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi vinā tāmasitēkśaṇām* ll” (If sītai could sustain herself for one month, she would be considered as having sustained herself for a very long time; without her, who has dark eyes, I will not sustain myself for more than a moment), isn’t this the benefit for emperumān who could not live without her even for a moment! īsn’t it emperumān’s benefit to attain his follower since he cannot sustain himself without her even for a moment? He is not capable of living for a month in separation, just as pirātti did, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam tsundara kāṇḍam 36-67 “*jīvitam dārayiśyāmi māsam daśaratājmaja*! l *ūrdvam māsān na jīvēyam styēnāham bravīmi tē* ll” (Oh, the great one! I will sustain my life for one month; I take a vow in your name that I will not be alive for more than a month).

**vēyar pugaḻ villiputtūr kōn kōdai sol** – periyāḻvār is praised by the brāhmaṇas in śrīvilliputtūr as “Most trustworthy person who speaks only the truth”. The divine verses composed by his divine daughter, āṇḍāḷ

**tūya tamiḻ mālai** – She had visualised these events, through her mind, in her dream. The experiences which she had, translated into verses, are like pure garland in tamiḻ. These are not like the other poems which are sung after attempting to join words formed in the mind.

**īraindum vallavar, vāyu nanmakkaḷaip peṟṟu magiḻvarē** – If those who recite these ten pāsurams, knowing their meanings, are ladies with the same age as āṇḍāḷ, they will attain husbands who are beautiful, like kaṇṇan. If they are married women or men, they will beget children or disciples, like periyāḻvār did, who have deep devotion towards emperumān. ḍidn’t periyāḻvār too mercifully say in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-7-11 “*māyan maṇivaṇṇan tāḷ paṇiyum makkaḷaip peṟuvargaḷē*” (They will beget children who are capable of vorśipping the divine feet of emperumān who has amazing activities and who has a bluish gemstone like divine form)!

Next, we will consider the pravēśam (introduction) for the seventh decad.

## 7.1. Karuppuram narumo

*karuppūram nāṟumō kamalappū nāṟumōtiruppavaḷac cevvāydān tittittirukkumōmaruppositta mādavanṟan vāyccuvaiyum nāṟṟamumviruppuṟṟuk kētkinṟēn sollāḻi veṇsangē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āḻi veṇsangē – Oh śrī pāncajanya āḻvān, who is majestic and white!; maruppu ositta mādavan than – one who snapped the tusk (of kamsan’s elephant, kuvalayāpīdam), kaṇṇapirān, his; vāy – divine mouth’s; suvaiyum – taste; nāṟṟamum – and fragrance; viruppuṟṟu – with desire; kētkinṟēn – I am asking (you); tiru pavaḷa sem vāy tān – (that emperumān’s) beautiful, reddish, like coral, divine mouth; karuppūram – like medicated camphor; nāṟumō – will it be fragrant?; kamalappū – like a lotus flower; nāṟumō – will it be fragrant?; tittittirukkumō – will it have sweet taste?; sol – tell me (as to how it will be)

**Перевод:**

**Oh śrī pāncajanya āḻvān, who is majestic and who is white in colour! I am asking you desirously about the fragrance and taste of the divine mouth of kaṇṇapirān, who had snapped the tusks of the elephant kuvalayāpīdam. Will his beautiful, reddish like coral, divine mouth be as fragrant as medicated camphor? ōr, will it be as fragrant as lotus flower? Will it have sweet taste? You should tell me as to how it is.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**karuppūram nāṟumō kamalappū nāṟūmō** – The fragrance of medicated camphor will have the qualities of pungency and acridity. The fragrance of lotus flower will have the quality of coolness, which may not be fresh. The fragrance of \[emperumān’s\] divine mouth will be without any of these deficiencies, and will have many times the completeness of their fragrances. cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 says of emperumān “*sarvaganda*: *sarvarasa*:” (he is the repository of all the good fragrances and all the good tastes). īsn’t his divine mouth a limb of such a divine form? Since no other entity has the eminences which this divine mouth has, she \[āṇḍāḷ\] is referring to a few entities \[such as medicated camphor and lotus\] which have a few of the eminences which his divine mouth has, and since his divine mouth does not have any of the deficiencies which those entities have, she is asking with a doubt whether it will be as fragrant as the other entities. Since the fragrances of entities such as medicated camphor, lotus flower etc could be experienced in this \[divine mouth\], it should be considered that she is citing a couple of those entities primarily, for confirmation.

**nāṟumō** – She is saying “If you know which of those fragrances are present in \[his divine mouth\], are you capable of saying it?”

**tiruppavaḷac cevvāydān tittittirukkumō** – going beyond the fragrance which could be experienced due to proximity \[to the divine conch\], she is asking if the taste of the inner mouth, when experienced, would be sweet. As seen in the cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*sarvaganda*: *sarvarasa*:”, doesn’t his divine form have all the sweet fragrances and the sweet tastes? While she had referred to two examples in the case of fragrance \[medicated camphor and lotus\], why has she not quoted any example, such as honey and sugar candy, here, for the sweetness of taste? Since this is like a stream entering the mouth, if someone were to enter it, they will get bewildered and would not be able to think of anything. Hence, she is not citing an example for taste.

**tiruppavaḷac cevvāy** – once the divine mouth became the target \[for her eyes\], it did not allow her to engage with anything else. Does it have such eminence? Since this is the repository for the most eminent *śabda sparśa rūpa rasa* and *ganda* senses (the five external sensory receptacles of sound, touch, form, taste and smell), isn’t this capable of nurturing all the five sensory organs?

**maruppositta mādavan than vāyccuvaiyum nāṟṟamum** – just as sītāp pirātti, losing out to the valour of śrī rāma, embraced him after he had annihilated kara, dūśaṇa and other monsters in janmastān \[in daṇḍakāraṇya forest\] as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 30-40 “*tam druśtvā śatru hantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham* l *babhūva hruśtā vaidēhī bhartāram pariśasvajē*” (sītāp pirātti, looking at her husband śrī rāma, who had annihilated his enemies and had given comfort to the sages, became very happy, and sustained herself. She tightly embraced her husband), \[his divine consorts\] losing out to the valour of kaṇṇa pirān who had killed the elephant kuvalayāpīdam, engaged deeply with him. īsn’t this the limb with which his divi9ne consorts engage deeply and from which they cannot come out easily?

**vāyc cuvaiyum nāṟṟamum** – at the time of experiencing, though she had spoken about fragrance and then about taste \[of his divine mouth\] in the first two lines of the verse after coming out of the experience, while mentioning about it, due to the way it which it had got fixed in her mind, she enquires about the taste first and then about the fragrance.

**viruppuṟṟuk kētkinṟēn** – I am asking about the taste and fragrance of the divine mouth, with deep desire. [bhagavat gītā 9-1](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-1/) says “*idam tu tē guhyatamam pravakśyāmyanasūyavē* l *jñānam vijñāna sahitam yajjñātvā mōkśyasē’śubhāt* ll” (I am instructing you, who are free from any jealousy, regarding this knowledge which is the most secretive and is in the form of devotion which is supplemented with distinguished knowledge). Unlike this situation, in my case \[āṇḍāḷ’s\] there is no need for absence of jealousy in the first place and then the manifestation of desire \[Desire is there from the beginning itself\]. Again, there is no need to caution her, unlike the way emperumān cautioned arjuna, as mentioned in [bhagavat gītā 18-67](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-67/) “*idam tē nātapaskāya nābhaktāya kadācana* l *na cāśuśrūśavē vācyam na ca mām yō’byasūyati*” (this sacred text, instructed to you (by me) is not fit to be instructed to anyone who is without devotion (towards me and you), who does not have desire in listening to it and who hates me). Alternatively, this could be construed as āṇḍāḷ telling śrī pāncajanyam “I am requesting this, of you, with lot of desire”. Even though I, as his \[emperumān’s\] consort, could ordain you, our servitor, and ask you to reveal to me, due to my present situation of helplessness, I am praying to you to reveal to me. Since śrī pāncajanya āḻvān is emperumān’s servitor, isn’t he her servitor too? āren’t servitors common to both husband and wife!

**āḻi – sangē** – Oh divine conch, who has the expanse of an ocean!  The implied meaning is that the divine conch should behave in a broadminded manner, just like the ocean. Alternatively, this could be construed as – oh divine conch, who took birth in ocean! In that case, the implied meaning will be – just like the ocean, which is your place of birth, sustains all lives, you too should sustain me by answering my question.

**veṇ sangē sol** – just as your white complexion entered my mind, your answer too should enter my mind. Alternatively, even as you are enjoying \[the divine fragrance and taste of emperumān’s divine mouth\], you have become drained of colour \[and have become white\], due to your distress. Since you know my distress, you should tell me the information which I am asking of you.

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.2. Kadalil pirandu

*kadalil piṟandu karudādu panjasananudalil vaḷarndupōy ūḻiyān kaittalattidaril kudiyēṟit tīya asurarnadalaip padamuḻangum tōṟṟattāy naṟsangē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal sangē – Oh beautiful conch!; kadalil – in the ocean; piṟandu – taking birth (from there); panjasanan udalil pōy vqaḷarndu – going into the form of a monster, called as panjasanan and growing there; karudādu – not thinking (of the place of birth and place of growing, like this); ūḻiyān – emperumān’s; kai talam tidaril – in the eminent location of divine hand; kudi ēṟi – dwelling; tīya asurar – monsters who are cruel; nadalai pada – to suffer; muḻangum tōṟṟattāy – you remain as one who has the eminence of roaring

**Перевод:**

**Oh, beautiful conch! You took birth in the ocean and went inside the form of panjasanan, a monster living in the sea. Not considering the lowliness of your place of birth and subsequent growth, you are dwelling on the eminent location of emperumān’s divine hand. You remain as one who has the eminence of roaring in such a way that the cruel monsters suffer.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells śrī pāncajanyam “Just as you destroy those who are not affectionate towards emperumān, you should also nurture those like me, who have affection towards him”. We could also consider the introduction as – she tells pāncajanyam “just as your birth and upbringing were meant for helping others, the activities which you undertake should also be for others only”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kadalil piṟandu karudādu panjasanan udalil vaḷarndu pōy ūḻiyān kaittalat tidaril kudiyēṟi** – despite being born in an ocean and growing inside the physical form of a monster *panjasanan* who had only hatred towards emperumān in his heart. Not considering the lowliness of your birth and growing up, you entered to dwell on the divine hand of emperumān, who was the destroyer of everything except time, during the period of deluge, and who contained all the worlds within his divine form. We could consider the verse ‘*kadalil piṟandu*’ as not referring to the lowliness of ocean but to the fact that it \[ocean\] nurtures many species of entities to dwell within in. In that case, this could also mean that just as you \[āṇḍāḷ addressing the divine conch\] were born in the sea which helps everyone, you should help people like me too.

**karudādu ….. vaḷarndu** – the underlying meaning is that just as you grew up inside the physical form of the monster without considering his faults, you should also help us, without looking at our faults.

**tīya asurar nadalaip pada muḻangum tōṟṟattāy** – you remained as the eminent one by roaring during the battles which emperumān fought \[with the demons and the monsters\], such that they suffered. Just as he was born in the clan of *yadu*, you were born in the ocean. Just as he grew up in tiruvāyppādi \[śrī gōkulam\] which was under the reign of monster kamsan, you too grew up inside the body of monster *panjasanan*. Just as he shifted to dvārakā, keeping it as his capital, in order to avoid invasion of entities such as *kālayavanan*, *jarāsandan* et al, you too shifted to the divine hand of emperumān. Just as he destroyed wicked entities such as duryōdana et al, you also roared, just as it has been mentioned in [bhagavad gītā 1-19](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/1-19/) “*sa ghōśō dārtarāśtrāṇām hrudayāni vyadārayat*” (the uproar of that pāncajanyam broke the hearts of the sons of drutarāśtra), you also roared such that all those who had the nature of monsters perished, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-21-29 “*yasya nādēna daityānām balahānirajāyata*” (through the uproar of which divine conch, the strength of monsters was destroyed). It is not that there are many features where you are equivalent to emperumān. She says that there is a special distinction for him over emperumān.

**nal sangē** – since emperumān creates entities based on their karmas (past deeds), he would take into account both the faults and good deeds, reward the entities for their good deeds and punish them for the bad ones. Thus, he acts like an *irukaraiyar* (river which has banks on either side). However, the significant distinction for you, the divine conch, over emperumān, is that you consider even the faults of those who are affectionate towards emperumān as desired qualities and do good for them. Even emperumān will not match you, on this, oh eminent conch! Here, the vyākyāta \[commentator\] of this prabandam, periyavāccān piḷḷai cites a narrative which signifies the *svāpadēśam* \[distinguished meaning\] of pāncajanyam being born in one place, growing up in one place and carrying out an activity. The narrative shows how a person should be born in *tirumantram*, grow up in *dvayam* and be firmly established in *dvayārtam* (the meaning of *dvayam*) \[*tirumantram* and *dvayam* are two out of the three esoteric principles which are very close to śrīvaiṣṇavas\]. The narration is as follows: A person by name *mādavācār*, who was popularly known as *vēdānti*, was living in *mēlainādu* (tirunārāyaṇapuram), in an affluent manner, in an environment of enjoyment. He was won over in a debate by {parāśara\] bhattar (son of kūrattāḻvān) and became bhattar’s disciple. After some time, wishing in his divine mind that he should shift to śrīrangam and serve bhattar, he gave up all his wealth, gave up his relatives too and became a *sanyasi* (renunciate). He was called as *nanjīyar*, by bhattar, respectfully. anantāḻvān, a disciple of emperumānār (bhagavad rāmānujar) had heard of his earlier affluent life and his present *sanyāsam*. He asked nanjīyar in a sorrowful tone “Oh jīyar! If you had been enjoying all the pleasures of life such as betel leaves, sandalwood paste, flower etc and taken refuge under emperumān, would you have been pushed out of paramapadam? Why did you renounce everything and become a sanyāsi?” Other śrīvaiṣṇavas, who were there, on hearing this, felt sad. However, they consoled themselves, thinking that due to his firm belief in his ācārya, he had given up everything. At that time, anantāḻvān mercifully said “It is not eminent if one gives up *gruhastāśramam* (living life as a married person) and takes up new birth in *sanyāśramam* (living life as a renunciate). By understanding well the meanings of the three parts of *tirumantram*, being born as a *śrīvaiṣṇavan* \[a person becomes a śrīvaiṣṇavan when he or she attains an ācāryan and surrenders to emperumān through the ācāryan, following five tenets\] is the true birth”. To emphasise this, he said thrice “Be born in *tirumantram*”. He mercifully said “All that you need to do is to be born in *tirumantram*, grow up in *dvayam* and be firmly engaged with meaning of dvayam”. The implied meaning of this is that “As said in *tirumantram* you should have *svarūpajñānam* \[the basic knowledge that we are servitors of emperumān and his followers\] and as said in *dvayam* we should consider emperumān as our means to attain him”.

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.3. Tadavaraiyin mide

*tadavaraiyinmīdē saraṟkāla candiranidaiyuvāvil vandu eḻundālē pōl nīyumvada maduraiyār mannan vāsudēvan kaiyilkudiyēṟi vīṟṟirundāy kōlap perum sangē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kōlam perum sangē – Oh, beautiful, huge conch!; saraṟkālam candiran – moon, during the period of autumn; uvā idaiyil – on the day of full moon; tada varaiyin mīdu vandu eḻundāl pōl – rising up, coming on the huge mountain on the eastern side; vada maduraiyār mannan vāsudēvan kaiyil – on the divine hand of vāsudēvan, the king of those who are in vada madurai \[maturā\]; nīyum kudiyēṟi – you had also taken residence; vīṟṟirundāy – you remained, manifesting all your eminence

**Перевод:**

**Oh, beautiful, huge conch! On the day of full moon, during the time of autumn, just like the moon rises up on the mountain on the eastern side, you have also taken residence on the divine hand of vāsudēvan, who is the king of the people of maturā, manifesting all your eminence.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – in this pāsuram, āṇḍāḷ celebrates the beauty of divine conch sitting on the divine hand of vāsudēvan, just like full moon on top of the mountain on the eastern side, during the period of autumn.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tadavaraiyin mīdē saraṟkāla candiran idai uvāvil vandu eḻiundālē pōl** – On the day of full moon, on top of the rising mountain, just like the full moon appears with all its sixteen parts shining brightly.

**tadavarai** – did not her father, periyāḻvār, too praise emperumān’s shoulder, as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-4 “*tadavaraittōḷ*” (shoulders which are like huge mountains)! The opinion here is that the way the divine conch sat on that divine shoulder was similar to the full moon rising on top of the mountain on the eastern side.

**saraṟkāla candiran** – during the time of autumn, the sky will be clear, not muddled by any cloud. Hence, she is citing the autumn moon as an analogy for the white, divine conch.

**idai uvāvil vandu eḻundālē pōl** – the term *uvā* refers both to new moon and full moon. Here, since moon has been cited as an analogy for conch, the term *uvā* would refer to full moon. The phrase *idai uvāvil* has got transformed into *uvā idaiyil* (during full moon).

**nīyum vada maduraiyār mannan vāsudēvan kaiyil kudiyēṟi vīṟṟirundāy** – you had also taken residence atop the divine hand of kaṇṇapirān, who is the soon of śrī vasudēvar and who is the king of maturā, inseparable from his divine hand, manifesting all your eminence. How great is your opulence! āṇḍāḷ shows through the phrase *vada maduraiyār mannan*, emperumān’s greatness, through the word *vāsudēvan*, his simplicity, and through the word *kaiyil*, his divine beauty. īsn’t this combination \[of eminence, simplicity and beauty\] one which the āḻvārs enjoy repeatedly!

**kōlap perum sangē** – Oh, the divine conch which is famous for beauty! The implied meaning is that apart from your beauty, because of your eminence, emperumān too has attained added beauty.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.
