## 7.4. Candira mandalam

*valam puriyē – ōh conc, vhic has curled on te right side!dāmōdaran kaiyil – on te divine hand of kaṇṇa pirāncandira maṇḍalam pōl – like te lunar regionandaram onṟu inṟi ēri – remaining vitout any breakavan seviyil – in his divine earsmandiram koḷvāy pōlum – you appeared as if you vere speaking secretive vordsindiranum – even indra (vho is famous for having lot of vealt)selvattukku – in matters of vealtunnōdu ēlān – vill not matc you*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the divine conch which has curled on the right side! You are remaining on the divine hand of kaṇṇa pirān, like a lunar region, without any break. You appear to be speaking secretive words with him. Even indra, who is famous for being wealthy, will not match you in matters of wealth.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells the divine conch “You, who are capable of uttering confidential matters in emperumān’s divine ears, should inform him about me” candiramaṇḍalam pōl dāmōdaran kaiyilandaram onṟinṟi ēri avan seviyilmandiram koḷvāyē pōlum valam puriyē!indiranum unnōdu selvattukku ēlānē Word-by-Word Manings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**candira maṇḍalam pōḷ dāmōdaram kaiyil ēri** – your seating on the divine hand of dāmōdaran (emperumān who has a scar on his midriff) appears like the lunar region. The way he his holding you in his divine hand, appears like princes who hold a flask of rosevater in order to treat themselves, when they suffer from cold.

**dāmōdaran kaiyil** – for those who see him, the scar on his stomach region would appear as if he had been very obedient to his mother yaśōdāp pirātti, when he allowed her to tie a rope around his stomach. The implied meaning here is that “if people see you \[addressing pāncajanyam) should you not appear to be obedient to emperumān and his followers and carry out our task?”

**dāmōdaran** – one who bears a scar on his stomach to indicate that he had been tied down by yaśōdāp pirātti with short ropes, joined together.

**andaram onṟu inṟī ēṟi** – she expresses her amazement that he is continuously on his divine hand, without separating from him ever, unlike his consorts who had to leave him at some point of time or the other. Alternatively, it could be construed that she is praising him that he is always on the divine hands of emperumān, without separating from him, unlike relatives who will be around when the person is doing well but will disappear when any misfortune befalls him. Even the divine disc, who is held by emperumān on his right hand, has to leave his divine hand, just as it has been mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-6-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/02/thiruvaimozhi-10-6-8-meynninru/) “*karudumidam porudu – kainninṟa cakkarattan*” (emperumān has the divine disc which goes where his heart desires and returns, to stay on his divine hand). You do not have such misfortune. The implied meaning is that even during the times of battle, you are residing on his hand or his mouth, without separating from him, ever.

**avan seviyil mandiram koḷvāyē pōlum** – you appear to be whispering something secretive in his divine ears. It appears as if you are whispering in his divine ear “There are many consorts who are unable to sustain themselves, after separating from you”.  The implied meaning is that “Tell him about me also”. The term *mandiram koḷḷugai* has transformed into *mandiram kodukkai*, which refers to telling him secretive information.

**valam puriyē** – while the other conces have curled towards the left side, in an anti-clockwise manner, and are termed as *idam puri* \[curled on the left side\], you have curled in a clockwise manner, towards the right and hence are called in a distinguished manner as *valam puri*. The meaning implied is that just as you have a distinguished name, having curled to the right, your action too should be distinguished, by uniting people like me, who are without any refuge, with him.

**indiranum unnōdu selvattukku ēlānē** – even indra \[lord of celestial entities\] who has the most distinguished wealth, will not equal you in matters related to *aiśvaryam* (opulence). Since she belongs to the clan of covherds who think that indra, who could make rains to pour down when he wishes to and deny rains when he wishes to, is a wealthy entity, she too considers him to be an example of wealthy person. āren’t covherds known for celebrating a festival for indra, just as it has been mentioned in [periya tirumoḻi 2-3-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/08/periya-thirumozhi-2-3-4/) “*indiranukkenṟu āyargaḷ edutta eḻil viḻavu*” (the distinguished celebration undertaken by covherds for the sake of indra)? Alternatively, just as periyāḻvār had mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-3-11 “*vēdānta viḻupporuḷin mēlirunda viḷakkai vittucittan virittananē*” (mercifully explained by viṣṇucittar who spoke about emperumān who is the most distinguished meaning among the eminent meanings mentioned in the upaniśats and who is effulgent), since she is the divine daughter of the most learned periyāḻvār, who knows that terms such as *indra, prāṇa* etc refer to emperumān who remains their *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) or even directly, we could consider the term *indra* to refer to emperumān himself. His wealth of *svātantryam* (being totally independent) will not match your wealth of *pāratantryam* (being totally dependent on emperumān). How is this possible? For a cētana entity \[one with knowledge), once *prātikūlyam* (unfavourable to emperumān) which had been with him since time immemorial, gets destroyed and he becomes an *ānukūlya* entity (one who is favourable to emperumān), emperumān’s *svātantryam* gets destroyed. The *pāratantryam* of a cētana entity is always indestructible. Thus, the *pāratantryam* of śrī pāncajanyāḻvān which lasts forever is better than emperumān’s *svātantryam* which will get destroyed at some point of time. That cētana’s *pāratantryam* is highly distinguished has been shown in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 18-28 “*sarvapriyakarastasya rāmasyāpi śarīrata*: l *lakśmanō lakśmisampannō bahi*: *prāṇa ivāpara*: ll” (lakśmaṇa, who has the wealth of servitude, was one who carried out all the affectionate deeds for śrī rāma, more than śrī rāma’s divine form, and was another *prāṇan* (soul) for śrī rāma, outside his physical form). śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 131-93 says “*sarvātmanā paryanunīyamānō yadā na saumitrir upaiti yōgam* l *niyujyamānō’pi ca yauvarājyē tatō’bhyaśinjat bharatam mahātmanā* ll” (Despite being requested in all manner and despite being ordained, since lakśmaṇa did not agree to being crowned as the *yuvarāja* (prince), śrī rāma, the most eminent entity, crowned bharata as the *yuvarāja*). Such lakśmaṇa, who refused to be crowned as the prince, despite a lot of compulsion, is fit to be called as *lakshmi sampannan* (one with lot of wealth) in the previous ślōkam \[śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 18-28\]. śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 33-28 says “*prāgēva tu mahābhāga*: *saumitrir mitranandana*: l *pūrvajasyānuyātrārtē  drumacīrairalankruta*: ll” (lakśmaṇa, who is the most fortunate, who makes those who are favourable, to be happy and who is the son of sumitrā, decorated himself with clothes made of tree bark, in order to accompany his elder brother śrī rāma, even before śrī rāma). Thus, even when perumāḷ \[śrī rāma\] avoided coronating himself, did not iḷayaperumāḷ \[lakśmaṇa\] crown himself as the prince of *pāratantryam*! Hence, it is clear that pāncajanyan’s *pāatantryam* which is unavoidable, is better than emperumān’s *svātantryam* which is avoidable.

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.5. Unnodu udane

*unnōdu udanē oru kadalil vāḻvāraiinnār inaiyār enṟ(u) eṇṇuvār illai kāṇmannāgi ninṟa madusūdan vāyamudampannāḷum uṇginṟay pāncasanniyamē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāncasanniyamē! – Oh, divine conch!; oru kadalil – in the same ocean; unnōdu udanē – along with you; vāḻvārai – many others who live; innār inaiyār enṟu eṇṇuvār illai kāṇ – see, no one respects as an entity; man āgi ninṟa – one who is the lord of every one; madusūdan – kaṇṇapirān’s; vāy amudam – nectar of the divine mouth; pal nāḷum – for a long time; uṇginṟāy – are drinking

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the divine conch! No one respects any of the many other conces, which live along with you in the same ocean, as an entity. You have been, for a long time, drinking the nectar from the mouth of madusūdanan, who is lord of all. How fortunate, are you!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – She praises pāncajanyan saying “Other conces will not be a match for you, who are constantly imbibing the nectar of madusūdanan”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**unnōdudanē ….. eṇṇuvārillai kāṇ** – In the same ocean where you lived, there are many other conces. But there is no one who has given a name to any of those conces. How great is it that you had lived a unique life! The implied meaning is that there is none who would think of the *svarūpam* (basic nature), referred to by the term *innār* in the pāsuram or their characteristics, referred to by the term *inaiyār* in the pāsuram, for all those other conces.

**mannāgi ninṟa** – One who is the head of all the worlds, as mentioned in mahābhāratam āśva parvam 43-13 “*rājādi rājas sarvēśām viṣṇur brahmamayō mahān* l *īśvaram tam vijānīma*: *sa pitā sa prajāpati* ll” (viṣṇu alone is the radiant entity who is the emperor of all the kings, who is eminent as *brahmasvarūpi* (with the nature of the supreme entity). We all know him alone as *īśvaran* (one who controls all). He alone is the father; he is also the progenitor).

**madusūdan** – one who annihilated the monster, madu. This (madusūdanan) is not merely a divine name, but implies that he has the nature of weeding out hurdles for his followers.

**vāy amudam** – the nectar from the divine mouth of one who has such eminence

**pannāḷum uṇginṟāy** – we, his consorts, are the ones who have to constantly drink in that nectar. However, you enjoy that always, making us to enjoy it on occasions only.

**pānca sanniyamē** – Oh, conch, born from the stomach of the monster, *pancasanan*! I, born to periyāḻvār, am falling at your feet while you, born to a monster, are enjoying every day.

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.6. Poyt tirtamadade

*pōyttīrttamādādē ninṟa puṇarmarudamsāyttīrttān kaittalattē ēṟik kudikoṇḍusēyttīrttamāy ninṟa sengaṇmāl tannudaiyavāyttīrttam pāyndāda vallāy valam puriyē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

valam puriyē! – Oh, the conch which has curled on the right side!; pōy – going for a long distance; tīrttam – in rivers such as gangā etc; ādādē – without having to undergo difficulties for taking a bath; ninṟa – standing as two trees, due to the curse of sage nārada; puṇar marudam – twin maruda trees \[a species of tree, Indian laurel\]; sāyttu īrttān – kaṇṇa pirān who felled them, breaking them into two, his; kaittalam ēṟi – going on to this divine hand; kudi koṇḍu – residing (there) permanently; sēy tīrttam āy ninṟa – the most sacred water for the entire world; sem kaṇ māl tannudaiya – kaṇṇapirān, who has lotus-like eyes, his; vāy tīrttam – in the water, which is in his divine mouth; pāyndu – settling; Ada vallāy – you have the fortune of having a bath

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the divine conch which has curled on the right side! For taking a bath you do not have to walk a long distance and face difficulties in having a bath in rivers such as gangā etc. You can get on to the divine hand of kaṇṇapirān, who snapped the twin maruda trees \[by going between then\], reside permanently there and have a bath in the most sacred water for the entire world, which is in the divine mouth of kaṇṇa pirān, who has lotus-like eyes. How fortunate are you!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – She praises pāncajanyam saying “You are fortunate that for taking a bath, instead of having to run around everywhere, remaining in the place where you are staying, you could take bath in the most pious water which is coming from the divine coral mouth”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pōyt tīrttamādādē** – people in the world walk for thousands of miles in order to immerse themselves in salty waters and brackish waters. While such is the situation, while staying in the same place where you are residing, you are immersing yourself in the most sacred, most enjoyable water; how to call your fortunate situation!

**ninṟa puṇar marudam sāyttu īrttān kaittalattē** – two celestial entities, *naḷa* and *kūbara*, due to a curse by sage nārada, became maruda trees. A monster, set upon kriṣṇa by kamsa, pervaded those trees which hardly had any gap between them. Creating a space between those two maruda trees, kaṇṇa pirān went between those two trees, dragging a mortar to which he had been tied, by yaśōdā. When the mortar went between them, the trees snapped and fell down. Hearing that noise, emperumān turned back, with a childish face. On the divine hand of such emperumān…

**ēṟik kudikoṇḍu** – Just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-3-11 “*upasamhara sarvātman*! *rūpam ētaccaturbhujam* l *jānātu mā’vatāram tē kamśō’yam ditijanmaja* ll” (Oh one who is all pervading! Please hide your divine form with four divine hands. Let not kamsan, the monster, know about your divine incarnation), śrī pāncajanyāḻvān had seen the danger which came when he hid himself, along with the divine disc, in order to fulfil the command of parents, when born as a human entity. Hence, he firmly decided “From now onvards, we should never separate ourselves from emperumān”. Hence, he dwells continuously on emperumān’s hand, without ever separating from him.

**sēyt tīrttamāy ninṟa** – remaining as the sweetest and most sacred water. This term could be taken as an adjective for *vāyttīrttam* and also for *sengaṇmāl*. śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 82-9 says “*pāvanas sarvalōkānām tvamēva rughunandana*” (Oh raghunandana! \[śrī rāma\] You are the one who purifies all the worlds); mahābhāratam vana parvam tīrtayātrā 88-26 says “*pavitrāṇām hi gōvinda: pavitram param ucyatē* l *puṇyānāmapi puṇyōsau mangalānānca mangaḷam* ll” (Among the pure entities, gōvinda is considered as the purest; among the virtuous entities, he is the most virtuous; among the auspicious entities, he is the most auspicious). [tiruvāimoḻi 2-8-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-8-6-thirththan/) says “*tīrttan ulagaḷanda sēvadi..*” (the divine feet of the purest entity who measured all the worlds..). As seen from these pramāṇams (authentic texts), when he is spoken of as the most pious entity, is it needed to say that the divine nectar from his mouth is the most distinguished one!

**sengaṇmāḷ tannudaiya** – cāndōjña upaniśat says that emperumān has divine eyes which are like lotus, just then blossomed by sūrya (sun). Such sarvēśvaran’s ….

**vāyttīrttam pāyndāda vallāy** – just like one immerses in that portion of the sea where nectar is available, you are capable of immersing in the nectar of his divine mouth which is the most pious. Are you not standing near the entrance to that divine mouth and having your bath!

**valam puriyē** – just as you have a distinction in your enjoyment, you have a distinction in your physical feature too, by curling on the right side, says āṇḍāḷ, praising him.

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.7. Sengamala nanmalar

*sengamala nāṇmalarmēl tēn nugarum annam pōlsengaṇ karumēni vāsudēvanudaiyaangaittalam ēri anna vasam seyyumsangaraiyā! un selvam sāla aḻagiyadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nāḷ – just then blossomed; sem kamala malar mēl – on a reddish lotus flower (settling down); tēn – honey; nugarum – drinking; annam pōl – like the bird swan; sem kaṇ karumēni vāsudēvanudaiya – kaṇṇa pirān, who has reddish divine eyes and dark; am kaittalam ēṟi – climbing on to his beautiful, divine hand; anna vasam seyyum – one who is reclining; sangu araiyā! – Oh pāncajanyam, the best among the conces!; un selvam – your wealth; sāla – to a great extent; aḻagiyadu – see, it is eminent

**Перевод:**

**Just like a swan which is drinking honey from a reddish lotus flower that had just then blossomed, you have climbed on to the beautiful, divine hand of vāsudēvan, who has reddish divine eyes and dark, divine physical form. Oh pāncajanyam, the best among the conces! Your wealth of reclining on the divine hand of emperumān is the most eminent.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – in this pāsuram, āṇḍāḷ praises the divine conch saying “The way you are remaining on the divine hand of vāsudēvan, which looks like a lotus, is like a swan which is drinking honey from a lotus flower”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sengamala nāṇmalar mēl tēn nugarum annam pōl** – The way śrī pāncajanyāḻvān is sitting on the reddish lotus-like divine hand of emperumān is similar to a swan sitting on a just then blossomed lotus flower and drinking its honey.

**sengaṇ karumēni vāsudēvanudaiya angaittalam ēṟi** – taittirīya upaniśat bhruguvalli 10-4 says “*aham annam aham annam aham annam* l *aham annādō’ham annādō’ham annāda*: ll” (\[the soul, when it reaches parama padam, says\] I am enjoyable, like food \[for emperumān\]; this is repeated two more times. I eat the food \[which is the happiness of emperumān (when he eats me)\]. This is also repeated two more times). As mentioned in this, due to the contact with pāncajanyan, who is enjoyable like food for the hungry, emperumān’s divine eyes manifest the noble quality of *vātsalyam* (forbearance) and his cool, divine physical form removes the anguish of the beholder. Climbing on to the divine hand of such vāsudēvan…

**annavasam seyyum** – just as those who wish to sleep, recline on mattresses and keep tossing from side to side in order to get the food digested, pāncajanyāḻvān also reclines. Just as nammāḻvār had said in [tiruvāimoḻi 9-7-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/08/thiruvaimozhi-9-7-3-thakkilame-kelirgal/) “*cekkamalattalar pōlum kaṇ kai kāl senganivāy, akkamalattilai pōlum tirumēniyadigaḷukkē*” (eyes, hands, divine feet and divine lips which are like reddish blossomed lotus flower, and a contrasting divine dark form like a dense leaf) emperumān has his divine limbs which are like lotus flowers, which hide the pond in which the lotus flowers have bloomed. His divine form is like a green leaf which shows up amongst the lotus flowers. Swans reach that place in order to drink honey from the lotus flowers. āṇḍāḷ enjoys the whitish complexion and the great beauty of śrī pāncajanyāḻvān who adds colour to the divine form and divine limbs of emperumān.

**sangaraiyā** – Oh, the king of conces! śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 1-61 says “*tatō vānararājēna vairānugatanam prati* l *rāmāyāvēditam sarvam praṇayāt du:kkitēna ca* ll” (Subsequently, sugrīva, the king of monkeys, who was in great distress, informed śrī rāma with affection, all that happened, while giving information to śrī rāma about how enmity developed with vāli). Just as sugrīva was the king of monkeys, śrī pāncajanyāḻvān was the king of conces. In the ślōkam cited above, it is to be relished that vālmīki referred to sugrīva as the king of monkeys even before he was coronated, since śrī rāma had decided in his divine mind that sugrīva is the king of monkeys. Just as śrī rāma had decided in his divine mind that sugrīva is the king of monkeys, he had also decided that jatāyu is the king of vultures, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam 68-23 ”*grudrarājyam parityajya pitrupaitāmaham mahat* l *mama hētōrayam prāṇān mumōca patagēśvara*: ll” (Giving up the huge kingdom of vultures which came to him through his ancestors, this jatāyu, the king of vultures, gave up his life for my cause). Since he has qualified the kingdom of vultures as huge, through the term *mahat* (*pitru paitāmaham mahat grudrarājyam parityajya*), perumāḷ \[śrī rāma\] thinks in his divine mind that the kingdom which jatāyu gave up for him was much bigger than his *ikśvāku* kingdom which he gave up when he left ayōdyā.

**sangaraiyā** – just as vālmīki had referred to sugrīva as the king of monkeys and jatāyu as the king of vultures, considering their closeness and affection towards perumāḷ, āṇḍāḷ too refers to pāncajanyāḻvān as the king of conces, considering his closeness and affection towards emperumān. The implied meaning is that his wealth is such great that all the conces fall at his feet and accept him as their emperor.

**un selvam sāla aḻagiyadē** – she says that his wealth is very distinguished, more than the way in which she had described his wealth in the fourth pāsuram of this decad when she said “*indiranum unnōdu selvattukku ēlānē*”.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.8. Unbadu sollil

*uṇbadu sollil ulagaḷandān vāyamudamkaṇpadai koḷḷil kadalvaṇṇan kaittalattēpeṇpadaiyār un mēl perum pūsal sāṟṟuginṟārpaṇbala seyginṟāy paṇcasanniyamē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāncasanniyamē! – Oh, conch!; uṇbadu sollil – to say about what you eat; ulagu aḷandāṇ vāy amudam – nectar from the divine mouth of emperumān who measured all the worlds; kaṇ padai koḷḷil – to talk about where you sleep; kadal vaṇṇan kaittalattē – on the divine hand of that emperumān who has the complexion of ocean; peṇ padaiyār – all the womenfolk; un mēl – on matters related to you; perum pūsal sāṟṟuginṟār – \[they\] are protesting loudly (that you are robbing them of their sustenance); paṇbu ala seyginṟay – you are carrying out unreasonable act (such that they are distressed)

**Перевод:**

**Oh, conch! You eat the nectar from the divine mouth of emperumān who measured all the worlds. You sleep on the divine hand of that emperumān, who has the complexion of ocean. Since this is the way that you eat and sleep, all the womenfolk are protesting loudly, in matters related to you. You are carrying out unreasonable acts.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – In this as well as the next pāsuram, āṇḍāḷ, appearing to accuse pāncajanyam, saying “You are doing inappropriate deeds”, is actually proclaiming his greatness.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**uṇbadu sollil ulagaḷandān …… kaittattē** – wealthy people in the world would be only eating and sleeping. You too are eating the most distinguished food which is the nectar from the divine mouth of one who measured the worlds and sleeping on his divine hand, which is softer than lotus flower. This is how distinguished are your eating and sleeping activities.

**vāyamudam kaittalattē** – your wealth is such that even after partaking of your food, there will still be plenty left on hand. The wealth, which is common to all followers of emperumān’s, is the kingdom of donning his divine feet on the head. Your wealth is not common, like that. It is the wealth of eating the nectar from emperumān’s divine mouth which is exclusive to his consorts. You eat, as emperumān feeds you, glance at his divine form which removes the ennui and recline on his divine hand. You are like the devotees who eat to their heart’s content, the most enjoyable, divine foods which are available in temples, don the garland given in the evening and rest in the small rooms in the temple, in comfort.

**peṇ padaiyār un mēl perum pūsal sāṟṟuginṟār** – All those born as women, for whom that nectar and the divine form would be the sustenance, are blaming you.

**peṇ padaiyār** – the implied meaning is that all the women in tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam) and all the women in maturā, have joined together as a huge army and are blaming you.

**paṇbu ala seyginṟāy** – you are indulging in unreasonable act which is not at all well-mannered. The act which you are indulging in, is not one which the most devoted followers of emperumān, who engage with matters related to emperumān and who desire more interaction with other devotees of emperumān, would do. Those who are close to emperumān would not enjoy anything for only self, but enjoy along with other followers.

**pāncasanniyamē** – this act appears to be appropriate for you, who had been born from a monster. She finds fault with pāncajanyam since both her father, periyāḻvār, who had said as in [tiruppallāṇḍu 3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-3-vazhatpattu/) “*vāḻātpattu ninṟīr uḷḷeerēl* ”, and she herself, who had said in [tiruppāvai 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-01-margazhi-thingal/) “*pōduvīr pōduminō*”, have only enjoyed emperumān along with other followers of emperumān.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.9. Padinaram ayiravar

*padināṟām āyiravar dēvimār pārttiruppamaduvāyil koṇḍāṟpōl mādavan tan vāyamudampoduvāga uṇbadanaip pukku nī uṇḍakkālsidaiyārō? unnōdu selvap perum sangē*

**Пословный перевод:**

peru selvam sangē – Oh, conch, which has huge wealth!; padināṟām āyiravar dēvimār – sixteen thousand consorts; pārttu iruppa – while they were expecting (to drink the nectar of the divine mouth of kaṇṇa pirān); poduvāga uṇbadu mādavan than vāy amudattai – nectar of that emperumān’s divine mouth which is to be consumed by all the followers, as a common food; nī pukku – you alone, seizing; madu vāyil koṇḍāl pōl uṇḍakkāl – if you imbibe, just as beetles imbibe honey; unnōdu sidaiyārō – will (the others) not argue with you?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, conch, who has a huge wealth! While sixteen thousand consorts (of kaṇṇapirān) were waiting (to desirously drink the nectar of that emperumān’s divine mouth), you seized it for only yourself. That nectar is common to all the followers of emperumān. If you were to drink that nectar like beetles drink honey, will the others not argue with you?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells pāncajanyam that it is not proper on his part to eat all by himself, the nectar of emperumān’s divine mouth when it is common to emperumān’s sixteen thousand consorts.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**padināṟām āyiravar dēvimār pārtiruppa** – should you eat all the food while sixteen thousand consorts \[of emperumān\] are made to fast?

**padināṟāmāyiravar ….. uṇḍakkāl** – just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 31-17 & 18 “*tā*: *kanyā narakēṇāsan sarvatō yās samāhrutā*: l *ēkasminnēva gōvinda*: *kālē tāsām mahāmunē* ll *jagrāha vidivat pāṇīn prutag gēhēśu darmata*: l *śōdaśastrī sahasrāṇi śatamēkam tatō’dikam* ll (Oh sage! kriṣṇa, at the same time, married all the sixteen thousand one hundred women, who were imprisoned by the monster *naraka,* individually, at their respective house (by taking as many forms)). While sixteen thousand consorts were waiting for a suitable time to drink the nectar of his divine mouth, if you alone were to consume it, which is common for all, just like beetles drink honey….

**sidaiyārō unnōdu** – if all the consorts join together and ostracise you, saying “He is outside our assembly”, will you not lose the wealth of drinking the nectar of his divine mouth and reach the level of having to eat soil and sand? What will you do if they decide to keep you away, just as my \[āṇḍāl’s\] father periyāḻvār said in [tiruppallāṇḍu 3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-3-vazhatpattu/) “*kūḻātpattu ninṟīrgaḷai engaḷ kuḻuvinil pugudalottōm*” (we will not allow those who go after materialistic wealth, to enter our assembly)? āren’t they ones who enjoy emperumān only after getting consent from followers of emperumān, just as periyāḻvār had said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-6-11 “*sādu kōttiyuḷ koḷḷappaduvārē*” (they will be allowed to come into the assembly of emperumān’s followers)?

**selvap perum sangē** – Are you not carrying out this activity because of the arrogance of your wealth? īsn’t your wealth superior to our wealth, which is the basis of his \[emperumān’s\] wealth!

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 7.10. Panca sanniyattai

*pāṇcasanniyattaip paṟpanābanōdumvāynda perum suṟṟamākkiya oṇ puduvaiēynda pugaḻ battarpirān kōdai tamiḻ īraindumāyndētta vallār avarum aṇukkarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāncasanniyattai – conch; paṟpanābanōdum – with emperumān; vāynda perum suṟṟam ākkiya – one who created great relationship with emperumān; oṇ puduvai – one who mercifully incarnated in the beautiful śrīvilliputtūr; ēynda pugaḻ – one who has complete eminence; battar pirān kōdai – āndāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār; tamiḻ īr aindum – these ten pāsurams in tamiḻ language; āyndu – meditating upon; ētta vallār avarum – those who could worship (emperumān, with these); aṇukkar – will be confidantes of emperumān (just like me, āṇḍāḷ)

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ created great relationship for the conch, pāncajanyam, with emperumān; she mercifully incarnated in the beautiful śrīvilliputtūr; she has complete eminence and is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār; she composed these ten pāsurams in tamiḻ language. Those who could worship emperumān, with these pāsurams, will become confidantes of emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – Since pāncajanyam did not respond to any of her questions, in this last pāsuram of this decad, where she tells the benefits of reciting this pāsuram, she severs her relationship with pāncajanyam and brings the decad to an end by saying as the benefit “Those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams will become confidantes of emperumān such that they can sever their relationship with pāncajanyam”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pāncasanniyattaip paṟpanābanōdum vāynda perum suṟṟamākkiya kōdai** – the meaning implied in this is that since āṇḍāḷ says “kōdai \[āṇḍāḷ\] made a great relationship for pāncajanyam with padmanābhan \[emperumān\]” it is clear that śrī pāncajanyam did not respond to āṇḍāḷ’s queries in this decad, due to which she severed her relationship with him and made him related only to emperumān.

**paṟpanābanōdum** – by bringing in the word *paṟpanāban* (padmanābhan), which refers to one who has navel which looks like lotus flower, she speaks about the beauty of emperumān’s divine form. Through the usage of this term, she tells pāncajanyam “Just as you alone enjoy, with unfettered liberty, the divine nectar of emperumān’s divine mouth which is food for us, who have nowhere else to go, you alone enjoy the divine beauty of his divine form too, which is to be enjoyed by everyone”.

**oṇpuduvai ēynda pugaḻp pattar pirān kōdai** – āṇḍāḷ incarnated in the radiant śrīvilliputtūr and is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār who has overfilling eminence. If some were to ask “Could āṇḍāḷ be angry with śrī pāncajanyāḻvān who is a confidante of emperumān?” the response would be “Since she is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, she has the right to get angry with śrī pāncajanyāḻvān and it is appropriate that she is angry with him”. She has fame throughout the world for this. The terms *oṇ puduvai* and *ēynda pugaḻ* could also be considered as speaking about the fame of periyāḻvār. In this case, *ēynda pugaḻ* would refer to periyāḻvār getting the fame of making emperumān to manifest himself to him when periyāḻvār established the supremacy of emperumān \[in response to the pāṇḍiya king’s doubt\], and he was praised by the pāṇḍiya king.

**kōdai tamiḻ īraindum āyndētta vallār** – those who are capable of reciting this tirumoḻi (divine words in this decad) mercifully composed by āṇḍāḷ, along with meditating on their meanings…..

**avarum aṇukkarē** – they will also become very close to emperumān, just like me, āṇḍāḷ, such that they too can get angry with śrī pāncajanyāḻvān.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the eighth decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 8.1. Vinneela melappu

*viṇṇeela mēlāppu virittāṟpōl mēgangāḷteṇṇīrpāy vēngadatten tirumālum pōndānēkaṇṇīrgaḷ mulaikkuvattil tuḷisōrac cōrvēnaippeṇṇīrmai īdaḻikkum idu tamkkōr perumaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

viṇ – throughout the sky; neelam mēlāppu virittāl pōl – remaining like a spread-out, blue coloured canopy; mēgangāḷ! – Oh, clouds!; teḷ nīr pāy – the place where clear streams are flowing; vēngadattu – mercifully dwelling on the hills of tiruvēngadam; en tirumālum – emperumān, who is the divine consort of śrī mahālakśmi; pōndānē – has he mercifully come (along with you)?; mulai kuvattil – on the edge of bosom; kaṇ nīrgaḷ tuḷisōra – with tears dripping; sōrvēnai – one who is in distress, my; peṇ nīrmai īdu aḻikkum idu – this (act of) destroying femineity; tamakku – for him; ōr perumaiyē – did it stand as a sign of eminence?

**Перевод:**

**Oh clouds, who are remaining like a spread-out blue canopy throughout the sky! Has emperumān, who mercifully dwells on the hills of tiruvēngadam, and who is the divine consort of śrī mahālakśmi, come with you all? Please ask him whether this act of his, by which he is making me feel distressed, with tears dripping on the edge of my bosom, stood as a sign of eminence.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells the clouds to ask tiruvēngadamudaiyān “Would destroying my (āṇḍāḷ’s) femineity, be an act of eminence for you?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**viṇṇeela mēlāppu virittāṟpōl** – clouds have spread out throughout the sky, as if a blue coloured canopy has been spread. āṇḍāḷ considers that this appears like a canopy which has been put out so that she and her consort could sit as if in a royal court. For both perumāḷ and pirātti, don’t nitya vibhūti (parama padam) and leelā vibhūti (this world) exist as servitors? She thinks that the reason for creating the world and spreading out the clouds is for emperumān and her to hold court.

**mēgangāḷ** – when sītāp pirātti had to send a message, hanuman alone was the messenger. The distinction for āṇḍāḷ \[over sītāp pirātti\] is that she has many clouds to be sent as messengers. śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 30-3 says “*yām kapīnām sahasrāṇi subhahūnyayutāni ca* l *dikśu sarvāsu mārgantē sēyamāsāditā mayā* ll” (She, who was searched in all directions by thousands of monkeys in many groups, was found out by me). hanuman felt sad that though thousands of monkeys had set out to search for sītāp pirātti, the others did not have the fortune to see her and that only he could see her. Clouds did not have that sadness, since all of them would be seeing emperumān. These two references have been cited to confirm that she called out to a group of clouds. When kings come out to participate in water games, a few people called as *mēgar* would come with him and take part in the water games. In the same manner, she thinks that these clouds too have participated in the water games and have come with wet physical forms.

**teṇṇīr pāy vēngadattu** – in tiruvēngadam hills, where clear streams keep flowing continuously. In all the divine abodes where he is standing, all the entities would have clarity, due to his proximity. śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 59-5 says “*upataptōdakā nadya*: *palvalāni sarāmsi ca* l *pariśuśkapalāśāni vanānyupavanāni ca* ll” ((Due to separation from śrī rāma) rivers, pools and lakes dried up; forests and gardens had parched leaves). Just as these dried up, my place too became dried up, says āṇḍāḷ.

**en tirumālum pōndānē** – when śrī rāma set out to go to the forest, sītāp pirātti said, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 27-6 “*yadi tvam prastitō durgam vanamadyaiva rāghava* l *agratastē gamiśyāmi mrudnatī kuśakaṇṭakān* ll” (Oh rāghava! If you set out to go to the forest which is difficult to be crossed over, I will go today itself, ahead of you, to remove the reed grass and thorns (such that your feet do not get hurt) by stepping on them with my feet). My tirumāl walked behind his consort, sītāp pirātti. Since there is no reason for him to make me tremble \[in fear whether he has come or not\], he too should have come with you. She is asking the clouds “Has he come with you all?” Since she is a servitor of pirātti and since she \[pirātti\] is her *puruśakāram* (one who recommends to emperumān for acceptance), to make these evident, she calls him as *en tirumāl* (my śriman nārāyaṇa). If they \[clouds\] had the ability to speak, they would have responded to her question whether he has come with them by saying “Yes, he is with us”. However, since they could not speak, they remained quiet. Due to her bewilderment, not realising that they cannot speak, she thinks that they are keeping quiet since he had not come. Tears started flooding her eyes, on knowing that he had not come. There was no one to tell her, just as hanuman told sītāp pirātti, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 33-4 “*kimartam tava nētrābhyām vāri sravati śōkajam* l *puṇḍarīka palāśābhyām viprakīrṇamivōdakam*” (Oh pirātti! Just like water coming out of lotus leaf, why are tears flowing out of your divine eyes?) Hence, she is talking on her own, starting with *kaṇṇīrgaḷ*. In the ślōkam cited above, hanuman’s query of “Why are tears of sorrow flowing from your two divine eyes?” is with the opinion “For uprooting which clan are tears of sorrow flowing from your divine eyes?” Alternatively, another opinion could be considered for hanuman’s question to sītāp pirātti as to why tears are flowing from her divine eyes, like water flowing from lotus leaf – before seeing pirātti, hanuman was totally engaged with perumāḷ (śrī rāma). After seeing pirātti, separated from perumāḷ, with teary eyes, his devotion towards her became deeper. In that situation, it could be construed that he has told her “Should not perumāḷ see you, shedding tears of sorrow, after being separated from him? The others who see you with tears are equivalent to *acētana* entities (without knowledge); when perumāḷ, who should see your tears, is not present here, what is the benefit of your shedding the tears?”

**kaṇṇīrgaḷ mulaikkuvattil tuḷisōra** – emperumān is dwelling in tirumalai continuously, only because the clouds are constantly staying there and sprinkling rain water there. āṇḍāḷ says that to make him come to her, she will create tirumalai in her divine form. Just as streams gain in quantum of water and start flowing on to the peaks in the tirumalai hills after the clouds have poured out their water, her tears, which flow out of her eyes, start flowing towards her bosoms.

**tuḷisōrac cōrvēnai** – the meaning for this is not this – as tears fall, I too become tired (*sōra* would mean ‘to fall’ and *cōra* would mean ‘to get tired’). The proper meaning for this verse is – since the tears of sorrow which fall are like sparks from fire, she is also getting tired. I, who am getting tired, because tears of sorrow are falling…

**peṇṇīrmai īdaḻikkumidu** – Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-30 “*śaraistu sankulām krutvā lankām parabalārtdana*: l *mām nayēd yadi kākutstas tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt* ll” (If śrī rāmapirān, who annihilates the strength of his enemies, bewilders lankā with his arrows and takes me with him, it will be apt for him), the nature of a lady is to attain emperumān through him and not through her efforts. āṇḍāḷ says that her act of sending clouds as messengers to him is similar to destroying her femineity. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-8-35 says “*dēvatiryang manuśyēśu punnāmā bhagavān hari*: l *strīnāmnī lakśmīr maitrēya nānyayōr vidyatē param*” (Oh maitrēya! Among the species of dēva (celestial), tiryak (animal), manuśya (human) entities, all males are representatives of bhagavān hari; all females are representatives of lakshmi; there is nothing superior to these two). As said in this, since he is the noblest male and she is the noblest female entity, is it not proper that due to his total independence, he could destroy her femineity and due to her strong urge in attaining him, she could have her femineity destroyed? Alternatively, taking the above-mentioned reference, since it could be said that masculinity ends with him and femineity ends with periya pirāttiyār \[śrī mahālakśmi\], he is destroying my \[āṇḍāḷ’s\] femineity. If we have to analyse her femineity, just as she had mentioned in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 1-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/06/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-1-5/) “*mānidavarkkenṟu pēccuppadil vāḻagillēn*” (I will not sustain myself if I were to be married to anyone other than emperumān), her femineity would get destroyed if she were to relate herself to anyone other than emperumān. If we have to analyse his masculinity, it would be similar to that which has been mentioned in varadarāja stavam 63 “*parijana paribūrhā bhūśaṇānyāyutāni pravaraguṇagaṇāśca jñānaśaktyādayastē* l *paramapadamatāṇḍānyātma dēhas tadātmā varada*! *sakalam ētat samśritārtam cakarta* ll” (Oh, varada! You have made all your servitors (such as viśvaksēnar, ādiśēśan et al), divine decorations, divine ornaments, divine weapons, esteemed and auspicious qualities such as knowledge, strength etc, śrī vaikuṇṭam, all the universes, your divine auspicious form, divine basic nature etc for the sake of your followers) – his total independence by which he creates all his wealth for the sake of his followers. These are the boundaries for femineity and masculinity. He is not manifesting his *svātantryam* (total independence), but is making her say “*tirumālum pōndānē*” (did tirumāl also come); she is sending clouds as her messengers, manifesting his *svātantryam* in destroying the *pāratantryam* (dependence on emperumān) which is her femineity.

**idu tamakkōr perumaiyē** – he has the eminence as being the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; if he does not manifest his face to āṇḍāḷ, who is a limb of śrī mahālakśmi, the result is only an offence on his part \[which he has committed\]. Could it be considered as a greatness? {not at all\]. śrī parāśara bhattar says in his śrī guṇaratnakōśam 26 “*bhōjñāvāmapi nāntarīyakatayā puśpāngarāgais samam nirvruttapraṇayātivāhanavidau nītā*: *parīvāhatām* l  *dēvi*! *tvāmanu neeḷayā saha mahī dēvya*: *sahasram tatā yābhi stvam stanabāhudruśtibhir iva svābhi*: *priyam ślāgasē* ll” (Oh mahālakśmi! Consorts such as bhūmi dēvi, neeḷā dēvi et al, through whom you make emperumān happy just as you make through your bosom, shoulders, glance etc, are like implements for your enjoyment such as flower, sandalwood paste etc. When your togetherness exceeds boundaries, they will be the channel for letting it out). As seen in this, when I \[āṇḍāḷ\], who am like the divine hands or divine eyes of periya pirāttiyār, suffer, if emperumān were to merely look at that \[without taking steps to remove my suffering\], is it not a shortcoming for his compassion, which is his *svarūpam* (basic nature)? When someone has a deficiency will he wait for someone else to remove that? Should he not remove that deficiency himself? Alternatively, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 37-38 \[when mārīca tells rāvaṇa when he seeks mārīca’s help in abducting sītāp pirātti\] “*apramēyam hi tattējō yasya sā janakātmajā* l *na tvam samartas tām hartum rāmacāpāśrayāmvanē* ll” (The effulgence of that śrī rāma, with whom sītāp pirātti, the daughter of janaka, is together, is beyond one’s imagination. You are not capable of abducting her, who has taken refuge under śrī rāma’s bow, in the forest), while āṇḍāḷ remains as one who is creating eminence for him, when she is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is his confidante, is it apt for him if she feels distressed at not attaining him, despite desiring him? Should he not take remedial action for removing her sorrow?

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.2. Mamutta nidi

*māmutta nidi soriyum māmugilgāḷ! vēngadattuccāmattin niṟam koṇḍa tāḷāḷan vārttai ennē!kāmattī uḷpugundu kaduvappattu idaikkangulēmattōr tenṟalukku ingu ilakkāy nān iruppēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mā muttam nidi soriyum – showering distinguished pearls and gold; mā mugilgāḷ – Oh, the rain bearing clouds!; vēngadattu – mercifully residing in tirumalai; sāmattin niṟam koṇḍa – one who has bluish complexion; tāḷāḷan – emperumān’s; vārttai ennē – is there any message?; kāmamtī – the fire of love; uḷ pugundu – entering inside; kaduvappattu – grabbing, distressed due to that; kangul – during night; idai ēmattu – in the middle portion \[midnight\]; ōr tenṟalukku – for a gently blowing breeze; nān ilakkāy – becoming a target, I; ingu iruppēnē – would I remain here?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, rain bearing clouds, who are showering distinguished pearls and gold! Do you have any message from emperumān, who has a bluish complexion and who is mercifully residing in tirumalai? The fire of love has entered me and grabbed me, causing me distress. Would I become a target for the gently blowing breeze, at the middle of night and remain here?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she asks the clouds “Has emperumān sent any message for me, who is being troubled by the soft breeze?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mā mutta nidi soriyum māmugilgāḷ** – Oh dark clouds, who are showering most invaluable pearls and gold! āren’t you entities, who have helping others alone as your nature, not having to put on an act of being magnanimous towards me?

**soriyum** – instead of giving to someone only when the person asks for it, aren’t you the ones who cannot sustain yourselves unless you give to others?

**māmugilgāḷ** – Oh, distinguished clouds! What is the distinction for these clouds? Instead of waiting for someone to seek alms or instead of giving, as a quid-pro-quo measure, giving it as a benefit for themselves, and becoming drained of colour if they do not give.

**vēngadattu … vārttai ennē** – Since you \[addressing the clouds\] reside in the same place where he resides, the message which he has conveyed for me cannot be unknown to you.

**sāmattin niṟam koṇḍa** – other than living in the same place, is he also not matching you, in the complexion?

**tāḷāḷan** – through the term *tāḷ*, which refers to the divine feet, she is talking about his entire divine form, which is subservient to him. In other words, he has his divine form as his servitor. While you \[clouds\] are holding something for others in your form, isn’t he having as his form what is meant for others, just as it has been mentioned in jitantē stōtram 1-5 “*na tē rūpam na ca ākārō na āyudāni na cāspatam* l *tatā’pi puruśākārō bhaktānām tvam prakāśasē* ll” ((your) divine basic nature is not for you; the divine auspicious form is also not for you; the divine weapons also are not for you; the abode of śrī vaikuṇṭam is also not for you. All these are for the sake of your followers only. You belong to your followers. Though you have *svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam* and *vibhūti* (basic nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth, respectively) which are for your followers, you shine as the supreme entity)? Alternatively, through customary usage, the term *tāḷāḷan* could be referring to an eminent person. The implied meaning is “Since we have such eminence, should we go on our own and embrace someone? Let those, who desire to embrace us, come”, standing aloof, with eminence.

**vārttai ennē** – even though he has not come \[here\], if he had thought that if he does not bestow his mercy on me, the loss is his, he would not have remained without uttering a word. Tell me, what is it?

**ennē** – what is his word? With the embarrassment that “We have not come forward to help her”, he would have said one of the following:

1. You find out as to what she is doing, or
2. the word mentioned by śrī rāma, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10  “*ciram jīvati vaidēhi yadi māsam dariśyati* l *na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi vinā tāmasitēkśaṇām* ll (If sītā could sustain herself for one month, she would be considered as having sustained herself for a very long time; without her, who has dark eyes, I will not sustain myself for more than a moment), or
3. just as duśyantan said, with regard to śakuntalā, “I do not know of any āṇḍāḷ who is related to me”

She thinks that she could sustain herself, hearing him say any of these words. Is there anyone who had sustained himself, hearing such words? Did not arjuna sustain himself, after hearing bhagavat gītā from emperumān, just as he had said in [bhagavat gītā 18-73](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/18-73/)? “*naśtō mōha*: *smrutir labdā tvatprasādān mayā’cyuta* l *stitō’smi gatasandēha*: *kariśyē vacanam tava* ll” (Oh, acyuta! My delusion (of false knowledge) has been destroyed. True knowledge has been attained by me, because of you. My doubt has been annihilated. I will carry out, as per your word (I will carry out this war)). Did not nammāḻvār too desire to hear a word from emperumān, even if it is an unfavourable one, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-7-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/thiruvaimozhi-4-7-3-iviladha-thivinaigal/) “*pāvī nī enṟu onṟu sollāy pāviyēn kāṇa vandē*” (You are not coming in front of me and telling me a word ‘you are a sinner’). Thus, despite āṇḍāḷ asking them “What is the message?” the clouds did not respond. Feeling very distressed due to that, she tells with anguish, in the second half of the pāsuram …

**kāmattī uḷ pugundu kaduvappattu** – is he thinking that this fire of love is like the normal fire seen in the world? Compared to fire seen in this world, fire in *narakam* (hell) will be many times more cruel. Wouldn’t this fire of love be many times more cruel than the fire in *narakam*?

**utpugundu** – it \[fire of love\] started burning out the physical form seen outside; after destroying it fully, it started singeing ātmā (soul) inside. Since this fire of love comes out of the affection from the ātmā, its act of singeing is also inside.

**kaduvappttu** – due to the act of seizing indulged in by the fire of love, the entire form withers off. After saying *utpugundu* where it was seen that the ātmā got burnt out fully, since the pāsuram again says *kaduvappattu*, it appears that the burnt-out part again got burnt out, like firewood.

**idaikkangul ēmattu** – should be transposed as *kangul idaiyil yāmattu* – in the middle of night. Alternatively, the term *ēmattu* could be construed as ‘in the night, at the place which is secured’. She wanted to enjoy with emperumān in a secure place. Since he did not come, that desire did not get fulfilled. The implied meaning is that since it is a lonely place with security, she was suffering since there was none to even console her. The term *ēmam* also has other meanings such as beauty, pain etc. Here, only time (midnight) and protection (secured place) would be appropriate.

**ōr tenṟalukku** – to the soft breeze which has unique strength to harass me. Is emperumān waiting, since he doesn’t know the strength of this breeze? śrī rāma tells lakśmaṇa, when separated from pirātti, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-105 “*padma saugandikavaham śivam śōkavināśanam* l *danyā lakśmaṇa*! *sēvantē pāmpōpavana mārutam* ll” (Oh lakśmaṇa! Only the fortunate people could enjoy the breeze which comes from the gardens located on the banks of river pampā, as it carries the fragrance of lotus and red water lily flowers and which is capable of wiping away my sorrows (by destroying me)). As mentioned in this pāsuram, when he incarnated as śrī rāma, he too had known the power of this breeze. For me, who is desirous of being touched by his divine foot, is it proper to be harassed by this wind?

**ingu** – if he had known that this breeze would come to torture me, should he not have put me under his divine feet? Only if he and I had been together, would this breeze have got destroyed.

**nān ilakkāy** – for this breeze which tortures all the beloveds, I alone became the target for being tortured.

**tenṟalukku ilakkāy** – I, who should have got sustained with the touch of his divine hand, instead got touched by this wind, reminding me of his touch, which I had lost. This thought tortured me. Did he think that even after this, I would be alive? When I am looking up, to his divine hands, is it proper to be pressed upon, by this wind?

**nān ilakkāy iruppēnē** – the opinion here is that he \[emperumān\] could be the target for this breeze, just as we had seen in the śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam ślōkam 1-105 (quoted in the vyākhyānam for the term *ōr tenṟalukku*). The vyākyātā periyavāccān piḷḷai has explained each term of that ślōkam here:

*padmasaugandikavaham* – bringing the fragrance of lotus and red lily flowers. This appears like a garland strung with a medley of flowers.

*śivam* – being auspicious. The opinion here is that it is providing the most eminent help to me. What is the help that it did for me?

*śōkavināśakam* – the implied meaning is – by destroying me, my distress gets destroyed permanently. By destroying me fully, without torturing me, it is rendering me an eminent help.

*danyā*: – those who have wealth will not bother about scarcity. In the same way, those who enjoy this breeze will not fear for the scarcity of enjoyment.

*sēvantē* – they experience. Since the term is in the present tense, the experience is a permanent one. Hence, not only are they not afraid of this experience, they would even seek out secluded places where they could experience this breeze.

*pampōpavana mārutam* – mere breeze singes like fire. The implied meaning is that the breeze coming from the gardens in the river pampā is singeing like the fire coming from *agnikuṇḍam* (the structure where ritualistic fire is nurtured).

**ilakkāy** – as the only target.

**nān** – Does he think that I, like he, am capable of putting up with this torture? Am I not one who trembles at the mere sight of breeze?

**iruppēnē** – this says that I vouldn’t be able to sustain myself. If he expects me to be alive, facing this torture, then that is going to be disproved. The implied meaning is that he is probably thinking that he could sustain himself after I am done with.

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 8.3. Oli vannam

*oḷivaṇṇam vaḷai sindai uṟakkattōdu ivaiyellāmeḷimaiyāl ittennai īdaḻiyappōyinavālkuḷir aruvi vēngadattu en gōvindan guṇam pādiaḷiyatta mēgangāḷ! āvi kāttiruppēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aḷiyatta mēgangāḷ – Oh, clouds who are bound to be merciful!; oḷi – radiance of the physical form; vaṇṇam – its complexion; vaḷai – and the bangles; sindai – as well as the heart; uṟakkattōdu ivai ellām – along with sleep, all these; eḷimaiyāl – due to my miserable state; ennai ittu – disregarding me; īdu aḻiya – such that I become weak; pōyina – have left; Al – alas!; kuḷir aruvi – having cold streams; vēngadattu – one who has mercifully taken residence in tirumalai; en gōvindan – my kaṇṇapirān’s; guṇam – divine, auspicious qualities; pādi – reciting to my heart’s content; āvi – my life; kāttiruppēnē – could I protect myself?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, clouds, who are bound to be merciful! The radiance of my \[physical\] body, its complexion, my bangles, my heart and sleep have all left me, disregarding me, since I am in a miserable state. As a result, I have become weak. Alas! Is it possible for me to protect myself, reciting the divine, auspicious names of kaṇṇapirān, who has taken residence mercifully in tiruvēngadam, which has cold streams?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – She asks “When I have lost everything, is it possible to sustain myself through chanting of his divine names?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**oḷivaṇṇam vaḷai sindai uṟakkattōdu ivai ellām pōyina** – distinguishing features of my physical form such as radiance, complexion etc, which came to me due to uniting with him, along with bangles which never used to slip out, my heart which was always for me and sleep, left me. The outer distinguishing features such as radiance of the body, its complexion and my bangles, as well as the inner distinguishing features such as my heart and sleep went away from me. The ornaments of the body as well as the ornaments of inner soul have left me, she says. ātmā is termed as *mey* (that which exists) and physical form is termed as *poy* (that which is not true), that which is not permanent. The ornaments for *mey* are radiance, complexion and bangles. *oḷi* – radiance, obtained because of uniting with him; *vaṇṇam* – complexion, which is the repository for that radiance; *vaḷai* – this is the ornament which is removed and fitted back while radiance, complexion and heart \[mind and heart are interchangeably used in āḻvārs’ pāsurams\] are ornaments which are never removed. *uṟakkattōdu* – along with sleep. *ivai ellām* – usage of the term *ellām*(all) confirms that there are many other features which I have lost. They are as spread-out as his twin vibhūtis (nitya vibhūti, the spiritualistic realm, and leelā vibhūti, the materialistic realm). Radiance, complexion and bangles really left the physical form (both the terms ‘real’ and ‘physical form’ have the same tamiḻ word, *mey*). Sleep also left, without having any compassion. What is the reason for all these features leaving?

**eḷimaiyāl** – this could be construed as – due to my miserable state or due to the meanness of the features which left me. They left me after seeing my state of sadness; alternatively, they left me because of their quality of meanness, leaving me when I was in a calamitous state.

**ennai ittu** – just as some *acētana* entities (those which do not have ability to think) are thrown away, these features of mine, threw me aside and left me. The inner features of radiance, complexion and bangles left me, without even thinking “We were moving with her as inseparables” while the external feature of sleep, without even thinking “We moved with her closely”, left me. When it went, it did so without any compassion.

**īdaḻiyap pōyina** – they left me, destroying my strength. Before he \[emperumān\] left me, he said that he was going to leave, asking me, with a smile on his face, to sustain myself till he returned, hugging me, making it clear to me that he did not have the mind to leave \[but he had to\]. Unlike him, just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-18-29 “*anurāgēṇa śaitilyamasmāsu vrajitē harau*: l *śaitilyamupayāntyāśu karēśu valayānyapi* ll” (when kaṇṇan goes, separating from us, we become thin, because of our affection towards him; the bangles on our physical form slip out, immediately), they left me, waiting for an opportune moment to separate from me.

**kuḷir aruvi …** – the implied meaning here is – is he thinking that by meditating on his auspicious qualities or reciting his divine names such as *gōvindahn* et al, I would be able to sustain myself?

**kuḷir aruvi vēngadattu** – once I recall the cillness of tiruvēngadam with its cool streams, even this anguish of mine would disappear. īsn’t it a divine abode \[of emperumān’s\] that it will remove all anguish?

**en** – even though he is making me anxious by not coming now, it is not possible to negate the fact that he made himself to be mine when he united with me! Hence, she says that he is her *gōvindan*

**gōvindan** – he manifested himself such that he is simplicity personified to all. This status cannot be forgotten or hidden.

**guṇam pādi āvi kāttiruppēnē** – taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 1-2 says “*sō’śnutē sarvān kāmān sahabrahmaṇā vipaścitā*” (muktātmā \[soul liberated from samsāram, and has reached śrīvaikuṇṭam\], enjoys all the auspicious qualities of the supreme entity, along with the omniscient brahmam \[supreme entity\]). As mentioned in this ślōkam, is he thinking that those who meditate upon him, when they are together with him in their state of meditation, and enjoy his auspicious qualities, will be able to sustain themselves even when they have separated from him, by meditating on his auspicious qualities and reciting his auspicious divine names?

**aḷiyatta mēgangāḷ** – Oh clouds, who shower your mercy! She says that unlike him who had separated from her and unlike her radiance, complexion etc which left her, they are with mercy, due to her fortune. During the time of calamity when he left her, aren’t they great souls that they have come to manifest themselves to her!

**āvi kāttiruppēnē** – will I be able to sustain myself? Am I the lord for my life? īsn’t protecting my life, his responsibility, being my lord? Apart from enjoying him, is it my activity to protect myself? Is it possible for a horse to carry the decorations and binding chains which are meant for an elephant? The opinion is that it is not possible for her to protect her soul, which is the activity of the supreme entity.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.4. Minnagattu

*minnāgattu eḻuginṟa mēgangāḷ! vēngadattuttannāgat tirumangai tangiya sīr mārvarkkuennāgattu iḷam kongai virumbittām nāḷ tōṟumponnāgam pulgudaṟku en purivudaimai seppuminē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āgattu min eḻuginṟa mēgangāḷ – Oh, clouds, which have lightning formed in your form!; en āgattu – present on my chest; iḷam kongai – youthful bosoms; tām virumbi – that emperumān desiring; pon āgam pulgudaṟku – in the matter of embracing with his beautiful chest; nāḷ tōṟum – permanently; en purivudaimai – the desire which I have; vēngadattu – in tirumalai; than āgam – on his divine form; tirumangai tangiya sīr mārvarkku – to the one who has the distinction of having a divine chest on which pirātti has mercifully taken residence; sepumin – tell him

**Перевод:**

**Oh, clouds, which have lightning striking in your form! emperumān is residing in tirumalai, carrying the distinction of having pirātti on his divine form. Tell him my desire that he should embrace my youthful bosoms, which are present on my chest, permanently, with his beautiful chest, desirously.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells the clouds to convey her desire to tiruvēngadattān (the lord of tiruvēngadam), keeping periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) as the puruśakāra bhūtai (recommending entity).*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**minnāgatteḻuginṟa mēgangāḷ** – the meaning hinted by āṇḍāḷ here is: Oh, clouds, you resemble his divine form, which was said as in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*neelatōyada madyastā vidyullēkēva bhāsvarā*” (the divine form of emperumān is radiant like the lightning imbibed by the bluish clouds) and in tiruccanda viruttam 28 “*neelamuṇḍa minnanna mēnip perumān*” (the divine form of emperumān is like a bluish cloud which has swallowed lightning), in both the samskruta vēdam and drāvida vēdam. During the time of separation from him, you manifest your forms, which match his form, to me, reminding me about him. If it is possible, you should make attempts to unite me with him. īsn’t lightning formed when clouds collide with each other? She refers to this through the term *āgatteḻuginṟa*. Alternatively, by splitting the verse as *min nāgattu eḻuginṟa* where the word *nāgam* would refer to sky, we can construe the meaning for this verse to be – Oh clouds which rise up such that lightning appears in the sky. Just as emperumān has periya pirātti, who is like lightning, on his divine chest, these clouds too have lightning formed on their divine forms. Just as periya pirāttiyār, who is like a lamp, shows the divine, dark form of emperumān, these clouds too roam around, manifesting their dark forms with the help of lightning.

**mēgangāḷ** – the opinion is that seeing the clouds for her was similar to *periya udaiyār* (jatāyu, the king of vultures) appearing in the sky during the calamitous time when rāvaṇa was abducting sītāp pirātti.

**vēngadattu** – you do not have to cross the ocean or to annihilate enemies, like hanuman did. I am not sending you to śrīvaikuṇṭam, which was described in mūnṟām tiruvandādi 51 as ”*kalangāp perunagaram*” (the great place which is without any bewilderment), as my messenger. Am I not sending you to tiruvēngadam, which is easily accessible for you!

**tannāgat tirumangai tangiya sīr mārbarkku** – she says that she is sending them as her messenger since his divine form is such that unless embraced by him, she cannot sustain herself. Even as periya pirāttiyār is dwelling on his divine chest, just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” (I cannot leave \[the divine chest\] even for a moment\]), she would get bewildered as to what harm will befall him and hence dwells permanently on his divine chest. One who has such a divine chest. The opinion is that he has the divine chest in which periya pirāttiyār dwells constantly so that she could recommend āṇḍāḷ and other consorts who desire to embrace his divine chest such that they would be able to sustain themselves and such that she too could get her existence (through her role of *puruśakāra bhūtai*). She tells the clouds to inform emperumān “Could I bear it when I, who am new to this experience, am denied the opportunity to embrace the divine chest, if periya pirātti herself cannot sustain without embracing that chest?” When periya pirātti is residing on his divine chest as the *puruśakāra bhūtai*, how could I lose him and not attain him? The opinion is that since periya pirātti is constantly embracing him, residing on his chest, there is no need for anyone to teach him about literature on love.

**ennāgattu iḷam kongai virumbi** – the implied meaning is that she, being a young person, would have much younger bosoms than periya pirāttiyār, who is much older. Could my bosoms wait just like sītāp pirātti did, as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-30 “*śaraistu sankulām krutvā lankām parabalārtdana*: l *mām nayēd yadi kākutstas that tasya sadruśam bhavēt* ll” (If śrī rāmapirān, who annihilates the strength of his enemies, bewilders lankā with his arrows and takes me with him, it will be apt for him)?

**iḷam kongai** – my bosoms are like children who will not listen to their mothers. They will not be patient if I tell them “We could embrace his divine chest, after a while”. Has she herself not mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 5-7 “*en kongai kiḷarndu kumaittuk kudukalittu āviyai āgulam seyyum*” (my bosoms will swell, become joyful, melt and make my life distressed)?

**iḷam kongai virumbitt tām** – just as sītāp pirātti had told hanuman in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 40-3 “*yatā tam puruśavyāgram gātrai*: *śōkābhikarśitai*: l *samsrupśēyam sakāmā’ham tatā kuru dayām mayi* :” (you should mercifully shower your grace on me such that I, who am withering due to my sorrow, would be able to touch desirously śrī rāma, who is like a tiger among men, with my limbs), she tells the clouds “My bosoms are desirous of embracing him, they should be made to embrace him. Just as when one is hungry, another has a feast, if this body were to fall down without embracing him, it should not happen that I have to take another birth to fulfil the desire. Make sure that I am able to embrace him with my bosoms which desire to embrace him”.

**nāḷ tōṟum** – at all times. The desire \[to embrace emperumān\] is such that even as it desires, the enjoyment should be forever. For the verse *iḷam kongai tām virumbi* it could also be construed as – the bosoms which emperumān desired.

**tannāgat tirumangai tangiya sīr marvarkku ennāgatu iḷam kongai tām virumbi nāḷ tōṟum ponnāgam pulgudaṟku en purivudaimai** – Here, the term *ponnāgam* would refer to distinguished chest. Since she has said “Tell him my desire to embrace his distinguished chest”, it is clear that she is not one who could sustain herself by recalling that time when she had united with him.

**en purivudaimai** – when she was united with him, since he was *puruśōttaman* (best among men), when he expressed his desire, she did that, in line with her femineity. As one who was totally dependent on him and as one who was his distinguished consort, she listened to him, who was totally independent. Now, due to the distress of having separated from him, since she has reached the stage where she is indulging in activities such as sending messengers etc, which are inappropriate for her femineity, she is telling the clouds “Convey my desire to him”. It is appropriate for an owner to desire the owned, but not the converse. Now, since the owned has started to desire to attain the owner, she says “The *rakśya-rakśaka bhāvam* (the concept of protected and protector) has got interchanged” and “Both our *svarūpams* (basic nature) have got destroyed”. īsn’t she thinking “It is not appropriate for my *svarūpam* to indulge in activities such as send+-ing messenger to him and attempting to attain him”?

**seppuminē** – the opinion is that she wants him to hear from them \[clouds\] that she has the deep affection towards him which he does not have. tātparyaratnāvaḷi 3 says “*dēśikāstatra dūtā*:” (in *svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning), clouds etc which are sent as messengers refer to ācāryas (preceptors)). As mentioned in this, she is telling *puruśakāra bhūtars* (those who recommend to emperumān regarding cētanas) “In order to remove the deficiency of our not uniting with him, convey to him our present status”

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.5. Van kondu

*vān koṇḍu kiḷarndu eḻunda māmugilgāḷ! vēngadattuttēnkoṇḍa malar sidaṟat tiraṇḍēṟip poḻivīrgāḷ!ūnkoṇḍa vaḷḷugirāl iraṇiyanai udal idandāntānkoṇḍa sarivaḷaigaḷ tarumāgil sāṟṟuminē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēngadattu – in tirumalai; tēn koṇḍa malar sidaṟa – flowers which are full of honey, to be scattered; tiraṇḍu ēṟi poḻivīrgāḷ – coming in huge gathering, and showering rains; vān koṇḍu kiḷarndu eḻunda – rising uproariously, swallowing the sky; māmugilgāḷ – Oh, monsoon clouds!; Un koṇḍa vaḷḷugirāl – with nails which are sharp and attached to flesh; iraṇiyanai udal idandān tān – emperumān who tore the body of monster hiraṇyan; koṇḍa – stolen from me; sarivaḷaigaḷ – bangles; tarum āgil – if he were to return; sāṟṟumin – inform him (my state of agony)

**Перевод:**

**Oh, monsoon clouds, who scatter flowers which are full of honey in tirumalai, by showering rains in huge assemblies and who rise uproariously by swallowing the sky! emperumān had torn the body of the monster hiraṇyan with sharp nails which are attached to flesh. If that emperumān were to return the bangles which he had stolen from me, tell him my present state of agony.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells the clouds to inform her state to emperumān who removes the enemies of his followers.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vānkoṇḍu kiḷarndu eḻunda māmugilgāḷ** – Oh clouds, which rose uproariously, swallowing the sky, which is your place of dwelling! Without considering that it is your place of dwelling, you had occupied the entire sky. Is it not famously known that sky is without any boundary? At the moment when you appeared, you had swallowed that sky, and risen uproariously. When carrying out this task, the clouds worked unitedly.

**vēngadattut tēn koṇḍa malar sidaṟat tiraṇḍēṟip poḻivīrgāḷ** – what is the purpose in scattering good entities such as flowers, which are full of honey! If you could convey message such that he and I unite, there will be benefit. If we look at the activity that you undertake, after all of you (clouds) join together in huge assemblies, it is only to destroy the flowers. What is the benefit for me, due to this? Instead of destroying flowers like this, see if you can find a way for uniting me, called as *kōdai* (garland) and who has the appearance of a garland, with him. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-25 “*naiśā paśyati rākśasya*: *nēmān puśpa paladrumān* l  *ēkasta hrudayā nūnam rāmamēvānupaśyati* ll” (this pirātti did not see the rākśasis (female demons); she did not see these fruits and trees. It appears that with a single focus, she is seeing only śrī rāma). As mentioned in this, did you \[addressing the clouds\] destroy the flowers, thinking that they were a hurdle to my uniting with him? The clouds appeared to ask her “What do you want us to do?”

**Un koṇḍa vaḷḷugirāl** – with nails which are sharp and which are attached to flesh

**iraṇiyan udal idandān** – When the father who sired him, turned out to be his foe, prahlādan had taken a solemn vow as in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/06/thiruvaimozhi-2-8-9-engumulan-kannan/) “*engumuḷan kaṇṇan*” (emperumān has pervaded everywhere); appearing from a pillar, emperumān had torn the body of hiraṇyan the enemy of prahlādan. For a son, there is none who is closer than a father. When his father himself turned out as a foe, emperumān became closer than hiraṇyan, to prahlādan. The reason for saying this here is – she is telling the clouds to inform emperumān that she is asking “when you help when others turn out as foes, could you not help when you become the foe?” She is asking the clouds to tell him “Being the head of all the celestial entities, you had transformed into a human body with the head of a lion, which was not appropriate to your eminence. There is no need for transforming yourself like that; if you come as you are, I would be able to sustain myself. You had helped prahlādan, a male, who was of my age. Could you not come to help me, a female?”

**ūnkoṇḍa vaḷḷugirāl** – he is one who will annihilate his follower’s enemy, with his own hands.

**iraṇiyanai udal idandān tān koṇḍa sarivaḷaigaḷ tarumāgil sāṟṟuminē** – the term *sarivaḷaigaḷ* would mean bangles which slide, viz those which come out of the hand. Alternatively, the term *sari* would refer to bracelet, by itself. Thus, this could be taken as referring to the ornaments, bracelet and bangles. Since it is called as *sarivaḷai*, it appears that it has the nature of sliding down the hand. Even when he comes, they would slide down.

**tarumāgil** – since the term *āgil* \[if\] denotes a doubt, it is clear that she thinks that his nature is not to give. The reason for her thinking that he would not give is – since he is sarvēśvaran (lord of all), who is totally independent, he may decide not to give. If that were the case, why send a messenger to him? Whether he gives or denies, her desire will not let her to remain quiet.

**sāṟṟuminē** – whether he gives the sliding bangles or not, you \[addressing the clouds\] convey my message to give the sliding bangles. Giving the bangles refers to emperumān coming and uniting with her.

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.6. Salam kondu

*salangoṇḍu kiḷarndu eḻunda taṇmugilgāḷ! māvaliyainilangoṇḍān vēngadattē nirandēṟip poḻivīrgāḷ!ulanguṇḍa viḷanganipōl uḷmeliyap pugundu ennainalangoṇḍa nāraṇaṟku en nadalai nōy seppuminē*

**Пословный перевод:**

salam koṇḍu – carrying cold water; kiḷarndu eḻunda – rising up and shining; taṇ mugilgāḷ! – Oh, cool clouds!; māvaliyai – from mahābali; nilam koṇḍān vēngadattu – in tirumalai, where emperumān, who had received earth through alms, is mercifully residing; nirandu ēri poḻivīrgāḷ! – oh clouds, who are spreading out fully, and showering rains!; ulangu uṇḍa – eaten by huge mosquitoes; viḷangani pōl – like a wood apple; uḷ meliya – to become wizened, inside; pugundu – entering inside me; ennai – my; nalangoṇḍa – one who usurped my glories; nāraṇaṟku – to nārāyaṇan; en nadalai nōy – my disease of wretchedness; seppumin – tell him

**Перевод:**

**Oh, cool clouds who have risen up and are shining, carrying cool water! Oh, clouds who have spread out and are showering rains on tirumalai where emperumān, who had received earth from mahābali through alms, is mercifully residing! nārāyaṇan had usurped my glories by entering inside me and making me withered, just like huge mosquitoes eat wood apple. Tell him about my disease of wretchedness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(īntroeduction) – She tells the clouds to inform nārāyaṇan, who had usurped her glories, about her suffering.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**salam koṇḍu kiḷarndu eḻunda** – the clouds have filled up water and are roaming at higher levels just like people roam around selling cardamom liquid, saying “*ēlakkuḻambu, ēlakkuḻambu*”. The implied meaning is that just as you carry water, which is the sustaining material for people, within your form without expecting anything in return, you should carry out my task too.

**taṇmugilgāḷ** – is there any need for you to shower the water \[as rains\]? īsn’t your form itself sufficient to invigorate people? Here again, the underlying opinion is that you, who remove the ennui of others, should remove my suffering too.

**māvaliyai nilam koṇḍān vēngadattē** – you are living in the place where the one who had obtained land as alms for celestial entities from māvali, by transforming himself as a mendicant, is dwelling. Should you not carry out a task for me when I am seeking from you? I am not sending you to a monster, just as the celestial entities did. Am I not sending you as messenger to nārāyaṇan, who owns us all!

**nirandu** – pervading everywhere

**ēṟip poḻivīrgāḷ** – sprouted vessels, carrying water, \[called in tamiḻ as *sāykaram*, are kept at a height in sheds for quenching thirst of travellers so that they could drink water comfortably. By looking at these vessels from a distance, people will know about it. Similar to that, you are also removing the thirst of people when they look at you, by climbing to a great height and showering rain. The meaning hinted is that since you are living in the same place where emperumān is residing, should you also not have mercy without any cause for showering, like him?

Assuming that they are asking her as to what they should do, she replies through the latter half of the pāsuram…

**ulanguṇḍa viḷanganipōl uḷmeliyap pugundu** – the term *ulangu* refers to a type of mosquito which is large in size. Once mosquitoes swarm a wood apple, the fruit will lose its juice fully and become dried up. In the same way, emperumān entered me and usurped my vellness, my fullness, my heart and my bangles, by embracing me.

**ennai nalam koṇḍa** – one who usurped everything from me

**nāraṇarkku** – he is the *ātmā* (indvelling soul) for all entities and all entities form a part of his divine form. Thus, one who considers leelā vibhūti, which is full of dirt, as an enjoyable material…

**en nadalai nōy seppuminē** – convey to him my disease of wretchedness brought about by separation from him. Ask him “Where is harmony between your \[emperumān’s\] quality of *vātsalyam* (motherly forbearance) and my suffering?”

**nadalai nōy** – the suffering in separation, leading to standing, sitting, falling down, getting up etc

**en nadalai nōy** – if he too had been suffering from this disease of separation, he would not have remained aloof, for me to send a messenger. Thus, the meaning hinted is that this wretchedness is only for me.

**seppuminē** – all that you have to do is to announce this disease to him.

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
