## 8.7. Sanga ma kadal

*sangamā kadal kadaindān taṇ mugilgāḷ! vēngadattuccengaṇmāl sēvadikkīḻ adivīḻcci viṇṇappamkongai mēl kungumattin kuḻambaḻiyap pugundu oru nāḷtangumēḷ ennāvi tangum enṟu uriyīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sangam – having conces; mā – having eminence; kadal – ocean; kadaindān – emperumān who had churned that ocean, the place where he has mercifully taken residence; vēngadattu – roaming in tirumalai; taṇ mugilgāḷ – Oh, cool clouds!; sem kaṇ māl – having reddish eyes, that emperumān’s; sē adi kīḻ – under his perfect, divine feet; adivīḻcci – this servitor’s; viṇṇappam – appeal; kongai mēl – (applied) on my bosoms; kungumattin kuḻambu – paste of vermillion; aḻiya – to be destroyed fully; oru nāḷ – for one day, at the least; pungundu tangum ēl – if he could come and unite; en āvi tangum enṟu – that my life will be sustained; uraiyīr – tell him (this appeal of mine)

**Перевод:**

**emperumān had churned ocean which has many conces and which is eminent. Oh, cool clouds who roam around tirumalai where that emperumān has mercifully taken residence! I, servitor to that emperumān, am appealing to his perfect, divine feet. Tell him that if he would come for, at the least, one day and unite with me such that the vermillion paste applied on my bosoms gets destroyed, my life will be sustained.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them to inform emperumān that she cannot sustain herself unless he embraces her.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sangamākadal kadaindān** – he churned the ocean, whose depth cannot be determined, such that conces, which were at the ocean bed, came to the top.

**kadaindān taṇ mugilgāḷ** – aren’t you residing in the same place where emperumān, who churned the ocean, putting his divine form to difficulties in order to give those who prayed to him what they desired and who sought other benefits from him \[instead of himself\], resides?

**kadaindān taṇ mugilgāḷ** – these clouds, by manifesting their cool forms, removed his ennui which resulted from churning the ocean.

**vēngadattuc cengaṇmāl** – tiruvēngadam is the place where he mercifully came to reside in order to shower his mercy on those who sought him, so that he could overcome the shortcoming which he experienced when he helped the celestial entities who sought other benefits from him. He would show his *vātsalyam* (motherly forbearance) through the glance of his eyes on those who came there to seek himself. When he is not coming to her to shower his mercy, why is she striving like this? This will be clear only when one goes to tiruvēngadam and sees emperumān, when one would realise the affection that he has for her and how he is not even sleeping, thinking of her. Did he not suffer like this, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 36-44 “*anidras satatam rāma*: *suptō’pi ca narōttama*: l *sītēti madurām vāṇīm vyāharan pratibhudyatē* ll” (śrī rāma (who has got separated from sītāp pirātti) is always without sleep. Even if he, who is the best among men, sleeps for a moment, he wakes up, always reciting the beautiful word *‘sītē’*)?

**sēvadikkīḻ adivīḻcci viṇṇappam** – fall at his divine feet and make an appeal. What is the appeal?

**kongai mēl kungumattin kuḻambu aḻiyap pugundu orunāḷ tangumēl ennāvi tangum** – tell him “Believing that you would come, in order to embrace you, I had applied vermillion paste on my bosoms. If you make it to provide benefit \[by embracing me\], I would be able to sustain myself”. Is he not omniscient? Is there a need to tell him? Since men, who have beards on their chins, do not understand the agonies experienced by women, who have bosoms, it has to be explicitly told. Instead of that, he might have thought “Even if I go, she will not be able to sustain herself”. In order to remove that doubt, tell him that there is a way by which I could sustain myself.

**kongaimēl kungamttin kuḻambu aḻiyap pugundu** – bosoms will not sustain themselves, when separated from him, even when they are not decorated. Decorating them with vermillion paste became the reason for hurting them further. *kuḻambu* – made into a paste such that it will never dry up. Even after these clouds convey the message to him, if he does not come, the vermillion paste, expecting that he would come, may dry up. Since he is eminent and totally independent, it cannot be said emphatically that “He will come by this time”. Since she is dependent on him fully, hoping that “He will come now” she may keep decorating her bosoms, keeping the paste wet.

**kuḻambu aḻiyap pugundu** – isn’t kaṇṇan of such nature that he will enter when no one would know that he is coming!

**oru nāḷ tangumēl** – if he could stay for one day. For decorating with vermillion paste continuously, would staying for one day be enough?

**en āvi tangum** – it may not be enough for enjoyment. But it is enough for me to sustain my life.

**oru nāḷ tangumēl en āvi tangum enṟu uraiyīrē** – The status mentioned in an earlier pāsuram (3<sup>rd</sup>) “*gōvindan guṇam pādi ……āvi kāttiruppēnē*” has been crossed. Tell him that only if he embraces me for one day, would my life be sustained.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.8. Kar kalattu

*kār kālattu eḻuginṟa kārmugilgāḷ! vēngadattuppōr kālattu eḻundaruḷip porudavanār pēr sollinīr kālattu erukkil am paḻa ilai pōl vīḻvēnaivār kālattu orunāḷ tam vāsagam tandu aruḷārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kar kālattu – during rainy season; vēngadattu eḻuginṟa – appearing on tirumalai; kār mugilgāḷ – Oh, monsoon clouds!; pōr kālattu – during the time of war; eḻundaruḷi – mercifully entering (the battle arena); porudavanār – emperumān, who fought victoriously, his; pēr – divine names; solli – reciting, with musical notes; nīr kālattu erukkil am paḻa ilai pōl vīḻvēnai – for me who is falling off like the yellow-coloured leaves of erukkam plant (a type of wild plant) during rainy period; vār kālattu – over a long period of time; oru nāḷ – for, at the least, one day; tam vāsagam tandu aruḷārē – will he not mercifully speak a word?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, monsoon clouds, which appear on tirumalai during the rainy season! I keep reciting, with musical notes, the divine names of emperumān, who fights victoriously in battlefields, during times of battle. I keep falling off like the yellow-coloured leaves of erukkam plant, during rainy reason. Will he not mercifully speak a word to me, for at least one day?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells the clouds to inform emperumān that not speaking a word, even for one day, is not proper on his part.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kār kālattu eḻuginṟa** – Weren’t you the ones who appeared during the monsoon period, which he \[emperumān\] had told me that he would appear? Though he did not come, since you, who have his complexion, have come, it is helpful.

**kār mugilgāḷ ….. porudavanār** – you have appeared at the time which was intended for you, with dark colour, in order to help the world \[to get water\]. emperumān too, in earlier times, would not appear until his followers faced any difficulty. Then, he too would appear, remove the enemies of his followers and get that dark complexion.

**pēr solli …… vīḻvēnai** – we had thought that during this bad phase, we could sustain ourselves by reciting his divine names. She says that that itself became the reason for her affliction.

**nīr kālattu erukkil am paḻa ilai pōl vīḻvēnai** – during the summer season, the leaves of *erukkam* plant would start drying up, drained of their milk. Once water droplets, coming from rains, fall on them, they would wither off. Similar to that, as soon as I recite his divine name, I too stagger and fall down. The meaning hinted is that when she was already anguished due to separation from emperumān, reciting his divine name weakened her further, making her to stagger and fall down.

**vār kālattu oru nāḷ** – one day, during the long period of time which is extending.

**oru nāḷ tam vāsagam tandu aruḷārē** – just as he is focussed on not showing his face forever, is he focussed on not saying a word too? The meaning hinted is that even though he is not appearing in person, could he not say a word? sītāp pirātti says in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-10 “*jīvantīm mām yatā rāmas sambhāvayati kīrtimān* l *tat tatā hanuman vācyō vācā darmamavāpnuhi* ll´ (You should tell a word to the eminent śrī rāmapirān to honour me who is alive). Just as it has been said here, he could have, at the least, said a word. Similar to sītāp pirātti telling a word to hanuman, here āṇḍāḷ says a word to the clouds, who are going as messengers.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.9. Mada yanai pol

*mada yānai pōl eḻunda māmugilgāḷ! vēngadattuppadiyāga vāḻvīrgāḷ! pāmbaṇaiyān vārttai ennē!gadiyenṟum tān āvān karudādu ōr peṇ kodiyaivadai ennum sol vaiyagattār madiyārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēngadattai – tirumalai; padi āga – as your dwelling place; vāḻvīrgāḷ – those who are living; madam yānai mēl eḻunda – rising with arrogance, like a rutting elephant; māmugilgāḷ – Oh, monsoon clouds!; pāmbu aṇaiyān – emperumān, who is lying on ādiśēśan, his; vārttai ennē – what is his word?; “tān – that emperumān; enṟum – all the time; gadi āvān – remaining as the protector; karudādu – without thinking (of damage to his basic nature); ōr peṇ kodiyai – a girl; vadai seydān – destroyed her”; ennum sol – a word like that; vaiyagattār – those who are on this earth; madiyārē – will they not consider?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, monsoon clouds, who have tiruvēngadam as your habitat and who rise uproariously like an elephant in rut! What is the word from emperumān who is reclining on ādiśēśan? If that emperumān, who is always the protector, does not think about the damage that will result to his basic nature, will the people of the world not consider him as having destroyed a girl?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells the clouds to inform emperumān that torturing her would lead to reproach from the world.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mada yānai pōl eḻunda māmugilgāḷ** – emperumān makes the clouds happy by manifesting his divine form etc to them, just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 7 “*ēśa* *hyēvānandayāti*”(isn’t this supreme entity making the jīvātmās (entities with knowledge) happy!). The opinion is that they are roaming around like elephants in rut because of that happiness.

**māmugilgāḷ** – taittirīya upaniśat bhruguvalli 10-4 says “*aham annam aham annam aham annam* l *aham annādō’ham annādō’ham annāda*: ll” (\[the soul, when it reaches parama padam, says\] I am enjoyable, like food \[for emperumān\]. I eat the food \[which is the happiness of emperumān (when he eats me)\]. Just as it has been mentioned in this, you are enjoying emperumān in groups of assemblies, similar to nityātmās (permanent residents of śrī vaikuṇṭam) enjoying emperumān.

**vēngadattaip padiyāga vāḻvīrgāḷ** – unlike those who come for a festival in the temple for a few days and return, aren’t you fortunate to reside permanently in tiruvēngadam? Apart from being more distinguished than *mumukśus* \[those who are desirous of mōkśam\], aren’t you more distinguished than muktātmās \[those who have got liberated from samsāram and attained śrīvaikuṇṭam\] too? The meaning hinted is that unlike muktātmās who had, for some time, suffered in samsāram and then got liberated from it, the clouds are always enjoying emperumān, just like nityasūris. Just like living in paramapadam, isn’t āṇḍāḷ glorifying life in tiruvēngadam itself! This is similar to toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār saying that living between the two rivers (koḷḷidam and kāviri) alone is worth living. Hasn’t āṇḍāḷ too said in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-5 ”*nallārgaḷ vāḻum naḷirarangam*” (eminent people who live in comfortable tiruvarangam)! Alternatively, this could be construed as those who live in tiruvēngadam, having the eminence of affection towards emperumān. Involvement with worldly matters would be a cause for destruction of one’s *svarūpam* (basic nature), just as it has been emphasised in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-9-9-avi-thigaikka/) “*pala nī kāttip paduppāyō*” (will you reveal all the worldly pursuits and destroy me?) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-9-8-arivilenukku-arulay/) “*puṟattittinnam keduppāyō*” (even now, when I have developed a desire for you, will you make me engage with worldly pursuits and torment me?). Involvement with emperumān will be the cause for celebration.

**pāmbaṇaiyān vārttai ennē** – what is the word mentioned by him, lying on the mattress of ādiśēśan? Since you \[clouds\] are living in tiruvēngadam, as his confidantes, just as ādiśēśan is privy to his words, so would you be. Tell me what that word is.

**gadi enṟum tānāvān** – isn’t he the refuge for everyone forever, just as it has been said in jitantē ślōkam 2? “*dēvānām dānavānām ca sāmānyam atidaivatam* l  *sarvadā caraṇa dvantam vrajāmi śaraṇam tava* ll” (You are the deity, a common refuge for *dēvas* \[celestial entities\] who have devotion \[towards you\] and *asuras* \[monsters\] who do not have that devotion; I emphatically consider your divine feet as the means \[to attain you\])

**enṟum** – unlike jīvātmās (entities with knowledge) who would say on occasions “I will be your servitor” and who would say on other occasions “I will not be your servitor”, he \[emperumān\] is always one who protects. īsn’t sarvēśvaran \[lord of all\] one who is looking for a cētana (entity with knowledge), just as it has been mentioned in [nānmugan tiruvandādi 60](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/08/nanmugan-thiruvandhadhi-60/) “*āḷ pārttu uḻi taruvāy*” (one who goes searching “Will I get anyone who will be under my control?”)

**karudādu** – without considering the damage which will come to his auspicious qualities, if he does not protect me

**ōr peṇ kodiyai vadai seydān ennum sol** – letting a creeper, which needs a supporting pole to grow, to spread on the ground, is equivalent to destroying it. In the same way, not coming to take me, who is like a creeper waiting to embrace him, is equivalent to torturing me. Did not *iḷayaperumāḷ* (lakśmaṇa) too say firmly that separating from emperumān is equivalent to death, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 53-31? “*na ca sītā tvayā hīnā na cāhamapi rāghava* l *muhūrtamapi jīvāvō jalānmatsyāvivōdrutau* \|\|” (Oh rāghava! sitā would not be considered as existing if separated from you; neither would I be; (Even if we exist) it would be for a few minutes only, just like a fish which has been separated from water)

**vaiyagattār madiyārē** – \[she tells the clouds to\] tell him “After destroying me, people of the world will not consider you as a protector”. Everyone accepts that when separated and suffering due to that, a person would be considered to be existing only because her beloved exists. If I am destroyed, this \[opinion\] also will become destroyed. The damage which this will cause to his eminence is intolerable for her and is torturing her. If a person is destroyed once, should she get destroyed again? For her, who is getting destroyed, how does it matter about loss of his eminence? Since she is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, even if she were to get destroyed, she thinks that emperumān should not suffer any loss of eminence.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 8.10. Nagattin anaiyanai

*nāgattin aṇaiyānai nannudalāḷ nayandurai seymēgattai vēngadakkōn vidu tūdil viṇṇappambōgattil vaḻuvāda puduvaiyarkōn kōdai tamiḻāgattu vaittu uraippār avar adiyār āguvarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nal nudalāḷ – having a distinguished face; bōgattil vaḻuvāda puduvaiyar kōn kōdai – āṇḍāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār who has no deficiency in his joyful engagements with emperumān; nāgattin aṇaiyān vēngadakkōn – tiruvēngadamudaiyān, who has tiruvanantāḻvān \[ādiśēśan\] as his mattress; nayandu – desiring him; urai sey – mercifully composed; mēgattai vidu tūdil viṇṇappam – appeal which came about as message sent through clouds; tamiḻ – these tamiḻ pāsurams \[hymns\]; āgattu vaittu – keeping in their hearts; uraippār avar – those who could recite; adiyār āguvar – they will get to carry out servitude eternally, to emperumān

**Перевод:**

**These tamiḻ pāsurams have been mercifully composed by āṇḍāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār who did not have any deficiency in his engagements with emperumān. Those who keep these pāsurams, which was an appeal made by āṇḍāḷ, who has a distinguished face and which came about as a message sent through clouds, in their hearts and recite them, will get to carry out servitude permanently to emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she brings the decad to an end by mentioning the benefits that those who recite this decad, will get.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāgattin aṇaiyānai** – all that is required is for you clouds to go and reveal my message. He is reclining on his bed, expecting my arrival.

**nannudalāḷ** – one who has the greatness of splendour on her divine face such that she could trample on that mattress and climb on it.

**nayandu urai sai viṇṇappam** – appeal, made with desire towards emperumān

**mēgattai vēngadakkōn vidu tūdil viṇṇappam** – in śrī rāmāvatāram, sītāp pirātti sent hanuman, who is an expert in *aindra vyākaraṇam* (grammar, treatise for which was composed by indra), as her messenger. Here, āṇḍāḷ is sending as her messenger, clouds which are in tirumalai and which are *acētana* (entities without knowledge). This is the difference between the two messengers. The commonness between them is that both had come from their beloveds.

**vēṇgadakkōn** – to ensure that those who came after that incarnation do not miss out on the essence of message, emperumān came to tirumalai expecting “Is there anyone who is sending a messenger to us, in tirumalai?”

**bhōgattil vaḻuvāda puduvaiyar kōn kōdai tamiḻ** – the basis for her desire towards emperumān was that she was the divine daughter of periyāḻvār who had no deficiency in his enjoyable experiences in matters relating to emperumān. Starting from the time when kaṇṇapirān was born, through the decad “*vaṇṇa mādangaḷ sūḻ*” he had experienced every stage of development in the nurturing of kaṇṇa pirān. He started his enjoyment with the first pāsuram 1-1-1 “*kaṇṇan kēsavan nambi piṟandinil*”. What is the qualification for learning this decad?

**āgattu vaittu uraippār avar** – those who are capable of reciting this decad, with the thought “How much is āṇḍāḷ striving, after desiring matters related to emperumān!”

**adiyār āguvarē** – without the need to send clouds as messengers, they will get to enjoy *bhagavat kainkaryam* (service to emperumān) which she had desired and will uplift themselves.

**adiyār āguvarē** – manu smriti 4-6 says “*sēvā śvavruttir vyākyātā tasmāt tām parivarjayēt*” (carrying out servitude to others is the work of a dog. Hence, one should shun that totally). manu smruti 4-160 says “*sarvam paravaśam du:kkam sarvamātmavaśam sukham*” (all that wherein one is a servitor to others is sorrowful; all that wherein one is a servitor to oneself is happiness). Looking at these *pramāṇams* (authentic proofs), one would think that carrying out servitude to emperumān is also to be avoided. āḻvārs incarnated only to sever such thoughts and to affirm that *bhagavat śēśatvam* (being a servitor to emperumān) is *parama puruśārtam* (supreme benefit). We see people carrying out servitude to their spouses \[in samsāram\] and considering that as enjoyment. Is it required to state that carrying out servitude to emperumān is the sweetest servitude!

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the ninth decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9.1. Sindurac cembodi

*sindurac cembodippōḷ tirumālirum sōlai engumindirakōbangaḷē eḻundum parandittanavālmandaram nātti anṟu madurak koḻum sāṟu koṇḍasundarat tōḷudaiyān suḻalaiyil ninṟu uydungolō?*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumālirum sōlai engum – in all the places seen in tirumālirum sōlai; indirakōbangaḷ – cocineal insects; sem – reddish; sindurap podi pōl – like vermillion powder; eḻundu – rising up; parandittana – have spread out; Al – alas!; anṟu – during that time (when celestial entities had taken refuge, praying for nectar to be given to them, after churning the ocean); mandara malai – the mountain mantara; nātti – anchoring it (as churning staff, in milky ocean); koḻu maduram – sweetest; sāṟu – pirātti, who is like the essence of nectar; koṇḍa – one who accepted; sundarat tōḷudaiyān – lord who has the most beautiful shoulders; suḻalaiyil ninṟu – from his web of intricate net; uydum kol – will we escape?

**Перевод:**

**Reddish cocineal insects, like vermillion powder, have risen up and spread out, covering all the places, which one could see on the hills at tirumālirum sōlai. Alas! During the time when celestial entities took refuge under emperumān, praying for nectar to be given to them after churning the ocean, emperumān had anchored the mantara mountain in the middle of milky ocean, as the churning staff, and churned the ocean. He accepted pirātti, who came out as essence of the sweetest nectar. Will we escape from the web of intricate net cast by the lord who has the most beautiful shoulders?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – cocineal insects \[red coloured insects, which inhabit tropical regions, known as pattuppūcci in tamiḻ\] have covered tirumālirunjōlai malai such that the hill could not be seen. She is asking whether she would escape from the net cast by aḻagar \[divine name of the presiding deity at tirumālirunjōlai\].*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sindurac cembodi ……. parandittanavāl** – āndāḷ says “When we have separated from him, we thought that we could sustain ourselves by seeing his divine abode of tirumālirum sōlai. However, even that did not fructify since the cocineal insects have covered the entire hilly region of tirumālirum sōlai. How unfortunate are we!” In tamiḻ literature, poets have sung “*avan nāttil kunṟum kodiyavō onṟum tōnṟā*”. The meaning for this is: even if one had not been able to see the beloved, one could sustain oneself by looking at mountains in his abode. Should even mountains be not visible here? Similar to that, āṇḍāḷ says ”We thought that we could see the divine hill which reminds of his divine form. Even that has become a rarity”.

**sindurac cembōdippōl** – the divine hill appears like an elephant in rut. The appearance of cocineal insects is like applying vermillion paste on its forehead. It is customary to apply vermillion paste on the forehead of elephants which are in tut. This is termed as *tuvarūttudal*. Now, that has been done to the mountain too, which looks like an elephant. The term *sinduram* refers to the reddish colour of that powder and the word *sem* refers to its beauty. Did not periyāḻvār too say in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-4-6 “*sinduramilangat than tiruneṟṟimēl*” (with vermillion shining on his divine forehead)!

**tirumālirum sōlai engum parandittanavāl** – this could be construed as āṇḍāḷ ruing that the cocineal insects, which have spread throughout the divine hill and which remind her of the reddish colour of emperumān’s divine lips, are tormenting her. During monsoon time, when these insects spread throughout the world, is it appropriate to say that the insects have spread throughout tirumālirunjōlai? Since it is only the divine hill which is visible to her eyes, it is acceptable to say that they have spread throughout the hill. Here, her saying “throughout tirumālirunjōlai” is equivalent to saying “throughout the world”.

**indirakōbangaḷē** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 41-99 “*dadruśū rākśasā dīnā*: *prākāram vānarīkrutam* l *hāhākāram prakurvanti rākśasā bhayamōhitā*: ll” (the demons who felt saddened, saw monkeys all over the fortified walls. The demons who became bewildered due to fear, said “hā hā”), similar to demons seeing monkeys who are devoted followers of emperumān everywhere, the cocineal insects, which torment us \[āṇḍāḷ\] are spreading everywhere.

**eḻundum parandittanavāl** – śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 26-20 and 26-22 say “*yē tu viśtabhya gātrāṇi kśvēḷayanti nadanti ca* l *uttāya ca vijrumbhantē krōdē na haripungavā*: ll *ya ēnamanugaccanti vīrā*: *candana vāsina*: l *ēśaivāśamsatē lankām svēnānīkēna marditum* ll” (These excellent monkeys, residing in sandalwood groves, yelling with a lion’s roar and having stiffened their limbs, are knitting their eyebrows in anger; these valorous monkeys, who are terrific and fierce, having a ferocious zeal, are following naḷa, their leader. naḷa himself, with his aforesaid army, aspires to smash lankā, forthwith). Just as the army of monkeys rose uproariously in lankā and spread out, the cocineal insects too, appear to be on the earth sometimes, appear to be rising up the sky at other times while some appear to rise up and settle above. Just as periyāḻvār had mercifully said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-2-9 “*indirakōbangaḷ emperumān kanivāyoppān sindum puṟavil*”, these cocineal insects remind me of emperumān’s divine fruit-like mouth and torment me.

**mandaram nātti anṟu madurak koḻum sāṟu koṇḍa** – anchoring the *mantara* mountain in the central part of milky ocean, churning the ocean such that the ocean brought out everything which it had. The meaning hinted is that he \[emperumān\] is capable of churning even great entities such as milky ocean which cannot be churned by anyone and accepting everything which it offers. While accepting, he will take the essence for himself, and give virtually nothing to them \[celestial entities\]. What is the reason for mentioning this here? āṇḍāḷ is hinting that he is destroying her femineity when she was thinking “Could he aggressively seize me and destroy me?”, taking away her bangles and everything else.

**madarak koḻum sāṟu koṇḍa** – churning the ocean like this, he obtained the nectar for celestial entities, which was nothing but salty essence, and periya pirātti, who was full of sweetness and beauty and who was the essence of sweetest nectar and beauty, for himself. When compared to the benefit which emperumān obtained, the nectar which the celestial entities got was nothing but the brackish water in the ocean. tirumangai āḻvār too, very clearly and mercifully mentioned this in his periya tirumoḻi 6-1-2 “*viṇṇavar amuduṇa amudil varum peṇṇamuduṇḍa emperumānē*” (Oh, emperumān, who mercifully accepted periya pirātti who appeared in the ocean when the celestial entities wanted nectar from that ocean!)

**sundarat tōḷudaiyān** – even if he torments, the beauty of his divine shoulders is such that he could not be given up

**suḻalaiyil ninṟu uydungolō** – in the net of this *mēghasruśti* (appearance of the cocineal insects on the mountains, appearing as if they had been created by the clouds) from which we could not escape. Just as it has been said in the *pramāṇam* (authentic proof) “*mēghōdayas sāgarasannivruttir vidyud vibhanga*: *spuritāni vāyō*: l  *indōr vibhāgō gatamuṣṇaraśmēr viṣṇōr vicitrā*: *prabhavanti māyā*: ll” (all the activities such as appearance of cloud, waves from ocean coming ashore and returning, appearance of lightning, wafting of breeze, the divisions of moon’s rays as they grow and disappear, journey of sun etc are all strange and amazing illusions of emperumān viṣṇu), the appearance of clouds is due to emperumān’s *sankalpam* (oath or divine thought). Is it possible for us to sustain ourselves in the net cast by one with the most beautiful shoulders? īsn’t our destruction a certainty?

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.2. Porkkaliru porum

*pōrkkaḷiṟu porum mālirunjōlai am pūmpuṟaviltārkkodi mullaigaḷum tavaḷa nagai kāttuginṟakārkkoḷ padākkaḷ ninṟū kaḻaṟīc cirikkat tariyēnārkkidugō? tōḻi! avan tār seyda pūsalaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pōr kaḷiṟu – elephants, for whom fighting in battles is regular work; porum – arena where they fight with each other; mālirunjōlai – tirumālirum sōlai’s; am pū puṟavil – in the most beautiful slopes; tār kodi mullaigaḷum – creepers with budding jasmine flowers; tavaḷam nagai – the whitish smile (of āḻagar, divine name of the presiding utsavar idol); kāttuginṟa – remind me, of; kārkkoḷ – pollinated; padākkaḷ – creeper, by the name of padā; ninṟu – blossoming; kaḻaṟic cirikka – flowering, as if they are smiling and saying “You cannot escape from me”; tariyēn – (seeing that) I am unable to sustain myself; tōḻī – Oh, friend!; avan tār – garland from his divine shoulder (which we desired); seyda – what it did; pūsalai – the despise; ārkku idugō – to whom shall we complain?

**Перевод:**

**Jasmine flowers, which appear to be smiling and are blossoming on the slopes of tirumālirunjōlai, remind me of the whitish smile of āḻagar; in tirumālirunjōlai, elephants which have fighting in battles as full-time occupation, are fighting with each other playfully. Further, the creepers of the plant padā, which have got pollinated, are flowering and smiling, as if saying “You cannot escape from us”. Oh, dear friend! To whom could we complain about the contempt which the garland on the divine shoulders of āḻagar, which we desired, showed us?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says “After desiring the garland on his shoulder, to whom could I share the travails that I am undergoing?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pōrkkaḷiṟu porum mālirunjōlai** – tirumālirunjōlai, the place where elephants, which have fighting in battles as full-time work, keep fighting with each other playfully. Male elephants were roaming joyfully, like emperumān, while female elephants were in distress, like me, being in separation.

**pōrkkaḷiṟu porum** – this would refer to the action of elephants which dig out soil from mounds in tirumalai \[here, tirumalai, which means ‘divine mountain’ would refer to tirumālirunjōlai malai\] with their tusks.

**am pūm puṟavil** – on the slopes of tirumalai, where plants have blossomed

**tārkkodi mullaigaḷum tavaḷanagai kāttuginṟa** – jasmine creeper, which has blossomed with beautiful flowers, reminds me of the time when emperumān smiled at us prior to his uniting with us, and is tormenting me. The jasmine creeper is tormenting me, who is also like a creeper \[looking for a supporting pole for sustaining myself\]. An entity could torment another entity which is from a different species. But how could an entity torment an entity from the same species \[both being creepers\]?

**kārkkoḷ padā ninṟu kaḻaṟic cirikkat tariyēn** – creepers, called as *padā*, which have got pollinated, had blossomed to such an extent that the flowers had completely covered the creepers. They reminded me, through their smile, the laughter of emperumān at the peak stage, due to his total independent nature, when he had united with me.

**ārkkidugō tōḻī avan tār seyda pūsalaiyē** – we had desired the garland which he \[emperumān\] had donned on his divine shoulders. To whom shall we narrate the “*mahābhārata battle*” \[severe war between the garland and me\] which ensued as a result and get consoled? Could it not be narrated to the one who she had called as *tōḻī*? That friend had already swooned, after seeing āṇḍāḷ’s travails. Hasn’t nammāḻvār too mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 9-9-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/09/thiruvaimozhi-9-9-5-yamudai-nenjamum/) “e*mmilmun avanukku māyvarālō*” (ahead of me, they became hurt, for his sake)? Since loss and gain are common between the two of us, you had already swooned ahead of me. Hence, I am unable to sustain myself by narrating my travails to you. Should I share my grief with others who are already roaming around, carrying many other worries which?

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9.3. Karuvilai on

*karuviḷai oṇmalargāḷ! kāyāmalargāḷ! tirumāluruvoḷi kāttuginṟīr enakku uyvaḻakku onṟu uraiyīrtiruviḷaiyādu tiṇḍōḷ tirumālirunjōlai nambivarivaḷaiyil pugundu vandi paṟṟum vaḻakkuḷadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

oṇ – beautiful; karuviḷai malargāḷ – oh, the dark blue flowers from wild creeper!; kāyāmalargāḷ – oh, the purple-coloured flowers!; tirumāl – emperumān, who is together with pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi), his; uru oḷi – complexion of his divine form; kāttuginṟir – remind; enakku – to me (who is felling sad, thinking about it); uyvaḻakku onṟu – the way to sustain; uraiyīr – tell me; tiru viḷaiyādu – the arena where periya pirāttiyār plays; tiṇ tōḷ – having strong, divine shoulders; tirumālirunjōlai nambi – aḻagar; il pugundu – entering (my) house; vari vaḷai – (my) beautiful bangles; vandi paṟṟum vaḻakku uḷadē – is it proper to forcefully steal?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the dark blue flowers from wild creeper! Oh, the purple-coloured flowers! You remind me of the complexion of the divine form of emperumān who is together with pirātti. You should tell me the way for me to sustain myself. Is it proper on the part of aḻagar, who has strong, beautiful shoulders which are the playing arena for periya pirātti, to enter my house and forcefully steal my beautiful bangles?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – Looking at a few flowers she says “You, who remind me of the divine complexion of tirumāl \[consort of śrī mahālakśmi\], have to tell me whether what he is doing is proper”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**karuviḷai oṇmalargāḷ** – oh radiant dark blue flowers which have blossomed from creepers!

**kāyā malargāḷ** – oh dark purple flowers!

**tirumāl oḷi kāttuginṟīr** – you remind me of the divine complexion of emperumān’s divine form when periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) and perumāḷ are together.

**enakku uyvaḻakku onṟu uraiyīr** – even though you have been created by him to torment me, instead of carrying out only his interest always, tell me also a proper way. Between him and me, while I am really distraught, should you not take pity on me? What is the reason for their tormenting her? They thought “We should follow the divine mind of emperumān who is svāmy \[lord\] for all of us. Hence, let us torment her, in line with his divine mind”. periyavāccān piḷḷai, the vyākyātā \[commentator\] for this prabandam, has narrated here, the following, while establishing that emperumān gets his divine complexion due to connection with pirātti and that everyone should follow emperumān’s divine mind.

During the time of our preceptor nanjīyar, sundarapāṇḍiya dēvar was the king of that place. The king used to get several doubts on śrī rāmāyaṇam, whenever he met nanjīyar. One day, there was a discussion on śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 1-9 ślōkam “*sa hi vīryōpapannaśca rūpavān anasūyaka*: l *bhūmāvanupamas sūnur guṇair daśatōpama*: ll” (That śrī rāma was valorous; he had great beauty of his form; he was without jealousy; he was the most peerless son. Through his qualities, he was on a par with his father, daśarata). nanjīyar explained this, saying “While emperumān incarnated as śrī rāmapirān with his divine qualities and had qualities such as *paratvam* \[supremacy\] etc which were natural to him, it was enough if he had half the qualities of emperor daśarata, as *cakravarti tirumagan* \[son of the emperor\]”. He said this based on the divine mind of śrī rāma as expressed in śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 120-11 when śrī rāma said “*ātmānam mānuśam manyē rāmam daśaratājmajam*” (I think of myself as a human being and as rāma, the son of daśarata) as well as based on the comparative word *daśatōpama* in the ślōkam quoted earlier, following the accepted logic that the subject of comparison will be inferior to the article with which it is compared. Hearing this, sundarapāṇḍiyan, the king, said “Should the *paramapuruśan* (supreme entity) get a distinction because he was born to someone? Is it not the clan in which he was born, that gets a distinction?” nanjīyar thought that the meanings which he had understood from the ślōkams were not known to the king and even if he explains them, he may not comprehend those. Instead of replying to the king’s query, he said “Let it be; what a beautiful word have you used when you said *paramapuruśan*!” bringing the conversation to an end. The meaning hinted here is that the logic that emperumān got his divine complexion due to his connection with pirātti is similar to saying that śrī rāma got his divine qualities because he was born as the son of daśarata. On another occasion, while discussing about śrī rāmāyaṇam with sundarapāṇḍiyan, the subject of vibhīśaṇa came up. sundarapāṇḍiyan said “What sort of activity did vibhīśaṇāḻvān indulge in! Is he not the one who gave up his brother during a perilous time?” blaming vibhīśaṇa. Since it was possible to explain this to the king such that he would understand, nanjīyar said “When there is a conflict between father and elder brother, the younger brother should take the side of father, rather than that of the elder brother. Here, śrī rāma is the father of all the worlds; hence, there is nothing faulty in leaving rāvaṇa, the elder brother and joining with perumāḷ \[śrī rāma\] who is the father”. Haven’t authentic texts too said resoundingly, as in mahābhāratam āraṇya parvam 192-56 “*sarvēśāmēva lōkānām pitā mātā ca mādava*: l *gaccatvamēnam śaraṇam śaraṇyam puruśarśaba*: ll” (Oh the best among people! It is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi who is the father and mother for all the worlds. Take refuge under him, who is apt to be taken refuge under). īsn’t rāvaṇa too included in the term *sarvēśām lōkānām*? The implied meaning here is that dark blue flowers as well as the purple flowers were thinking “Is it not our duty to follow the divine mind of emperumān who is the father of all the worlds”.

**uyvaḻakku onṟu uraiyīr** – āṇḍāḷ tells them “Is it only the convention of destruction that you have learnt? Could you not say the convention of uplifting someone?” Couldn’t you say “We will hide ourselves, without tormenting you, such that you do not feel distressed”? She hints that they are appearing like flowers but behaving like tigers.

**onṟu uraiyīr** – She is beseeching them to tell her one word. īsn’t her condition such that if they become knowledgeable and tell a word, she could sustain her life?

**tiruviḷaiyādu tiṇḍōḷ tirumālirunjōlai nambi** – he is not one who does not have affection towards his consort; he is not one who has not been born in a \[divine\] place. Would those who were born in a place where eminent people were born, fight in a way which will harm others?

**tiruviḷaiyādu tiṇḍōḷ** – divine shoulders which were like tall hills so that periya pirātti could play there.

**tirumālirunjōlai nambi** – in ancient times, when the southern parts of the country \[bhārat\] were like a forest, the eminent people in the northern parts of the country used to term it in a despicable way, as a *mlēcca bhūmi* (inferior place) inhabited by demons and monsters. In such a southern part of the country, emperumān had manifested himself to the lowly people with simplicity and with the quality of an eminent entity moving with the lower ones, just as it has been mercifully said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-3-3 “*inakkuṟavar pudiyaduṇṇum eḻil mālirunjōlai endāy*” (Oh emperumān who is mercifully residing in the beautiful tirumālirunjōlai malai where hilly tribal people offer you fresh food with millets and eat).

**nambi** – complete with all the auspicious qualities

**varivaḷai il pugundu vandi paṟṟum vaḻakkuḷadē** – if we go to his place, tormenting us could be appropriate. However, when we are in our residences, how is it apt to take away our bangles \[which are like our lives\]?

**vandi paṟṟum vaḻakkuḷadē** – is it appropriate to steal our bangles without our consent? While we wear the bangles, thinking “These are bangles which he desired”, how is it correct to steal them? It is only in a place of dispute that one would take a bribe \[bangles\]. If he were to take away our lives \[bangles\] you tell us as to how we could sustain ourselves.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.5. Tunga malarppolil

*tunga malarppoḻil sūḻ tirumālirunjōlai ninṟasengaṇ karumugilin tiru uruvam pōl malar mēltongiya vaṇḍinangāḷ! togu pūnjunaigāḷ! sunaiyiltangu sendāmaraigāḷ! enakkōr saraṇ sāṟṟuminē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tungam malar poḻil sūḻ – surrounded by gardens which have rising flowers; tirumālirunjōlai – in tirumālirum sōlai; ninṟa – mercifully remaining in standing posture; sem kaṇ karumugilin – emperumān who has divine eyes which are like lotus and divine form which is like rain bearing clouds, his; tiru uru pōl – resembling his divine form; malar mēl tongiya vaṇḍinangāḷ – oh, swarms of beetles which have settled atop flowers!; togu – being close; pū sunaigāḷ – oh, beautiful ponds!; sunaiyil tangu – present in those ponds; enakku – for me; ōr saraṇ sāṟṟumin – tell me a refuge

**Перевод:**

**emperumān, with divine eyes which are like lotus and divine form which is like monsoon clouds, is mercifully remaining in standing posture in tirumālirum sōlai, which is surrounded by gardens which have rising flowers. Oh, swarms of beetles, which have a form similar to that emperumān’s divine form and which have settled on the flowers! Oh, beautiful ponds, which are present there! Oh, reddish lotus flowers which are present in those ponds! You tell me a refuge.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she is beseeching beetles, ponds and lotus flowers to tell her a refuge.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tunga malarppoḻil sūḻ tirumālirunjōlai ninṟa** – In śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 28-17, ”*itīva dēvi bahudā vilapya sarvātmanā rāmam anusmarantī* l *pravēpamānā pariśuśkavaktrā nagōttamam puśpitamāsasāda* ll” (\[sītāp\] pirātti, crying like this, thinking of only śrī rāma, trembling, with mouth dry, reached an eminent mountain full of blossomed flowers), it has been said that for those who are inconsolable, and who reach places which are full of blossomed flowers, it is similar to entering fire. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-25 “*naiśā paśyati rākśasya*: *nēmān puśpa paladrumān* l *ēkasta hrudayā nūnam rāmamēvānupaśyati* ll” (this pirātti did not see the rākśasis (female demons); she did not see these fruits and trees. It appears that with a single focus, she is seeing only śrī rāma), for sītāp pirātti, looking at trees which are with flowers and fruits is reason for being reminded of śrī rāma and being tormented due to that. Hence, it was impossible to look at the blossomed trees, being similar to looking at demon-like women. For āṇḍāḷ too it was impossible to look at tirumālirunjōlai, which was surrounded by gardens with rising flowers.

**sengaṇ karumugilin** – entire mercy within the divine mind of emperumān is manifested in his divine eyes. However, in order to enjoy that, one has to cross the fire of gardens with rising flowers.

**tiru uruppōl malarmēl tongiya vaṇḍinangāḷ** – Oh swarms of beetles, which have emperumān’s divine form and which are settling on the flowers! To her, seeing them is similar to stepping on fire.

**vaṇḍinangāḷ** – You are together in swarms without any shortcoming; should you not overcome my deficiency of living alone?

**togu pūnjunaigāḷ** – Oh ponds, which are closely together! Water in the ponds is reminding her of emperumān’s divine form and is tormenting her.

**sunaiyil tangu sendāmaraigāḷ** – red lotus flowers, which have blossomed in those ponds, remind her of emperumān’s divine limbs such as eyes, hands, legs etc and are tormenting her.

**enakku ōr saraṇ sāṟṟuminē** – you should reveal the way by which I could escape from you all. In mahābhārata war, yudiśtra approached great warriors such as bhīśma, drōṇa et al, on the opposite side, and requested them “Teach me as to how I could kill you”. Similar to that, she is asking them, who are tormenting her, to tell her a way by which she could escape from them, due to excess anguish in her. She is telling them “bhīśma had told yudiśtra that he would drop his weapons if they keep śikaṇḍi, who is a lady, in the form of a male, in the forefront; drōṇa said that if he comes to know that his son aśvattāma has been killed, he would stop fighting. In the same way, you too tell me the way by which I could escape from you”. She expects them to say “We are not present in such and such place; you could go there and sustain yourself”

**enakkōr saraṇ sāṟṟuminē** – she is without any refuge such that she has to ask them to tell her a refuge.

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.6. Naru narum

*nāṟu naṟūm poḻil sūḻ māliunjōlai nambikku nānnūṟu tadāvinil veṇṇey vāy nērndu parāvi vaittēnnūṟu tadā niṟainda akkāravadisil sonnēnēṟu tiruvudaiyān inṟu vandu ivai koḷḷungolō*

**Пословный перевод:**

naṟu poḻil nāṟum – having sweet fragrance due to having fragrant gardens; mālirunjōlai – in tirumālirunjōlai (where he has mercifully taken residence; nambikku – for emperumān; nān – this servitor \[I\]; nūṟu tadāvil – filled in hundred pots; veṇṇey – butter; vāy nērndu – saying it through words; parāvi vaittēn – proffered; nūṟu tadā niṟainda – filled in hundred pots; akkāra adisil – sweet disjh, called as akkāravadisil; sonnēn – I proffered through my words; ivai – these \[pots of\] butter and akkāravadisil; ēṟu tiru udaiyān – aḻagar, who has wealth, which keeps increasing (as time passes); inṟu vandu – mercifully coming today; koḷḷūngol – will he accept in his divine mind?

**Перевод:**

**tirumālirunjōlai is fragrant due to the presence of fragrant gardens. To the emperumān who has mercifully taken residence there, I proffered hundred pots of butter, through words, as well as hundred pots of sweet dish, akkāravadisil, again through my words. Will emperumān, who has wealth which keeps increasing day by day, come here today and accept these, in his divine mind?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she desires to proffer hundred pots of butter and hundred pots of akkāravadisil (a sweet dish made by boiling rice in milk, ghee and sugar) \[In later years, after he had fulfilled her desire, when emperumānār (śrī rāmānujar) went to śrīvilliputtūr, āṇḍāḷ gave him the title of kōyilaṇṇar (elder brother from śrīrangam) and praised him\]*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāru naṟum poḻil mālirunjōlai nambikku nān** – the natural fragrance, present in tirumālirunjōlai, became complete after uniting with the fragrance of āṇḍāḷ, who is the incarnation of *bhūmi dēvi*, who is the epitome of fragrance.

**nambikku nūṟu tadāvinil veṇṇey** – to attract one who is without any deficiency, it appears that butter has to be used!

**nān nūṟu tadāvinil veṇṇey** – since āṇḍāḷ is in the pathos of a cow-herd girl, she is initially proffering butter, which is the material desired by covherd girls to proffer to emperumān. Looking at both his nature and her nature, isn’t proffering butter, the most apt!

**vāy nērndu parāvi vaittēn** – it is not sufficient if only merely thinks of proffering. I had proffered by saying through my words. Just as iḷaiyaperumāḷ \[lakśmaṇa\] had requested śrī rāma to say through words, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 15-7 “*paravānasmi kākutsta tvayi varśaśatam stitē* l *svayam tu rucirē dēśē kriyatām iti mām vada*” (Oh one born in the clan of kakutsta! I am always your servitor. You should yourself order me “Let a hermitage be brought up in a beautiful place”), she too, ap0art from thinking to proffer the material, says it too through her words.

**nūṟu tadā niṟainda akkāra adisil sonnēn** – is it not famous that emperumānār had proffered a hundred pots of butter and a hundred parts of *akkāravadisil* thinking “The little girl had prayed for; there is no evidence that she had done it. īsn’t it our duty to fulfil her wish, having been born in her clan \[of servitors\]?”mahābhāratam uddāna parvam 87-13 says “*anyat pūrṇādapām kumbhāt anyat pādāvanējanāt* l *anyat kuśalasampraśnāt na cēccati janārdana*: ll” (kriṣṇan does not desire anything other than a *pūrṇakumbham* \[a pot of water containing water which is offered to a guest at the time of receiving the guest\], he does not desire anything other than washing his divine feet and does not desire anything other than a kind word, enquiring about his well-being). nampiḷḷai, one of our preceptors, asking of his ācārya, nanjīyar “When emperumān is satiated with small things, why did she commit excessively to a hundred pots of butter and a hundred pots of *akkāravadisil* to emperumān?” nanjīyar mercifully responded, saying “When compared to the wealth in tiruvāyppādi \[śrī gōkulam\], these are very trivial, like a *pūrṇakumbham*”. When one recalls the first part of dvayam \[an esoteric mantram which establishes the means to achieve emperumān and the benefit which accrues\], it would be clear that emperumān is the means, without expecting anything. Hence, one should not take even a couple of steps with the thought that one is aiding the means. Thus, those who are firmly engaged with the first part of dvayam will be without any self-fulfilling effort. If one were to recall the second part of dvayam, it would say that one should carry out all *kainkaryams* (services) to emperumān. śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-25 says “*bhāvāmstu saha vaidēhyā girisānuśu ramsyatē* l *aham sarvam kariśyāmi jāgratas svapataśca tē* ll” (you would be engaging with vaidēhi \[sītāp pirātti\] on the slopes of the mountains; when you are awake as well as asleep, I would carry out all tasks of servitude to you\]. As said in this, one should be zealously carrying out services to emperumān, without any break. Those who are firmly engaged with the second part of dvayam will be engaged with activities of servitude, like iḷaiyaperumāḷ. Thus, those who are meditating on the first part of dvayam will be like sītāp pirātti, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-30 “*śaraistu sankulām krutvā lankām parabalārtdana*: l *mām nayēd yadi kākutstas tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt* ll” (If śrī rāmapirān, who annihilates the strength of his enemies, bewilders lankā with his arrows and takes me with him, it will be apt for him), waiting for emperumān to accept us while those who are meditating on the second part of dvayam will be like iḷaiyaperumāḷ, carrying out all kainkaryams, without sleeping. Since āṇḍāḷ is now meditating on the second part of dvayam, she desires to proffer hundreds of pots.

**ēṟu tiruvudaiyān** – one who has wealth which keeps increasing with passing of each day. Since he is complete with wealth, and does not have the necessity to obtain something from anyone, he does not become enthusiastic if someone does a lot; neither does he despise if someone does a little.

**inṟu vandu ivai koḷḷungolō** – instead of making it something which will disappear with my mere words, will be make me capable of proffering these and will be accept them? Just as he had gone to the hermitage of the great sage atri, saying “I am rāma, she is mytili and he is lakśmaṇa” accepting the hospitality of atri and anasūyā, will be accept my proffering too?

**ēṟu tiruvudaiyān** – kūrattāḻvān, while composing sundarabāhustavam, translated this term as “*ārūdaśrī*” (one who has śrīdēvi on his divine chest). That this meaning too has been accepted in our sampradāyam has been brought out through a narration in the vyākhyānam by periyavāccān piḷḷai. vangipurattu nambi was one of śrī rāmānuja’s disciples. He had a disciple called as siṟīyāttān or siṟiyāccān. Once, when siṟīyāccān had come from hoysāla rājya (kingdom) to tamiḻ nādu, he went to a place called as rājakēsari, to speak to nambi, his ācāryan. During their conversation, nambi told āccān “Those who are in śaivam and other philosophies accept Sivan as their supreme entity and praise him as omniscient. The distinction for us, who are followers of the philosophy stated in vēdas, over them is that we consider lakśmīnātan (the consort of śrī mahālakśmi) as our refuge, thus holding on to them both”. Since our philosophy is like this, through the first part of dvayam, when we attain emperumān, we do so with the puruśakāram (recommendatory role) of pirātti. In the second part of dvayam, when we attain emperumān as the ultimate benefit, we do so in her presence. Just as atarvaśikhā upaniśat says “*kāraṇam tu dyēya*:” (only the causative entity \[emperumān\] is apt to be meditated upon), while being the cause for the worlds is an identity for supreme being, since several others such as brahmā et al are also engaged in creating some entities, there could be doubt whether they are also causative entities. If the *śrīya*:*patitvam* (being consort of śrī mahālakśmi) as established in puruśa sūktam is taken as the identity for being the supreme entity, there will be no doubt at all since that identity is not available with any entity other than emperumān. Hence, it will be the most apt to consider that by using the term *ēṟu tiruvudaiyān*, āṇḍāḷ was referring only to emperumān’s connection with śrī mahālakśmi.

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.7. Inru vandu

*inṟu vandu ittanaiyum amudu seydidappeṟil nānonṟu nūṟāyiramāk koduttup pinnum ālum seyvantenṟal maṇam kamaḻum tirumālirunjōlai tannuḷninṟa pirān adiyēn manattē vandu nērpadilē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tenṟal maṇam kamaḻum – southerly breeze wafting in, with fragrance; tirumālirunjōlai tannuḷ – in tirumālirunjōlai; ninṟa – mercifully residing; pirān – aḻagar, who is the lord; inṟu – today; vandu – mercifully coming here; ittanaiyum – hundred pots of butter and hundred pots of akkāravadisil; amudu seydidap peril – if he were to mercifully consume; adiyēn manattē vandu nērpadil – if he were to reside permanently in my mind; nān – I, the servitor; onṟu – for each pot; nūṟu āyiram A koduttu – proffering one hundred thousand pots; pinnum – beyond that too; āḷum seyvan – I will carry out all types of servitude

**Перевод:**

**Southerly breeze is wafting in, carrying fragrance, to tirumālirunjōlai, where aḻagar, the lord of tirumālirunjōlai, is mercifully residing. If he were to mercifully come here today and consume the hundred pots of butter and hundred pots of akkāravadisil \[which I had proffered to him earlier\] and if he were to reside permanently in my mind, I, his servitor, will proffer him a hundred thousand pots for each pot consumed and will also carry out all types of servitude to him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that if emperumān were to eat the hundred pots of butter and hundred pots of akkāravdisil, she will carry out kainkaryam (service) by giving further hundred thousand pots for each pot consumed.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**inṟu vandu ittanaiyum amudu seydidappeṟil** – The term *indṟu vandu* could be split either as “*inṟu vandu*” \[coming today\] or as “*inṟu uvandu*” \[being happy, today\]. In the word-by-word meanings above, the meaning for the first case \[*inṟu vandu*\] has already been shown.

**inṟu uvandu amudu seydidap peril** – it is not enough if he were to come and consume the food, as I had beseeced him to, without refusing it. He should consume it with happiness making it clear that he would not be able to sustain himself, without consuming it.

**ittanaiyum** – the hundred pots of butter and the hundred pots of *akkāravadisil*. Just as periyāḻvār had said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-5-1 “*attukkuvi sōṟṟup paruppadamum tayir vāviyum neyyaḷaṟum adangap ottattuṟṟu*”, for one who had eaten completely a mountain of cooked rice, a stream of curd and a mound of ghee, and who had swallowed all the worlds \[at the time of deluge\], this is not a great quantity of food.

**amudu seydidappeṟil** – this alone is her benefit. She was not born in a clan which will make this as a means to attain some other benefit. īsn’t she the daughter of periyāḻvār who had said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-1-4 “*kūṟai śōṟu ivai vēṇḍuvadillai*” \[we will not pray to you for clothes, food etc\]! Moreover, isn’t she mercifully saying that she will proffer a hundred thousand pots of food to him? What is the benefit which he will derive then? There is a saying in the world “*prayōjanam anuddiśya na mandōpi pravartadē*” (even a fool will not undertake an activity without expecting any benefit).

**nāṇ onṟu nūṟayiramāk koduttup pinnum Alum seyvan** – I have come only to proffer and not to accept; if he consumes hundred pots of food, I will give a hundred thousand pots. Along with that, I will carry out further servitude. The hundred thousand pots of butter, hundred thousand pots of *akkāravadisil* and my servitude are the benefits which he will derive. The happiness which he gets out of consuming all these is the benefit for me.

**nān** – Am I not one who had beseeced him such that he is happy? For him, the benefit is consuming the hundred thousand pots of food proffered to him. For me, who is trembling with the urge to attain the benefit, carrying out kainkaryam is the benefit.

**pinnum āḷum seyvan** – When I am going to give him hundred thousand pots of what he was happy to consume, will I let go of the kainkaryam which I desire?

**tenṟal maṇam kamaḻum tirumālirunjōlai tannuḷ ninṟa pirān adiyēm manattē vandu nērpadil …. pinnum āḷum seyvan** – more than the hundred pots of butter and *akkāravadisil*, isn’t her servitude which he is desiring? Instead of attaining something novel, isn’t getting servitude, which is ancient, ready always but which he had missed for a longtime, the one which he will be happy with? Did he not incarnate as kaṇṇan and steal butter etc, only to enslave *cētanas* (entities with knowledge)?

**āḷum seyvan** – she is desiring the same which nammāḻvār, in whose clan she was born and who is like her father, had desired, as he had said in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-1-ozhivil-kalam/) “*vaḻuvilā adimai seyyavēṇḍum nām*” (we should carry out faultless servitude).

**tenṟal maṇam kamaḻum … pirān adiyēn manattē vandu nēr padil** – since he is emperumān who has as his nature showering benefits to others, not entering my mind is not due to any deficiency on his part. When he set out to enter my mind, the gentle breeze which wafted in tirumālirunjōlai, carrying fragrance, should have tied him down to that place. Despite that hurdle of residing in that abode, if he were to mercifully reside in my mind ….

**adiyēn manattē vandu nērpadil ….. pinnum āḷum seyvan** – just as I had desired “*vaḻuvilā adimai seyya vēṇḍum nām*”, if he were to mercifully reside in my mind, I will carry out servitude.

**adiyēn** – isn’t carrying out servitude the natural benefit since time immemorial? Am I, the servitor, praying for something new?

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9.8. Kalai elundirundu

*kālai eḻundirundu kariya kuruvikkaṇangaḷmālin varavu solli maruḷ pādudal meymmai kolō?sōlai malaip perumān tuvarāpadi emperumānālinilaip perumān avan vārttai uraikkinṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kariya kuruvi kaṇangaḷ – flocks of dark sparrows; kālai – at the time of dawn; eḻundirundu – waking up; sōlaimalai perumān – being the lord of tirumālirunjōlai; tuvarāpadi emperumān – as well as being the lord of dvārakāpuri; ālin ilia perumān avan – and the lord who reclined on a banyan leaf, that sarvēśvaran’s; vārttai – words; uraikkinṟa – they are speaking; mālin – emperumān’s; varavu – arrival; solli – speaking; maruḷ pādudal – singing of the tune maruḷ; meymmai kol – will it happen in reality?

**Перевод:**

**Flocks of dark sparrows, getting up at the time of dawn, are speaking the words of the lord of tirumālirunjōlai, who is also the lord of dvārakāpuri and is the lord who reclined on a tender banyan leaf. Will their singing in the tune maruḷ, foretelling the arrival of emperumān, fructify?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she is asking whether the sounds of sparrows foretelling the arrival of emperumān would be fulfilled.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kālai eḻundirundu** – getting up early in the morning. A query was raised whether sparrows, which are low on intelligence, could get up early in the morning and foretell the arrival of emperumān. siṟiyāccān \[who was mentioned in the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad, referred to as the disciple of vangipurattu nambi) responded to that query, saying “In the place where āṇḍāḷ was born, is there any other pastime for birds? Do they have sleep? Hence, getting up at dawn and foretelling emperumān’s arrival are possible”. This was quoted mercifully by nanjīyar. Just as nammāḻvār had mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-7-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/03/thiruvaimozhi-6-7-2-urum-nadum/) “*ūrum nādum ulagamum tannaippōl avanudaiya pērum tārgaḷumē pidaṟṟa*” (making the people in the place where she was born, in the country and in the world, to babble the divine name and the divine icons \[conch, disc et al\]), all the entities born in the place where āṇḍāḷ was born, would also talk only about emperumān. vangipurattu nambi, in his divya grantam, had said “*brāhmē muhūrtē cōttāya cintayēd ātmanō hitam* l *harir harir haririti vyāharēt vaiṣṇava*: *pumān*” (One who is a vaiṣṇava should get up early in the morning and think of good things for himself / herself; (s)he should recite the divine name of hari, three times). That which has been ordained for cētanas (entities with knowledge) is being followed by birds too, which do not have \[the same\] knowledge.

**kariya kuruvikkaṇangaḷ mālin varavu solli** – Since they are dark sparrows, resembling the dark form of emperumān, they showered benefit through their form. Since they foretold the arrival of emperumān, they showered benefit through their words too.

**mālin varavu solli maruḷ pādudal meymmai kolō** – they were singing in the tune of *maruḷ* *indaḷam,* the way emperumān was anxiously coming to see āṇḍāḷ, and how he was bewildered, without getting in proximity to her place. āṇḍāḷ is wondering whether this is true or her bewilderment. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 34-23 when sītāp pirātti wondered when she saw hanuman in front of her, speaking to her “*kinnu syācittamōhō’ýam bhavēt vātagatis tviyam* l *unmādajō vikārō vā syādiyam mrugatruṣṇikā* ll’ (Is it due to my heart’s bewilderment? Is it the ploy of deity for wind? Have I lost my senses? Has my nature got changed? Is it a mirage?), āṇḍāḷ suffered in the same manner in which sītāp pirātti had suffered.

**sōlai malaip perumān** – one who has mercifully taken residence in tirumāliriunjōlai in order to protect all the cētanas.

**tuvarāvapadi emperumān** – the term tuvarāpadi refers to \[kriṣṇa’s\] sixteen thousand consorts through illustration. By referring to him as *perumān*, it is hinted that just as horse will always win in a race with elephant despite the large form of elephant, in matters related to love, even if all the sixteen thousand consorts join together, he will be superior to all of them, being a connoisseur.

**ālin ilaip perumān** – one who has the cleverness of *aghatitaghatanā* \[ability to bond entities which can never be bonded\] by swallowing all the worlds and reclining on a tender banyan leaf which had just then sprouted.

**avan vārttai urakkinṟadē** – by speaking words about  him, will their hint of emperumān’s arrival turn out to be true?

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9.9. Kongu alarum

*kōngalarum poḻil mālirunjōlaiyil konṟaigaḷ mēltūngu pon mālaigaḷōdu udanāy ninṟu tūnguginṟēnpūngoḷ tirumugattu maduttūdiya sangoliyumsārngavil nāṇ oliyum talaipeyvadu enjjñānṟugolō?*

**Пословный перевод:**

kōngu alarum poḻil – having gardens with kōngu trees (a kind of cotton tree, silk-cotton), which have blossomed; mālirunjōlaiyil – in tirumālirunjōlai; konṟaigaḷ mēl – on top of konṟai trees (Indian laburnum); tūngu – hanging; pon mālaigaḷōdu udanāy ninṟu – matching those golden hued garlands; tūnguginṟēn – I am remaining in a futile way; pū koḷ – having beauty; tirumugattu – on his divine lips; maduttu – kept there; ūdiya – blown; sangu – śrī pāncajanyam’s(divine conch); oliyum – its triumphant sound; sārngam vil nāṇ oliyum – the sound of śārngam bow (emperumān’s divine bow); talaippeyvadu – going in proximity; enjjñāṇṟu kol – when will it be?

**Перевод:**

**In tirumālirunjōlai, where its gardens are blossoming with kōngu trees, on top of konṟai trees, golden hued garlands are hanging, in a futile way. I am also much similar to those garlands, lying in a futile way. When will I get to hear śrī pāncajanyam’s triumphant noise made from the divine lips of emperumān and the sound of his śārngam bow?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that she is lying in a futile way, like the konṟai trees (Indian laburnum) which are lying in tirumālirunjōlai. She is asking as to when she will hear the triuamphant blowing of emperumān’s conch and the resounding noise of emperumān’s sārngam (divine bow) and sustain herself.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kōngalarum poḻil …. tūnguginṟēn** – I am lying in a futile way, just like the golden hued garlands which are hanging from the top of *konṟai* trees which are in tirumālirunjōlai, where silk-cotton trees are blossoming in gardens. Will the bunches of flowers of *konṟai* trees be of no use? Since this is the mountain of emperumān’s, devotees of Siva, who are filled with *tamō* guṇa (qualities of laziness, ignorance etc) will not come there. śrī vaiṣṇavas (devotees of lord viṣṇu), who are filled with *satva*guṇa (noble qualities) will not use these flowers, which are meant for Siva. Hence, these *konṟai* flowers, which are blossoming in bunches, are lying in futile, with no one to use them. Being born as periyāḻvār’s divine daughter, having mercifully said as in nācciyār tirumoḻi 12-4 “*gōvindanakku allāl vāyil pōgā*” (they will not enter anyone’s mouth other than emperumān’s) and in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 1-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/06/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-1-5/) “*mānidavarkkenṟu pēcdcuppadil vāḻagillēn*” (I will not be alive if anyone spoke about marrying me to any human entity), āṇḍāḷ considers herself as apt only for emperumān. periyavāccān piḷḷai, one of our preceptors and the commentator for this prabandam, would feel terrible that such āṇḍāḷ, who belongs only to emperumān, was suffering like this.

**pūngoḷ …. enjjñānṟu kolō** – there are deep meanings to the reference of the sounds of divine conch and divine bow. In śrī rāmāvatāram (incarnation of śrī rāma), sītāp pirātti was in aśōka vanam, waiting to hear the sound of śrī rāma’s divine bow. In kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation of kriṣṇa), rukmiṇip pirātti was waiting to hear the sound of kriṣṇa’s pāncajanyam, after her marriage with śiśupālan had been fixed. āṇḍāḷ feels that her situation was much worse than their respective situation and that she could sustain herself only if she hears the sounds of both the divine weapons which they were eagerly waiting to hear. When her marriage with śiśupāḷan was about to take place, rukmiṇip pirātti sustained herself only after hearing the triumphant call of śrī pāncajanyam, from the outer gardens. In aśōka vanam, after hearing the divine sound of śrī śārngam, made by śrī rāma when he entered lankā, sītāp pirātti sustained herself even when rāvaṇa showed her the illusory severed head of śrī rāma. Since āṇḍāḷ’s anguish is much more than those two pirāttis’ anguish, she says that she could sustain herself only if she hears the sounds of the divine conch and the divine bow, together.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 9.10. Sandodu karagilum

*sandodu kāragilum sumandu tadangaḷ poruduvandiḻiyum silambāṟudai māḷirunjōlai ninṟasundaranaic curumbār kuḻaṟ kōdai togutturaittasendamiḻ pattum vallār tirumāladi sērvargaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sandodu – along with sandal wood logs; kāragilum – logs from a kind of eagle wood, which are used as incense; sumandu – pushing them; tadangaḷ porudu vandu – running fast, overflowing the banks; iḻiyum – flowing abundantly; silambāṟudai – having nūpura gangai \[a stream\]; mālirunjōlai ninṟa sundaranai – regarding aḻagar who has mercifully taken residence in tirumālirunjōlai malai; surumbu Ar kuḻal kōdai – āṇḍāḷ, who has locks where beetles are flocking; toguttu uraitta – mercifully and beautifully mentioned; sem tamiḻ pattum vallār – those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams which; tirumāl adi – the divine feet of śrīman nārāyaṇan; sērvargaḷ – will attain

**Перевод:**

**nūpura gangai, in tirumālirunjōlai, is flowing abundantly, pushing logs of sandalwood and a type of eagle wood, going past the banks. Those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams, mercifully and beautifully composed by āṇḍāḷ, who has beetles swarming her locks, regarding aḻagar who has mercifully taken residence in tirumālirunjōlai, will get to attain the divine feet of śrīman nārāyaṇan.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āndāḷ brings the decad to its end, by mentioning the benefits accrued to those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sandodu … silambāṟu** – just as women take wealth from their place of birth to the place of husband, silambāṟu, which was born in the mountain, is pushing logs of sandalwood and a type of eaglevood \[used for making incense\] to tirumālirunjōlai, the divine abode of emperumān, overflowing the banks on either side, and increasing in its volume.

**tadangaḷ porudu** – this river is coming like a rutting elephant which is piercing the banks with its tusks.

**silambāṟudai** – just like virajai river for paramapadam, this river is for tirumālirunjōlai.

**māḷirunjōlai ninṟa sundaranai** – she sang about the most beautiful aḻagar, who has taken residence in the most beautiful tirumālirunjōlai. Who sang these pāaurams?

**surumbār kuḻaṟ kōdai** – āndāḷ, who has beetles swarming her locks. āṇḍāḷ, who is capable of winning over the beauty of tirumālirunjōlai and that of aḻagar, with her one limb \[of her locks\], had to strive like this.

**togutturaitta** – just like stringing a garland with many types of gemstones, stringing a *pāmālai* (an encomium in poetical form) of aḻagar’s auspicious qualities

**pattum vallār** – those who are capable of reciting these ten pāsurams with their meanings

**tirumāladi sērvargaḷē** – she suffered since she could not carry out servitude to the union of perumāḷ and pirātti, which she had desired. However, those who are capable of reciting these pāsurams will not suffer like she did, but will attain the benefit which she desired.

**adi sērvar** – they will attain the *parama puruśārtam* \[ultimate benefit\] of *kainkaryam* \[servitude\]. Did she desire servitude? varāha purāṇam says “*aham śiśyā ca dāsī ca bhaktā ca puruśōttama* l” (Oh, the best among all entities! I am your disciple, servant and devotee). Just as it has been mentioned in this, when she was bhūmip pirātti, did not varāha purāṇam say that she had desired to carry out servitude, by being emperumān’s servant? *aham śiśyā ca* – I had learnt all the matter which I had to learn, from you directly. *dāsī ca* – when you and periya pirāttiyār are together, and tell me “Take such and such material; keep such and such material etc”, I am duty bound to carry out those orders. *bhaktā ca* – I am going to compose poems by being born in the clan of āḻvārs who are your devotees. Could the same entity exhibit different forms, separately? *puruśōttama* – You are the most supreme among all entities. Wouldn’t your devotees engage to the extent to which you make them to get engaged? Even when a river flows through various channels and sub-canals, the quantum of water which reaches the ocean will be without any shortcoming. In the same way, despite enjoying emperumān as his disciple, servant and devotee, for bhūmip pirātti, love will not only not diminish, but it will grow to be bigger than ocean.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the tenth decad.
