## 10.10. Nalla en toli!

*en nalla tōḻi! – ōh, my dear friend!nāga aṇaimisai – one vho is reclining on te mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan)nam parar – our perumāḷselvar – one vho is opulentperiyar – superior to everyone elsenām – ve, on te oter handsiṟiu mānidavar – are lovly human entities(We, vho are like tis)en seyvadu – vhat could ve do?villi puduvai vittu cittar – periyāḻvār, vho is te controller of śrīvilliputtūrtangaḷ dēvarai – tat emperumān, vho is obedientvalla parisu – in vays possible for himvaruvipparēl – if he vere to inviteadu kāṇḍum – ten, ve vill get to vorśip (him)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my dear friend! namperumāḷ, who is reclining on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān, is opulent; he is superior to everyone. We, on the other hand, are lowly human entities. What could we do? If periyāḻvār, who is the controller of śrīvilliputtūr, were to invite that perumāḷ, who is obedient to him, through ways possible for him, then, we could get to worship him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – in the last pāsuram of this decad, āṇḍāḷ’s friend feels more saddened than āṇḍāḷ. She thinks “She considers being born as the daughter of periyāḻvār as the reason for attaining the ultimate benefit, and emperumān’s words that he will not give up on those who are affectionate. In the fourth pāsuram of this decad, she had expressed a doubt ‘kumaranār sollum poyyānāl nānum piṟandamai poyyanṟē’ wondering whether those two reasons would give her the ultimate benefit. There seems to be no way to keep her alive. Through which means should we make her live?” Knowing her thoughts through her face, āṇḍāḷ tells her “There is no need for you to fear. There is a way for our life. Our respected elder, periyāḻvār, will get us emperumān through one way or the other. You will see that”. nalla en tōḻi! nāgaṇai misai nambararselvar periyar siṟumānidavar nām seyvadu envilli puduvai vittucittar tangaḷ dēvaraivalla parisu varivipparēl adu kāṇḍumē Word0by-Word Meanings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nalla en tōḻi** – aren’t  you the one who is more distressed at seeing my loss, than I!

**nāgaṇai misai nam parar selvar … seyvadu en** – What could we, the lowliest human entities, do to him, who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān, who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi and who is famous as the most supreme entity? nammāḻvār had attempted to take refuge under emperumān in his [tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-10-agalagillen-part-5/) “*adikkīḻ amarndu pugundēnē*” (I have surrendered, by being seated with exclusive focus on kainkaryam, beneath your divine feet); he sought the recommendation of periya pirāttiyār by saying “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu alarmēl mangai uṟai mārbā*!” in the same pāsuram (Oh, one who has the divine chest from which periya pirātti will not separate even for a moment) and tiruvanantāḻvān in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-10-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/thiruvaimozhi-5-10-11-naganai-misai/) “*nāgaṇaimisai nambirān caraṇē śaraṇ namakku*” (only the divine feet of our perumāḷ who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān are our refuge). However, āṇḍāḷ is bewildered due to pangs of separation from emperumān since she had said that periya pirāttiyār and tiruvanantāḻvān have not recommended her case to emperumān.

**nāgaṇai misai nam parar** – since paramātmā (emperumān) is called as *anantaśayanan* (one who is reclining on ādiśēśan) in paryankavidyai, there is nothing which is superior to reclining on ādiśēśan.

**nam parar** – taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 says “*nārayaṇa param brahmam tatvam nārāyaṇa*: *para*: l *nārāyaṇa parō jyōtir ātmā nārāyaṇa*: *para*:” (nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme brahmam; nārāyaṇan is the supreme entity. nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme effulgence; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme ātmā). Since vēda vākyams (lines from sacred texts) through using the word nārāyaṇa in many ways do not refer to anyone else, he has the distinction of being eminent.

**nam selvar** – he is eminent as the consort of śrī mahālakśmi. He is praised as the most eminent in [tiruvāimoḻi 2-8-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-8-1-anaivadhu-aravanaimel/) “*aṇaivadu aravaṇaimēl pūmbāvai āgam  puṇarvadu*” (he reclines on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān and he embraces śrī mahālakśmi who resides on the lotus flower) and in puruśa sūktam 2-5 “*hrīśca tē lakśmīsca patnyau*” (śrī mahālakśmi and bhūmip pirātti are your divine consorts) through both the samaskruta and drāvida vēdāntams.

**periyar** – He is distinguished, due to these reasons. The difference between him and us is similar to the difference between a mountain and an atom.

**siṟu mānidavar** – we cannot go even a little bit near him since we have not been born in the species of celestial entities; we cannot go close to him since we are not knowledgeable like the sages. We are not like tiruvanantāḻvān who, despite being highly knowledgeable, remains as an *acit* (entity without knowledge), as his mattress. We are not like the covherd girls, who despite not knowing the difference between the left hand and the right hand, remain embracing him. āren’t we the lowly human entities?

**nām seyvadu en** – since he had earlier said a word “I will protect followers” could we compel him? Assuming that her friend is asking her “In that case, do we have to forego engagement with him?”, she says “There is no need for us to lose”. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 41-4 “*tvayi kincit samāpannē kim kāryam sītayā mama* l *bharatēna mahābāhō*! *lakśmaṇēna yavīyasā* ll *śatrughnēna ca śatrughna*! *svaśarīrēṇa vā puna*: ll” (Oh, sugrīva, who has long hands! If something disgraceful had happened to you, what will be the benefit for me after I get sītā? What will be the benefit through bharata, lakśmaṇa who is younger and śatrughna? Oh, one who annihilates enemies! What is the benefit of my physical form?) she says through the second part of the pāsuram that for one who is like this, there is one matter which we have to point out.

**villipuduvai vittucittar tangaḷ dēvarai … kāṇḍumē** – we are not taking refuge under him because he is common to both celestial entities and to monstrous entities. She says that we are taking refuge under him only because periyāḻvār, who is our ācāryan, had kept him in his mind, like in a temple, due to which he is obedient to periyāḻvār. paramātmā (supreme entity) is under the control of ācāryan. A cētanan should think that emperumān will come to us only due to our connection with ācāryan. We should not think that while for both the ācāryan and us, the connection with emperumān is common as that between the master and servant, what is the need for an ācāryan, in between? While the connection between emperumān and us as the master and servant is permanent, the reason for our not attaining emperumān till date is only because we did not have a connection with an ācāryan. Hence, the *śāstrārttam* (meaning from traditional texts) that we should take refuge under emperumān only through ācāryan as the recommending entity is mentioned here. Apart from considering ācāryan as the recommending entity, there are some great entities, who are firmly established in *ācāryaniśtai* \[devoted fully to ācāryan\], as mentioned in [kaṇṇinuṇ ciṟuttāmbu 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/kanninun-chiru-thambu-2-navinal/) “*dēvum maṟṟaṟiyēn*”(I do not know of anyone other than ācāryan as emperumān),who will even discard emperumān and consider ācāryan as the means. To emphasise that she is in that assembly, āṇḍāḷ says “*tangaḷ dēvarai*”, severing her relationship with emperumān and establishing relationship with periyāḻvār. She desires emperumān only because periyāḻvār desires him. A narrative is mentioned here in the mūla vyākhyānam of periyavāccāṇ piḷḷai – A few persons asked a regal lady *tripurādēviyār*, who was a disciple of śrī rāmānujar “Why do you not worship other deities?” She replied, saying “Since emperumānār is not vorśipping other deities, I too am not vorśipping. If emperumānār were to worship Sivan, who is the presiding deity for the north-eastern corner where waste water is drained, we will also worship him”

**valla parisu varuvipparēl** – there are many ways by which periyāḻvār would invite emperumān. If he invites emperumān through any of those ways, then we will attain him. He could invite him by offering a flower as in  periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-7-1 ”*seṇbagappūccuttavārāy*” (you should come to don campaka flower); he could invite him by singing as in [tiruppallāṇḍu 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-1-pallandu/) “*pallāṇḍu pallāṇḍu*” (may you live long); he could invite him by reciting the required vēdas to establish emperumān’s supremacy; he could invite him by collecting all the materials required for giving *tirumanjanam* (divine bath) and recite as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-4-1 “*nāraṇā nīrādavarāy*” (Oh nārāyaṇa! Please come for having a bath); if he gathers materials for combing his divine hair and invites him, he has to come; if he calls him as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-8-1 “*andiyampōdiduvāgum aḻaganē*! *kāppidavārāy*” (dusk is settling in. Oh, the beautiful entity! You should come for me to shield you from evil spirits), emperumān cannot say he will not come; if periyāḻvār calls him for taking a bath as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-4 or for decorating him with flowers as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-7 or for shielding him from inimical entities as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 2-8, or through various other pāsurams for carrying out various other activities, would he not come? Since there are so many ways by which periyāḻvār could invite emperumān, āṇḍāḷ says *vallaparisu*.

**adu kāṇḍumē** – if periyāḻvār could get him by any of these means, it is certain that we would attain him. Did not āḷavandār too ask emperumān to shower his mercy on him through his connection with his grandfather and *paramācāryar* (supreme ācāryan) nātamunigaḷ, as mentioned in his [stōtra ratnam 65](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/sthothra-rathnam-65/) “*akrutrima tvaccaraṇāravinda prēma prakarśāvadim ātmavantam* l *pitāmaham nātamunim vilōkyaprasīda madvruttamacintayitvā*” (Without considering my conduct, you should grant me mercy by considering nātamunigaḷ who is the outer limit for the supreme affection which comes naturally at your divine lotus-like divine feet and who is my grandfather).

āḷavandār had earlier surrendered to emperumān, as mentioned in [stōtra ratnam 22](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/01/sthothra-rathnam-22/) “*na darmaniśtōsmi na cātmavēdī na bhaktimāmstvaccaraṇāravindē* l *akincanō’nanya gati*: *śaraṇya*! *tvat pādamūlam śaraṇamprapadyē*” (Oh emperumān who is appropriate for surrendering to! I am not rooted in *karmayōgam* (path of deeds); I do not have *ātmajñānam* (knowledge about the supreme entity, the path of knowledge); I do not have *bhakti yōgam* (the path of devotion) at your divine feet. I, who do not have any means and who am without any other refuge, affirm with certainty that your divine lotus-like feet are the means). He thought “Since I have carried out the action of surrendering, even that could be without any strength”. In order to overcome that shortcoming, in the last ślōkam of stōtra ratnam, he desired to seek benefit keeping nātamunigaḷ in the forefront. In the same way, āṇḍāḷ is seeking benefit, keeping periyāḻvār in the forefront.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the next decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 11.1. Tam ugakkum

*tāmugakkum tam kaiyil sangamē pōlāvō?yāmugakkum em kaiyil sangamum ēndiḻaiyīr!tīmugattu nāgaṇaimēl sērum tiruvarangarā! mugattai nōkkārāl ammanē! ammanē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēndu iḻaiyīr! – oh, ladies who have worn ornaments!; yām ugakkum em kaiyil sangam – the bangles which I have worn on my hand, desirously; tām ugakkum tam kaiyil sangam pōlāvō – will \[my bangles\] not match with the divine conch which he is wearing on his divine hand?; tī mugattu – having cruel faces; nāga aṇai mēl – on top of the mattress, tiruvanantāḻvān \[ādiśēśan\]; sērum – one who is mercifully reclining; tiru arangar – śrīranganātar; mugattai – (my) face; nōkkār Al – he is not looking at; A! – alas!; ammanē! ammanē! – My misfortune!

**Перевод:**

**Oh, ladies who have worn ornaments! Will not the bangles which I have worn desirously on my hand match the divine conch which he \[emperumān\] is wearing on his divine hand? śrīranganātar, who is mercifully reclining on top of the mattress, tiruvanantāḻvān, who has cruel faces, is not looking at me. Alas! It is my misfortune.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – looking at the mothers and friends, she tells them to ask emperumān ”Is your action proper?” She tells them to ask him “When you ask him ‘She is suffering so much; what is the reason for your not coming to help her?’ Is he thinking that there is a reason for his inaction? Ask him”. ānalysing “Is there any shortcoming in my desiring him?” she says “It is not correct since I am happy with him. If he were to analyse whether there was any deficiency in his desiring me, then that is also not correct. Is there any deficiency in his ability to protect? The answer to that is also that there is no deficiency. Is there any deficiency in my quality of whether I am appropriate to be protected, then this is also not the reason. Thus, what reason is he going to give, for not helping me?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tāmugakkum tam kaiyil sangamē pōlāvō yāmugakkum em kaiyil sangamum** – isn’t my desiring the bangles which I wear on my hand similar to his desiring his divine conch which he is wearing on his divine hand? If he desires to take my bangle, should he not give me the divine conch that he is wearing? If he responds saying “I am *svatantran* (totally independent); I will do whatever I wish” then it would not fit well with his quality of a *paramapraṇayi* (one who is deeply in love with his beloved). If he desires to respond, saying “You, who are a *paratantrai* (totally dependent on emperumān) should only see what I am doing, and not compel me”, that will not be apt since it would have been said without knowing my distress. If he says “We will not give anything from our hands; we will seize things from others’ hands” that will not be apt since it would not be in line with righteous behaviour. If he says “Women, who are tender, should just see what men, who are strong, do and not speak anything”, I too have means such as *madal ūrdal* \[a custom by which a woman would tell the people of her town as to how she had loved her beloved and how she had been deceived by her beloved, seeking their help in uniting her with him\] etc in my hand. When I ask him “Why do you refuse to give me the conch in your hand”, if he says “I desire it” then I would say “In the same way, I too desire my bangles. Return the bangles to me”. Has she not always desired to see the beauty of śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch) and the hand \[holding it\]? If he says “We too desire our pāncajanyam. How can we give that to you?”, then I would say “Is it not ours?” just as it has been mentioned in jitantē ślōkam 1.5 “*na tē rūpam na ca ākārō na āyudāni na cāspatam* l *tatā’pi puruśākārō bhaktānām tvam prakāśasē* ll” ((your) divine basic nature is not for you; the divine auspicious form is also not for you; the divine weapons also are not for you; the abode of śrī vaikuṇṭam is also not for you. All these are for the sake of your followers only. You belong to your followers. Though you have *svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam* and *vibhūti* (basic nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth) which are for your followers, you shine as the supreme entity).

**tāmugakkum tam kaiyil sangamē pōlāvō yāmugakkum em kaiyil sangamum** – is it apt for you to say “The bangle which we desire should be on our hands; the conch which others desire should also be only on our hands; we will not give the bangles which we desire; we will not give the conch which others desire”? If those who desire were to get what they desire, then just as my bangles are on your hands, your conch should come to my hand. If the owned were to get what they own, then my bangles should be only with me. These two events could happen only when there is a union.

When the mothers and friends ask her “He seized your bangles only because you desired his conch. Why do you now hate that conch?” she responds saying “Just because I had desired one conch which was on his hand, as in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 5-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/08/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-5-2/) ‘*veḷḷai viḷisangu idangaiyil koṇḍa vimalan*’ he has made me lose all my bangles, as mentioned in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 8.5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/09/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-8-5/) “*tān koṇḍa sarivaḷaigaḷ*”. Since I desired to wear one conch which he desired, he did not leave behind even one bangle on my hand”. When asked “You too could have desired all the conces on his hand”, she says “Just as he is the only *īśvaran*, there is only one conch in his hand, which is unique. I cannot desire many conces”.

**tam kaiyil sangamē pōlāvō yām ugakkum em kaiyil sangamum** – when asked “īsn’t the divine conch on his divine hand, a weapon? Why did you desire it?” she says “Just as bangles on my hand, the conch on his hand looks like an ornament only to me. Hence there is nothing wrong in my desiring it”. She thinks that just as she is *nārīṇām uttama vadu* (the best among women), wearing the bangles as an ornament, as a sign of that, he is also *puruśōttaman* (the best among men), wearing the conch as an ornament.

**ēndiḻaiyīr** – oh, those who have donned ornaments! When I am suffering like this, how did you escape from this danger, similar to escaping from the danger of deluge? She is surprised that while she has lost her bangles, they are still wearing their bangles and other ornaments. śrī rāma was surprised that while he had lost sītāp pirātti, the peacock has not lost his female, as brought out in the ślōkam in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-49 “*māyūrasya vanē nūnam rakśasa na hrutā priyā* l *tasmān nrutyati ramyēśu vanēśu saha kāntayā* ll” (the female of the peacock has not been abducted in the forest; he is dancing with her in the beautiful forest). Thus, she is thinking that all the women have lost their bangles and is asking them “How did you escape from this situation?” It appeared to her that since everyone unites with the lord of all the worlds, none could have escaped from losing her bangles. Those who have lost their jewels can express their deficiency only to those who have donned jewels; those who have shortcomings could express their deficiency only to those who do not have any shortcoming. Would youth express their complaints to renunciates? They will only express to those who are youthful, like themselves. What is the benefit in telling someone “He has stolen my bangles” when they respond saying “You keep his conch for some days”? Since they do not understand her suffering, these people are only like men.

**tīmugattu nāgaṇaimēl sērum tiruvarangar** – while these people are like this, what should I say about ādiśēśan who is giving his physical form to emperumān, despite knowing my situation? Even if he does not know the pangs of separation, he knows the flavour of togetherness. Though he is together, he has a misplaced doubt whether “separation would occur” and is experiencing the distress felt by those in separation. Thus, since he is the mattress with cruel face, he knows the pangs of separation. Could such ādiśēśan give his physical form to emperumān to enjoy when emperumān has separated from me? Could anantāḻvān be termed as *tīmugattu* (cruel face), despicably? Since she is thinking of him, like she is thinking of periyāḻvār, who would unite her with emperumān and since he is not uniting her with emperumān but feeling happy with emperumān’s reclining on him, she is saying *tīmugattu*. When a lady is separated from her husband, if her relatives from her side enjoy their time with her relatives from her husband’s side, just as she would speak despicably about her relatives from her side, she is despising ādiśēśan. Did she not mercifully say in the last pāsuram of the previous decad, “*tangaḷ dēvarai*” while referring to periyāḻvār? ḍidn’t sītāp pirātti too talk despicably as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 28-14, when separated from śrī rāma? “*piturnidēśam niyamēna krutvā vanān nivruttaścarita vrataśca* l *strībhistu manyē vipulēśaṇābis tvam ramsyasē vītabhaya krutārta*:” (Having fulfilled your father’s command as per the order of his words and observed your vow, you return from the forest fearlessly and having accomplished your purpose, I think you will enjoy with large-eyed women).

**tīmugattu** – instead of showing compassion towards people who are in distress like me, saying “Alas”, this ādiśēśan, is spitting fire such that no one could come near him.

**sērum tiruvarangar** – in the matter of cruelty, it appears that emperumān could go together with this tiruvanantāḻvān to a *gurukulam* (school under a sage) and learn about it. Alternatively, we could construe this verse to mean that when this tiruvarangar goes to recline on ādiśēśan, he need not carry weapons to annihilate enemies. This tiruvanantāḻvān with his face spitting fire, is enough to destroy the enemies. The meaning hinted is that if anyone like her, who is not favourable to emperumān, attempts to approach emperumān, he is there to destroy the person, just as it has been said in [mūnṟām tiruvandādi 66](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/05/munram-thiruvandhadhi-66/) “*ēynda paṇakkadirmēl vevvuyirppa – vāynda madu kaidabarum vayiṟurugi māṇḍār*” (the radiance emitted by the rays coming from the gems in the spread hoods of tiruvanantāḻvān, even as he sighed ….the demons madu and kaidabar who came there at that time, got killed even as their bowels started rotting).

**sērum** – the conduct of emperumān is just the opposite of the norm of the world. On the bed which is common for both me and him, he is capable of reclining alone!

**tiruvarangar** – one who came to tiruvarangam in order to protect those who are in sorrow, forgetting that, he is reclining on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān, desiring the softness, coolness and fragrance of that mattress! Even if he had not taken me under his refuge while he was in paramapadam, I could have consoled myself. But, even after coming here, he is indifferent to me.

**A!** – Alas!

**mugattu nōkārāl** – one could tolerate if the beloved, in separation, is not displaying affection. But should he give up *kaṇṇōkkam* too (looking with eyes; compassion)?

**ammanē! ammanē!** – this is an expression which is used to indicate fear. During the time of separation, one who was sweet such that one would salivate upon thinking about him, is now causing fear as if hearing the words such as “Tiger! Cobra!” How was he before and how is he, now! He has lost his sweetness and is causing fear! mahābhāratam śānti parvam 46-139 says “*yam yōgina*: *prāṇaviyōga kālē yatnēna cittē vinivēśayanti* l *sa tam purastāt harimīkśamāṇa*: *prāṇān jahau prāptapalō hi bhīśma*: ll” (that bhīśma, seeing hari, who saints attempt to establish in their hearts at the time of giving up the life, right in front of his eyes, gave up his life. īsn’t he the one who attained benefit!) Just as it has been mentioned in this, it appears that it is only for men that emperumān will manifest himself, at the last moment. It is not for girls, like me.

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 11.2. Elil udaiya

*eḻiludaiya ammanaimīr! ennarangattu innamudarkuḻalaḻagar vāyaḻagar kaṇṇaḻagar koppūḻileḻukamalap pūvaḻagar emmānār ennudaiyakaḻal vaḷaiyait tāmum kaḻal vaḷaiyē ākkinarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

eḻil udaiya – having beauty; ammanaimīr – Oh, mothers!; arangattu – mercifully residing in śrīrangam; en in amudar – being my sweet-like-nectar; kuḻal aḻagar – having beautiful divine locks of hair; kaṇṇaḻagar – having beautiful, divine eyes; koppūḻil eḻukamalap pū aḻagar – having beauty due to the lotus from the divine navel; emmānār – aḻagiya maṇavāḷar, who is my svāmy (lord); ennudaiya – my; kaḻal vaḷaiyai – bangle, which is called as kaḻal vaḷai, by convention; kaḻal vaḷaiyē ākkinar – made it as kaḻanṟu oḻiginṟa (that which slips out and disappears) bangle, by its derivative name \[a name derived from its purpose; here it refers to bangle which is slipping out and disappearing\]

**Перевод:**

**Oh, mothers, who are beautiful! My lord, who is mercifully residing in śrīrangam, is sweet to me, like nectar. He has beautiful locks of hair, beautiful eyes and has beauty due to the lotus rising from his divine navel. That aḻagiya maṇavāḷar, made my bangle which is merely called as kaḻal vaḷai, into a bangle which slips out and disappears.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she reveals her state to her mothers, from whom she was supposed to hide it.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**eḻiludaiya ammanimīr** – In the previous pāsuram, she mentioned her state of being without ornaments to those who were donning ornaments. In this pāsuram, she mentions about her state of being without beauty to those who have beauty.

**ammanaimīr** – her status has changed so much that she has to talk about her being without beauty to those to whom she was to hide her real state and put up an act of having beauty. Did not śrī rāma reveal his distress \[caused by separation from sītāp pirātti\] to iḷaiyaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) from whom he had to hide, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-3 “*saumitrē śōbatē pampā vaidūrya vimalōdakā* l *pulla padmōtpalavatī śobhitā vividair drumai*: ll” (Oh, lakśmaṇa! pampā river is shining like the gem lapis lazuli, having purest water, with blossomed lotus, blue water lily etc, made radiant by many trees)?

**ennamudar** – he is sweet like nectar, for me, unlike the brackish nectar desired by the celestial entities.

**arangattu amudar** – this nectar is not in heaven; it is in tiruvarangam.

**innamudar** – he is not like the other nectar which gives firmness to the body; he is the ecstatic nectar which gives firmness to life.

**kuḻalaḻagar vāyaḻagar kaṇṇaḻagar koppūḻil eḻukamalap pūvaḻagar** – he is the nectar imbibed through the eyes, more distinguished than the nectar which is consumed with mouth. Considering the distinction in the meanings, the verse should be altered as “*kaṇṇaḻagar kuḻalaḻagar vāyaḻagar koppūḻil eḻukamalap pūvaḻagar*”. Losing out to the beauty of his eyes, just like losing out to an arrow, she tried to shift her gaze to his divine locks. The wave from the divine locks pushed her to the shore of his divine mouth. Losing out to the divine mouth, she tried to fall at his feet. In between, his divine navel caught hold of her, viz. the beauty of lotus from his divine navel caught her attention, stealing her eyes. Since she is consumed by love towards him, she engages with his divine form, rather than his divine qualities.

**emmānār** – one who made me subservient to him, manifesting his beauty. Just as the ātmā lost out to the beauty of emperumān’s divine eyes, as said in *jitantē* ślōkam 1 “*jitantē puṇḍarīkākśa namastē viśvabhāvana*: l *namastē’stu hriśīkēśa mahāpuruśa pūrvaja* ll” (Oh one with reddish lotus-like eyes! (this ātmā) has been won over by you; (this is) not mine; this is yours. Oh, one who created all the worlds! Oh, one who controls the senses! Oh, one who is the most distinguished in giving! (this ātmā) should be only for you, avoiding being for me), her words too reflect her losing out to his divine form, instead of to the natural *śēśitvam* (quality of being the lord).

**ennudaiya kaḻal vaḷaiyait tamum kaḻal vaḷaiyē ākkinarē** – Earlier, reflecting that he had stolen my beauty, I had addressed my mothers as “*eḻiludaiya ammanimīr*”. In reality, it does not appear that there was any fault on his part. We could blame him only if there is any shortcoming in his affection. What blame could we place on him when he is of the same mind as we are? Even if he has separated, we had put on firm bangles which will not separate. However, he made that *kaḻalvaḷai*, which came as an arbitrary name, to be *kaḻanṟoḻigiṟa vaḷai* (bangle which will slip out and disappear), thus making it as a causative name. How can we blame him for this? By including *um* \[also\] to *tām* \[as in *tāmum*, meaning he too\]\] she conveys the meaning that he is of the same mind as she is. This is a nice way of hiding one’s hatred and saying it in the exact opposite manner. By using the term *ennudaiya kaḻal vaḷai*, her displeasure at his bangle \[conch\] not slipping out is shown. If his bangle had slipped out, since she would have known his anguish, her bangle would have stayed put. If she knew that he also has distress, her bangle would stay put and not slip out. Assuming that some people ask her “Why do you say that you alone have lost your bangle. Has not everyone who has separated from him not lost her bangles?” she says “Only I lost my bangles. Is there anyone who has lost her bangles?”

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 11.3. Pongodam sulnda

*pongōdam sūḻnda buvaniyum viṇṇulagumangādum sōrāmē āḷginṟa emperumānsengōludaiya tiruvarangac celvanārengōlvaḷaiyāl idar tīrvar āgādē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pongu – having waves agitating; ōdam – with ocean; sūḻnda – surrounded by; buvaniyum – this earth; viṇ ulagum – and parama padam; angu ādum sōrāmē – without any shortcoming occurring there; āḷginṟa – one who is the controller; em perumān – as my svāmy (lord); sengōl udaiya – capable of ruling with his sceptre; tiru arangam selvanār – śrīmān (one who has wealth) who is reclining in the temple; en – my; kōl vaḷaiyāl – with bangle on my hand; idar tīrvar āgādē – will he not overcome his shortcomings?

**Перевод:**

**emperumān, who is śrīmān, is capable of ruling with his sceptre, both this world, which is surrounded by ocean with agitating waves and parama padam, without any shortcoming. Will he not overcome his shortcomings with the bangle on my hand?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – when some people ask her “Was it not due to desire towards you that he took bangle from your hand” she says, with disgust, in this pāsuram “Is there any deficiency to his wealth, to fulfil which, he had to take away my bangle? This act is only to torment me”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pongōdam sūḻnda buvaniyum** – earth which is surrounded on all sides by ocean which has agitating waves. Though ocean is throwing out waves against earth, it will not cross the shore. It is hinted that though he is residing on earth \[in śrīrangam\] which does not cross limits \[by not going beyond the shore\], emperumān is carrying out activities crossing limits.

**viṇṇūlagum** – parama padam too, but which does not have a limit like earth. It is hinted here that even though it is a place without any limit, he does not carry out any action which crosses the limit, there, unlike the way that he does with me.

**angu ādum sōrāmē āḷginṟa** – ruling, without any shortcoming in either of the two vibhūtis (leelā vibhūti and nitya vibhūti). When leelā vibhūti is about to be destroyed, he keeps it within his stomach and protects it. During the time of creation, he establishes it with gross form. By giving nityas and muktas the opportunity to enjoy him constantly, he gives them sustenance and protects nitya vibhūti.

**emperumān** – keeping me as the third vibhūti, outside his ambit of protection, he rules by constantly tormenting me. He made leelā vibhūti such that people will be happy, constantly engaging with pursuits such as *śabdam* \[sound\] etc. He made nitya vibhūti such that they will be happy, constantly enjoying him. He made me such that I will be in the middle, always suffering, without joining with either of the other two.

**sengōludaiya tiruvarangac celvanār** – not content with making everyone follow his orders which he issued through his *sankalpam* (vow) from parama padam, he came to kōyil (śrīrangam) and reclined. Is periya perumāḷ ruling over both vibhūtis?

**sengōludaiya** – the meaning hinted here is that it is periya perumāḷ who is ruling, but through a servitor, sceptre.

**en kōl vaḷaiyāl idar tīrvar āgādē** – Is his shortcoming such that he would not be able to sustain himself without this bangle and that he would be able to overcome that shortcoming only by taking that bangle?  Has this act not been carried out to torment me? In the previous pāsuram, he took away my strong bangle. In this, he has taken my bangle on the hand, too.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 11.4. Maccani mada

*maccaṇi māda madiḷ arangar vāmananārpaccaip pasundevar tām paṇḍu nīrēṟṟapiccaik kuṟaiyāgi ennudaiya peyvaḷai mēliccai udaiyarēl itteruvē pōdārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

maccu – with upper storeys; aṇi – decorated; mādam – mansions; madiḷ – and having compound walls; arangar – mercifully residing in tiruvarangam; vāmananār – (earlier) incarnated mercifully as vāmanan \[divine, dwarf form\]; paccaip pasundēvar – periya perumāḷ, who is a very beautiful lord; tām – he; paṇḍu – earlier (with mahābali); nīr ēṟṟa piccai kuṟai āgi – with some shortcoming in the alms which he had taken through water; ennudaiya – my; pey vaḷai mēl – on the bangle laid (on the hand); iccai udaiyarēl – if he had a desire; itteruvē – through this street; pōdārē – will he not mercifully come?

**Перевод:**

**periya perumāḷ, who is very beautiful, has mercifully taken residence in tiruvarangam which has mansions which have been decorated with upper storeys and compound walls. He had earlier incarnated as vāmanan and taken water on his hand, as a symbol of taking alms from mahābali. If he had some shortcoming due to that, to overcome which he desired to take the bangle which had been laid on my hand, would he not mercifully go through this street?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – When people in proximity told her “While other women are also wearing bangles, isn’t the reason for his taking your bangles due to his desire towards you? Instead of feeling happy, should you feel sad?” She responds, saying “That is alright. It doesn’t appear that the desire towards my bangles has been shown towards me. I was thinking that since it is a bangle which he desired, I could sustain myself by wearing it. However, if he thought that he could not sustain himself without having that bangle which has connection with me, and he takes it desirously from me, should he not walk once through this street in front of me such that I could sustain myself?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maccaṇi māda madiḷ arangar vāmananār** – śrīrangam has mansions which are decorated with upper storeys and fortified with compound walls such that enemies could not enter. emperumān is mercifully reclining inside the big temple, in such śrīrangam, with the vow that he would not leave the place until he attained his followers, who are his possession, just like he had got during his incarnation as vāmanan.

**maccaṇi māda madiḷ arangar vāmananār** – he had only learnt about how to build mansions and compound walls but had not learnt about how to move with me, his loving consort.

**vāmananār** – since it was a wealth attained by a small person, he did not know as to what to do.

**vāmananār** – since she would have learnt from her father periyāḻvār as said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-9-6 “*cattiramēndit taniyoru māṇiyāy uttaravēdiyil ninṟa oruvanai, kattiriyar kāṇak kāṇi muṟṟum koṇḍa pattirākāran*” (mahābali was unmatched in his magnanimity. Even as kings under him were watching, emperumān, with a distinguished form as a unique bachelor, with an umbrella, took the entire world as his own), would she not have desired the bachelor vāmanan?

**paccaip pasundēvar** – referring to a beautiful, greenish complexion, the term *paccaip pasundēvar* refers to ‘a very beautiful lord’. His beautiful form is such that even though he has not called me and enjoyed, his form cannot be wished away. Just as bharata talks of śrī rāma’s form, as said in śri rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 83-8 “*mēghaśyāmam mahābāhum stirasatvam drudavrutam* l *kadā drakśyāmahē rāmam jagata*: *śōkanāśanam* ll” (When will we see śrī rāmapirān who is dark like cloud, who has long hands, who has stable strength, who has firm vow and who removes the sorrows in the world?) did he not say “The anger which came about due to having been born to kaikēyi will get removed only if I wake up to the beautiful form of śrī rāma”? Instead of considering this meaning, just as we consider *paccaip pasum pon* as pure gold without any adulteration, *paccaip pasundēvar* could be taken as referring to having the quality of bhagavān without any śortcoing. Did not nammāḻvār say that emperumān, even during the time of incarnation, incarnates with auspicious qualities such as *paratvam* (superiority) etc, without any deficiency, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-5-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thiruvaimozhi-3-5-5-sadhu-sanaththai/) “*ādiyanjōdi uruvai angu vaittu ingup piṟanda*” (one who was born here with the same divine, primordial, spiritual form having divine splendour)?

**dēvar** – one who has auspicious qualities such as radiance etc, typical of emperumān

**tām paṇḍu nīrēṟṟa** – just as it has been mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-8-9-kolvan-nan/) “*koḷvan nān māvali mūvadi tā*” (Oh, mahābali! I will accept three steps of land; give) unable to sustain himself unless he retrieved the world which belonged to him, getting the land through taking water from mahābali earlier, as mentioned by poygai āḻvār in [mudal tiruvandādi 79](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/mudhal-thiruvandhadhi-79/) “*oṇḍārai nīrangai tōya nimirttilaiyē*” (did you not grow immediately after the radiant stream of water fell on your beautiful hands and washed your divine feet?)

**piccaik kuṟaiyāgi …. udaiyarēl** – he had taken earth as alms from mahābali and felt deficient, thinking “I had to take earth as alms from someone who thought proudly that it was his”. If he desires to take as alms from my hand, when I do not have such pride …

**piccaik kuṟaiyāgi ennudaiya peyvaḷaimēl iccai udaiyarēl** – even though he had lot of eminence, he did not have the eminence that he had taken as alms *for the sake of* his followers. To overcome that shortcoming, he had taken three steps of land as alms from mahābali. In a similar vein, if he had the deficiency that he had not taken alms *from* his followers, he could overcome that deficiency by taking my bangle as alms. This is the meaning hinted through this verse.

**itteruvē pōdārē** – just as it has been mentioned in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 4-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/07/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-4-5/) “*nādi nanderuvin naduvē vandittu*” (searching \[for our house\] and coming to the centre of the street where we reside), should he not mercifully walk in our street, in order to take alms from me? Should he not manifest the gait, which he had shown in the arena of the monster \[mahābali\] where the ritual was conducted, on my street? She thinks that she could sustain herself, if she sees the beauty of his divine form, if he walks in her street.

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 11.5. Pollak kuraluruvay

*pollāk kuṟaḷuruvāyp poṟkaiyilnīrēṟṟuellā ulagum aḷandu koṇḍa emperumānnallārgaḷ vāḻum naḷir aranga nāgaṇaiyānillādōm kaipporuḷum eyduvān ottuḷanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pollā kuṟaḷ uru āy – as distinguished form of vāmana; pon kaiyil – with beautiful hand; nīr ēṟṟu – taking alms; ellā ulagum – all the worlds; aḷandu koṇḍa – making it as his own; emperumān – as svāmy (lord); nallārgaḷ vāḻum – eminent people, who are residing; nāga aṇaiyān – periya perumāḷ who has tiruvanantāḻvān as his mattress; illādōm – one without any means, my; kaipporuḷum – entity which is the means (my physical form); eyduvān ottu uḷan – appeared as if he were going to steal

**Перевод:**

**My svāmy, with a distinguished form as vāmana, took alms in his beautiful hand, by which he made all the worlds as his own. He is reclining on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān, at śrīrangam, where eminent people are residing. While we are without any means with us, it appeared that emperumān was going to steal our physical form.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that apart from her bangle, it appeared that he wanted to steal her physical form too.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pollāk kuṟaḷ uruvāy** – We could construe this as – āndāḷ saying *pollā* as an adjective for his form of vāmana, in order to ward off evil spirits. Alternatively, we could consider this as *pollākkuṟaḷuru* – a form which is not at all seen in the world. Since *aḻagiya kuṟaḷ uru* could make one think of other beautiful entities in the world, she is saying *pollāk kuṟaḷ uru*.

**kuṟaḷ uruvāy** – Just as a huge quantity of milk is kept on heating in order to get a sweet concentrate which is very low in volume, the huge form of *trivikrama* is condensed to a short, but very sweet, form of *vāmana* such that it would be enjoyable to those who experience him.

**poṟkaiyil nīrēṟṟu** – taking water on his beautiful hand \[symbolic of accepting the alms\]. While in the world, people give alms to those who hold pots made of clay, mahābali had the fortune of giving alms to one who had golden pot!

**ellā ulagum aḷandu koṇḍa** – The reason for accepting water on his golden hand, while he had the divine form of vāmana, is to take \[three\] steps to steal his land. īsn’t he the type who, when he accepts something from someone, will push the person to a deep place in the nether world! Did not periyāḻvār too say, as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-9-7 “*iṟaippoḻudil pādāḷam kalavirukkai koduttuganda emmān*” (in a moment, placing \[mahābali\] in the nether world and feeling happy \[that he had fulfilled the request of indra\])? Is he not one who feels happy in pushing those who give him alms, into deep crevices?

**emperumān** – through that act, he made me exist only for him and for none else. This is the divine act of his, in which all the āḻvārs engage deeply, as mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 9-4-2 “*mūvadi maṇ koṇḍu aḷanda mannan saridaikkē mālāgip pon payandēn*” (I lost my complexion, due to my bewilderment, after hearing the narrative about the lord of all who measured three steps of land). The meaning hinted is that instead of being cautious with someone who pushes those who give him alms into deep crevices, I reached the stage of losing whatever little I had on my hand, after engaging with that act.

**nallārgaḷ vāḻum naḷir arangam** – this is the divine abode inhabited by those who do not expect any benefit other than himself. Did not periyāḻvār too say in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-8-2 “*maṟaip perundī vaḷarttiruppār varuvirundai aḷittiruppār siṟappudaiya maṟaiyavar vāḻ tiruvarangam enbaduvē*” (tiruvarangam is the divine abode where those who nurture rituals as ordained in the vēdams and who provide distinguished hospitality to śrīvaiṣṇavas reside)?

**vāḻum** – merely living in that divine abode is getting uplifted. Did not nācciyār too say in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/09/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-8-5/) “*vēngadattaip padiyāga vāḻvīrgāḷ*” (those who live considering tiruvēngadam as your dwelling place)?

**naḷir arangam** – cool tiruvarangam. The term *naḷir* would refer to coolness. It removes the ennui of those who reside in that place. It is the divine abode where three tāpams (distress or anguish) of *ādyātmikam* (distress brought on by oneself), *ādibhaudikam* (distress brought on by the five elements) and *ādidaivikam* (distress brought on due to action of god) do not affect one.

**nāgaṇaiyān** – one who is reclining on tiruvanantāḻvān. How he is fitting on the mattress, leaving me aside, his consort!

**illādōm kaipporuḷum** – the material with me, who does not have the fortune to step on to that mattress. ōr, the material with me who does not have the material which he desires. ōr, one who does not have any one to assist other than oneself.

**kaipporuḷum** – the trivial materials such as clay lid which those who seek alms have. She thinks of her physical form and entities which are together with it.

**eyduvān ottuḷanē** – he appeared to steal those materials.

A few words from another explanation given by the commentator, periyavāccān piḷḷai:

**naḷir arangam** – īsn’t the one who is residing in this cool, divine abode, carrying out the deed which is done by those who kill women?

**nallārgaḷ vāḻum arangam** – it is the place where all the fortunate people, who are living in various places come and remain forever. īsn’t it the abode where all the āḻvārs gather, as mentioned in [tiruccanda viruttam 52](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2023/11/thiruchchandha-viruththam-52/) “*aṟṟapaṟṟar suṟṟi vāḻum andaṇīr arangam*” (tiruvarangam is the huge temple, having beautiful, cool vaterbodies, where ascetics, who have renounced everything, think of living forever)?

**nallārgaḷ** – instead of carrying out attempts on one’s own to attain emperumān, this is the divine abode inhabited by people who enjoy the ultimate benefit. Since the supreme entity, who has to be attained after reaching paramapadam, resides in that place forever, easily accessible to all, for those who reside in that abode, *bhagavat anubhavam* (enjoying emperumān) will be a regular pastime. For those who desire to reside in that divine place, is there anything other than *bhagavat anubhavam*? It is said that those who live there will be trembling “What will we do if have to lose this experience and are asked to go to parama padam?” Did not toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār, who knew none other than periya perumāḷ, mercifully say in [tirumālai 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thirumalai-2-part-1/) “*iccuvai tavira yān pōy indira lōgam āḷum ahcuvai peṟinum vēṇḍēn arangamā nagaruḷānē*” (Oh, emperumān, who is permanently residing in śrīrangam! Even if I were given the opportunity to rule over śrīvaikuṇṭam, leaving the experience which I get here, I would not do that)?

When \[parāśara\] bhattar was suffering due to discomfort in his back, periya perumāḷ, after honouring him with temple respects, asked him “Did you fear?” bhattar responded saying “Oh emperumān! There is no eminence which you have not mercifully done for me. Even when I am here, you have assured me that you will grant me parama padam. There is no shortcoming for me in that. If I have to lose the fortune of seeing your cool, divine face and the scar of your divine *tirumaṇ kāppu* (divine mark on the forehead), will I not fear?”

Among the sons of āḷavandār was sottai nambi. Since he used harsh words against tirukkōśtiyūr nambi, one of āḷavandār’s main disciples, āḷavandār told him “Do not come near me. Go far away”. He went to the capital of the cōḻa king and was engaged in regal work, in *laukikam* \[leading life in samsāram, carrying out deeds typical of samsāris\]. In his final days, some people, desiring to check “Does he have firm belief in śrī vaiṣṇavam, though he has gone into *laukikam*?” asked him “What are you thinking of?” He said “Since I have connection with āḷavandār’s divine feet, I will certainly get paramapadam. When I go there, if the divine face of śrīvaikuṇṭanātan \[the lord of śrīvaikuṇṭam\] is not as cool as the divine face of *namperumāḷ* (utsavar at śrīrangam temple), I will break the wall of universe and return to śrīrangam itself”. He also said “The only deficiency in that is that I will be going against the saying of vēdam” referring to cāndōjña upaniśat 1-15-1 “*sa ghalvēvam vartayan yāvadāyuśam brahmalōkamabhi sampadyatē* l *na ca punarāvartatē*! n*a ca punarāvartatē*! ll” (that mumukśu \[one who is desirous of attaining mōkśam\], lives like this until his lifetime is completed; once his lifetime is over, he attains the abode of brahmam. He does not return here ever).

**naḷir arangam** – cool arangam. To get rid of the distress of *ādyātmikam, ādibhaudikam* and *ādidaivikam*, one need not go to paramapadam. This tiruvarangam alone is sufficient for that      .

**nāgaṇaiyān** – when enjoying the supreme entity who is attained, instead of enjoying him in the sitting posture, like paramapadanātan’s, just as it has been said in [tirumālai 23](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thirumalai-23/) “*kidandadōr kidakkai*” (after seeing and enjoying the unparalleled lying posture), one can enjoy the reclining posture which is more beautiful than the sitting posture. Such a lying posture.

**illādōm kaipporuḷum** – the material which is with us, who do not have anything with us. Since we and our materials are his, there is nothing that we have. Hence, whatever he takes, will be his possession only. When such is the case, why does he engage in an activity as if he is usurping our materials? When we have nothing with us, could we say *kaipporuḷ* (material in our hand)? āṇḍāḷ says that since emperumān desires me and my possessions thinking that they are his, in his \[emperumān’s\] thinking it is “*kaipporuḷ*”. Alternatively, for the term *illādōm*, the meaning could be construed as “Not having any refuge other than himself”. The term *illādōm kaipporuḷ* would refer to the physical form, which is tacking along with us. That is all, which remains.

**eyduvān ottuḷanē** – Thinking that the physical body is the ultimate benefit, there are people who strive hard to affirm it. emperumān thinks that my \[āṇḍāḷ’s\] physical form is his ultimate benefit and strives to attain that, thinking that he has got something which he does not have.

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 11.6. Kaipporulgal munname

*kaipporuḷgaḷ munnamē kaikkoṇḍār kāviri nīrseyppuraḷavōdum tiruvarangac celvanārepporutkum ninṟārkkum eydādu nānmaṟaiyinsoṟporuḷāy ninṟār en meypporuḷum koṇḍārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāviri nīr – water from the river kāviri; sey puraḷa ōdum – having plenty of water resources such that water flows through all the agricultural fields, in abundance; tiru arangam – one who has mercifully taken residence in tiruvarangam; selvanār – as śrīmān (having lot of wealth); epporutkum ninṟu – remaining as simplicity personified for even the lowliest entities; ārkkum eydādu – not accessible even for the most eminent; nāl maṟaiyin sol poruḷ āy ninṟar – periya perumāḷ who is the meaning for all the words mentioned in the four vēdas \[sacred texts\]; munnamē – already; kaipporuḷgaḷ – all the materials in the hand; kaikkoṇḍār – having stolen (now); en meypporuḷum – the entity of my physical form too; koṇḍār – he stole

**Перевод:**

**tiruvarangam has abundance of water resources such that the river kāviri flows through all the agricultural fields. periya perumāḷ, who is the meaning for all the words mentioned in the four vēdas, and who is a śrīmān, resides mercifully in śrīrangam. He is simplicity personified, for the lowliest of entities, while he is inaccessible for even the most eminent. He has already stolen all the materials on my hand; now he has stolen my physical form too.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – In the previous pāsuram, she said that it appeared that emperumān was going to steal her physical form. In this pāsuram, she says that he did that.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kaipporuḷgaḷ munnamē kaikkoṇḍār** – he has already stolen my belongings such as bangle, complexion etc. Who is the one who had stolen like that?

**kāviri nīr seyppuraḷa ōdum tiruvarangac celvanār** – the wealthy person who is residing in tiruvarangam, which has plenty of water resources such that river kāviri flows through all the fields, abundantly. The abode is so eminent that even water, an *acētanam* (entity without knowledge) is protecting everything which is to be protected. The abode is like that; he is *parama cētanar* (one who is omniscient). What is the harmony that exists between his living in that abode and stealing all my belongings?

**epporutkum ninṟu** – remaining as a simplistic entity for śabari, a hunter tribal lady, for sugrīva, a monkey and for yaśōdā, a herd-woman.

**ārkkum eydādu** – difficult to access for those who, even if they are highly knowledgeable, try to attain him with their efforts. Through these two terms \[*epporutkum ninṟu* and *ārkkum eydādu*\] āṇḍāḷ mercifully conveys the same meaning which nammāḻvār mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-4-yarumor-nilaimaiyan/) “*ārumōr nilaimaiyan ena aṟivariya emperumān ārumōr nilaimaiyan ena aṟiveḷiya emperumān*” (emperumān is difficult to be estimated by the most knowledgeable people if they are not favourable to him and is easy to be estimated by those who, even if they are ignorant, are his followers).

**nānmaṟaiyin sol poruḷāy ninṟār** – he remained as the meaning for all the words mentioned in the four vēdas. Did not emperumān himself mercifully say in [bhagavat gītā 15-15](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/15-15/) “*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya*:” (only I am known through all the vēdas)? Did not her father, periyāḻvār, too mercifully say in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-3-11 “*vēdānta viḻuppouḷin mēlirunda viḷakku*” (emperumān is the most distinguished meanings mentioned in vēdāntams and is self-evident)?

**en meypporuḷum koṇḍārē** – he seized both my ātmā (soul) and my physical form.

There is another vyākhyānam \[for the verses seen so far\]

**kaipporuḷgaḷ munnamē koṇḍār** – *munnamē* – refers to the stage of infancy. Even when her \[āṇḍāḷ’s\] mother suckled her, vasn’t affection towards emperumān fed to her by periyāḻvār too! śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 40-5 says “*sruśtastvam vanavāsāya svanuraktas suhrujjanē* l *rāmē pramādam mā karśī*: *putra*! *bhrātari gaccati* ll” (Oh, child! You have been created only to live in forest. You are affectionate towards śrī rāma who has a kind heart towards you. When your elder brother walks in the forest, (engaging with his gait) do not be inattentive (in protecting him)). Just as sumitrā fed lakśmaṇa rāmabhakti \[devotion towards śrī rāma\] too along with her milk even when he was an infant, it appears that āṇḍāḷ, even as she was born, due to separation from emperumān, came without her bangles. Saying this is fully apt for her, and is not a hyperbole. As mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 18-28 “*bālyāt prabhruti susnigdō lakśmaṇō lakśmivardana*: l *rāmasya lōkarāmasya bhrātur jyēśtasya nityaśa*: ll” (lakśmaṇa, who was filled with the wealth of devotion, and who makes all the worlds happy, had deep affection towards his elder brother śrī rāma, right from his days of infancy), similar to iḷaiyaperumāḷ \[lakśmaṇa\], sustaining herself \[reference here is to āṇḍāḷ\] if she is united with emperumān and inability to do so in separation from him had started for her right from her birth!

**kāviri nīr seyppuraḷa ōdum tiruvarangac celvanār** – emperumān resides in tiruvarangam where kāviri river, which is the means to nurture crops, flows continuously into fields which are the readily attainable entities. This does not happen once in a while, but forever. For upāsaka (one who attempts to attain emperumān with his own efforts through *bhakti yōgam* \[path of devotion\]), the end benefit may not be attained, even after carrying out his efforts fully. It may be due to the strong *karmas* \[results of past deeds\]. Unlike this, for those in tiruvarangam, emperumān is readily available at hand. The verse *kāviri nīr seyppuraḷa ōdum* denotes the richness of water resource. The svāpadēśam \[distinguished meaning\] for this has been indicated here – When one holds on to emperumān as the *upāyam*, though his action of holding on is there, he considers that only emperumān could grant himself as the means. The *lakśaṇa* \[indication\] for *upāyam* (means) is one who will not stop until he gives the benefit. This is the reason for one to hold on to emperumān as the means. When one thinks of emperumān as the means, one cannot attain emperumān through that thought alone. The ability to grant means rests solely with emperumān; neither the person who wishes to attain emperumān nor his thought that emperumān is the means, could grant him the result. The flowing of river kāviri indicates all these.

**tiruvarangac celvanār** – one who has realised eminence through his relationship with that abode.

**epporutkum ninṟu** – easily accessible to sugrīva, a monkey, śabari, a tribal hunter lady and yaśōdā, a herd-woman. śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 4-19 says “*pitā yasya purā hyāsīt śaraṇyō darmavatsala*: l *tasya putra*: *śaraṇyaśca sugrīvam śaraṇam gata*: ll” (śrī rāma, who was the son of daśarata, who was deeply attached to righteousness, and who was apt to be taken refuge under, now took refuge under sugrīva). The previous ślōkam in the same kāṇḍam, 4-18 says “*ēśa datvā ca vittāni prāpya ca anuttamam yaśa*: l *lōkanāta*: *purā bhūtvā sugrīvam nātam iccati* ll” (This rāmapirān, who has given all the materials to all the people, who has attained eminent fame, who was the lord of the entire word earlier, desires sugrīva (today) as the lord). Apart from saying that he is a simplistic person, he also makes those who are nearby to say that he is so. In the latter ślōkam, the purpose of being the lord of the world, was to be obedient to sugrīva, by learning from the world. If one were to ask – aren’t *pāratantryam* (being dependent on someone) and *svātantryam* (being totally independent) contradictory to each other, it could be said that emperumān has sufficient *svātantryam* to get for himself the quality of *pāratantryam*.  A person, who has no leadership qualities, even by lowering himself, cannot be said to have displayed an eminent quality. Hence, the qualities of *pāratantryam* and *svātantryam*, manifested within limits, could not be termed as contradictory. In the last *pramāṇam*, it is seen that emperumān desired to have sugrīva as his lord. Anyone will desire a feature that (s)he does not have. Since emperumān does not have a lord for himself, he desired sugrīva as his lord. The verse *epporutkum ninṟu* indicates his ability to remain obedient to yaśōdāp pirātti such that she could tie him and beat him, and his granting permission to śabhari to carry out confidential kainkaryam to him, just like lakśmaṇa carried out.

One day, emperumānār \[rāmānujar\] was explaining the qualities of emperumān, starting from *paratvam* (eminence) down to *saulabhyam* (simplicity). Hearing this, embār, his disciple, was in tears. Looking at him with compassion, emperumānār said “When his simplicity was mentioned, people of the world withdrew, saying ’Is he that simple?’ since they did not have the fortune to go near him. Only you have the fortune to approach him thinking ’Such an eminent person could be this simple’!” It is pertinent to recall the pramāṇam as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 4-21 “*yasya prasādē satatam prasīdēyu*: *imā: prajā*:l *sa rāma*: *vānarēndrasya prasādam abhikānkśatē*” (That rāmapirān, who is such that when he is happy, the people of the entire world are always happy, seeks benefaction from sugrīva, who is the leader of monkeys). In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 rāma tells the assembly of his advisors “*mithra bhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana* l *dōśō yadyapi tasya syāt satāmētat agarhitam* ll” (I will not let down this vibhīśaṇa who has come as a friend. Even if he has any faults, let them be; for good people, accepting him is not to be reproached). In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-33, śrī rāma tells sugrīva “*sakrudēva prapannāya tavāsmīti ca yācatē* l *abhayam sarvabhūtēbhyō dadāmi ētat vratam mama* ll” (To the person who surrenders to me once and to the person who tells me ‘I am your servitor’ I offer protection from all entities alive. This is my solemn pledge). It would appear that śrī rāmapirān is in dissonance with sugrīva with regard to taking in vibhīśaṇa. \[parāśara\] bhattar would offer his explanation saying “Each person is engaged in protecting the one who has surrendered to him”. Hence there is no contradiction here. As just seen in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 4-19, śrī rāma has surrendered to sugrīva. Hence, sugrīva is taking pains to protect him from vibhīśaṇa. vibhīśaṇa had surrendered to śrī rāma, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 17-16 “*sō’ham paruśitastēna dāsavaccāvamānita*: l *tyaktvā putrāmśca dārāmśca rāghavam śaraṇam gata*: ll” (I, who am like that, have been rudely treated by rāvaṇa, being treated like a servant; I have left behind my wife and children, and am taking refuge under rāghava). Hene, śrī rāma is taking pains to protect vibhīśaṇa. sugrīva will not let down śrī rāma who has surrendered to him; śrī rāma will not let down vibhīśaṇa who has surrendered to him. sugrīva thinks that allowing vibhīśaṇa to surrender to śrī rāma will result in handing over śrī rāma to vibhīśaṇa. śrī rāma thinks that it is appropriate for him to accept vibhīśaṇa after making sugrīva to accept him. Hence, he is beseeching sugrīva “Protecting those who surrendered to us has been a long-standing vow for us. Due to affection towards us, please do not prevent us from carrying out that act. You should also accept this and fulfil our desire”.

\[we now return to the main pāsuram, after seeing the second explanation in the vyākhyānam for the first part\]

īsn’t there any eminence for him \[emperumān\] who has made himself as very easily approachable for even the lowly entities?

**ārkkum eydādu** – he has the eminence of being unapproachable for even people like brahmā et al, who have high levels of knowledge. Did not toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār too mercifully say in [tirumālai 44](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/12/thirumalai-44/) “*peṇṇulām sadaiyinānum piramanum unnaik kāṇbān eṇṇilā ūḻi ūḻi tavam seydār*” (even Sivan, who carries gangā on his matted hair and brahmā, carry out penance for long periods of time, in order to see you)?

**epporutkum ninṟu ārkkum eydādu** – this could also be construed as – pervading every entity as *antaryāmi* (in-dwelling soul) and due to that reason alone, not comprehensible to anyone

**nānmaṟaiyin poruḷāy ninṟār** – he remained as the meaning of vēdams. He remained as the meaning for every word mentioned in the four vēdams.

**en meypporuḷum koṇḍārē** – supreme being, who is known only by hearing through vēdam, came in front of our eyes and seized our physical form, which has an inseparable relationship with us and which we use for carrying out our tasks. Just as those, who enjoy him, leave aside his *svarūpam* (basic nature), *guṇam* (auspicious qualities) etc and enjoy his divine form, he too, in the case of his followers, leaves aside their *svarūpam*, *guṇam* etc and desires their physical form alone. Just as āṇḍāḷ desires his physical form, he too desires her physical form. It is pertinent to recall here the words of tirumālaiyāṇḍān ” Though we have heard that mūlaprakruti (primordial matter) and other *acētanam* (entities without knowledge) which derive from it are to be given up, there is no need to give up this physical form, which is derived from mūlaprakruti. Due to the thought that it helps in attaining the divine feet of āḷavandār, I desire this physical form”

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 11.7. Unnadu urangadu

*uṇṇādu uṟangā(du) oli kadalai ūdaṟuttuppeṇṇākkai āppuṇḍu tāmuṟṟa pēdellāmtiṇṇār madiḷ sūḻ tiruvarangac celvanāreṇṇādē tammudaiya nanmaigaḷē eṇṇuvarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiṇ Ar madiḷ sūḻ – being surrounded by walls which are strong (such that they cannot be destroyed even during deluge); tiru arangam – mercifully residing in the kōyil \[temple\] at śrīrangam; selvanār – perumāḷ who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; peṇ ākkai āppuṇḍu – desiring the physical form of a lady (sītāp pirātti); uṇṇādu – without eating; uṟangādu – without sleeping (feeling distressed); oli kadalai – ocean which is resounding (with the agitation of waves); ūdu aṟuttu – interrupting it (by building a bridge); tām uṟṟa – (like this) what he had endured; pēdu ellām – all his affliction; eṇṇadu – forgetting; tammudaiya – his; nanmaigaḷē – only \[his\] greatness; eṇṇuvar – he is thinking of

**Перевод:**

**perumāḷ, the divine consort of śrī mahālakśmi, is mercifully residing in śrī rangam, which is surrounded by strong walls. During his incarnation as śrī rāma, desiring the physical form of a lady, without eating or sleeping, he interrupted an uproarious ocean by building a bridge across it. Without thinking of all the affliction which he had undergone earlier, he is now thinking of his greatness alone.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that emperumān, forgetting all the suffering that he had when he was separated from sītāp pirātti, is now thinking of his eminence, tormenting her and is thinking of that as a great deed.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**uṇṇādu** – just as it had been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam, sundara kāṇḍam 36-41 & 42 “*na māmsam rāghavō bhunktē na cāpi madu sēvatē* l *naivadamśān na maśakān na kītān na sarīsrupān* l *rāghavō’panayēt gātrāt tvad gatēna antarātmanā* ll (Oh dēvi (divine consort of emperumān)! śrī rāma, who has a divine mind which is fully engaged with you, does not eat meat; does not take intoxicating materials; does not push away insects such as flies, mosquitoes, worms etc as well as snakes (which crawl over his divine form)), since sitāp pirātti, who used to feed him, knowing his taste, has separated from him, who will now feed him with such affection? Does he not have his younger brother? Since her glance was his food, as mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 8-10-1 “*māmalar mangai maṇanōkkam uṇḍān*” (one who has the joyful glance of periya pirāttiyār, who was born in a distinguished flower, as his sustenance), after separating from her, there was no food for him.

**uṟangādu** – just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 36-44 “*anidras satatam rāma*: *suptō’pi ca narōttama*: l *sītēti madurām vāṇīm vyāharan pratibhudyatē* ll” (śrī rāma (who has got separated from sītāp pirātti) is always without sleep. Even if he, who is the best among men, sleeps for a moment, he wakes up, always reciting the beautiful word *‘sītē’*) he couldn’t sleep either.

**uṇṇādu uṟangādu** – without eating and sleeping, and just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 4-19 “*pitā yasya purā hyāsīt śaraṇyō darmavatsala*: *tasya putra*: *śaraṇyaśca sugrīvam śaraṇam gata*: ll” (śrī rāma, son of daśarata, who was deeply attached to righteousness, and being apt to be taken refuge under, now took refuge under sugrīva) falling at the feet of a monkey

**oli kadalai ūdaṟūttu** – building a bridge through an ocean which was uproarious due to agitating waves. In other words, slicing it into two. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-30 “*ēvamuktastu darmajña*: *pratyuvāca vibhīśaṇa*: l *samudram rāghavō rājā śaraṇam gantumarhati* ll” (vibhīśaṇa, who knows righteousness, said ‘rāghava, the king, should surrender to the ocean’), becoming a disciple of vibhīśaṇa, who was born in the clan of demons, surrendered to the ocean, as mentioned by him. Thinking of all these afflictions…

**peṇṇākkai āppuṇḍu** – being enslaved by the divine physical form of pirātti, who was like a creeper. The term *ākkai* would refer both to physical form and to creeper. Thus, perumāḷ, who was like a *kaṟpaga* (wish-fulfilling) tree, was bound by a lady, who was also like a *kaṟpaga* creeper.

**tām uṟṟa pēdellām eṇṇādē** – without thinking of all the afflictions which he had suffered from.

**tiṇṇār madiḷ sūḻ tiruvarangac celvanār** – one who has the wealth of residing in tiruvarangam, which is surrounded by strong and tall walls.

**pēdellām eṇṇādē** – śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 1-17 says “*sa ca sarvaguṇōpēta*: *kausalyā nanda vardana*: l *samudra iva gāmbhīryē dairyēṇa himavāniva* ll” (that rāma, who nurtured bliss for kausalyā, was complete with all the auspicious qualities. He was like an ocean in his quality of *gāmbhīryam* (unfathomable). He was like himavān (mount himālaya) in courage). One who had immovable strength like himālaya, lost his courage; without thinking about that…

**tammudaiya nanmaigaḷē eṇṇūvarē** – looking at my state, thinking “Those who have separated from us are indulging in activities such as observing rituals, falling at the feet of a deity, *kāman* \[1<sup>st</sup> tirumoḻi in this prabandam\], engaging with *kūdal* \[4<sup>th</sup> tirumoḻi\], sending messengers \[8<sup>th</sup> tirumoḻi\] etc, and are suffering”, he is only focussed on his greatness. She says that forgetting how he had suffered due to his engagement with sītāp pirātti, because of his arrogance of reclining in śrīrangam, as the leader of both nitya vibhūti and leelā vibhūti, he is looking at my suffering due to separation from him, as his eminence.

A few words from another *vyākhyānam*:

**uṇṇādu** – manifesting to me his state of being without food and sleep, having separated from a lady, he bewildered me. Making me to go without food and sleep due to separation from him, is his fault and not mine.

**ākkaiyāppuṇḍu** – thinking of pirātti’s divine physical form and getting tied down to it, not engaging with the basic nature of the ātmā, inside that physical form, or its qualities.

**tāmuṟṟa** – when he, the disciple of sage vasiśta, was overcome by lust and suffered due to that, how much would I, born as an ignorant girl, have suffered? Did he not confide in his younger brother, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-115 “*danyā lakśmaṇa sēvantē pampōvana mārutam*” (only the fortunate could enjoy the breeze which is coming from the gardens of river pampā), how he suffered due to separation from his beloved?

**eṇṇādē** – Could he forget his earlier separation due to his present arrogance in reclining in śrīrangam, with its mansions and compound walls? …… Does he have arrogance?

**tiruvarangac celvanār** – his arrogance has changed him, says āṇḍāḷ.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 11.8. Pasi turttu

*pāsi tūrttuk kidanda pārmagatkup paṇḍorunāḷmāsudambil nīrvārā mānamilāp panṟiyāmtēsudaiya dēvar tiruvarangac celvanārpēsiyiruppanagaḷ pērkkavum pērāvē*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇḍu oru nāḷ – at an earlier time; pāsi tūrttuk kidanda – with algae spread; pār magatku – for śrī bhūmip pirātti; māsu udambil nīr vārā mānam ilāp panṟi Am – having a form of boar, without any shame, with water dripping from the form which has gathered dirt; tēsu udaiya dēvar – emperumān, who has splendour; tiruvarangac celvanār – śrīranganātan; pēsi iruppanagaḷ – the words which he had spoken (earlier); pērkkavum pērā – even if one tries to remove them (from one’s heart) they cannot be removed

**Перевод:**

**At an earlier point in time, emperumān, for the sake of śrī bhūmip pirātti, who had algae all over her physical form, had taken the form of a boar, without any shame, with water dripping from his physical form which had gathered dirt. The words which he had spoken then, could not be removed from one’s heart, even if one tries to remove them.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – when people in her proximity asked her “Could you feel dejected and blame emperumān saying \[as in the previous pāsuram\] ‘tāmuṟṟa pēdellām eṇṇādē tammudaiya nanmaigaḷē eṇṇuvarē’? Should you not forget what you have lost and try to sustain yourself by recalling his auspicious qualities?” she responds saying “I am also trying to forget him and sustain my life, but I am unable to forget him”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pāsi tūrttuk kidanda pārmagatku** – earth was under deluge for a very long time, due to which it had algae spread all over it. Was it not on *pār* (world) that algae had spread? Could we say that it was on *pārmagatku* (lady of the world)? Since bhūmip pirātti is the *abhimāni dēvatai* (the representative deity) for the world, and since she considers earth to be her physical form, the algae which had spread on the world could be extended to śrī bhūmip pirātti. Moreover, since it is her attribute, she is called as bhūmip pirātti. Those who are connoisseurs would not like to have a clean physical form, removing all the dirt from it, when their beloved wives have dirt on their physical forms. In the same way, emperumān took the form of a wild boar which will not detest water and mud, after seeing algae formed on bhūmip pirātti’s physical form.

**paṇḍoru nāḷ** – That was a day when I \[āṇḍāḷ} had embraced him. Is today too a day, when I have separated from him?

**māsudambil nīr vārā mānamilāp panṟiyām** – he took a form which was inferior to the *aḻukkudambu* (deficient body) referred to by *mumukśus* (those who are desirous of attaining mōkśam), inferior to the body of samsāris who consider the body as ātmā and nurture the body by keeping it clean, considering dirt to be sweet, with dirty water dripping from the physical form and not feeling ashamed that the physical form is dirty.

**mānamilā** – kriṣṇa tells the covherd boys when they ask him as to who he was, when he lifted the gōvardana mountain, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-12 “*nāham dēvō na gandarvō na yakśō na ca dānava*: l *aham vō bāndavō jātō naitaccintyamitō’nyatā* ll” (I am not a *dēvan* or *gandarvan* or *yakśan* \[these are all different forms of celestial entities\] or *asuran* \[monster\]. I have been born as your relative; my birth is not to be thought of (by you) as anything different from this). In śrī rāmāyaṇam, śrī rāma identified himself as a human being and as the son of emperor daśarata, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-11 “*abravīt tridaśaśrēśtān rāmō darmabhrutām vara*: l *ātmānam mānuśam manyē rāmam daśaratātmajam* ll” (śrī rāma, the most supreme among noble people, addressing the distinguished celestial entities said “I consider myself as rāma, a human entity, the son of daśarata”). emperumān went below the level of human entities, with change in type of food required to sustain the physical form. He had to consume *kōraikkiḻangu* (sedge tuber, a type of tuber which is the food for boar) and took over the qualities of a wild boar. He syncronised himself with all these qualities of a wild boar. He lowered himself such that he had to eat *kōraikkiḻangu* which is offered as *muttakkāsu* for varāhap perumān \[emperumān in the form of wild boar\].

When *mārīca* \[demon rāvaṇa’s uncle\] took the form of an illusory deer \[to help rāvaṇa in abducting sītāp pirātti\], other deer \[in the forest\] came near that illusory deer and ran in fear, after knowing that it was not one of their species. Unlike that, when emperumān incarnated as wild boar, other wild boars came near it, and affirmed that it was one of their species. Thus, *māmamilā* is to be construed as one without any shame.

**panṟiyām tēsudaiya dēvar** – the splendour which he manifested, having lowered himself for the sake of his followers. Just as it has been mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-4-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/01/thiruvaimozhi-6-4-7-manap-parippodu/) “*tanakku vēṇḍurukkoṇḍu”* (accepting the dear forms which he desires), he takes the divine forms as per his desire to protect his possession, instead of taking a form as per *karma* (past deeds).

**tiruvarangac celvanār** – in order to eliminate our sorrow that we had not been able to carry out servitude during his incarnation as *varāha* (wild boar), he has mercifully come to the temple and reclined so that he could accept faultless servitude from us.

**selvanār pēsiyiruppanagaḷ** – one who is wealthy will not fear to speak up his mind. Without thinking about how his words would affect others, those who are wealthy are wont to speak up their minds. In the same manner, when emperumān had lifted śrī bhūmip pirātti from deluge, he had spoken to her many words, manifesting his affection towards her. He has mercifully given the varāha carama ślōkam, śrī rāma carama ślōkam and kriṣṇa carama ślōkam, regarding protection of his followers. The opinion here is that āṇḍāḷ is suffering, engaging with him after believing his carama ślōkam. Alternatively, we could construe this to mean that he had spoken to her when they were united, saying “I will not separate from you; if I separate from you, I will not be able to sustain myself”.

**pērkkavum pērāvē** – even if I try to somehow carry on, forgetting these words, I am unable to remove them from my heart. I am feeling distressed, by thinking about these words, repeatedly. Even if he forgets us without thinking about the suffering that he had to endure in separation from sītāp pirātti, and remembering only his greatness, just as it has been said in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-3-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thiruvaimozhi-5-3-5-kadiyan-kodiyan/) “*kodiya en nenjam avan enṟē kidakkum*” (my heart will remain, considering he is the only refuge), I am unable to sustain myself, forgetting him.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
