## 11.9. Kannalam kodittu

*kaṇṇālam kōdittu kanni tannaik kaippidippāntiṇṇārndirunda sisupālan tēsaḻinduaṇṇāndirukkavē āngavaḷaik kaippidittapeṇṇāḷan pēṇum ūr pērum arangamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇālam kōdittu – carrying out nicely, all the arrangements for marriage; kanni tannai – rukmiṇip pirātti, the bride; kaippidippān – in order to marry her; tiṇ ārndu irunda – thinking firmly, without an iota of doubt; sisupāḷan – śiśupālan, his; tēsu aḻindu – losing his radiance; aṇṇāndirukkavē āngu – during the time when he \[śiśupālan\] was looking at the sky; avaḷai – that rukmiṇip pirātti; kai piditta – one who mercifully held her hand; peṇṇāḷan – emperumān who is famous as one who helps all the women; pēṇum – mercifully taken residence, with desire; Ur – divine abode; arangamē – it is only tiruvarangam

**Перевод:**

**śiśupālan had carried out all the arrangements for marrying rukmiṇip pirātti and was very firm on this, without any trace of doubt. During the time that śiśupālan was looking at the sky, emperumān mercifully held that rukmiṇip pirātti’s divine hand. tiruvarangam is the divine abode where that emperumān, who is famously known to help all women, has mercifully taken residence, with desire.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – Through the first six pāsurams of this decad, āṇḍāḷ mentioned about how he had stolen her belongings. In the 7th pāsuram, she said that instead of recalling the distress that he had suffered in separation, desiring the divine form of sītāp pirātti, he was happy, looking at her distress. In the previous pāsuram, she said that though she tried to forget him and sustain herself, his auspicious qualities and divine activities would not let her forget him. In this pāsuram, she recalls the way emperumān had removed the hurdle of rukmiṇip pirātti and had helped her, sustaining herself, saying “īsn’t the help which he rendered to rukmiṇip pirātti equivalent to helping all the girls?” This is similar to all of us sustaining ourselves, believing the carama ślōkam which he told arjuna. The words mentioned by one, who is common to all, towards one person, is to be considered as applicable to all who are dependent on him.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kaṇṇālam kōdittu** – śiśupālan had spent all the wealth which he had accumulated over a long time, on the dinner prior to his marriage, for his friends. This was his arrangement for the marriage.

**kanni tannaik kaippidippān** – just as one takes efforts to smell a flower which no one else had smelt before, śiśupālan had taken lot of efforts in order to marry rukmiṇip pirātti.

**tiṇṇārndirunda sisupāḷan** – just as we have been stealing ātmā, which belongs to emperumān, thinking that the ātmā is ours, śiśupālan was of the firm mind that rukmiṇip pirātti, who is emperumān’s wealth, was his belonging. *tiṇṇārndu iruttal* – being firm.

**tēsaḻindu aṇṇāndirukkavē** – he lost the radiance which comes out of being modest; taking pride that he was the bridegroom, unlike matured crops which will look down, he was looking up at the sky. If we have to describe the state of śiśupālan, before kaṇṇan held the hand of rukmiṇip pirātti, we have to consider the meaning this way; when this term is combined with the next term *āngu avaḷaik kaippiditta*, with kaṇṇan having taken hold of rukmiṇip pirātti’s hand, the meaning will change.

**aṇṇāndirukkavē** – kaṇṇan held rukmiṇip pirātti’s hand such that śiśupālan, losing out all his radiance, having been insulted by kaṇṇan, feeling ashamed to look at anyone’s eyes, had to look at the sky.

**āngavaḷaik kaippiditta** – kaṇṇan performed only *pāṇigrahaṇam* (holding the hand of the bride), since śiśupālan had completed all the earlier rituals, prior to *pāṇigrahaṇam* and kaṇṇan did not want to repeat the same rituals.

**peṇṇāḷan** – was this act carried out only for the sake of rukmiṇip pirātti? The meaning hinted is that this was carried out for all who were born as women and who had lost out to emperumān for this act.

**pēṇum Ur** – the divine abode, where he desirously dwells.

**pērum arangamē** – just as he remains as the supporting pillar for all women, the abode where he desirously lives is also śrī rangam (the place where periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) blissfully dances).

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 11.10. Semmai udaiya

*semmai udaiya tiruvarangar tām paṇittameymmaip peruvārttai vittucittar kēttiruppartammai ugappārait tām ugappar ennum soltammidaiyē poyyānāl sādippār ār iniyē?*

**Пословный перевод:**

semmai udaiya – having honesty (of syncronising the faculties of mind, speech and action); tiru arangar – śrīranganātar; tām paṇitta – mercifully spoken through his mouth (earlier); meymmai – being truthful; peru – having eminence; vārttai – the words of carama ślōkam; vittucittar – (my divine father) periyāḻvār; kēttu – hearing from his guru (ācāryar, teacher); iruppar – (as mentioned in that ślōkam) would have been without any worry \[on his mind\]; tammai upgappārai tām ugappar ennum sol – the proverb “One would desire a person who desires him \[the first\] person”; tammidaiyē – with him; poy ānāl – if it becomes false; ini – beyond that; sādippār Ar – who could control (him) (there is none)

**Перевод:**

**śrīranganātar has the quality of honesty by which he syncronises the faculties of mind, speech and action. periyāḻvār, through his teacher, would have heard the truthful and most eminent carama ślōkam, mercifully revealed by emperumān himself, through his mouth, and would have conducted himself as per that \[carama ślōkam\], being free from any worry. If the proverb “One would desire a person who desires himself” becomes false, who could compel that emperumān?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – in the last pāsuram of this decad, recalling the firm belief which her father periyāḻvār had shown after hearing the meanings of carama ślōkam, she asks “Being born to that periyāḻvār as his daughter, and desiring emperumān, if emperumān goes against the grain of the saying ‘one should desire one who desires him/her’ and he decides to give up on me, who could compel him?” bringing the decad to its end.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**semmai udaiya tiruvarangar** – emperumān has the quality of honesty of having his mind, speech and action syncronised such that he could reform and take under his refuge *cētanas* (entities with knowledge) who go by the saying “*manasyanyat vacasanyat karmaṇyanyat durātmanām*” (for the wicked people, mind, speech and action would be different). s*emmai* – honesty. He has a mind which desires good bodings for everyone, as mentioned in [bhagavat gītā 5-29](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/5-29) “*bhōktāram yajña tapasām sarvalōka mahēśvaram* l *suhrudam sarvabhūtānām jñātvā mām śāntim ruccati*” (I consume rituals and penance. I am the controller of all the worlds. I am the friend for all the creations. One who knows me this way, attains peace). In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 rāma tells the assembly of his advisors “*mithra bhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana* l *dōśō yadyapi tasya syāt satāmētat agarhitam* ll” (I will not let down this vibhīśaṇa who has come as a friend. Even if he has any faults, let them be; for good people, accepting him is not to be reproached). Thus, one who says that he will not give up on those who had committed offences, putting those words into practice, mercifully reclined in the big temple in śrīrangam.

**semmaiyudaiya tiruvarangar tām paṇitta** – mercifully said by one who had syncronised his faculties of mind, speech and act.

**meymmaip peruvārttai** – it was a word which was truthful, distinguished and easy to be followed for all. That is the kriṣṇa carama ślōkam mercifully given on the divine chariot – ‘I am here to carry out your work. Do not worry about anything. Keep all your burden on me and remain without any worry’. If asked “This ślōkam was given exclusively for arjuna. What is the benefit for him \[periyāḻvār\]?” the response is – since emperumān is common for everyone, this is the carama ślōkam given earlier itself for everyone who thinks that (s)he is unable to carry out his task. Hence there is no issue in periyāḻvār hearing it and living as per the ślōkam.

**kēttiruppar** – he would have heard that from his ācāryan and remained aligning with that. After hearing that word, he would not have moved even a small bit of scrap as *upāyam* \[an enabling act for the means\]. He would carry out everything as the *prāpyam* \[the act itself is the benefit\].

**tammai ugappārait tām ugappar ennum sol** – there is a saying in the world: when asked “For whom are you a good person”, the response would be “I am good person for a good person”. This could be transformed with a negative connotation by asking “Is there a wicked person for a good person?” These colloquial usages are the meaning for the term *sol* here.

**tammidaiyē poyyānāl sādippār Ar iniyē?** – emperumān sets up the boundary for the world. If he himself starts discriminating, is there anyone who could order him “You should not do like that”? Even if there were someone who could order him, what could be done? Could he be tied down and asked to desire someone who desires him?

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the twelfth decad.

## 12.1. Marru irundirgal

*maṟṟirundīrgatku aṟiyalāgā mādavan enbadōr anbu tannaiuṟṟirundēnukku uraippadellām ūmaiyarōdu sevidar vārttaipeṟṟirundāḷai oḻiyavē pōyp pērttoru tāyil vaḷarnda nambimaṟporundā maṟkaḷam adainda maduraip puṟatu ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṟṟu irundīrgatku – you, who are different (from my state); aṟīyal āgā – not being known; mādavan enbadu ōr anbu tannai – regarding love towards mādavan; uṟṟirundēnukku – to me, who has attained \[that love\]; uraippadu ellām – everything, which you say; ūmaiyarōdu sevidar vārttai – it is a waste, similar to a conversation between a dumb person and a deaf person; peṟṟirundāḷai oḻiya pōy – leaving behind dēvaki, who had given birth, after enduring all the pain; pērttoru tāy il – in the house of yaśōdā, another mother; vaḷarnda – one who grew up; mal porundā mal kaḷam adainda – one who had reached the wrestling arena, ahead of the wrestlers; nambi – kaṇṇapirān’s; madurai – madurāpuri; puṟattu – in its proximity; ennai – me; uyttidumin – carry me

**Перевод:**

**I have attained love towards mādavan, which you people, who are different from my state, would not know. The words which you people speak are similar to the conversation between a dumb person and a deaf person. I want only one help from you. Carry me to the proximity of maturā, where kaṇṇapirān, who left behind his mother, dēvaki who had given birth to him painstakingly, and grew up in the house of another mother, yaśōdā, had reached the wrestling arena ahead of the wrestlers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – while those in her proximity told her “Since the ultimate benefit has to come through him, you should wait patiently, in the proper way, until he comes, and not be in a hurry like this”, she says in this pāsuram “you people too, who do not know my sorrowful state, do not have any qualification to speak a few words like this; I too do not have the qualification to hear your words”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maṟṟirundīrgatku** – for you all, who are different from me. Unlike me, for you, who have firm belief in *upāyam* (means)….. Alternatively, the term *maṟṟu* could be combined with the term *uraippadellām* which is coming later, and the meaning for that combination could be construed.

**irundīrgatku aṟilyalāgā** – just because you are staying in the same place where I am staying, you cannot know about my state. Was it not mentioned in mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 68-5? “*māyām na sēvē bhadram tē na vrutādarmamācarē* l *śudda bhāvam gatō bhaktyā śāstrāt vēdmi janārdanam* ll” (I do not engage with deceit; let auspicious events happen to you! I will not carry out deceitful deeds. I will have the nature of a pure person. I know about janārdana \[emperumān\] through śāstras \[sacred texts\] which are immersed in devotion). drutarāśtra, looking at sanjaya his charioteer, says “I too learnt śāstrams, just like you. There is no deficiency in my knowledge. Despite that, how is it that good meanings come to you easily and I have to listen from you. What is the reason for this?” śanjaya then tells him this ślōkam “I do not engage with deceitful activities. I am with pure qualities. You are trying to decipher meanings from śāstras which you have learnt while I am trying to decipher them through śāstras which are immersed in devotion. Your state is similar to those who seek materials which are not visible to the eyes, with the help of their eyes, while my situation is similar to those who look for them after applying pigment to their eyes \[looking deeper than normal\]”

**mādavan enbadōr anbu tannai** – if I had desired brahmacāri nārāyaṇan alone \[bachelor; i.e nārayaṇan alone, without śrī mahālakśmi\], I could have waited patiently. Did I not desire śrīman nārāyaṇan?

**mādavan enbadōr anbu** – this could be construed either as “Affection about mādavan” or “Affection called as mādavan”, In the second meaning, it is to be seen that instead of appearing differently as “Affection” or “Repository for affection” it would appear as “mādavan is the epitome for affection”. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17-16 “*sarvēśām hi sa darmātmā varṇānām kurutē dayām* l *caturṇām hi vayastānām tēna tē tamanuvratā*: ll” (That rāma, who is the epitome of righteousness, shows his mercy on the four varṇas \[brāhmaṇa, kśattriya, vaiśya and śudra\] as well as to people in the four stages of life \[infancy, adolescent, youth and old age\] and to all others. They are also affectionate towards him), doesn’t emperumān initially develop love towards his followers and then create affection in them \[towards him\]? Just as it has been said in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/thiruvaimozhi-10-10-7-kola-malarp-pavaikku/) ”*kōlamalarp pāvaikku anbāgiya en anbēyō*” (You are having great love towards me as well due to your love for the beautiful and enjoyable pirātti), isn’t it the most distinguished matter that she \[āṇḍāḷ\] desired? It was towards that *mādavan* that she carried out her love.

**anbu tannaiyuṟṟu** – this could be construed as either – attaining mādavan who is the epitome of love or as one who has total devotion in the matter of affection towards mādavan. Just as it has been said in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-10-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/thiruvaimozhi-5-10-1-pirandha-arum/) “*niṟandanūdupukku enadāviyai ninṟu ninṟu urukki uṇginṟa*” (\[matters relating to kriṣṇa\] have entered the inner regions of my heart, melted my ātmā and consumed me, every moment), she has complete devotion \[toward emperumān\].

**irundēnukku** – when I am unable to sustain myself in separation from him, isn’t it apt for me to reach his place? But I am unable to go since I have lost power in my legs and hence am remaining here.

**uraippadellām** – for me, who has the desire to attain him, all the words which are spoken to dissuade me from attaining him…

**ūmaiyarōdu sevidar vārttai** – you have no mouth to speak the words when you do not understand my situation and I do not have the ears to listen to them. The verse has to be transformed to “*sevidarōdu ūmaiyar vārttai*” to get the correct meaning. A dumb person does not have the qualification to speak, and that is your situation; a deaf person does not have the qualification to hear and that is my situation.

When people in her proximity ask her “How do you say that your situation is incomprehensible? Wouldn’t the one who created this situation for you, know it?” she responds, through the next verse, that one who does not know the anguish of a mother would not know the anguish of his beloved.

**peṟṟirundāḷai oḻiyavē** – observing a ritual for begetting a son, with lot of suffering, dēvaki had given birth to kaṇṇan. However, on the day that he was born, did she not have to let go of him? āṇḍāḷ says that next…

**pōyp pērttu** – unable to bear her separation from her son kaṇṇan, dēvaki was in tears. kaṇṇan, who had not had milk from her bosom, without having any mercy for her, left her immediately.

**pērttu oru tāi il vaḷarnda** – he grew up in the house of another mother, yaśōdā. While the mother who gave birth to him was in sorrow, he grew up with yaśōdā, allowing her to tie him down to a mortar and to beat him. ḍidn’t dēvaki too say, as in [perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7.5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2023/08/perumal-thirumozhi-7-5/) “*tiruvilēn onṟum peṟṟilēn ellām deyva nangai yasōdai peṟṟāḷē*” (I, who did not have any good fortune, did not enjoy anything. Only yaśōdā, who is a divine lady, enjoyed \[all the childish activities\])? Just as it has been said in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7.8, did not yaśōdā reach the boundary of ultimate bliss “*asōdai tollai inbattiṟudi kaṇḍāḷ*” (yaśōdā saw the boundary of unbounded pleasures)?

**peṟṟirundāḷai oḻiyavē pōy…..** – it has become his natural quality to harass those who suffer in separation from him. kulaśēkśara nāyaki \[kulaśēkhara āḻvār, in the pathos of a lady\] had mercifully said in [perumāḷ tirumoḻi 6.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2023/07/perumal-thirumozhi-6-4/) “*tāy mulaippālil amudirukkat tavaḻndu taḷar nadai ittuc cenṟu pēy mulai vāy vaittu nanjai uṇḍu pittan enṟē piṟar ēsa ninṟāy*” (Even as mother yaśōdā had nectar-like milk in her bosom, you went crawling and walking unsteadily, and drank the poisoned milk from the demon pūtanā. People reviled you, calling you as being mad, for your action). yaśōdāp pirātti could not sustain herself, without suckling you. While she was in sorrow since you had not taken her milk, you had drunk milk from pūtanā, who had come to kill you by applying poison on her bosoms. Due to this, you were reviled by people around, who said that you did not know the difference between those who had affection and those who had deceit in their thoughts.

**nambi** – he has this quality alone, in full measure.

**maṟporundāmaṟ kaḷamadainda** – while I was waiting, with deep affection, to embrace you tightly with my form, you would have gone to the wrestling arena, to embrace the wrestlers. *maṟporundāmal* – instead of uniting with me. Alternatively, *maṟporundā maṟkaḷam* – he reached the wrestling arena, much before the wrestlers arrived there in order to engage in the sport of wrestling.

**maduraip puṟattu ennai uyttidumin** – carry me to the proximity of vada madurai, such that I could approach him before he goes to wrestle with the wrestlers.

Next, we will consider 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 12.2. Nani iniyor

*nāṇi ini ōr karumamillai nālayalārum aṟindu oḻindārpāṇiyādu ennai marundu seydu paṇḍu paṇḍu ākka uṟudirāgilmāṇi uruvāy ulagaḷanda māyanaik kāṇil talai maṟiyumāṇaiyāl nīr ennaik kākka vēṇḍil āyppādikkē ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

ini – hereafter; nāṇi – feeling shy; ōr karumam illai – there is no benefit; nāl ayalārum – all the people in the town; aṟīndu oḻindār – have learnt (about me); pāṇiyādu – without any delay; marundu seydu – carrying out required remedial measures; ennai – me; paṇḍu paṇḍu ākka uṟudir āgil – if you are thinking of making me regain my complexion which I had before my state of uniting with him, when I had not known about anything related to emperumān, which itself was before my previous state of uniting with him; nīr – you; ennai – me; āṇaiyāl – truly; kākka vēṇḍil – if you desire to protect me; ennai – me; āyppādikkē – to tiruvāyppādi; ūyttidumin – carry me; māṇi uruvāy ulagu aḷanda māyanai kāṇil – if I get to worship perumāḷ who went (to mahābali) in the form of vāmana \[dwarf\] and measured all the worlds \[as trivikraman); talai maṟiyum – (this disease) will get relieved

**Перевод:**

**Everyone in the town has come to know of my state. There is no point in feeling shy any more. Without any further delay, carrying out suitable remedy, if you truly wish to make me regain my complexion which I had before I united with him, when I had not known anything about emperumān, please take me to tiruvāyppādi. There, if I get to worship emperumān, who had gone to mahābali in the form of a dwarf, but grew as trivikraman, measuring all the worlds, this disease will get relieved.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – when people in her proximity asked her “Whatever you may do, if you go so daringly to his place in search of him, it would lead to a blemish on him. Could you create a blemish for him? Should you not remain with shyness, typical of a lady?”, she responds, saying “There is no purpose in remaining with shyness. Take me to āyppādi (gōkulam)”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāṇiniyōr karumamillai** – what benefit will I get if I protect my femineity with the quality of shyness, with this? The term *nāṇam* refers to one among the four feminine qualities of *accam* \[fear\], *madam* \[simplicity\], *nāṇam* \[shyness\] and *payirppu* \[abhorrence towards anything offensive\], viz. feeling ashamed at carrying out an inappropriate activity. īsn’t withdrawing, arising out of shame, from carrying out inappropriate activities, only apt for those who desire to live for some more time and attain their beloved? Since she \[āṇḍāḷ\] is not interested in living any further, her quality of shyness left her completely. Feeling distressed due to separation, after reaching the stage of ‘I cannot live any more’, there is no purpose in feeling shy, like those who wish to live for a longer time. After reaching that stage, could I, who am not desirous of living, feel shyness? I can feel shy only if I exist. If my existence itself is disappearing, how could I protect my quality of shyness? The quality of *nāṇam*, is an attribute for me, who am a substrate for that quality. If the existence of substrate itself is in question, how can I protect the attribute of *nāṇam*?

**ini** – from this term, it is clear that she had tried her best to protect her quality of shyness. When some people asked her “What has happened to you now to say that there is no benefit in feeling shy?” she says ….

**nālayalārum aṟindu oḻindār** – There is benefit for her if she feels shy, trying to hide her distress due to separation, from others. Only if there are a few people who did not know of her distressed state, could she protect it from others. When no one in the town knew of her condition, she had protected it from her mothers and friends. Since there were in proximity, they guessed her state and came to know of it. Still, she protected her distressed state from the people of the town, who were not close to her. Then, someone in the town came to know of it. From that person, it spread to the others, and in course of time, everyone in the town came to know of it. It then passed on to people in the surrounding towns too. Hence, what is the benefit now in hiding her shyness from anyone?

**aṟindoḻindār** – She had been hiding her distressed state from others, just like tirukkōśtiyūr nambi was hiding *bhagavad viśayam* \[matter related to emperumān\] from unrelated people. Just as the matter which he had hidden, came to be revealed through emperumānār, her state of distress too, which she had hidden from others, came to be known to all the people in the town.

The vyākhyānam now narrates about incidents in the life of tirukkōśtiyūr nambi \[one among the five ācāryas of rāmānujar\]. tirukkōśtiyūr nambi would sit on the *pial* \[raised platform near the entrance to the house, called in tamiḻ as *tiṇṇai*\] of his tirumāḷigai \[divine house\], meditating on the *rahasyatrayams* \[the three esoteric concepts in śrī vaiṣṇavam, namely *tirumantram, dvayam* and *carama ślōkam*\], without anyone knowing about it. Following periya nambi’s \[rāmānujar’s primary ācāryar\] order, rāmānujar went to seek meanings of these from tirukkōśtiyūr nambi. On reaching tirukkōśtiyūr, he asked the people in the town, as to where the divine house of tirukkōśtiyūr nambi was. When they pointed out the direction to his house, he started prostrating from that place, to reach his abode. It was only after this that the people of the town realised the eminence of tirukkōśtiyūr nambi, thinking “emperumānār is prostrating in the direction of nambi’s house!” There are two other narratives to understand that nambi would hide meanings. kūrattāḻvān and mudaliyāṇḍān (primary disciples of rāmānujar) wanted to know some meanings, after carrying out servitude to tirukkōśtiyūr nambi, at his divine residence. Keeping his silence for those six months when they were carrying out servitude, is it not well known that nambi revealed the meanings which they wanted to learn! (1) rāmānujar sent mudaliyāṇḍān to nambi, to learn the meanings of *carama ślōkam* from him. After staying for six months and carrying out servitude, since nambi did not say anything, mudaliyāṇḍān decided to return. At that time, nambi told him briefly, “Learn the meanings of *carama ślōkam* from rāmānujar himself” (2) In the assembly of rāmānujar, once a doubt was raised on whether the distinguished identification of an ātmā was one of *śēśatvam* \[servitude\] or *jñānānandam* (knowledge and bliss\]. Thinking in his divine mind that the decision for this should be obtained from ācāryan, rāmānujar sent kūrattāḻvān to tirukkōśtiyūr nambi. Despite carrying out servitude to nambi for six months, since nambi did not say a word on this, kūrattāḻvān took leave of nambi. At that time, nambi mercifully told him “Haven’t you seen nammāḻvār’s words in [tiruvāimoḻi 8-8-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/04/thiruvaimozhi-8-8-2-adiyen-ullan/) ‘*adiyēn uḷḷān?’*” āḻvān, realising that he has got the answer for the doubt, felt happy at his fortune and returned to rāmānujar to convey this. The explanation for the answer here, is: the tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram says “*adiyēn uḷḷān udal uḷḷān*”. Since the term *udal uḷḷān* has been removed \[by nambi, in his response\], *udal*, referring to the physical form, has been removed. The term *adiyēn uḷḷān* refers only to the *ātma svarūpam* (basic nature of ātmā). If *jñānam* were the distinguished identification for ātmā, āḻvār, to make it explicit that ātmā is *jñānasvarūpi*, would have mercifully used the term *ennuḷḷān*. Instead of that, to make it clear that ātmā is only *śēśabhūtan* \[servitor\], since āḻvār has used the term *adiyēn* referring to ātmā which is distinct from *dēham* \[physical form\], nambi’s divine mind is that āḻvār wanted to convey the meaning that it is *śēśatvam* which is the distinct identification for the ātmā rather than *jñānānandam*. This meaning has been emphasised in *tirumantram* too. In *tirumantram*, the third syllable *makāram* in the first term *praṇavam* \[the syllables *a*, *u* and *ma*\] says that ātmā has *jñānānandam* as its identification, having *jñānam* \[knowledge\] as its quality, and is distinct from *acētanam* \[entity without knowledge\]. However, before ascertaining this, in the first two syllables, by hinting that ātmā is *ananyārhaśēśam* to emperumān \[unconditional servitude\], has it not been clearly emphasised that only *śēśatvam* is the distinct identification for ātmā, rather than *jñānānandam*?

\[vyākhyānam now returns to the main pāsuram\].

Mothers in her proximity told āṇḍāḷ “You might have withdrawn from protecting your *svarūpam*. But we will not do that, in your matter. Due to the affection which we have for you, would we not protect your *svarūpam* by telling you the benefits?” āṇḍāḷ tells them further “If you really wish to protect me, please attempt to show me the form of *vāmana* who made himself as a mendicant in order to get back the worlds, his possession”…..

**pāṇiyādu ennai marundu seydu** – without delay, carrying out all the remedies for me. *pāṇittal* – delaying time. *marundu seydal* – carrying out remedy to cure disease (of love)

**paṇḍu paṇḍākka uṟudirāgil** – if you wish to recreate the complexion which I had before I united with emperumān, when I had not known about him. The first term *paṇḍu* refers to the state when I had united with him, prior to the present sate. The second *paṇḍu* refers to the state before that state, when I had not known about him. From the time since she united with him, since separation has been coming on and off, the state of union with him was the basis of her thinning. Thus, she says “If you had thought of bringing back the complexion which I had, before I had united with him for the first time…”

**māṇi uruvāy** – with the form of vāmana, the bachelor. Since he had taken the incarnation to take alms, whether people offered him alms or closed the door on him saying that they will not offer alms, he went with a smile on his face, without any distinction, carrying all the insults on his form.

**ulagaḷanda māyanaik kānil** – only if I see the amazing entity who measured the three worlds. Just as she had said in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 8-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/09/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-8-3/) “*gōvindan guṇam pādi āvi kāttiruppēnē*” (could I sustain myself by reciting the auspicious qualities of kaṇṇan, to my heart’s content?) she is not in a state to sustain herself by meditating on the auspicious qualities of emperumān. She says that she could sustain herself only if she saw him.

**talai maṟiyum** – my disease which came about due to separation will be cured. Just as hitting a serpent which is chasing \[would make it to runaway\], my disease will submissively leave me.

**āṇaiyāl nīr ennaik kākka vēṇḍil** – I am taking a vow on you! If you intend protecting me…. The term *Al* is a metric syllable. Alternatively, this could be construed to mean – if you protect me such that I become clear from my disease and obey your order.

**āyppādikkē ennai uyttidumin** – take me to āyppādi (śrī gōkulam) where kaṇṇan’s mother is supporting his misciefs. Hasn’t yaśōdā said, as in periya tirumoḻi 10-7-11? “*anganam tīmai seyvargaḷō nambī*! *āyar madamakkaḷai..*” (Oh, one who is full of auspicious qualities! Is it apt to carry out wicked acts on the herd-girls who are obedient?) Did she not call him as *nambī* (one who is full of auspicious qualities) even after seeing his wicked deeds? Is it not this quality which she too spoke about in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 3-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/07/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-3-9/) “*anjavurappāḷ yasōdai āṇāda vittirukkum*” (she would not reprimand kaṇṇan on his evil deeds; she would let him carry out those misciefs)? Leave me in front of that yaśōdā who had said as in periya tirumoḻi 10-4-8 “*undam adigaḷ munivar unnai nān en kaiyiṟ kōlāl nondida mōdavum killēn*” (your father \[nandagōpar\] would be angry; I will not hit you with the stick in my hand such that it would pain you). īsn’t she feeling sorry that kaṇṇan had not played wicked deeds, as said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-2-6 “*padiṟu pala seydu ippādi engum tiriyāmē – en piḷḷaiyaip pōkkinēn*” (Why did I send kaṇṇan to go behind the cows instead of allowing him to go around this āyppādi, engaging in many deceitful activities in the houses of herd-folk?) The meaning hinted here is that if she were left in front of yaśōdā, she \[yaśōdā\] would feel happy that kaṇṇan had harmed her.

Here, the vyākyātā \[commentator, periyavāccān piḷḷai\] has explained the nācciyār tirumoḻi pāsuram 3-9:

**anja urappāḷ asōdai** – if girls who were target of his wicked deeds, thinking “He is not one who has appeared from nowhere; he has a mother; we will inform her of his wicked deeds” go to yaśōdā to complain about kaṇṇan, she would not scold him such that he would be afraid. She would scold him such that he would indulge in wicked deeds a hundred times more. She will break into a smile, as if she were enjoying his wicked deeds and say “*anganam tīmaigaḷ seyvargaḷō nambī*” (Oh, one who is complete with auspicious qualities! Would you engage in such wicked deeds?) He too, taking the cue from her, would engage in wicked activities, in that place and at that time itself, as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 1-7-5 “*iḷavāycciyar kaṇṇinuḷ vidavē seydu viḻikkum pirān*” (one who is exchanging glances with youthful covherd girls, which are known only to the girls and to him).

**āṇāda vittirukkum** – she \[yaśōdā\] would be blissful, allowing him \[kaṇṇan\] to do as he pleased. She would appear as if she had attained the purpose of her taking birth and had attained a great benefit by thinking “Losing out to my son, how are these girls coming to me and complaining!”

Next, we will consider 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 12.3. Tandaiyum tayum

*tandaiyum tāyum uṟṟārum niṟkat tanivaḻi pōyināḷ ennum solluvanda pinnaip paḻi kāpparidu māyavan vandu urukkāttuginṟānkondaḷamāKkip parakkaḻittuk kuṟumbu seyvān ōr maganaip peṟṟanandagōpālan kadaittalaikkē naḷḷirutkaṇ ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

tandaiyum – father; tāyum – and mothers; uṟṟārum – and other relatives too; niṟka – even when they are all there; tani vaḻi pōyināḷ ennuum sollu – the word that “She went on her own way, in the street”; vanda pinnai – after the word spread around in the world; paḻi kāppu aridu – it is not possible to stop that blame; māyavan – kaṇṇapirān, who has amazing activities; vandu – coming in front of me; uru kāttuginṟān – he is pulling me towards him by manifesting his divine form; kondaḷam ākki parakkaḻittu kuṟumbu seyvān ōr maganaip peṟṟa – one who sired a; nandagōpālan – nandagōpar’s; kadaittalaikkē – entrance of his divine house; naḷḷirutkaṇ – at midnight; ennai uyttidumin – take me and leave me there

**Перевод:**

**After the word “Even as her father, mothers and other relatives were present, she went her own way, in the street” spread throughout the word, it is difficult to stop that blame. It is not possible for me not to go my way since kaṇṇapirān, who has amazing activities, is manifesting his divine form to me and pulling me towards him. Now, all that you have to do is to take me, in the middle of the night, to the entrance of the divine house of nandagōpar, who had sired a son who had fought with a girl, creating a blame for her and who is indulging in mistakes and leave me there.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells in this pāsuram “This yaśōdāp pirātti was also like us, born as a woman. There is no purpose in blaming her for not bringing up kaṇṇan properly. Despite being his father who had the duty to bring him up with due control and having the power to do that, śrī nandagōpar did not do that. Take me to his door-step and leave me there”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tandaiyum tāyum uṟṟārum niṟka ….. paḻi kāpparidu** – Once a blame emerges, it is difficult to stop it. Since you are my father and mother who sired me, and my relatives, it is not possible for you to remain indifferent saying “Let the blame come”. Now, before the blame sets in, try to avoid it. If you people were to say “You have been patient for all these days. Couldn’t you remain patient for a few more days and prevent the blame from coming?” since I could tolerate all these days, I had been putting up with it. I had been patient, looking at you all. Now, I am not able to remain patient. Hence, before the blame comes my way, try to prevent it.

**tani vaḻi pōyināḷ ennum sollu vandapinnaip paḻi kāpparidu** – There is no blame larger than the blame of renouncing the very people who were to unite me with my beloved and going my way. Once the blame “Renouncing father, mother and relatives, she went her way. Even there, instead of following the way of her beloved, she went her own way” starts spreading in the world, it will be impossible to erase it.

**vandapinnaip paḻi kāpparidu** – Did not śrī rāma feel sad that he could not make up the loss which he had suffered, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 5-5 “*na mē du*:*kkam priyā dūrē na mē du*:*kkam hrutēti vā* l *ētadēvānuśōcāmi vayō’syā hyativartatē*” (I am not distressed that the who is sweet to me is in a far-away place; I am neither distressed that she had been abducted. I am distressed only because she is growing older)? She tells them to ensure that such a situation doesn’t befall them. perumāḷ \[śrī rāma\] did not remain tolerant thinking “Once we redeem pirātti, we are going to be united with her”. Did he not feel despicable, thinking that he had lost the benefit of being together with her, for these ten months? The opinion is that they \[her parents and relatives\] have to ensure that they do not reach the stage of despise.

Assuming that they tell her “Knowing that this is a blame and thinking that this should be removed, could you not be patient, for our sake, waiting for the one who owns \[you\], to come?” she says ….

**māyavan urukkāttuginṟān** – when his amazing divine form, auspicious qualities such as simplicity are coming in front of me and troubling me, could I remain patient, for the sake of my father and mother? The amazing entity, with his amazing divine form and auspicious qualities, through his appearance in my mind, manifests himself in front of me. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-20…22 says “*kāciccāvasada syāntē stitvā drustvā bahirgurum* l *tanmayatvēna gōvindam dadyau meelitalōcanā* ll *taccitta vimalāhlāda kśīṇa puṇyacayā tatā* l *tadaprāptimahādu*:*kkha vileenāśēśa pātakā* ll *cintayantī jagadsūtim parabrahma svarūpiṇam* l *nirucvāsatayā muktim gatānyā gōpakanyakā* ll” (hearing kaṇṇan playing on the flute, a herd-girl came out of the house, and seeing her father outside, stopped in her tracks, closed her eyes, meditating on kriṣṇa. Due to the ecstasy which resulted from her heart engaging with him fully, all her *puṇya* \[virtues\] got eliminated. Due to the sorrow of not having attained him directly, all her *pāpa* \[sins\] got eliminated. (Due to the elimination of *puṇya* and *pāpa* which are reason for repeated births, having experienced both fully\], her breath stopped totally and she attained *mukti* \[liberation from samsāram\]). āṇḍāḷ asks, “Is my love towards kaṇṇan limited, as in the case of cintayanti \[the person who was described in the ślōkam seen above\] such that I would stop looking at my father and mother?” For this ślōkam, \[parāśara\] bhattar would mercifully say “Among the people who united with kaṇṇan in tiruvāyppādi, there is none who is new, like cintayanti” In other words, she could see her parents; that prevented her from running towards kaṇṇan. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-19 says “*yayau ca kācit prēmāndā tatpārśvam avilambitam*” (a cow-herd girl, with her heart getting dark due to love \[with darkness guiding her\] went hastily to the proximity of kaṇṇan who was playing on the flute). Another herd-girl, as mentioned in this ślōkam, did not even have the capability to see her surroundings, due to her love for kaṇṇan. When compared with her, cintayanti is a new entrant. The ślōkam said that a girl, due to her love for kaṇṇan, became blind in her eyes. Who then guided her to kaṇṇan? Due to her love, her heart became darkened. She went, with that darkness as the guide. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-18-22 says “*gurūṇāmagratō vaktum kim pravīśi na na*: *kśamam* l *gurava*: *kim kariśyanti dagdānām virahāgnitā* ll” (Are you saying that we cannot reveal this matter in the presence of elders? What can our elders do to us when we are being burnt by the fire of separation?). āṇḍāḷ is following the state of covherd girl who went to kaṇṇan, renouncing her parents.

It is pertinent here to recall periyāḻvār 3-6-1 pāsuram “*tūvalamburi udaiya tirumāl tūya vāyil kuḻalōsai vaḻiyē, kōvalar siṟumiyar iḷam kongai kudugalippa udaluḷaviḻndu engum kāvalum kadandu…*” The meaning for this is – With the fear that kaṇṇan would abduct them, the herd-people kept their girls in the middle and protected them with compound walls. However, the divine sound from kaṇṇan’s flute went past the compound walls, putting to sleep all the guards who were guarding them, and entered the ears of covherd girls, as a messenger. The moment the bosoms of the covherd girls realised that it was the sound from kaṇṇan’s flute, they woke the girls up and told them “The sound of flute has come in; come, let us go” The girls said “But, those who are guarding us will wake up”. The bosoms then said “Alright, in that case you people come after they have gone to sleep; we shall go ahead”. The term *kudugalippa* (becoming rapturous or ecstatic) indicates that these limbs, which are *acētana* (entities without knowledge) became similar to *cētana* (entities with knowledge) and were urging to go ahead of them. *iḷam kongai* – like children, who will not listen to their mothers. *kāvalum kadandu* – when the water flow in a river increases, we have seen it going past small stones. In the same way, when the force \[of water\] increases, we have seen the river flowing over the hills too. In the same way, the reason for their not considering the protection as important is because of the excessive desire which they had. Similar to this, the divine beauty of emperumān’s form made her \[āṇḍāḷ\] to be indifferent to her parents.

**kondaḷamākkip parakkaḻittu** – fighting with and creating blame.

**kuṟumbu seyvānōr maganaip peṟṟa** – śrī nandagōpar, in order to control those in his kingdom who were indulging in misciefs, sired a son. Since he was born after śrī nandagōpar had engaged with many rituals, he did not control his son \[kaṇṇan\] \[since he was a precocious child\]. Thus, kaṇṇan too, being uncontrollable, started carrying out misciefs with covherd girls.

**nandagōpālan kadaittalaikkē** – it is heard that bhattar would mercifully say as to how nandagōpar’s *kadaittalai* (entrance to his house) would look like – the girls, who were harmed by kaṇṇan, being unable to walk, would be carried on cots \[like stretchers\], in a line, to the entrance of nandagōpar’s house, with their complexion worn off. This will be similar to people, who have been bitten by poisonous creatures, being carried, in a line, to the house of the one who will cure them.

**naḷḷirutkaṇ ennai uyttidumin** – carry me to his front yard, in the middle of the night. If you carry me during daytime, feeling shy to come in the presence of his parents, kaṇṇan may not come out at all. To ensure that it doesn’t happen that way, carry me in the middle of night. Just as it has been said in [periya tiruvandādi 26](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/11/periya-thiruvandhadhi-26/) “*iruḷanna māmēni*” (emperumān’s great divine form appears as if darkness had taken a form), he would come out during night which matches his complexion and will embrace me.

**maganaip peṟṟa nandagōpālan** – has he not observed many rituals to sire him? To ensure that he suffers from that wicked act \[here, siring kaṇṇan has been indicated as the wicked act\] carry me to his front yard. When you carry me like that, ensure that you do not get any blame; hence carry me in darkness. nandagōpālan, feeling ashamed to look in the eyes of his relatives, will refuse to come out if you carry me in daylight. Apart from not getting to see kaṇṇan, I would be unable to see nandagōpālan too, due to which I will end my life. Hence, to ensure that you do not get that blame, carry me and leave me at the entrance to his divine house, in the middle of the night.

Next, we will consider 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 12.4. Angait talattidai

*angaittalattidai āḻi koṇḍān avan mugattanṟi viḻiyēn enṟusengaccuk koṇḍu kaṇṇādai ārttuc ciṟumānidavaraik kāṇil nāṇumkongaittalam ivai nōkkik kāṇīr gōvindanukkallāl vāyil pōgāinguttai vāḻvai oḻiyavē pōy yamunaik karaikkennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

am kaittalattidai – on the beautiful, divine location of hand; āḻi koṇḍān avan – kaṇṇa pirān, who is holding the divine disc; mugattu anṟi viḻiyēn enṟu – will not look at the face of anyone other than his divine face; sem kaccu ādai koṇḍu – with the dress of good bodice; kaṇ ārttu – closing their eyes; siṟu mānidavaraik kāṇil nāṇum – feeling shy to look at lowly people; kongaittalam ivai – these bosoms; gōvindanukku allāl – apart from kaṇṇa pirān; vāyil pōgā – will not look at the entrance of anyone else’s house; inguttai vāḻvai oḻiyap pōy – getting rid of life here; ennai yamunaik karaikkē uyttidumin – carry me to the bank of river yamunā

**Перевод:**

**Oh, mothers! Look minutely at these bosoms which have closed their eyes with good bodice. These will not look at the face of anyone other than the divine face of kaṇṇa pirān who is holding the divine disc in his divine hand and they will feel shy to look at any lowly person. They will not look at the entrance of anyone else’s house, other than kaṇṇa pirān. Carry me to the bank of river yamunā, leaving aside life here.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says in this pāsuram “Leave me, who is the servitor exclusively for emperumān and for none else, at the banks of river yamunā”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**angaittalattidai āḻi koṇḍān avan mugattanṟi viḻiyēn** – this is the vow taken by the bosoms, as if they are in *cētana samādi* (in the state of an entity having knowledge). Their verb is in the first person, *viḻiyēn* (I will not look). In the army of kings, there is a division called as “*pūvēlaikkārar padai*”. They will kill themselves if any disturbance happens even to the flower worn by the king. In the same way, these bosoms have taken a vow that they “Will not look at anyone other than him, who is holding the divine disc in his divine hand”. They will say “Even if he comes without his divine disc, we will not desire him”.

**angaittalattidai āḻi** – His mere hands will be such that evil spirits would have to be warded off. When he is holding the divine disc, just like the *kaṟpaga maram* (wish-fulfilling celestial tree) having blossomed flowers, one cannot describe that beauty. Alternatively, it could be construed that she is saying in dislike “When his mere hand is capable of destroying me, should I speak about what will happen when he is holding the divine disc too?” Just as nammāḻvār says in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-6-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/02/thiruvaimozhi-10-6-8-meynninru/) “*karudumidam porudu* – *kainninṟa cakkarattan*” (one with the divine disc which will go and attack, where his divine heart desires, and return to stay on that divine hand), the divine disc will leave the divine hand to destroy enemies and destroy those who are affectionate \[towards emperumān\] by merely remaining on the hand.

**sengaccu ādai koṇḍu kaṇ ārttu** – with red coloured bodice, the bosoms covered their eyes. The pāsuram gives the reason for this action, further…

**siṟu mānidavaraik kāṇil nāṇum** – they will feel shy if they have to look at lowly persons. The term *siṟu mānidavar* thus distinguishes puruśōttaman (emperumān) who is *peru mānidavar* (eminent person). emperumān himself says in [bhagavat gītā 9-11](https://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-11/) “*avajānanti mām mūdā mānuśīm tanumāśritam* l *param bhāvam ajānantō mama bhūtamahēśvaram* ll” (Those without knowledge, not realising that I am the īśvaran, above all the creations, and that I have come in the form of a human entity, insult me)

**kāṇil nāṇum** – these bosoms, which are huge, will shrink. When bharata was coming towards citrakūtam, lakśmaṇa protested to śrī rāma about his intentions. śri rāma then told him “*ucyamānō’pi bharatō mayā lakśmaṇa*! *tatvata*: l *rājyamasmai prayaccēti bādam ityēva vakśyati* ll *tatōktō darmaśeelēna bhrātrā tasya hitē rata*: l *lakśmaṇa*: *pravivēśēva svāni gātrāṇi lajjayā* ll” (Oh, lakśmaṇa! If bharata is ordered by me to give the kingdom to you, he will carry it out truly. lakśmaṇa, who was told this way by his elder brother, who does not veer from the path of righteousness, and who is very much attached in the well-being of his elder brother, out of shame, felt śrivelled). Did not lakśmaṇa shrivel due to shyness? While brothers in the world fight with each other for the sake of attaining kingdom, how the kingdom is tossed about, at the hands of these brothers! After giving up his kingdom, when śrī rāma reached citrakūtam, lakśmaṇa, who went along with śrī rāma \[to the forest\], saw bharata coming towards them. Mistaking his intention, lakśmaṇa told śrī rāma with anger clearly seen on is face “I will kill bharata, I will hit him”. Despite śrī rāma telling lakśmaṇa about the eminent qualities of bharata, since lakśmaṇa did not listen to him, śrī rāma told him “Oh, dear! It appears that you have a desire to rule the land. As soon as I tell bharata ‘Oh, dear, give the kingdom to lakśmaṇa’, bharata will hand over the kingdom to you, as if relieved of a huge load on his head. You go ahead to ayōdyā. I will proceed further with bharata”. As soon as he heard this, didn’t lakśmaṇa shrivel completely!

**kongait talam ivai** – just as sītāp pirātti had told hanuman in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 40-3 “*yatā tam puruśavyāgram gātrai*: *śōkābhikarśitai*: l *samspruśēyam sakāmā’ham tatā kuru dayām mayi*” (you should mercifully shower your grace on me such that I, who am withering due to my sorrow, would be able to touch desirously śrī rāma, who is like a tiger among men, with my limbs), āṇḍāḷ desires to embrace kaṇṇapirān with her bosoms similar to sītāp pirātti desiring to embrace śrī rāma.

**ivai nōkkik kāṇīr** – just as sītāp pirātti showed her emaciated physical form caused by separation from śrī rāma, to hanuman, āṇḍāḷ showed her bosoms which are suffering due to separation, to her mothers and friends. She tells them “Let others think whatever they wish. Look at my children shedding tears. Do something to stop their tears”, beseeching them just like someone would ask for alms, showing their children.

**ivai nōKkik kāṇīr** – look at how they have grown huge, thinking of him.

**gōvindanukkallāḷ vāyil pōgā** – other than kaṇṇan, who went behind cows indulging in misciefs, they will not look at even the son of emperor daśarata, who observed *ēkadārai vrata* (observing the principle of having only one wife) who did not look at anyone other than his wife.

**pōgā** – even if I wish to, they will not follow in my path. śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 59-1 has been explained here “*mama tu aśvā nivruttasya na pravartanta vartmani* l *uṣṇamaśru pramuncantō rāmē samprastitē vanam* ll” (After śrī rāma entered the forest, my horses, which returned, shed warm tears and went in the wrong path). Just as sumantran, who separated from perumāḷ \[śrī rāma\] said that his horses did not follow him, she says that of her bosoms. m*ama aśvā*: – my horses. sumantran is surprised that his horses did not follow him, who is an expert on horses and who knows the minds of horses. *tu* – these horses, which have been following my mind all along, are behaving on their own, only now. *aśvā*: – since the syllable *aśva* comes from the etymology *aśū-vyāptau*, the horses ran, knowing my mind. *nivruttasya* – they went in the correct way, while taking perumāḷ \[towards the forest\]. It is only on the way back, without him, that they are behaving like this. *na pravarttanta vartmani* – instead of proceeding on the main path, they started going in the path towards forest.

\[The vyākhyānam now returns to the pāsuram\]

**inguttai vāḻvai oḻiyavē pōy yamunaikkaraikkennai uyttidumin** – I am not satisfied with the present living where I could visualise the divine feet of emperumān only in my mind. Carry me to the bank of river yamunā such that I could see the divine feet in front of me.

īsn’t it the yamunā, about which she had praised in [tiruppāvai 5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-05-mayanai/) “*tūya perunīr yamunait tuṟaivan*” (one who plays on the bank of river yamunā, which has divine and deep water)? She is probably thinking of engaging in water games with kaṇṇan in the river.

Next, we will consider 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 12.5. Arkkum ennoy idu

*ārkkum ennōy idu aṟiyalāgā(du) ammanaimīr! tuḻadippadādēkārkkadal vaṇṇan enbān oruvan kaikaṇḍa yōgam tadavat tīrumnīrkkarai ninṟa kadambai ēṟik kāḷiyan ucciyil nattam pāyndupōrkkaḷamāga niruttam seyda poygaikkaraikku ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

ammanaimīr! – Oh, mothers!; en – my; idu nōy – this disease; ārkkum – whosoever it may be; aṟiyal āgādu – is not possible to know; tuḻadip padādē – without feeling sad (by carrying out some remedy); nīr karai ninṟa – on the bank of kāḷinga madu (a mound in the pond); kadambai ēri – going atop kadamba (Indian oak) tree; kāḷiyan ucciyil – on the hood of snake kāḷiyan; nattam pāyndu – jumping, in the pose of a dance; pōrkkaḷam āga – making that pond as a battlefield; niruttam seyda – where the dance took place; poygai karaikku – on the bank of that pond; ennai uyttidumin – carry me; kārkkadal vaṇṇan enbān oruvan – kaṇṇapirān, who has the divine complexion of a bluish ocean; tadava – if he massages (with his divine hand); tīrum – this disease will be cured; kaikaṇḍa yōgam – this is the means which will give instantaneous result

**Перевод:**

**Oh, mothers! This disease of mine is difficult for anyone to know. Without feeling sad about carrying out remedial measures, carry me to the bank of the mound where kaṇṇapirān had climbed atop a kadamba tree and jumped on the spread hoods of the serpent kāḷiyan in a dancing pose, and danced on that kāḷiyan to make the pond into a battlefield. If kaṇṇapirān, who has the divine complexion of a bluish ocean, gently massages me with his divine hand, my disease will get cured. This is the means which will give instantaneous result.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – looking at her distressed condition, her mothers started taking efforts to understand what her disease is due to and to find a suitable remedy for it. She says “Being related to me alone is not enough for you, to know my disease”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ārkkum ennōyidu aṟiyalāgādu** – is it only for mothers like you? Even for emperumān, who created this disease, it is not possible to estimate this disease. Did not nammāḻvār too mercifully say in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-6-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/thiruvaimozhi-4-6-2-thisaikkinradhe/) “*idu mikka perum deyvam isaippinṟi nīr aṇangādum iḷandeyvam anṟidu*” (\[her disease\] has been caused by sarvēśvaran, who is the greatest among all. It has not been caused by the petty deity which you people are engaged with, which is not fitting to your nature)? kēnōpaniśat 2-3 says “*yasyāmatam tasya matam matam yasya na vēda sa*: *avijñātam vijānatām vijñātam avijānatām*” (brahmam (supreme entity) becomes known to that person who is not able to determine and know it. It is not known to one who thinks that he knows about it. \[brahmam\] is unknown to the knowledgeable and is known to the one who does not know). In this *vēdavākyam* (words from vēdam), emperumān is included in the group of those who do not know about him! As mentioned in [periya tirumoḻi 5-2-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2023/07/periya-thirumozhi-5-2-1/) “*tām tam perumai aṟiyār*” (He, who is omniscient, does not know his greatness), when he does not know his own greatness, how will he know about the disease which he had created for me?

**en nōy** – could this disease, which is exclusively existing for me, come to you? śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 87-9 says “*putra vyādir na tē kaccit śarīram paribādatē* l *asya rājakulasyādya tvadadīnam hi jīvitam*” (O, son \[bharata\]! I hope that no ailment afflicts your body. Now, the life of this royal clan is indeed dependent on you). kausalyā, who raised this query of bharata, was steady only and did not swoon, like bharata did. She says “Oh, son! Your face has got drained of its colour. Looking at that, it is possible that śrī rāma would return, thinking that if he doesn’t return, he may lose you forever. For that to happen, you have to be alive. Anticipating that, this royal clan is dependent on your life”. The meaning for the first part of the ślōkam cited above is this: As soon as guhan, a boatman, said “śrī rāma, lakśmaṇa and sītāp pirātti have gone to the other side of river gangā” bharata swooned, just like alchemists will swoon upon getting a pellet. At that time, on behalf of the mothers, kausalyā asks bharata “Oh son! Which disease is troubling you?” They did not realise that he had swooned due to separation from śrī rāma. They would be able to understand the reason for his bewilderment only if they too swooned due to separation from śrī rāma! It is not surprising that bharata swooned in a country where trees were withering with their fruits and flowers due to separation from śrī rāma, as brought out in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 59-4 “*viśayē tē mahārāja rāma vyasana karśitā*: l *api vrukśā*: *parimlānā*: *sa puśpānkura kōrakā*: ll” (Oh king! In your kingdom, trees, suffering due to separation from śri rāma, have withered with their flowers, buds and sprouts). Since bharata was affected by something which didn’t affect them, kausalyā asked that query of bharata, in the ślōkam cited above. āṇḍāḷ’s case of speaking about *en nōy* to her mothers is similar to that.

**en nōy** – just as it has been mentioned in [tiruviruttam 53](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/02/thiruviruththam-53/) “*deyva nannōy idu*” (the disease that she has, is a distinguished one, caused by śrīman nārāyaṇan), since it has been created due to love for emperumān, this is a disease which is desired by me.

**ammanaimīr** – just because you had given birth to me, would you know about my disease which happened to me?

**ammanaimīr** – since you are old, you are now counselling me to await his arrival. At a younger age, vouldn’t you too have been like me, with the urge to attain your goal?

**tuḻadip padādē** – without suffering. *tuḻadi* – sorrow. If you seek remedial measures for my disease through foretelling etc known to you, you may have to lose me, causing you sorrow.

Her mothers say “You may step back from protecting yourself. However, we will not withdraw from that. Even if you consider that we will not be able to take proper remedial measures ourselves, if you tell us the remedy which you think would be better for you, we will carry it out if you inform us”. She tells them further …

**kārkkadal vaṇṇan enbān oruvan** – she says that only the one who has a divine form which is invigorating like the dark ocean, is tormenting her like this. Initially she points out to the person who is the root-cause for her disease and then says the remedy for it. Since they \[her mothers\] are bewildered at her condition, they have to listen from her the root-cause of her disease as well as the remedy.

**oruvan** – you could hear from me the remedy for my disease, but you would not be able to measure the eminence of the entity who is the root-cause for my condition.

**kārkkadal vaṇṇan enbān ōruvan – tadavat tīrum** – only if he, who is the cause for my disease, strokes me, would my disease be cured. When asked “How did you find this out?” she says …

**kaikaṇḍa yōgam** – this is the remedy that I have known already. *yōgam* – use of medicine. Alternatively, from the lexicon, “*yōga*: *sannahanōpāya*”, the term *yōgam* could refer to means. Thus, this could be construed as the means as seen by many people. She says “Do not think that this would not give result similar to various other words which did not fructify. This is the means which has been found out to be true by many”. What is the *pramāṇam* \[authentic proof\] for this? [bhagavat gītā 9-3](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-3/) says “*kśipram bhavati darmātmā śaśvaccāntim nigaccati* l *kauntēya*! *pratijānīhi na mē bhakta*: *praṇaśyati* ll” ((one who attained me) becomes a great entity soon. He attains mōkśam which is in a permanent state of peace. Oh arjuna! Take a vow that my devotee will not get ruined). To establish the fact that the followers of emperumān will not be destroyed, did not kaṇṇan ask arjunan to take a solemn vow, as said in this? Did not emperumānār too, in his gītā bhāśyam 9-2 say “*kauntēya tvamēvāsminnartē pratijñām kuru*” (Oh, the son of kunti! You take the pledge in this matter)? Through the term *mē bhakta*: (my devotee) emperumān indicates “Don’t you know my eminence?” For those who attain me, harm will never come their way. That harm will be similar to the śāstra vākyam “*agninā sincēt*” (let it be wet by fire), something which will never come about. pēyāḻvār too, in his [mūnṟām tiruvandādi 27](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/03/munram-thiruvandhadhi-27/) said “*ārē tuyar uḻandār*? *tunbuṟṟār āṇḍaiyār*? *kārē malinda karum kadalai nērē kadaindānaik kāraṇanai nīraṇaimēl paḷḷi adaindānai nāḷum adaindu*”. The meaning for this is as follows: *kāraṇananai adaindu ārē tuyar uḻandār* – Among those who had taken refuge under emperumān, just as it has been said in brahma vaivartam-prakruti kaṇḍam 26-90 “*avaśyam anubhōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham l* *nābhuktam kśīyatē karma kalpakōti śatairapi* ll´ (The effects of deeds of vice and virtue, carried out by a person, have to be necessarily experienced. The effects cannot be wished away, even after passage of hundreds of crores of *kalpam*), is there anyone who, as the result of his sins, has gone away from emperumān, and got immersed in the hell of samsāram? *tunbuṟṟār āṇḍaiyār* – Is there anyone, who, after carrying out offences during the course of his stay here, taking refuge under other deities, just as was done by entities as hiraṇya, rāvaṇa et al, had received punishment for that? The concise meaning of these two *pramāṇams* is that “After entering samsāram, carrying out sinful deeds, but after attaining emperumān, there are only people who got uplifted, such as vibhīśaṇa, prahlāda et al, but none got destroyed like hiraṇya kaśyap, rāvaṇa et al*”*. Even if people had been caught in the web of samsāram, due to the result of previously committed sins, after attaining him, sins will run helter-skelter, as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-4-3 “*nāttiluḷḷa pāvamellām summenādē kaivittōdit tūṟugaḷ pāyndanavē*” (sins of the people in the world, unable to breathe, hid themselves in the bushes) or emperumān will shift these sins on the heads of the enemies of his followers just as he did when he changed the direction of his arrow, aimed at varuṇa, on the heads of varuṇa’s enemies or he would take the sins on himself, just as he did when he took the arrow, released by bhagadattan on arjuna, on his chest, as mentioned in mahābhāratam śānti parvam33-18 “*visruśtam bhagadattēna tadastram sarvaghādukam* l *urasā dārayāmāsa (pratijagrāha) pārtam sancātya mādava*: ll” (mādavan \[emperumān\] took on his chest the arrow, which was shot by bhagadattan, which was capable of destroying everything, by hiding arjuna from it). Thus, his words “My devotee will not be destroyed” are infallible.

When they ask her as to what they should do for the remedy, she says …

**nīrkkarai ninṟa – uyttidumin** – she tells them that instead of carrying out inappropriate remedies at the place where they are residing, leading to disastrous results, they should carry her to his place where she can sing *pallāṇḍu* for him \[carrying out *mangaḷāśāsanam* or wishing him well\].

**nīrkkarai ninṟa kadambai ēṟi** – he got atop the k*adamba* tree, which, having grown on the bank of the bank which had the poisonous serpent *kāḷinga* in it, had poison all over it. How much of fear is that creating in our minds! He let out an uproarious sound even as he jumped from the top of the tree to land on the spread hoods of kāḷiyan, waking him up in the bargain.

**kāḷiyan ucciyil nattam pāyndu** – even as he jumped, he made sure that his feet pressed on kāḷiyan’s head. Alternatively, this could be construed as falling head over heels, on kāḷiyan.

**pōrkkaḷamāga niruttam seyda poygaik karaikku** – nampiḷḷai had told periyavāccān piḷḷai, about the explanation given by parāśara bhattar for this verse: On hearing that kaṇṇan has gone to the mound where kāḷiyan had taken residence, all the people from tiruvāyppādi ran to the bank of the pond, to prevent him from going there. Upon hearing “kaṇṇan has jumped on kāḷiyan’s head” people swooned in their respective place. Without anyone at the bank to prevent kaṇṇan from jumping on to kāḷiyan, the bank appeared like a battlefield, with corpses lying everywhere. nampiḷḷai would narrate another incident to establish that if people hear about a danger which had befallen someone they love, their lives should go off in a natural way and that they should not attempt to end their lives themselves – during the last days of emperumānār, his disciple anantāḻvān had come from tirumalai to see him. As he neared the northern bank \[of river kāviri\] he saw some of emperumānār’s disciples, with tonsured heads, saying “emperumānār has ascended to paramapadam” climbing up the northern bank. On hearing this, nambi guhadāasar, who had come with anantāḻvān \[to see emperumānār\] started climbing up a tree nearby to kill himself by jumping from the tree. Hearing this, anantāḻvān told him “As soon as you heard that emperumānār is no more, you should have swooned dead, instantaneously. Instead of that, when you are clear enough to climb the tree to jump from there and die, you will not die even if you jump from there. Hence, come down from the tree”.

**poygaik karaikku ennai uytidumin** – the previous explanation for this verse was that āṇḍāḷ was asking her mothers to take her to the bank of the pond so that kaṇṇapirān who has the complexion of an ocean could stroke her gently such that she could find the remedy for her disease. Alternatively, this could be construed as āṇḍāḷ telling them that instead of remaining here, with clarity, if she were carried to the bank of the pond, upon hearing the danger which came his way, she could also lose her consciousness and swoon, like the rest, on that bank.

Next, we will consider 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 12.6. Karttan mugilum

*kārttaṇ mugilum karuviḷaiyum kāyā malarum kamalap pūvumīrttiduginṟana ennai vandittu irudīkēsan pakkal pōgēyenṟuvērttup pasittu vayiṟasaindu vēṇḍadisil uṇṇum pōdu īdenṟupārttirundu nedu nōkkuk koḷḷum pattavilōcanattu uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

kār – formed during rainy season; taṇ – being cool; mugilum – cloud; karuviḷaiyum – dark blue flower from a wild creeper; kāyā malarum – dark purple flower; kamalam pūvum – lotus flower, all these; vandittu – standing in front of me; irudīkēsan pakkal pōgu enṟu ennai īrttiduginṟana – they are pulling me saying “Go towards kaṇṇa pirān”; vērttu – sweating (on account of grazing the cattle); pasittu – feeling saddened due to hunger; vayiṟu asaindu – feeling weak in the stomach; vēṇḍu adisil uṇṇum pōdu īdu enṟu – thinking “This is the time to eat the desired food”; pārttirundu – expecting the arrival of riśi patnis \[wives of sages\]; nedunōkkuk koḷḷum – the place where he was looking keenly for a long time; patta vilōcanattu – in the location called as “patta vilōcanam”; ennai uyttidumin – carry me to that place

**Перевод:**

**The dark cloud during monsoon time, the dark blue flower from a wild creeper, the dark purple flower and the lotus flower come in front of me and pull me, saying “Go towards kaṇṇa pirān”. Hence, carry me to the place called as patta vilōcanam where kaṇṇa pirān, sweating profusely due to grazing the cows, feeling saddened due to hunger, consequently feeling weak in his stomach, was looking keenly for a long time, expecting the arrival of riśi patnis so that he could eat the food which he desired.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells in this pāsuram to take her to the place where kaṇṇan was looking for food sent by riśi patnis (wives of sages).*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kārttaṇ mugilum karuviḷaiyum kāyā malarum** – the cool cloud during monsoon time, the dark blue flower from a wild creeper and the dark purple flower from the plant *kāyā*. While she wanted to cite only the cloud as a metaphor for emperumān’s divine form, since that alone was not sufficient as an allegory, she picks up several other materials as metaphors for his divine form.

**kamalap pūvum** – while she had cited several materials as allegories for his divine form, she picks up the lotus flower alone for his various limbs such as divine eyes, divine hands, divine feet, divine reddish mouth etc.

**īrttiduginṟana ennai vandittu** – coming in front of me, they pull me strongly. What do they say while pulling her?

**irudīkēsan pakkal pōgē enṟu** – just as śrī rāma followed the path of lakśmaṇa, who was dear to him, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 107-3 “*adyaivāham gamiśyāmi lakśmaṇēna gatām gatim*” (I will follow the footsteps of lakśmaṇa, today itself), after your sense organs have gone towards emperumān, who is their lord, isn’t it proper on your part too, to go behind them? īsn’t emperumān the lord for both sense organs and the jīvātmā who has them, as mentioned in the pramāṇams (authentic proofs) such as bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat ślōkam 5-7-22 “*yasyātmā śarīram*” (for that paramātmā for whom ātmā is śarīram (physical form)) and in śvētāśvatara upaniśat ślōkam 6-9 “*sa kāraṇam karaṇādipādipa*: *na cāsya kaścijjanitā na cādipa*: ll” (He \[emperumān\] alone is the causative factor for jīvān \[one with knowledge\] who is the lord of sense organs); there is none who gave birth to him; there is none who is his lord). As seen in these ślōkams, emperumān is the lord of both the sense organs and the jīvātmā which has these sense organs, as their antaryāmi, without any distinction between them. In line with the saying “*hriśīkāṇīndriyāṇyāhu*: *tēśāmīśō yatō bhavān* l *hriśīkēśa iti khyāta*: ” (The sense organs are called as hriśīkam. Since you are the head of these sense organs, you are known by the name hriśīkēśan), if the sense organs see the *svāmy* (lord) of their *svāmy* (jīvātmā), they will not stay with their svāmy, jīvātmā.

**vērttup pasittu ….. uyttidumin** – She says “Carry me to that place where everyone would think that I am going to offer him food rather than getting the blame of going after him in contrary to the *svarūpam* (basic nature) of a lady who should not go on her own and seek her beloved”. “Carry me there to prevent the blame that I had gone there in order to get rid of the pangs of separation from him. Carry me to *patta vilōcanam* where he keeps looking at intently for his food, such that I get the eminence of going there to rid him of his hunger”.

**vērttup pasittu vayiṟasaindu vēṇḍadisil uṇṇumbōdu īdenṟu pārttirundu nedunōkkuk koḷḷum** – kaṇṇan will take the cows over a long distance inside the forest to graze them. Letting them to graze in one place, he and his friends will feel tired due to the long walk undertaken by them. Added to that, hunger would force them to be weak in their stomachs. Determining that “This is the time to eat the desired food” he will send his friend to the hermitage of the sages to get food. However, the sages will be intent in their work \[of carrying out rituals to emperumān\], not bothering about the guest. Since they were intent in the conduct of their rituals, kaṇṇan would send his friend to the place where the wives of the sages would be present, asking for food. When they send the food for them with affection, since his divine physical form would start perspiring and his stomach would feel weak, he would eat that food with desire. Hence, the food became *vēṇḍadisil* (desired food sent by the wives of sages). emperumān accepts the food offered by riśis \[as *havis*\] in the ritual, while desiring a benefit, as a duty, just as svāmys, during *tiruvadyayanam* \[*śrāddam*\] accept the food, after wetting their feet. However, he accepts the food affectionately offered by the wives of *riśis*, gleefully. The food offered by *riśi patnis* is similar to the food offered with affection by the divine daughter of periyāḻvār \[āṇḍāḷ herself\], as mentioned in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 9-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/09/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-9-7/) “*inṟu vandu ittanaiyum amudu seydidappeṟil onṟu nūṟāyiramāgak koduttup pinnum āḷum seyvan*”. She says that she would like to go to *patta vilōcanam* carrying food which he desires, as was done by the *riśi patnis*, not losing out the benefit which she would get as the divine daughter of periyāḻvār.

**pārttirundu nedunōkkuk koḷḷum patta vilōcanattu uyttidumin** – Carry me to the place where he expectantly looked for a long time to see “whether food is coming”. The term *pattam* refers to cooked food. The term *vilōcanam* refers to seeing. Thus, the term *patta vilōcanam* refers to the place where he was looking for food.

Next, we will consider 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 12.7. Vannam tirivum

*vaṇṇam tirivum manam kuḻaivum mānam ilāmaiyum vāy veḷuppumuṇṇal uṟāmaiyum uḷ melivum ōdanīr vaṇṇan enbān oruvantaṇṇam tuḻāy ennum mālai koṇḍu sūttat taṇiyum pilamban tannaippaṇṇaḻiyap paladēvan venṟa pāṇḍivadattu ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇṇam tirivum – the difference in the complexion of (my) form; manam kuḻaivum – tiredness in mind; mānam ilāmaiyum – being without shame; vāy veḷuppum – mouth having become pale; uṇṇal uṟāmaiyum – not desiring food; \|uḷ melivum – knowledge having got shrunk, all these; ōdam nīr vaṇṇan enbān oruvan – kaṇṇapirān, who is unique, and famous for being with the complexion of the ocean, his; taṇ am tuḻāy ennum mālai koṇḍu sūtta – if the cool, beautiful, divine tuḷasi garland is donned; taṇiyum – will get removed; baladēvan – balarāman \[elder brother of kaṇṇan\]; pilamban tannai – the monster pralambāsuran; paṇ aḻiya – such that the joints of his bones were deformed; venṟu – the place where he was destroyed; pāṇḍi vadattu – near the banyan tree called as pāṇḍīram; ennai uyttidumin – carry me and leave me

**Перевод:**

**All the changes which I have undergone, such as losing my complexion, becoming tired in my mind, being without a sense of shame and being pale in my mouth, will get removed only if I wear the divine, beautiful, cool tuḷasi garland worn by kaṇṇapirān, who has the dark complexion of the ocean and who is unique. Since you cannot get that divine tuḷasi garland here, carry me to the banyan tree, called as pāṇḍīram, where balarāman destroyed the monster pralambāsuran in such a way that the monster’s bones were deformed.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – When her mothers and others ask her “Through which means will all these changes, which have happened in you due to separation from him, get overcome?” she says that since these will be overcome only if she dons his divine tuḷasi garland, she tells them to leave her in the place where his victorious garland has been placed.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vaṇṇam tirivum** – becoming pale in complexion due to separation.

**manam kuḻaivum** – the tiredness of heart, which normally doesn’t get tired because it has got something or it has lost something.

**mānam ilāmaiyum** – losing shame which is part of the primary qualities of ladies, *accam* (fear), *madam* (simplicity), *nāṇam* (shyness) and *payirppu* (modesty) which will normally never be lost, irrespective of whatever may happen.

**vāy veḷuppum** – just as she had said in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 1-8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/06/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-1-8/) “*vāyppuṟam veḷuttu*” (her lips, which are always reddish due to chewing betel leaves constantly, have now become pale since she has stopped using such materials of enjoyment, in separation)

**uṇṇal uṟāmaiyum** – Since kaṇṇan is the food that she takes, the water that she drinks and the betel leaves which she enjoys, she is unable to sustain herself in separation from him. Since she is not able to eat anything, her physical form has become weak.

**uḷ melivum** – Shrinking of knowledge, which is the reason for all these effects. The term *uḷ* refers to knowledge.

**ōda nīr vaṇṇan enbān oruvan** – the divine physical form \[of emperumān’s\] which is the reason for all these changes in her. Just as the ocean invigorates those which see it, his physical form too removes the ennui of his followers.

**oruvan** – he is more eminent than all the others. \[He is unique\]

**taṇ am tuḻāy ennum mālai koṇḍu sūttat taṇiyum** – just as he is more distinguished than all the others, this divine tuḷasi garland too is more distinguished than the rest. If that garland, which has connection with his divine form, is applied on me, all these changes will get negated.

**pilamban tannaip paṇṇaḻiyap paladēvan venṟa pāṇḍi vadattu ennai uyttidumin** – carry me to the place near that banyan tree, where kaṇṇapirān’s elder brother, balarāman, had destroyed the monster *pralambāsuran* such that his bone joints got disfigured completely, and where his victorious garland is still present, with which my changes will get reversed. Is it apt to call the victorious garland of balarāman’s as kaṇṇapirān’s? When iḷaiya perumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) killed indrajit \[son of rāvaṇa\], just as that victory was termed as śrī rāma’s, here too, there is no shortcoming in terming the victory of balarāman’s as kaṇṇapirān’s.

**pāṇḍivadam** – when they were grazing calves under this banyan tree, balarāman killed the monster *pralambāsuran* who came there to kill them.

**venṟa pāṇḍivadattu ennai uyttidumin** – when he coronates himself for his valour and his victory, since I too should be there so that I too could don those victorious garlands, carry me to that place and leave me there.

Next, we will consider 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.
