## 12.8. Karrinam meykkavum

*kaṟṟinam mēykkavum mēykkap peṟṟān kādu vāḻ sādiyum āgap peṟṟānpaṟṟi uralidai āppum uṇḍān pāvigāḷ! ungalukkēccuk kolōkaṟṟana pēsi vasavuṇādē kāligaḷ uyya maḻai taduttukkoṟṟak kudaiyāga ēndi ninṟa gōvarttanattu ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

kanṟu inam – herd of calves; mēykkavum mēykkap peṟṟān – carried out grazing as his work; kādu vāḻ sādiyum āgap peṟṟān – (leaving aside his house) was born in the clan of herd-people, inhabiting the forest; paṟṟi – being caught (while stealing butter); uralidai – to the mortar; āppum uṇḍān – he got tied down; pāvigāḷ! – Oh, sinners, who consider auspicious qualities as faults!; ungaḷukku – for you all; ēccukkolō – have these become the reason for finding fault?; kaṟṟana pēsi vasavu uṇādē – after speaking about whatever you have learnt, instead of hearing scolding (from me); kāligaḷ uyya maḻai taduttu – preventing heavy rains such that cows would be protected; koṟṟak kudaiyāga – as a victorious umbrella (faultless umbrella); ēndi ninṟa – being held (by kaṇṇa pirān); gōvarttanattu – in the proximity of the mountain gōvardanam; ennai uyttidumin – carry me and leave me there

**Перевод:**

**(kaṇṇa pirān) considered grazing herds of calves as his work; leaving his house, he was born in the clan of covherds who considered forest as their habitat; being caught in the act of stealing butter, he was tied down to a mortar. Instead of considering these amazing activities as the boundary for his simplicity, have these become the reason for you all to find fault with him? It does not matter as to what you have said so far. From now onvards, do not speak about what you have learnt and listen to my scolding. Carry me to the proximity of the mountain gōvardanam, where he held the mountain as a victorious umbrella to protect the cows from getting drenched in the heavy rains.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says in this pāsuram that they \[people nearby\] should not consider emperumān’s auspicious qualities as faults and get scolded by her. She tells them to carry her to gōvardana mountain. People in her proximity, looking at her anguish, thought that if they tell her about his faults, she will withdraw from her engagement with him. This pāsuram is her response to them.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kaṟṟinam mēykkavum mēykkap peṟṟān kādu vāḻ sādiyum āgap peṟṟān paṟṟi uralidai āppum uṇḍān** – when people near her told her “Misfortune will befall you! Why are you getting involved with him and suffering from anguish? īsn’t he a small boy who is running after cows and calves, thinking as if he were heading the assembly of nityasūris? Instead of permanently residing in a place, isn’t he, as a member of covherd clan, searching for forests which have water and grazing land for the cows? Apart from engaging with lowly craft of looking after cows, belonging to a lowly clan, is he not one who is stealing butter and roaming around, eating such simple food? Do you have to suffer this much in order to attain such an entity?” she tells them, repeating what they told her…

**kaṟṟinam mēykkavum mēykkap peṟṟān kādu vāḻ sādiyum āgap peṟṟān** – not being happy with being the prince in the kingdom of nityasūris, as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-6-3 “*vāniḷavarasu*” (prince of śrīvaikuṇṭam), he chose to be born in the clan of covherd people, and considered grazing cows as superior to ruling over śrīvaikuṇṭam, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-3-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/11/thiruvaimozhi-10-3-10-avaththangal-vilaiyum/) “*tivattilum pasunirai mēyppuvatti senganivāy engaḷ āyar dēvē*” (Oh, the enjoyable lord in the clan of herd-people who have beautiful lips similar to reddish fruit! Your tending to the cows is more enjoyable for you than being present in paramapadam). He considered tending to calves, which could not protect themselves like the cows, as a great benefit, as mentioned in [tirunedundāṇḍagam 16](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/08/thirunedunthandakam-16/) “*kanṟu mēyttu iniduganda kāḷāy*” (Oh the youthful person who became joyful, tending to the calves!). In order to overcome the deficiency of not having had a birth and having come on his own, he incarnated in the clan of herd-people, searching for water and grazing land for the cows. While these are his auspicious qualities, these people are terming these as his faults!

**paṟṟi uralidai āppum uṇḍān** – More than the previously mentioned qualities, should this quality, which is greater than the earlier ones, be termed as a fault? He considered butter, touched by those who are affectionate towards him, as his sustaining material. Since it was not readily available, he entered houses of his dear ones to steal the butter. Before he could eat it, he was caught by a lady, who pulled him to a mortar and he allowed her to tie him to it. śvētāśvatara upaniśad 6-16 says “*pradānakśētrajñapatir guṇēśa*: *samsāra bandastiti mōkśahētu*:” (paramātmā alone is the lord of *prakruti* (primordial matter) and *jīvan* (entity with knowledge), is full of auspicious qualities and is the reason to remain bound to samsāram as well as to get liberated from it). Thus, he, who is the reason for jīvātmās to get tied down to samsāram or to get liberated from it, could not get liberated from that mortar. Are you people calling this as a fault? When śrī rāma pirān went to the forest, there was a possibility for those who did not like him to say that he was driven away from his country by his father to the forest. Even that reason does not exist in his \[kaṇṇapirān’s\] matter.

**pāvigāḷ** – what sort of horrible sins have you people committed, to consider such auspicious qualities as sins?

**ungaḷukku ēccukkolō** – from the way you are looking at auspicious qualities as faults, it appears that you were born around the time that śiśupālan was born. While those born in our clan \[āṇḍāḷ is referring to the clan of āḻvārs\] would look at it in amazement, calling it as “*ettiṟam*” \[How amazing!\] as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-1-paththudai-adiyavar/), the same \[auspicious qualities\] has become the reason for your reviling!

**kaṟṟana pēsi vasavuṇādē kāligaḷ uyya maḻai taduttuk koṟṟak kudaiyāga ēndi ninṟa** – Do not show off all that you have learnt by speaking as you like, ending up with getting reviled by me, in return for your abuses. Wasn’t he the one who held the mountain which was near him as an umbrella to sustain the cows, which did not know the help which he rendered, as well as the covherd-people who were likewise, by removing the danger posed to them by the torrential rains! īsn’t he the one who, unmindful of his sufferings, looks at protecting others alone as his sustenance!

**kāligaḷ uyya maḻai taduttuk koṟṟakkudaiyāga ēndi ninṟa gōvarttanattu ennai uyttidumin** – when indra, who is the lord of the three worlds, started to torment them, to rid them \[herd-people and herd\] of the trouble which they faced, he held the mountain in his proximity, as an umbrella, to protect them. Carry me to that place such that instead of letting him to hold that mountain alone, I could lend him a helping hand.

Next, we will consider 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 12.9. Kuttilirundu kili

*kūttil irundu kiḷi eppōdum gōvindā! gōvindā! enṟaḻaikkumūttakkodādu seṟuppanāgil ulagaḷandān! enṟu uyarak kūvumnāttil talaippaḻi eydi ungaḷ nanmai iḻandu talaiyidādēsūttu uyar mādangaḷ sūḻndu tōnṟum tuvarāpadikku ennai uyttidumin*

**Пословный перевод:**

kiḷi – parrot (which I had nurtured); kūttil irundu – from inside the cage; eppōdum – at all times; gōvindā gōvindā enṟu aḻaikkum – was crying out \[emperumān’s name of\] gōvindā! gōvindā!; ūtta kodādu – without giving food (if I starve it); seṟuppan āgil – if I torture it; ulagu aḷandān enṟu – calling out ulagaḷanda perumāḷ! \[emperumān who measured he worlds\]; uyara kūvum – it will cry out at a high pitch; nāttil – in this world; talai paḻi eydi – earning a huge calumny; ungaḷ nanmai iḻandu – losing your reputation; talai idādē – such that you do not have to bow down your head (being unable to look at; sūdu uyar mādangaḷ sūḻndu tōnṟum – shining, surrounded by tall mansions; tuvarāapadikku – in dvārakā; ennai uyttidumin – leave me there

**Перевод:**

**The parrot, which I nurtured, staying inside its cage, will, all the time, cry out the divine name gōvinda! If I do not feed it and torture it, in a high pitch, it will cry out the divine name ulagaḷandān! I would swoon as soon as I heard these divine names. Hence, if you wish not to spoil your reputation and earn a huge blame, and do not wish to hang your heads in shame, carry me to dvārakā, which is surrounded by tall mansions.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says in this pāsuram “If you wish to escape from any blame, carry me to dvārakā and leave me there”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kūttilirundu kiḷi eppōdum gōvindā gōvindā enṟu aḻaikkum** – While āṇḍāḷ was suffering in separation from emperumān, the parrot, which she had nurtured, and to which she had taught emperumān’s divine names, started reciting his divine names at a time when she was not in a position to listen to, flying hither and thither. She caught it and put it inside a cage, thinking “Since it is roaming all over the place freely, it is tormenting me by reciting his divine names”. From inside the cage, it started crying out *gōvindā*! *gōvindā*!

**gōvindā gōvindā** – there are many other divine names such as *nārāyaṇan* for him. She too would have taught it the name of *nārāyaṇan*, as she had said in her first pāsuram in tiruppāvai “*nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān*”. The parrot did not recite any of the names which signify his *paratvam* (eminence). Knowing her secret that she would engage only with his divine names which signify his simplicity, the parrot recited his divine name of *gōvinda*, torturing her. Her heart would be melting with his habit of going behind cows and tending to them, without looking after himself. Knowing that secret, it recited that divine name, now.

**ūttakkodādu seṟuppanāgil ulagaḷandān enṟu uyarak kūvum** – When it was made to starve, by not being offered food, it started crying out loudly the divine name *ulagaḷanda perumāḷē* (Oh, one who measured all the worlds!) After it recited the divine name of gōvindā, she thought that “Since it is being fed well, it is arrogantly torturing me by reciting his divine name of *gōvinda*. If I reduce its food, it will not recite his divine name”. Thinking of the proverb *ūṇadanga vīṇadangum* (when food is reduced, arrogance will be reduced), she reduced its food. However, the result was contradictory. During the festival of *adyayana utsavam* when *araiyars* (those who recite divya prabandam in temples) apply oil to their throats and starve, their throats will become lighter and they would be able to sing in high pitch. In a similar manner, the parrot also, on being denied food, started singing in high pitch.

**uyarakkūvum** – it started singing in high pitch, as emperumān started spreading his divine feet \[to measure the worlds\]. Even when it stopped singing the divine name of gōvinda, it went to another incarnation, vāmana, which was similar to his incarnation as kriṣṇa, reciting that divine name, making me to swoon again, thus tormenting me.

**nāttil talaip paḻi eydu** – incurring the worst blame in the world. Incurring the blame “While the divine names of emperumān remove the distress for others and sustain them, they make her to get bewildered. How will this end?”

**ungaḷ nanmai iḻandu talaiyidādē** – not spoiling your reputation and not having to hang your heads in shame. Not getting the blame for your clan when people say “Instead of waiting for her beloved to come to her, in line with her *svarūpam* \[basic nature\], she went to reach out to his place on her own”. The term *talaiyidādē* refers to the situation when one, unable to look at anyone on the person’s face, hangs his head in shame.

**sūttu uyar mādangaḷ sūḻndu tōnṟum tuvarāpadikku ennai uyttidumin** – the term *sūttu* refers to forehead; here it refers to the high rise of mansions. Leave me in dvārakai which is surrounded by tall mansions. There, emperumān’s sixteen thousand consorts have a mansion each. Leave me there such that he can enjoy me too, just as he enjoys his other consorts, by building a mansion for me.

Next, we will consider last pāsuram of this decad.

## 12.10. Mannu madurai

*mannu madurai todakkamāga vaṇ tuvarāpadi tannaḷavumtannait tamaruyttup peyyavēṇḍit tāḻ kuḻalāḷ tuṇitta tuṇivaipponniyal mādam polindu tōnṟum puduvaiyarkōn vittucittan kōdaiinnisaiyāl sonna senjol mālai ētta vallavarkku idam vaigundamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāḻ kuḻalāḷ – having locks which come down well; pon iyal mādam polindu tōnṟum puduvaiyar kōn vittu cittan kōdai – āṇḍāḷ the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the leader of those who reside in śrīvilliputtūr, which is shining due to its golden mansions; mannu madurai todakkam āga vaṇ tuvarāpadi than aḷavum – in a few divine abodes (mentioned) from śrī maturā to śrī dvārakā; tannai – her, āṇḍāḷ; tamar – her relatives; uyttu peyya vēṇḍi – to carry her; tuṇitta tuṇivai – regarding her firm resolve; in isaiyāl sonna – mercifully composed with sweet tune; sem sol mālai – this tirumoḻi (divine words) which is a garland with beautiful words; ētta vallārkku – those who are capable of reciting; idam – place to reside; vaigundam – is only parama padam

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ, who has locks which come down well and who is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, the leader of those who live in śrīvilliputtūr, which is shining with mansions made of gold, had told her relatives firmly, in this tirumoḻi, to carry her to a few divine abodes from śrī maturā to śrī dvārakā. Those who are capable of reciting this tirumoḻi, mercifully composed by her with sweet tunes, as a garland with beautiful words, will reside only in parama padam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – āṇḍāḷ brings to an end, this decad in which she had firmly told her relatives to carry her to various divine abodes \[of emperumān\], by stating the benefit to those who recite this decad, which is that they will reach śrīvaikuṇṭam, through the path of arcis \[effulgence\].*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mannu madurai todakkam āga vaṇ tuvarāpadi tannaḷavum** – starting with his place of birth of maturā, which has connection with emperumān, till dvārakā where emperumān gave himself to his sixteen thousand consorts. Starting with the first pāsuram “*maduraip puṟattu*” until the ninth pāsuram “*tuvarāpadikku ennai uyttidumin*” since āṇḍāḷ speaks about his divine abodes, it could be said that she considers only these divine abodes as earth.

**mannu madurai** – has she herself not mercifully mentioned in [tiruppāvai 5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-05-mayanai/) ”*mannu vadamadurai*”? It is pertinent to recall the words mercifully mentioned in tiruppāvai mūvāyirappadi vyākhyānam “It is an abode which does not change from its connection with emperumān. Earlier, this was the *siddāśramam* \[hermitage\] of emperumān when he carried out penance for a long time during his incarnation as vāmana. Later, śatrugnāḻvān made it as his capital, after destroying his enemies. Thus, this is an abode where sarvēśvaran keeps incarnating”.

**vaṇ tuvarāpadi** – has she herself not mercifully mentioned in her [nācciyār tirumoḻi 7-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/09/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-7-9/) “*padināṟām āyiravar dēvimār parttiruppa ….. poduvāga uṇbadanai*” confirming that it is the abode where he offered himself to his sixteen thousand consorts?

**tannait tamar uyttup peyya vēṇḍi** – since she was not in a position to walk herself \[due to weakness brought about by separation\] she had to request her relatives to carry her.

**tāḻ kuḻalāḷ tuṇitta tuṇivai** – since she did not attain him, she had not kept herself impeccably; she had let her locks loose, instead of combing and tying. She had firmness in her mind that he would lose out to her condition and come her way.

**ponniyal mādam polindu tōnṟum puduvaiyar kōn vittu cittan kōdai** – śrīvilliputtūr was shining due to the golden mansions. She is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār. who was the leader of those who resided in that divine abode. The opinion here is that this abode matched the mansions which were present in the opulent dvārakā.

**innisiayāl sonna senjol mālai ētta vallārkku** – this *tirumoḻi* has been composed with words which are set to sweet tune, and which are overflowing with affection. Those who are capable of reciting this *tirumoḻi.*

**idam vaikundamē** – unlike āṇḍāḷ, who could not walk due to her distress caused by separation and who had to request her relatives to carry her to the divine abodes which he desired to reside in, those who recite this *tirumoḻi* will be welcomed by the assembly of *ātivāhikar* \[those who help the muktātmā travel in the path to parama padam, under instruction from emperumān\], will travel through the path of *arcis* and enjoy emperumān and his followers in śrī vaikuṇṭam.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam for the thirteenth decad.

## 13.1. Kannan ennum

*kaṇṇan ennum karum deyvam kātcippaḻagik kidappēnaipuṇṇil puḷippu eydāṟpōlap puṟaninṟu aḻagu pēsādēpeṇṇin varuttam aṟiyāda perumān araiyil pīdagavaṇṇa ādai koṇḍu ennai vāttam taṇiya vīsīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇan ennum – being known as śrī kriṣṇan; karu deyvam – supreme entity who is dark; kātci – in the vision; paḻagik kidappēnai – one who is fully immersed in, regarding me; puṟam ninṟu – being with a different perspective; puṇṇil puḷippu eydāṟpōla – similar to adding tamarind water to \[an already formed\] wound; aḻagu pēsādē – avoiding speaking about the benefits; peṇṇin varuttam aṟiyāda perumān araiyil – applied to the divine waist of kaṇṇa pirān, who does not know the distress of those who were born as women; pīdaga vaṇṇa ādai koṇḍu – bringing his divine yellow coloured dress; vāttam taṇiya – such that (my) withering due to separation is removed; ennai vīsīr – fan me (with that dress)

**Перевод:**

**I am fully immersed in the vision of kaṇṇa pirān, who is a dark, supreme entity. By remaining with a perspective, which is different from mine, do not speak about my benefits. This action of yours will be similar to one adding tamarind water to a wound. Bring the divine yellow dress, which kaṇṇa pirān is wearing on his divine waist, and fan me with that so that my distress of being in separation is removed.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them to bring the divine yellow vastram (dress) which he is donning on his divine waist and fan her with it so that her wilting will dimmiś.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kaṇṇan ennum karum deyvam** – a deity known as kaṇṇan. Is it not famous in the world that the meaning of “*kaṇṇan*” is one who makes himself to be subservient to others? Now, that has changed such that, despite incarnating in the species of humans, he has become a supreme deity who is out of bounds for anyone. We had been thinking all along that *kaṇṇan* is one who will be abiding by his consorts. Now, all that love has gone and he makes us to abide by him. We had been thinking that the name *kaṇṇan* would refer to one who waits at the doorstep of his consorts, waiting for a suitable time. Now, that situation has changed and he has become one among various deities who are far away from us and who fill up their stomachs. He has become one like celestial entities such as indra et al, not wishing to touch this earth with their legs, standing above it with disgust and consuming the *havis* (offerings) given in rituals by humans. Though he has got the eminence that he is under the control of those who are desirous of him, only the quality of his being a supreme entity has become factual.

**karundeyvam** – Just as his *svarūpam* (distinct quality) of being dependent on his followers has changed, should not his *svabhāvam* (general quality) also change? His dark form, which has been remaining as before and which is tormenting me, has not changed. The name kriṣṇan (one who is dark) for him, has become true now. Just as he is dark on the exterior, he is dark, inside too. The eminence that he is under the control of his followers has turned out to be a lie, as much as people say that there is an apparition on the banyan tree. He has put on an act all along that being a simple person for his followers is his basic nature.

**kātcip paḻagik kidappēnai** – I had thought that I could sustain myself by recalling my earlier times with him; however, that has increased my distress due to separating from him and made me to be bedridden, thus rendering that too to be of no use for me.

**kidappēnai** – when I am bedridden, my situation has become such bad that I cannot even turn from one side to the other. It is pertinent to recall the words of agaḷanga nāttāḻvān, one of our preceptors, who had said “When one is lying bedridden, it is not proper to remain on only one side”. Now, even embracing emperumān does not appear to be a priority. It will be proper if I could turn from left to right side, on the bed. śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 41-20 says “*yē tu rāmasya suhrudas sarvē tē mūdacētasa*: l *śōkabhārēṇa cākrāntā*: *śayanam na jahustadā* ll” (All the friends of śrī rāma, due to the stress of sorrow, felt that they have come under a huge load, with their intellect blunted, not getting out of the bed). When śrī rāma was about to set out for the forest, vasn’t the condition of his friends like this? vyākyātā periyavāccān piḷḷai has explained this ślōkam here – *yē tu* – śrī rāma’s father emperor daśarata, had allowed him to leave for the forest. kausalyā, his mother, carried out *mangaḷāśāsanam* (wishing him) for śrī rāma who was leaving for the forest. When there are eighteen languages spoken in this country, just as a few people speak in the nineteenth language, the activity of his friends was different from that of his parents. *rāmasya suhruda*: – friends of śrī rāma. āren’t friends such confidantes that one could reveal secrets to them, which have been hidden even from the parents? The opinion here is that since they were his friends, they struggled like this. This is the meaning when we consider the term *suhruda* with its arbitrary meaning *(rūdyartam*). When we consider the meaning for *suhruda* from etymology (*yaugikārtam*) as those with good hearts, it would mean that without looking after their well-being, since they were only looking for the well-being of śrī rāma, they struggled like this. *sarvē tē* – just as nityas \[permanent residents of nitya vibhūti\] and muktas \[those who have attained nitya vibhūti, after being liberated from samsāram\] are matched in their involvement with the supreme entity in nitya vibhūti, these friends of śrī rāma were matched in their behaviour of not leaving the bed, having become bewildered \[at the turn of events\]. *mūda cētasa*: – they had bewildered hearts. If they are really friends of śrī rāma, should they not have got out of their beds, fallen at his divine feet and prevented him from going to the forest? Had they retained their intellect, they would have perhaps done that. However, since they swooned on hearing that he was going to proceed to the forest, they did not do that. What is the reason for their bewilderment? *śōkabhārēṇa ākrāntā*: – they were overcome with the burden of sorrow; when they are pressed upon by sorrow, like being pressed upon by a mountain, could their intellect remain normal? *śayanam na jahustadā* – they did not get up from the bed. Had they got up from the bed, śri rāma could not have gone against their word, and that would have been equivalent to bringing him back. However, none of them could get up from the bed.

\[Now the vyākhyānam returns to the pāsuram\]

**puṇṇil puḷippu eydināṟ pōla** – just like pouring tamarind water into the mouth of a wound. The opinion is that it was creating anguish.

**puṟam ninṟu aḻagu pēsādē** – Here, the term *puṟam* does not refer to outside the door-step. Thus, āṇḍāḷ is not telling them, not to talk standing outside her door-step. Here, uniting āṇḍāḷ and kaṇṇan is *agam* (inside) and redeeming her from kaṇṇan is *puṟam* (outside). She tells them that they should only speak that which will make her unite with kaṇṇan and not speak words which will destroy her affection towards him and which will enable them to redeem her from kaṇṇan.

**aḻagu pēsādē** – here, the term *aḻagu* does not refer to his beauty. It is the benefit that they are thinking for her. When they are trying to redeem her from him, will they talk about his divine beauty? The meaning hinted here is that they should not tell her about the benefit that will come to her “if you avoid this engagement with him and look at the benefits which you will accrue”.

**pēsādē** – the opinion here is that whatever you people say will fall into my ears only as words and not as meanings of those words.

Through the latter half of the pāsuram she tells them that it would be better that they spend their efforts in finding out the remedy for her disease rather than in telling her about the benefits.

**peṇṇin varuttam aṟiyāda perumān** – when her mothers and others ask her “The two of you desire each other. Is there anything which we can do towards uniting you?” she says “Even though our desires are common, there is a difference between us in that he is a man and I am a lady. Is he not like one who does not understand the distress of a lady?” Is it not well known that \[parāśara\] bhattar had said “A man who has moustace and beard on his chin does not know the distress of a lady who has bosoms”? kaṇṇan had learnt everything from his teacher, sage sāndīpani. He would have certainly learnt that he should show mercy on those who take refuge under him. We should consider this as her terming him as one who does not understand the distress of a lady out of her urge to attain him, since she is unable to bear even the slightest delay in his coming to her.

**perumān** – just as hanuman had said of śrī rāma pirān, when he was separated from sītāp pirātti, that he was like a lord, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 15-53 “*duśkaram krutavān rāmō hīnō yadanayā prabhu* l *dārayatyātmanō dēham na śōkēnāvasīdati* ll” (Since śrī rāma pirān, living in separation from this pirātti, is sustaining his physical form, and since he has not withered away in destruction due to his distress, śrī rāma could be considered as having carried out a rare deed), she is saying that when separated from her, kaṇṇan was like a *perumān.*

**perumān araiyil pīdaga vaṇṇa ādai koṇḍu ennai vāttam taṇiya vīsīrē** – since he is a perumān, he could delay time, despite having separated from me. Without him, it is not possible for us to sustain ourselves. īsn’t our existence under his control? Even if he does not come, there is a way by which we could protect ourselves. Only for our experience, he, who is like a lord, is required. For our existence, it is enough to get a material which is connected to him. If you get that material and fan me with that, my withering away would be removed and I would be able to live. When they ask her “What is such a material? Could we bring that?” she responds, saying “The divine yellowish dress on his divine waist”. īsn’t this the time when he goes out to graze cows? When he goes like that, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-8-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/thiruvaimozhi-4-8-4-niraivinal/) “*kaṟaiyinār tuvarudukkai kadaiyāvin kaḻikōṟkaic caṟaiyinār*” (he is wearing tree bark as his dress, with stains on it; he is having a bamboo container \[to collect milk from cow\] and is wearing waist string with bells), vouldn’t he leave behind his yellow divine dress, which he wears on his waist, in order to torment the girls in śrī gōkulam, by drawing them towards him? āṇḍāḷ tells her mothers and others to bring that yellow dress and fan her with it to remove her withering.

**araiyil pīdaga ādai koṇḍu** – when nanjīyar asked bhattar “Instead of getting the yellow dress which he wears on his chest, why is she asking them to get the yellow dress which he wears on his waist and fan her with that?” bhattar explained saying “Sine she is the best among ladies, she would have desired the scent of her consort’s sweat. Thus, she is asking them to fan her with the dress worn by him on his waist”

**ennai vāttam taṇiya vīsīrē** – “If I ask you to unite me with him such that both of us could enjoy each other, that will not be possible for you; however, there is a way to get rid of my withering. Try to carry that out. Bring his dress worn on the divine waist, creating a connection for me and make me to sustain my life”

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 13.2. Pal alilaiyil

*pāl ālilaiyil tuyil koṇḍa paraman valaippattirundēnaivēlāl tunnam peydāṟpōl vēṇḍiṟṟellām seyyādēkōlāl nirai mēyttu āyanāyk kudandaik kidanda kudamādineelār taṇṇam tuḻāy koṇḍu neṟimen kuḻal mēl sūttīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāl ālilaiyil – on a tender banyan leaf, which was secreting milk \[latex\]; tuyil koṇḍa – one who mercifully reclined; paraman – perumān’s; valaiyilē – in the net; pattirundēnai – me, who has been trapped; vēlāl tunnam seydāṟpōl – just like punching a hole with spear (in a cruel manner); vēṇḍiṟṟu ellām pēsādē – avoiding speaking whatever comes to your mind; āyanāy – as a herd-boy; kōlāl – with a stick (which is typical of the clan of herd-people \[to control cattle\]); nirai mēyttu – as one who tended to herds of cows; kudandai kidanda – one who reclined mercifully in the divine abode of tirukkudandai; kudam ādi – kaṇṇapirān, who danced with pots, his; neel Ar taṇ am tuḻāy koṇḍu – with fresh, cool, beautiful, divine tuḷasi; neṟi men – having bristles and being soft; en kuḻal mēl – on my locks; sūttīrē – don it

**Перевод:**

**I have been trapped in the net of emperumān who is reclining mercifully on a tender banyan leaf, which is secreting milk. Instead of speaking whatever comes to your mind, about me, bring the fresh, cool and beautiful divine tuḷasi, donned by kaṇṇapirān, who tended to herds of cows with a stick, who reclined mercifully in tirukkudandai and who danced with pots, and don it on my locks which are bristling.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them in this pāsuram to bring his divine tuḷasi garland and don her with that.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pāl ālilaiyil tuyil koṇḍa** – Just as it has been said in mahābhāratam āraṇya parvam 188-94 “*katam nvayam śiśu śetē lōkē nāśamupāgatē* l *śākāyām vatavrukśasya pallavē tu śucismita*: ll” (How is this infant lying on a tender banyan leaf, with a smile, after all the worlds have got destroyed?) did he not mercifully recline on a tender banyan leaf which was oozing milk? Did not tirumangai āḻvār too mercifully say in [periya tirumoḻi 2-10-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2022/04/periya-thirumozhi-2-10-1/) “*ālin mēlōr iḷandaḷiril kaṇvaḷarnda īsan*” (the lord who mercifully reclined on a tender banyan leaf which was oozing milk)?

**tuyil koṇḍa** – she says that he reclined mercifully on the tender banyan leaf, which created fear in the minds of those who beheld him, as if he were sleeping with yaśōdāp pirātti’s full protection, in the cradle.

**paraman** – in terms of *aghatitaghatanā sāmartyam* (ability to bind entities which can never be tied together), even the suckling baby of yaśōdā’s is no match for the *vatadaḷaśāyi* (one who is reclining on the tender banyan leaf).

**valaippattu irundēnai** – isn’t she the divine daughter of periyāḻvār who was expressing fear in places where there was no need for being afraid of and who carried out *mangḷāśāsanam* (wishing well) for emperumān? Hence, since she was keeping her heart on the emperumān who was reclining on the tender banyan leaf, without anyone coming there to pray for his good health, she was unable to take her heart away from him and was trapped in that place itself.

**vēlāl tunnam peydāṟ pōl** – this could be construed either as making a hole with the help of a spear or as making a hole on top of the spear. In the previous pāsuram, she had spoken about *puṇṇil puḷippu eydāṟpōla*. Since the torment was more severe here, she says *vēlāl tunnam peydāṟ pōl.* If tamarind water is poured on a wound, it will lead to a burning sensation, However, if a hole were made with a spear on an injury, it will aggravate the injury, creating severe distress. She says that while she is concerned that there is none to protect him when he is reclining on the banyan leaf, their \[those in her proximity\] speaking without any restraint is causing her severe distress.

**kōlāl nirai mēyttu āyanāy** – should you not remove my fear when I am concerned with his safety on the banyan leaf, by saying that kaṇṇapirān, who is tending to cows with a stick in his hand, is a protector for himself? Should you not narrate how he became a protector for himself and remove my fear? Unlike śrī rāma, who held a bow in his hand and protected the sages in forest, does he \[kaṇṇa pirān\] not have the simplicity of having a stick in his hand and protecting the cows with it!

**kudandaik kidanda kudamādi** – just as it has been mentioned in [siṟiya tirumadal](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2020/03/siriya-thirumadal-11/) “*sīrār kudam iraṇḍu ēndic ceḻum teruvē ārār enac colli ādum*” (Holding two distinguished pots in his divine hand, going through this huge street, crying out “Is there anyone who could escape from these pots?” dancing with the pots), he manifested his divine form such that everyone could enjoy it exclusively. Due to the tiredness resulting from his dancing with the pots, he reached kudandai and reclined there mercifully.

**neelār taṇṇam tuḻāy koṇḍu en neṟi menkuḻal mēl sūttīrē** – bring the fresh, beautiful divine tuḷasi and don it on my locks. The freshness for this tuḷasi is that it has been proffered by those who do not desire any benefit from emperumān. In other words, those such as periyāḻvār, who had proffered it to him – singing pallāṇḍu with his mouth and proffering tuḷasi with his hand. Did not tirumangai āḻvār too mercifully say in periya tirumoḻi 11-1-9 “*mikka sīrttoṇḍar itta pūnduḷavin vāsam*” (The fragrance of divine tuḷasi proffered by śrīvaiṣṇavas who do not desire any other benefit)? Followers of emperumān are classified into three types – (1) *toṇḍar* – those who show affection in return for a lowly benefit, as mentioned by periyāḻvār in [tiruppallāṇḍu 3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-3-vazhatpattu/) “*kūḻātpattu ninṟīr*”, (2) *sīrt toṇḍar* – the upāsaka who follows the path of devotion in order to attain mōkśam and (3) *mikka sīrt toṇḍar* – those who are like periyāḻvār who do not desire anything from him but who are concerned with his well-being and carry out *mangaḷāśāsanam* for him. The tuḷasi proffered by those who expect some other benefit from emperumān would be singed by the fault in their thoughts, just as it has been mentioned in yajur 5-3 “*dēhi mē dadāmi tē*” (Oh, deity! Grant me the benefit; I am offering you the *havis* (offering made in a ritual)).

**neṟimen kuḻal mēl** – don it on my soft locks which are bristling. Alternatively, the term *men* could be construed as referring to a shortcoming and the meaning would change as – don it on my bristling locks which have not been taken care of.

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 13.3. Kanjaik kaynda

*kanjaik kāynda karuvilli kadaikkaṇ ennum siṟaikkōlālnenju ūduruva vēvuṇḍu nilaiyum taḷarndu naivēnaianjēl ennān avan oruvan avan mārvil aṇinda vanamālaivanjiyādē tarumāgil mārvil koṇarndu purattīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kanjai – kamsan; kāyndu – one who destroyed him; karuvilli – kaṇṇapirān who has eyebrows, resembling a huge bow; kadaikkaṇ ennum – called as glance; siṟaik kōlāl – arrow which has wings; nenju ūduruva – throughout the heart; (E)vēvuṇḍu – getting singed (after being shot at); nilaiyum taḷarndu – becoming ruined; naivēnai – looking at me, who is suffering; anjēl ennān avan oruvan – not mentioning a word “Do not be afraid”, belonging to another species; avan – that emperumān; mārvu aṇinda – donned on his divine chest; vanamālai – divine garland; vanjiyādē – without deceit; tarum āgil – if he were to give mercifully; koṇarndu – bringing (that garland); mārvil – on (my) chest; purattīr – roll it (such that the heat disappears)

**Перевод:**

**I am suffering after being shot at my heart fully, by the winged arrow of the glance of kaṇṇapirān, who has eyebrows looking like a huge bow and who destroyed kamsan. My heart has become singed and I feel like I am ruined. He did not mention even a word “Do not be afraid”. If he were to give his vanamālai, which he has donned on his chest, bring it and roll it on my chest such that the heat disappears.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – my heart has become burnt after being targeted by the arrow of his glance. Bring the vanamālai \[divine garland worn by emperumān\] which he is wearing on his divine chest and roll it on my bosom such that my heart cools down.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kanjaik kāynda** – kamsan had organised a festival of bows and invited kaṇṇa pirān to it. He had planned to kill kaṇṇan through his royal elephant, kuvalayāpeedam, wrestlers such as cāṇura and muśtika and in case kaṇṇan were destroyed, he wanted to cry in deceit that “By mistake they have killed my nephew!”. However, kaṇṇan killed him such that he perished along with his thought.

**karuvilli** – a man with a huge bow. Since he had come for taking part in the festival of bows, she is describing him this way. Alternatively, this can be construed as one who has eyebrows like a huge bow in order to torment women like me.

**kadaikkaṇ ennum** – those who are in love, will look at others, straight into their eyes. They will glance at only those with whom they are in love. This is similar to the owner of an orchard, who glances at his orchard with pride. Is it not said famously “*tādruśya*: *kalu muktakaṇṭamaparān paśyanti na svīkrutān*” (noble women will look at others with a straight look; they do not look at their husbands like that (they will only glance at them))?

**karuvilli kadaikkaṇ ennum siṟaik kōlāl** – don’t poets describe eyebrows as bows and eyes as arrows!

**siṟaikkōlāl** – arrow, which is like a stick, having wings. *kaḻisiṟai* (which has meanings of arrow as well as a wicked person) will not be visible when it comes. Only upon seeing the targeted persons falling, would one know of its presence. It \[the arrow\] could be prevented if seen. However, since it is shot, unseen, it is not possible to prevent it from reaching its target. If kaṇṇapirān were to look at her in a straightforward way, she could avoid by not being the target. Since he is glancing at her, it is not possible to go outside his vision.

**nenju ūduruva vēvuṇḍu** – this could be separated as either *nenju ūduruva ēvuṇḍu* or as *nenju ūduruva vēvuṇḍu*. The term *ēvuṇḍu* would be appropriate for saying \[that his glance is like an\] arrow.

**ēvuṇḍu nilaiyum taḷarndu** – what is the effect of being struck by the arrow? Instead of falling down after four feet, she fell at the same place. This reflects on her strength and the power of his glance. There are various types of *upāsakars* (those who attempt to attain emperumān through the path of devotion, on their own efforts) – those who are firmly rooted in the path of *sadvidyā* or those who are firmly rooted in the path of *dahara vidyā* etc. In that classification, these people \[who are like kaṇṇa pirān who win over their targets through their glance\] could be termed as *nōkku vidyā niśtar* \[those who are firmly rooted in the path of glance\].

**naivēnai** – me, who has had her bones and joints in a state of debilitation.

**anjēl ennān** – if he knew the cruelty of his arrow having pierced me, should he not have come running and embraced me saying “Don’t be afraid”?

**avan oruvan** – unable to bear the suffering of vāli, having fallen down after being pierced by his arrow, śrī rāmapirān shed tears, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 24-24 “*ityēvam artasya raghupravīra*: *śrutvā vacō vālyanujasya tasya* l *sanjātabhāśpa*: *paravīrahantā rāmō muhūrtam vimanā babhūva* ll” (Hearing the words of sugrīva, the brother of vāli and who was very much in distress, śrī rāma, who annihilates warriors who are his enemies, shed tears and was bewildered in his mind for a *muhūrtam* (about 48 minutes)). That śrī rāman, who was an ocean of compassion, was a person; this kaṇṇan, who becomes happy after seeing those who have been targeted by his glance tremble in distress, is also a person!

**avan mārvaṇinda vanamālai** – it is not some garland which would satiate her. Her desire is to get the garland which he donned with desire.

**vanjiyādē tarum āgil** – after you go and inform him of my condition, if he would give that \[divine garland\] without deceiving you just as he deceived me through his smile and glance; the meaning hinted is that if he would not torment you similar to the way in which he tormented me.

**mārvil koṇarndu purattīrē** – roll the divine garland which he had donned on his divine chest, on my chest which is throbbing after having been pierced by his glance. Is it not on the spot of injury that the medicine has to be applied! Simulating the *vanamālai* for his chest with which she wanted to embrace tightly is to be appreciated.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 13.4. Are ulagattu

*ārē ulagattu āṟṟuvār āyarpādi kavarndu uṇṇumkār ēṟu uḻakka uḻakkuṇḍu taḷarndum muṟindum kidappēnaiārāvamudam anaiyān tan amuda vāyil ūṟiyanīrdān koṇarndu pularāmē parukki iḷaippai nīkkīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āyarpādi – entire tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam); kavarndu uṇṇum – one who had stolen and enjoyed; kār ēṟu – kaṇṇan who is like a dark bull; uḻakka – as he tormented; uḻakkuṇḍu – (due to that) getting distressed; taḷarndum muṟindum kidappēnai – me, who has been distressed in many ways; ulagattu – in this world; āṟṟuvār – one who consoles; Ar – who is there? (There is none); ārā amudam anaiyān than – kaṇṇapirān, who is like a nectar, even after enjoying whom however much one could, one will not get satiated, his; amuda vāyil – in the divine mouth where nectar secretes; ūṟiya – remaining, after secretion; nīrdān – that juice, at the least; koṇarndu – bringing it; parukki – making me drink it such that my body does not wither away; iḷaippai nīkkīr – try to remove my fatigue

**Перевод:**

**I am suffering after being tormented by kaṇṇan, who is like a dark bull and who enjoys entire tiruvāyppādi after stealing and who is tormenting me. Who is there in this world who would comfort me? There is none. He is one who will never bestow satisfaction, however much one enjoys him. \[Addressing mothers and others in proximity) Bring the nectar-like secretion which remains in his divine mouth and make me drink it such that my physical form will be prevented from withering away.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them to make her imbibe the divine nectar from the mouth of mādavan and remove her fatigue.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ārē ulagattu āṟṟuvār** – sītāp pirātti tells hanuman in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-30 “*śaraistu sankulām krutvā lankām parabalārdana*: l *mām nayēd yadi kākutstas tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt* ll” (If śrī rāmapirān, who annihilates the strength of his enemies, bewilders lankā with his arrows and takes me with him, it will be apt for him). Is it possible for sītāp pirātti, who was looking at her *svarūpam* (basic nature of being totally dependent on emperumān) to comfort me? sītāp pirātti was not bewildered, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 19-4 “*rāmasya vyavasāyajñā lakśmaṇasya ca dīmata*: l *nātyartam kśubhyatē dēvī gangēva jaladāgamē* ll” (Since \[sītāp pirātti\[\] knew the firmness of śrī rāma and that of lakśmaṇa, who is wise, dēvi did not get muddled even a little bit, just like gangā doesn’t, during monsoon time when it rains); as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-33, when śrī rāma tells sugrīva “*sakrudēva prapannāya tavāsmīti ca yācatē* l *abhayam sarvabhūtēbhyō dadami ētat vratam mama* ll” (To the person who surrenders to me once and to the person who tells me ‘I am your servitor’ I offer protection from all entities alive. This is my solemn vow) and as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 5-93 “*avagarahyārṇavam svapsyē saumitrē* ! *bhavatā vinā* l *katancit prajvalan kāma*: *sa mām suptam jalē dahēt* ll” (Oh lakśmaṇa! (Since looking at you reminds me of sītā) I am going to leave you and enter the ocean to get some sleep. The fire of love, which is singeing, will burn me, who is going to sleep in the waters, with difficulty only). Since sītāp pirātti knew well the auspicious qualities of śrī rāma, who has taken a solemn vow to protect those who take refuge under him, she was able to sustain herself. Has śrī rāma not said often that he will not give up on those who have a little bit of affection for him and that if he had to give up on them, he would not sustain his life any longer? Has he not told sītāp pirātti herself, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 10-19 “*apyaham jīvitam jahyām tvām vā sītē sa lakśmaṇām* l *na tu pratijñām samśrutaya brāhamaṇēbhyō viśēśata*: ll” (Oh sitā! I may give up my life, you and lakśmaṇa too; but I will not give up on my solemn vow, especially the promise given to brāhmaṇas). Thus, there is no shortcoming in sītāp pirātti sustaining herself. The opinion is that kaṇṇan is not like that.

In the śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam seen earlier “*rāmasya vyavasāyajñā*” the meaning for the term “*lakśmaṇasya ca dīmata*:” has been explained. Even when śrī rāma went after the illusory deer, swayed by his consort sītāp pirātti’s desire, not knowing that it is the demon mārīcan in disguise, iḷaiya perumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) was clear in his mind that it was only an illusion and not a real deer. Since sītāp pirātti knew the firmness of lakśmaṇa, who was wise, she did not become bewildered. The meaning for the term “*nātyartam kśubhyatē dēvī*” (sītāp pirātti was not bewildered even a little bit) is – Since she was very dear to śrī rāma such that he was proud of her, even though there were reasons for her to be bewildered, she did not get bewildered. This is the reason that she was able to sustain herself even in a situation when she could have lost her life. The meaning for the term “*gangēva jaladāgamē*” (like river gangā during rains) is – just as river gangā does not become muddled even if it rains incessantly, sītāp pirātti was not muddled during her distressed time.

**āyarpādi kavarnduṇṇum kārēṟu uḻakka uḻakkuṇḍu** – just as sītāp pirātti was able to sustain herself, by meditating on the auspicious qualities of śrī rāma, is it possible for those who were hurt by the misciefs of kaṇṇan? There were five lakh families in śrī gōkulam. When kaṇṇan diverts all the love that he had towards all the girls in those families, towards me alone, how could I survive?

**taḷarndum muṟindum kidappēnai** – just as he, who is like a dark bull, tormented the girls in five lakh families of śrī gōkulam by stealing their hearts, he trampled my heart too, without any compassion, in such a manner that my bones and joints have become weakened, making people to say “Hereafter, we cannot see her in one piece”. I have been reduced to such a state.

**ārāvamudanaiyān than amuda vāyil ūṟiya nīr** – In the sanjīvani hill which hanuman, in śrī rāmāyaṇam, had brought, there were two herbs, called as *vicalyakaraṇi* and *sandānakaraṇi.* Of these two, while *vicalyakaraṇi* removed the arrows which had pierced the bodies of the army of monkeys, *sandānakaraṇi* set right the injuries created by the arrows. She \[āṇḍāḷ\] desires kaṇṇan, who is like an insatiable nectar, to be *vicalyakaraṇi* and desires the nectar-like water secreted in his divine mouth as *sandānakaraṇi*. Alternatively, we could construe this as āṇḍāḷ considering kaṇṇan, who is *ārāvamudamanaiyān* (insatiable nectar), himself as both *vicalyakaraṇi* and *sandānakaraṇi*.

**than amudavāyil ūṟiya nīrdān koṇarndu** – She had said in the previous pāsuram “*mārvil koṇarndu purattīr*”. She says that even if they could not get a garland which had connection with several limbs in his divine form, they could get the divine water from one divine limb of his – the divine mouth.

**pularāmē parukki** – sprinkling that divine water on my physical form such that it does not wither away and making me to drink it. Just like saliva is a medicine for snake-bite etc, she says that for her disease too, the divine water secreted in kaṇṇan’s divine mouth is a medicine.

**iḷaippai nīkkīrē** – for my condition, he should himself be brought. Since that could not be done, she tells them to bring, at the least, this divine water and remove her fatigue. Just as saliva is a medicine for a poisonous bite, for her disease too, isn’t his saliva a medicine?

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 13.5. Alilum tolilum

*aḻilum toḻilum urukkāttān anjēl ennān avan oruvantaḻuvi muḻusip pugundu ennaic cuṟṟic cuḻanṟu pōgānāltaḻaiyin poḻil vāy niraippinnē nedumāl ūdi varuginṟakuḻalin tuḷai vāy nīr koṇḍu kuḷira mugattut tadavīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aḻilum – even if I cry; toḻilum – or if I worship him; urukkāttān – one who does not manifest his divine form; anjēl ennān avan – (even if he does not manifest his form) one who does not even say “Do not fear”; oruvan – a great entity (kaṇṇapirān); pugundu – coming here; ennait taḻuvi muḻusi – embracing me tightly; suṟṟic cuḻanṟu – coming in front and going behind me; pōgān – he is not leaving; Al! – alas!; taḻaiyin poḻil vāy – under the orchard of peacock feathers; nirai pinnē – behind the herds of cows; nedumāl – kaṇṇapirān, who has deep love; ūdi varuginṟa – dripping from blowing; kuḻalin tuḷai vāy – formed from the holes in \[his\] flute; nīr koṇḍu – bringing the water; mugattu – on my face; kuḷira tadavīr – apply it, in a comfortable manner

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them in this pāsuram “Even if the divine nectar from his mouth, which is exclusively mine, is not made available for me, bring the water which flows through the holes in his flute, which would otherwise fall on the ground, and apply it on my face”.*

**aḻilum toḻilum urukkāttān** – whether I cry out shedding tears, or I worship him, he is not manifesting his divine form. In *svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning) the term *aḻilum*, being one of the indicators for *bhakta lakśaṇam* (indicators of devotion), refers to *bhakti* (devotion) and t*hoḻilum*, being an act of *namaskāram* (performing *anjali* or cupping the palms together as a mark of salutation) refers to *prapatti* (act of surrendering to emperumān). Whether one follows the mode of devotion which is self-propelled, though it is against one’s *svarūpam* (basic nature) or the mode of *prapatti* which will never fail, like a *brahmāstram* (an ultimate weapon), he is not manifesting his divine form. *aḻilum*– even if one were to cry and be obstinate, he does not show himself up. *toḻilum* – beyond that, while he should worship me, even when I carry out the daring act of vorśipping him, he is not showing his form.

**toḻilum urukkāttān** – is this the way by which he follows what has been said in śāstram (religious treatise) such as jitantē stōtram 1-5 “*na tē rūpam na ca ākārō na āyudāni na cāspatam* l *tatā’pi puruśākārō bhaktānām tvam prakāśasē* ll” ((your) divine basic nature is not for you; the divine auspicious form is also not for you; the divine weapons also are not for you; the abode of śrī vaikuṇṭam is also not for you. All these are for the sake of your followers only. You belong to your followers. Though you have *svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam* and *vibhūti* (basic nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth) which are for your followers, you shine as the supreme entity)? While mahābhāratam udyōga parvam 42-35 says “*yō’nyatā santamātmānam anyatā pratipadyatē* l *kim tēna na krutam pāpam cōrēṇātmāpahāriṇā* ll” (What sin has not been committed by one who covets ātmā thinking that it belongs to him, while it is the possession of bhagavān?), clarifying what happens when a jīvan (entity with knowledge) steals the ātmā, he \[emperumān\] is stealing the ātmā (his divine form) which belongs to his followers! While śāstram has denoted both *bhakti* and *prapatti* as separate means \[to attain emperumān\], did not āḻvārs remove the distinction between these two by saying that their devotion itself is the act of surrendering and their act of surrendering itself is devotion? While śāstrams have said that both the *svarūpam* (basic nature) and *tirumēni* (his divine form) are to be considered as acceptable, did not āḻvārs clarify firmly that emperumān’s *tirumēni* and the qualities present in it are more acceptable than his *svarūpam* and the qualities present in it? This is the reason why āṇḍāḷ, who is considered as the heir to the clan of āḻvārs and who had exceeded the deeds of all the other āḻvārs, had said that both *aḻilum* and *toḻilum* which represent devotion and act of surrendering are same to her, without any distinction between them and that emperumān’s divine physical form alone is acceptable to her \[when she said that he is not manifesting his divine form through the term *urukkāttān*\]. Devotion, as mentioned in śāstrams, is to be carried out as a means for attaining emperumān while devotion for these āḻvārs is not like that. This comes to them as a result of anguish of not experiencing him. While śāstrams have mentioned that qualities of *svarūpam* are apt to be accepted, for āḻvārs who have overflowing affection towards emperumān, there is nothing beyond the divine form. Haven’t they said this in their pāsurams such that the entire world would know about them? īsn’t it for this reason that āḻvārs attained the pathos of a *nāyaki* (consort of emperumān) and enjoyed him as his beloveds? If this is the condition for āḻvārs who were born as men and had to take on the pathos of a lady should we even talk about āṇḍāḷ who was born naturally as a lady and had said “*mānidavarkkenṟu pēccuppadil vāḻagillēn*” (I will not live if anyone talks about marrying me off to a human entity)!

**anjēl ennān avan oruvan** – Oh friends! Let it be that he does not know the sufferings of those who have got separated from him. He should have, at the least, known about the nature of his divine form. If he had known about the quality of his divine form which is that “Those who have separated from us will not be able to sustain themselves”, śouldn’t he have come immediately and embraced me?

**avan oruvan** – in the third pāsuram, in the verse “*anjēl ennān avan orvan*” it was mentioned about the poor disposition of kaṇṇapirān’s qualities vis-à-vis śrī rāma’s, who is sweet to the heart. Here, through this verse, it shows that despite being born as kaṇṇan “He had not known about his divine physical form”. Hadn’t āṇḍāḷ said in the first pāsuram of this decad “*kaṇṇan ennum karum deyvam*”, mentioning that he had come to manifest the beauty of his eyes only for namesake, and that he remained with the eminence of his being a deity.

**taḻuvi** – embracing all my limbs such that the pangs of separation, which I had, would get negated. śri viṣṇu purāṇam 5-18-2 says “*sō’pyēnam dvajavajrābja kruta cihnēna pāṇinā* l *samspruśyākruśya ca prītyā sugādam pariśasvajē* ll” (that kriṣṇa bhagavān too, with his divine hands identified with flag, *vajra* (diamond) and lotus leaves, pulled akrūra \[a minister in kamsan’s court\] and embraced him well, affectionately). āṇḍāḷ desires that just as he embraced akrūra well such that he was not even seen, emperumān should embrace her too.

**muḻusi** – she desires that after he embraces her, he should smell the top of her head with his nose such that his nose gets caught in her locks, being unable to retrieve himself.

**pugundu ennaic cuṟṟic cuḻanṟu** – after smelling her locks on top of her head, unable to involve himself with anything else, he should remain in her proximity, going around her.

**pōgānāl** – after enjoying like this in union, unless there is a gap, it will be difficult to sustain. These are the steps which take place in their own order, during union. She had desired that all these should happen in her case, but is in distress since he did not do these. Alternatively, it could be construed that due to the maturity in her thoughts, she imagines that she is able to envision him in her mind, making her anguished that she is not able to enjoy him in person, tormenting her, and at the same time, not leaving her too.

**taḻaiyin poḻil vāy niraippinnē** – even when he comes in person, he comes as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-4-1 “*taḻaigaḷum tongalum tadumbi*” (surrounded on all four sides with umbrellas made of peacock feathers). *taḻai* – peacock feather. *taḻaiyil poḻil* refers to an orchard of such feathers decked on the head, while he is coming. The term *niraippinnē* refers to his coming behind the cattle. While returning from grazing, instead of coming in front of the cattle, he would come right at the end, to demonstrate that he grazes all these cattle. This is the way he decks himself up, while returning in the evening, in order to get rid of the ennui of having had to graze these cattle during the noon time.

**nedumāl** – when he returns, just like a huge pond has got muddled up, he would come, manifesting his affection for the girls. While he comes right at the end to see the girls who are suffering in separation from him through the daytime, he would tell them “I too have my parents. I had to go after the cows to graze them, in line with the task given by my parents. Now, I will not separate from you; should I separate, I cannot sustain my life”

**ūdi varuginṟa** – the way he plays on the flute, displaying his affection for them, would be such that the girls would on their own seek him out and embrace him.

**kuḻalin tuḷaivāy nīr koṇḍu** – if you give all the divine nectar-like water which flows out of the holes in the flute, then too I will not be able to sustain my life. She tells them to give a little bit.

**kuḷir mugattut tadavīrē** – she tells them to get that divine nectar from the flute and smear it on her face such that her fatigue gets removed.

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 13.6. Nadai onrilla

*nadai onṟillā ulagattu nandagōpan magan ennumkodiya kadiya tirumālāl kuḷappuk kūṟu koḷappattuppudaiyum peyarā killēn nān pōtkan miditta adippāttilpodittān koṇarndu pūsirgaḷ pōgā uyir ennudambaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nadai onṟu illā ulagattu – in this world where all the boundaries have \[already\] been demolished; nandagōpan magan ennum – being famous as the son of śrī nandagōpar; kodiya – without compassion; kadiya – being selfish; tirumālāl – by the consort of śrī mahālakśmi (kaṇṇan); nān – I, who am weak; kuḷappuk kūṟu koḷappattu – having been tormented to a great extent; pudai peyaravum killēn – I do not have the strength even to move from one side to another; pōtkan – that kaṇṇan, who is devoid of shame; miditta adippāttil – in the place where his divine foot had pressed; podi dān – at the least, the śrīpādadūḷi (dust from under the divine foot); koṇarndu – bringing it; pōgā uyir en udambai – on my physical form, which has life unwilling to go; pūsīrgaḷ – please apply

**Перевод:**

**In this world, where boundaries have already been demolished, I, who am weak, have been tormented to a very great extent by kaṇṇapirān, who is without any compassion, who is selfish, who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi and who is famously known as the son of śrī nandagōpar. I am without strength even to move from one side to the other. This being the case, bring the śrīpādadūḷi from the place where he had stepped with his divine foot and apply it on my physical form, from which my life is unwilling to depart.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them to apply the \[sand\] particles from under the divine feet of that kaṇṇan, who is a shameless person.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nadai onṟillā ulagattu** – *nadai* refers to limit, boundary. Already, in this world, the boundary for what could be done, by whom / what should not be done has mostly got demolished. After kaṇṇan was born, and the way he tormented people like me, the crossing of boundary has become ten times more severe. During this time, when I am unable to even move, if he comes in front of me, all the boundaries would get restored to their normal levels.

**nandagōpan magan ennum** – How did a person like kaṇṇan become nandagōpar’s son? When there was no love-quarrel \[between āṇḍāḷ and kaṇṇan\], she had said in [tiruppāvai 17](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-17-ambarame-thannire/) “*emperumān* *nandagōpālā eḻundirāy*” calling him with respect, since he was the father of kaṇṇan. Now, when she is having a love quarrel with kaṇṇan, she is speaking despicably about the relationship between nandagōpar and kaṇṇan, by asking as to how he sired such a son. When she was unable to tolerate her anguish, she said, as in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 12-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/10/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-12-3/) “*nandagōpālan kadaittalaikkē naḷḷīrutkaṇ ennai uyttidumin*”; when she considered nandagōpan as a righteous person, who was proffering nourishment, sustenance and enjoyment, she said in [tiruppāvai 17](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppavai-17-ambarame-thannire/) ”*ambaramē taṇṇīrē sōṟē aṟam seyyum emperumān nandagōpālā*”. It was the entrance to the house of such nandagōpālan that she wanted to be carried and left. He was one with great compassion, similar to one of our preceptors, siṟiyāttān, who could not tolerate even a blade of grass being stepped upon.

**kodiya** – It is not only that. Even if terms of being without compassion, he has gone beyond anyone else.

**kadiya** – even among selfish people, there will be people who will only look after their interests and will not harm others. However, kaṇṇan is not like that. He would not bother about how others are getting harmed; he will be only focussed on getting his interests fulfilled.

**tirumālāl** – yaśōdā and nandagōpar, who assists her, have given birth to him who is without compassion and is selfish, just as she \[āṇḍāḷ\] had said in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 3-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/07/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-3-9/) “*anja urappāḷ asōdai āṇāda vittirukkum*” and in nācciyār tirumoḻi 13-7 “*peṟṟa tāy vēmbēyāga vaḷarttāḷē*”. Further, just as it has been mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 8-10-1 “*vaṇḍār pūmāmalar mangai maṇanōkkam uṇḍānē*” (oh, one who has as your sustenance, the divine glance, with happiness, of periya pirāttiyār who was born in a beautiful, distinguished lotus flower which is full of beetles), he has been nurtured by the glance of periya pirāttiyār and brought up as an entity without compassion and selfishness. Hence, āṇḍāḷ says that there is no point in despising him. Since the context is one of talking harshly due to the pangs of separation, she points out to those who are *puruśakāram* (recommending entities) and says that they are the ones who are responsible for her pangs of separation, despising them. ḍidn’t nammāḻvār to despise like this, as said in tiruvāimoḻi 1-4-7 “*aruḷāda tirumālārkku*” (emperumān who is dear to periya pirāttiyār and not blessing me))? When explaining this sequence, our pūrvācāryars \[preceptors\] used to say that āṇḍāḷ, by saying “*tirumālāl*” is meditating on periya pirāttiyār as *puruśakāra bhūtai* (recommending entity). nanjīyar had asked piḷḷān “When talking about his absence of compassion, meditating on her \[periya pirāttiyār’s\] recommendatory role is not fitting properly. What is the meaning for this?” piḷḷān responded, saying “She \[āṇḍāḷ\] despises pirāttiyār too since she thinks that emperumān has learnt about tormenting her from pirāttiyār” When nanjīyar apprised bhattar about this, bhattar told him “When āṇḍāḷ considers that it is pirāttiyār who is the cause for attaining emperumān, there is no deficiency in thinking that she is the cause when emperumān is not attained too”, affirming piḷḷān’s explanation.

**kuḷappukkūṟu koḷappattu** – In the fourth pāsuram of this decad, she had spoken about how kaṇṇan, as a bull, had tormented her through the verse “*kārēṟu uḻakka uḻakkuṇḍu*”. Here, since āṇḍāḷ does not have any refuge and since there is no one else available for this bull to torment, she speaks about the bull trampling her on the place where she had been already trampled upon.

**pudaiyum peyaragillēn** – her condition now is such that it is not necessary for her even to see him. It is enough if she were able to move from side to side.

**nān** – I am suffering on account of kaṇṇan, just as much śrī rāma had suffered on account of sītāp pirātti, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam, sundara kāṇḍam 36-41 & 42 “*na māmsam rāghavō bhunktē na cāpi madu sēvatē* l *naiva damśānna maśakān na kītān na sarīsrupān* ll *rāghavō’panayēt gātrāt tvadgatēnāntarātmanā* ll” (Oh dēvi (divine consort of emperumān)! śrī rāma, who has a divine mind which is fully engaged with you, does not eat meat; does not take intoxicating materials; does not push away insects such as flies, mosquitoes, worms etc as well as snakes (which crawl over his divine form)). It appears that if she heard that kaṇṇan too was suffering, being unable to turn from side to side, she vouldn’t be suffering! However, that kaṇṇan, being a shameless person, would not suffer like śrī rāma did. īsn’t he of the type who would make various others too to suffer on account of him?

**pōtkan miditta adippāttil podittān koṇarndu pūsīrgaḷ** – when he goes like this \[to torment others\] in order to captivate them, it is enough to carry the *ammān podi* as mentioned in [nācciyār tirumoḻi pāsuram 2-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/06/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-2-4/) “*maiyalēṟṟi mayakka unmugam māyamandiram tān kolō*” (is your face a magical powder to blunt our knowledge and to make us get infatuated with you?). He doesn’t need the sand particles from the place where he had stepped on with his divine feet. It is required only for me, to sustain my life. Thus, just as I am telling you, bring those sand particles from underneath his divine feet and apply them on me so that I will be rid of my disease.

**adippāttil podi** – his path is to go after girls and to harass them. Her path is also going after his path in order to get the particles from the places where he had stepped on, when he goes in search of girls to torment them.

**pōgā uyir en udambaiyē** – even if I desire, my life is refusing to leave my physical form. Hence, if someone could bring a weapon or poison and sever my life, that would be a deed of righteousness. When asked “Could you not do that yourself?” she says “Since my body is his possession, even if I desire, I do not have the *svātantryam* (independence) to get rid of my life. I have to sustain this for his sake”. Did not sītāp pirātti complain that there was none to give her a weapon or poison to finish off her life, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 28-16 “*sā jīvitam kśipramaham tyajēyam viśēṇa śastrēṇa śītēna vāpi* l *viśasya dātā na hi mē’sti kaścit śastrasya vā vēśmani rākśasasya* ll” (I, who am like that, am going to give up my life through a sharp weapon or poison. (But) in the house of demon, there is none who would offer me a weapon or poison)? Did she not indicate to hanuman, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 36-30? “*na cāsya mātā na pitā na cānya*: *snēhād viśiśtōsti mayā samē vā* l *tāvat tvaham dūta jijīviśēyam yāvat pravruttim śruṇuyām priyasya* (None, including his mother and father, has greater or same love towards that śrī rāma pirān as I. Oh messenger! I desire to live only until the time that I hear that my dear one is making efforts to redeem me).

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 13.7. Verrik kurulakkodi

*veṟṟik karuḷakkodiyān tan mīmīdādā ulagattuveṟṟa veṟidē peṟṟa tāy vēmbēyāga vaḷarttāḷēkuṟṟamaṟṟa mulai tannaik kumaran kōlap paṇaittōḷōduaṟṟa kuṟṟam avai tīra aṇaiya amukkik kattīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

karuḷan veṟṟik kodiyān than mīmīdu ādā ulagattu – in this world where the order of emperumān, who has periya tiruvadi (garuda) as his victorious flag, is not transgressed; peṟṟa tāy – yaśōdā, who gave birth to him; veṟṟa veṟidē – without being of any benefit to anyone; vēmbu āgavē – being bitter, like the neem seed; vaḷarttāḷ – nurtured him; kuṟṟam aṟṟa – without the fault (of desiring anyone other than that kaṇṇan); mulai tannai – (my) bosoms; kumaran – that perumān who is youthful, his; kōlam paṇai tōḷōdu – divine shoulders which are beautiful and which are like the branches of kaṟpaga tree (celestial tree); aṟṟa kuṟṟam avai tīra – (giving up on me) to get rid of the fault of existing only for them (those shoulders); aṇaiya amukkik kattīr – tie firmly to hug them

**Перевод:**

**In this world, where the order of emperumān, who has garuda as his victorious flag, is not transgressed, yaśōdāp pirātti nurtured her son kaṇṇan such that he was of no benefit to anyone and was also bitter like the neem seed. Take my bosoms, which do not have any fault, and tie them firmly to the beautiful shoulders of that kaṇṇan, which are like the branches of kaṟpagam tree, in such a manner that they \[the bosoms\] get rid of the fault of existing only for his shoulders.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she tells them in this pāsuram “Even if he doesn’t come head over heels, on his own, to embrace me just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10 “ciram jīvati vaidēhi yadi māsam dariśyati l na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi vinā tāmasitēkśaṇām ll” (If sītai could sustain herself for one month, she would be considered as having sustained herself for a very long time; without her, who has dark eyes, I will not sustain myself for more than a moment), it would be enough if you carry my bosoms and tie them firmly with his shoulders. Hence, do that”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**veṟṟik kuruḷak kodiyān than mīmīdu ādā ulagattu** – isn’t emperumān such that he has garuda as his victorious flag, manifesting in the world that his order is always abided by? He is ensuring that his *svātantryam* (total independence) and my *pāratantryam* (total dependence on him) do not get destroyed. However, he does not engage in any activity by which his *āśrita pāratantryam* (being dependent on his followers) or his *praṇiyitvam* (quality of affection towards his beloved) gets manifested. She says that since he is the *sarvēśvaran* (lord of all) his order is binding on her too, though she is dear to him. Did not parānkuśa nāyaki \[nammāḻvār in the pathos of a lady\] mercifully say, in [tiruviruttam 33](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/01/thiruviruththam-33/)? “*agal visumbum nilanum iruḷār vinai kedac cengōl nadāvudir īngōr peṇpāl poruḷō enum igaḻvō ivaṟṟin puṟattāḷ enṟennō*” (You are ruling both the expansive sky and this earth such that sins which are caused by ignorance run away. Is it because you feel contemptuously that a girl is also an entity in these realms or is it because you think that she is outside the area of your kingdom?) Did not vēdam and śāstrams which came to explain the vēdams, say uproariously, as in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 8-1 “*bīśāsmād vāta bhavatē bhīśōdēti sūrya*: l *bīśāsmād agniścēndraśca mrutyur dāvati pancama*: ll” (Wind blows due to its fear of the supreme entity; sūyran (sun) rises due to fear towards him; due to fear towards him only do agni \[the lord of fire\], indra \[the lord of celestial entities\] and yama, the fifth entity \[the lord of righteousness\] perform their activities, hurriedly), and as mentioned in the authentic proof “*mēghōdayas sāgarasannivruttir vidyud vibhanga*: *spuritāni vāyō*: l  *indōr vibhāgō gatamuṣṇaraśmēr viṣṇōr vicitrā*: *prabhavanti māyā*: ll” (all the activities such as appearance of cloud, waves from ocean coming ashore and returning, appearance of lightning, wafting of breeze, divisions of moon’s rays as they grow and disappear, journey of sun etc are all strange and amazing illusions of emperumān viṣṇu )?

**mīmīdādā ulagattu** – the word *mīdu* has got shortened as *mī*, the meaning of which is ‘above’ (it could be superior, eminent, above, beyond etc). The first *mī* refers to \[emperumān’s\] eminence while the second *mī* refers to beyond. Thus, the opinion here is that beyond the eminence of the one who has garuda as his flag, nothing can perform in this world.

**veṟṟa veṟidē peṟṟa tāy** – how is it that a mother gives birth to a son who will not be of any benefit to her, but would only harm others! The usage of the term *veṟṟa veṟidē*, where the word gets repeated, indicates that apart from harming others, there is no benefit in this.

**peṟṟa tāy** – in the previous pāsuram, by saying “*nandagōpan magan ennum*” she had despised nandagōpan, who had sired kaṇṇan. In this pāsuram, she despises the mother. The meaning for using the term *peṟṟa tāy* is – despising nandagōpan is not proper since he has the responsibility of protecting plenty of cows, bullocks, herd-people et al. Since he would be fully engaged in those activities, it could not be said that it was his fault in not controlling kaṇṇan. When we \[āṇḍāḷ referring to herself\] were engaged with him in enjoying him, just as we had not corrected kaṇṇan, nandagōpan too had not corrected him, engaging in the activities of protecting his cattle and herd-people. Hence, only the mother, who has no work other than bringing him up properly, has to be faulted. We were thinking that she was the one who gave birth to him considering her activities of tying him up to a mortar, beating him, his crying and his suckling her etc. However, considering the way that she has nurtured him, it is clear that she was only a foster-mother. The term *peṟṟa tāy* denotes foster-mother, ironically meaning the exact opposite!

**vēmbēyāga vaḷarttāḷē** – instead of doing a mix of good and bad to others, just like offering sugar cane sometimes and neem, at other times, she nurtured him such that he did only bad deed to others, just like offering only bitter neem. āṇḍāḷ thinks that he grew up as only neem, only because of yaśōdā. She considers that there is no point in blaming him for it; the entire blame is yaśōdā’s.

When people in her proximity tell her “If he doesn’t come, let him be. īsn’t the loss yours? What is the point in reviling his mother, in-between?” she says

**kuṟṟamaṟṟa mulai** – what is my fault in this? Even though he is like neem, just as it has been said in periya tirumoḻi 11-8-7 “*vēmbin puḻu vēmbanṟi uṇṇādu*” (the worm which lives on neem tree will not eat anything other than neem seed or fruit), aren’t my bosoms those which know none other than kaṇṇan? Hasn’t she herself mercifully said in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 1-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/06/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-1-4/) “*avaraip pirāyam todangi enṟum ādarittu eḻunda en tada mulaigaḷ tuvaraip pirānukkē sangaṟpittut toḻudu vaittēn*” and in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 12-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/10/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-12-4/) “*kongait talamivai nōkkik kāṇīr gōvindanukkallāl vāyil pōga*”? īsn’t she one who knows well that the *svarūpakruta bhakti* \[devotion arising out of one’s basic nature\] based on his quality of *svāmitvasvarūpam* (nature of being the lord of all) is more eminent than the *guṇakrutabhakti* \[devotion arising out of his qualities\] based on his auspicious qualities? When sītāp pirātti, along with śrī rāma, visited the hermitage of sage atri and his wife anasūyā, anasūya had told her “For a lady, the supreme god is her consort alone”. sītāp pirātti, responding to her said “Since my consort is full of auspicious qualities and since these qualities will never separate from him, it would appear to those who see me that ‘’Since śrī rāma is full of auspicious qualities, she considers him as a supreme entity. The natural devotion which I have towards him appears to be a made-up one on account of his qualities. Since he is full of auspicious qualities, my natural affection for him has not been revealed. This is my loss, due to his qualities”, revealing her *svarūpakruta bhakti*.

**kuṟṟamaṟṟa mulai** – fault for the bosoms is to remain indifferent, waiting for him to come when he chooses to. Are her bosoms of that type? The meaning for this could be considered as her bosoms suffering in separation from him. When they asked her “What should we do to remove this suffering?” she says further…..

**kumaran** – he is one who has youthful state, apt for these bosoms.

**kōlappaṇait tōḷōdu** – Since his mother had nurtured him like neem, though his heart was full of falsities, there was no deficiency to the beauty of his shoulder. His shoulder would be such that it would be impossible to sustain unless it is embraced. kaṇṇan has decorated his shoulder so well that even when separated now from him, she is referring to his shoulder as *kōlap paṇait tōḷ*.

**aṟṟa kuṟṟam avai tīra** – these bosoms exist exclusively for that shoulder. Even though they are together with me, their engagement is only with that divine shoulder. Not fitting with me, existing exclusively for that shoulder, the deficiency \[in her bosoms\] of being separated from that shoulder has to be set right.

**aṇaiya amukkik kattīrē** – since the word *amukki* (firmly) has been used apart from *aṇaiya* (bind) the opinion here is that even if he refuses to get bound to them, compel him and bind them firmly to his shoulder. Similar to bāṇāsuran chaining his daughter uśai with kaṇṇan’s grandson, aniruddan, together and putting them in the same prison, she is asking whether there is none to bind her bosoms with his shoulder. Will her mothers, who are trying to separate them, bind her bosoms firmly with his shoulder? Though they had tried to separate them, to the extent possible, since she has reached a stage where if separated further, she will die, uniting them will become the responsibility of mother and father. Hence, there is no deficiency in binding firmly.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
