## 13.8. Ulle urugi

*uḷḷē urugi naivēnai uḷaḷō ilaḷō ennādakoḷḷai koḷḷik kuṟumbanaik kōvarttananaik kaṇḍakkālkoḷḷum payan onṟillāda kongai tannaik kiḻangōdumaḷḷip paṟittittu avan mārvil eṟinden aḻalait tīrvēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

uḷḷē urugi naivēnai – regarding me, who is melting inside \[my heart\] and languishing; uḷaḷō ilalō ennāda – not enquiring whether I am alive or dead; koḷḷai koḷḷi – one who has stolen all my possessions; kuṟumbanai – one who carries out misciefs (towards girls); gōvarttananai – kaṇṇa pirān; kaṇḍakkāl – if I were to see him (by chance); koḷḷum payan onṟu illāda – not being of any use (like the appendix on a goat’s neck); kongai tannai – this bosom (of mine); kiḻangōdum aḷḷi paṟittittu – uprooting it; avan mārvil eṟindu – throwing on that kriṣṇan’s chest; en aḻalai – my sorrow

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇa pirān is not bothered about whether I, who am melting inside and languishing, am alive or dead. He has stolen all my possessions and carries out misciefs towards girls. If I were to see him. by chance, I will uproot this useless bosom and throw it against his chest, thus ridding myself of my sorrow.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that if she were to see him, who has neglected her, “I’ll uproot my useless bosoms and throw them on his chest, getting rid of my sorrow”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commengary)**

**uḷḷē urugi naivēnai** – just as it has been said in tirumalai 34 “*uḷḷuvāruḷḷiṟṟellām udanirundu aṟidiyenṟē*” (you know the thoughts of those who are thinking, by being together with them, since you the *sarvāntaryāmi* (indvelling soul of everything and everyone), I cannot say that he is neglecting me by not knowing that I am melting inside and languishing.

**naivēnai** – when asked whether you cannot say that “I am languishing in this manner”, the response is – when rivers and ponds get filled up, the excess water alone overflows; when I am languishing inside, through my words and deeds, you could comprehend to an extent my extent of languishing, but there is no way that I could fully speak about the extent of my languishing.

**uḷalō ilaḷō ennāda** – though his affection towards me has vanished, will even compassion towards a girl vanish? He has not even asked, on account of compassion, whether “She is alive or dead”.

**koḷḷai koḷḷi** – Did he merely stop enquiring about me, without any compassion? Has he not stolen every one of my possessions? Is this correct?

**kuṟumbanai** – How did śrī nandagōpar give birth to a boy who will carry out misciefs in his śrī gōkulam and wander around everywhere!

**gōvarttananai** – he will nurture cows such that they multiply manifold. However, he will put down the girls such that they cannot live.

**kaṇḍakkāl** – it is difficult to see him since he keeps running behind cows. If one were to see him, after striving a lot…

**koḷḷum payan onṟillāda** – since he has gone after the cows, neglecting us, there is no benefit to be attained through these bosoms which exist solely for him.

**kongai tannai kiḻangōdum** – Here, the *kiḻangu* (root) for the bosom is her ātmā (soul) alone. In *svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning), bosom represents *bhakti* (devotion) which is enjoyable for the supreme being. It is pertinent to note that the repository for that *bhakti* is *ātmā*. Don’t wise people say “For this body, which has several limbs, the sustaining force is *ātmā*. That is permanent, indestructible”? bhagavad gītā 2-24 says “*accēdyōýam adāhyōýam aklēdyō’śōśya ēvaca* l *nityas sarvagata*: *stāṇuracalōýam sanātana*: ll” (ātmā, which permeates all through the body, cannot be severed; it cannot be burnt; it cannot be wet; it cannot be dried. Hence, ātmā is permanent, firm, immovable and existing since time immemorial). Thus, she is trying to uproot that ātmā, which cannot be caught hold of by hand, along with her bosom. This is the extent to which her pang of separation is.

**avan mārvil eṟindu** – with the opinion that he should suffer the way that she has suffered all along, with her bosom, she says that even while throwing the bosom she is not saying that she will throw it on the floor, but is saying that she will throw it on his chest.

**en aḻalai tīrvēnē** – only if he were to come and hug, could one become ecstatic. She says that she will get the benefit of ridding herself of her sorrow by throwing the bosom alone on his chest.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 13.9. Kommai mulaigal

*kommai mulaigaḷ idar tīrak kōvindaṟkōr kuṟṟēvalimmaip piṟavi seyyādē inippōyc ceyyum tavam dān ensemmai udaiya tirumārvil sērttānēlum oru nānṟumeymmai solli mugam nōkki vidaidān tarumēl miga nanṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kommai mulaigaḷ – (my) bosoms which are huge and have risen, their; idar tīra – to rid of \[their\] annoyance; gōvindaṟku – for kaṇṇa pirān; ōr kuṟṟēval – an esoteric servitude; immaip piṟavi – in this birth; seyyādē – instead of carrying out that; ini – after this birth ends; pōy – going to paramapadam; seyyum – that which is to be carried out; tavandān en – why a penance?; semmai udaiya tirumārvil – on the divine chest which has the honesty (of existing only for embracing his followers); sērttānēlum – if he embraces (me); nanṟu – that will be good; oru nānṟu – one day; mugam nōkki – looking at my face; meymmai solli – speaking only the truth; vidaidān tarumēl – if he sends me off, making it clear “I do not need you; you may go”; miga nanṟu – that will be even better

**Перевод:**

**Instead of carrying out a confidential service to kaṇṇapirān in this birth itself such that the irritation of my bosoms which are huge and which have risen, is removed, why should I carry out a penance after completing this birth, and reaching paramapadam? If he were to embrace me with his honest chest, that will be good. If, on the other hand, one day, speaking only the truth, if he were to look at my face and tell me “I do not need you; you may go”, that will be even better,**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – when people in her proximity told her “How could you say so harshly ‘kiḻangōdum aḷḷip paṟittittu’? Since there is an entity \[ātmā\] which is sustaining it, and there is an indvelling soul, emperumān, who is there, should you not carry out a means to attain that emperumān and enjoy him?” She says “I wanted to enjoy him with this physical form. Leaving aside this form, taking another form which is different from this and carrying out an effort on my own, as the means, to attain him, is not necessary for me”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kommai mulaigaḷ idar tīra** – just like children who have grown up, pester their mother during times of famine, “Give us food” repeatedly, these bosoms pester me, saying “Show me the divine chest of gōvindan, which is apt for us”. To rid the sorrow of these bosoms., which are huge and have risen. Their sorrow will be removed only if they belong to gōvindan.

**gōvindaṟku** – these will not go to śrī vaikuṇṭha nātan \[lord of paramapadam\]. They will exist only for the one who goes behind cows.

**ōr kuṟṟēval** – it is a servitude which cannot be carried out by those who have a masculine form. It is an unparalleled servitude which can be carried out only by a lady with bosom. Could embracing with bosom be termed as a servitude? Yes, since servitude is that which is carried out by the limbs which one has.

**immaip piṟavi seyyādē inippōyc ceyyum tavam dān en** – just as sītāp pirātti had told hanuman in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 40-3 “*yatā tam puruśavyāgram gātrai*: *śōkābhikarśitai*: l *samsrupśēyam sakāmā’ham tatā kuru dayām mayi* :” (you should mercifully shower your grace on me such that I, who am withering due to my sorrow, would be able to touch desirously śrī rāma, who is like a tiger among men, with my limbs), āṇḍāḷ wishes to embrace kaṇṇan with her physical form in this birth. If she does not get to carry that out, she is thinking that going to paramapadam, getting another physical form and embracing parama pada nātan is similar to sages carrying out severe penance standing amidst four ritualistic fires, with the sun on top acting as the fifth fire.

**semmai udaiya tirumārvil** – his divine chest has the honesty of not refusing when one wishes to hug it.

**sērttānēlum** – embracing him, who is her ordained lord, is the ultimate benefit for her. If she gets this, she says that this will be good. Since she has used the term “*ēlum*” (if) it appears that there is a doubt in this. She says further that if he does something else, it will be even better.

**oru nānṟu meymmai solli** – just as she has mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 14-3 “*ēlāp poygaḷ uraippānai*”, even though he has as his habit the quality of telling only lies, is there any compulsion that he should not speak the truth even once? Let him speak the truth on one day. The lie which he speaks all the time is that when they are united, knowing in his heart that he is going to separate from her soon, he will say “I will not leave you. If I leave you, I cannot sustain myself”. Instead of saying such false words, if he were to speak only the truth, on one day….

**mugam nōkki vidaidān tarumēl miga nanṟu** – instead of avoiding me, looking here and there, if he looks straight into my face and says “I do not have affection towards you. I have given you up. You may go”, making us to go away from him as we desire, that will be even better.

**sērttānēl nanṟu, vidaidān tarumēl miga nanṟu** – if he embraces my bosoms with his divine chest tightly, it will be good. If he looks at my face and tells me “You are not needed, go”, allowing me to leave him and go, that will be very good. How is this? “If I desire to hug him and get embraced by him, it is certain that on some day in the future, I have to separate from him, resulting in sorrow since I cannot embrace him then. Instead, if he discards me saying ‘You may go’ I can get rid of this life, getting relieved from this sorrow forever and that will be even better”. It will be similar to the separation experienced by sītāp pirātti when śrī rāmapirān arranged for leaving her in the forest, quoting something which a vaśerman had said when she was in her advanced stage of pregnancy, in such a way that there was no possibility of ever living with her.

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad.

## 13.10. Allal vilaitta

*allal viḷaitta perumānai āyar pādikku aṇi viḷakkaivilli puduvai nagar nambi vittucittan viyan kōdaivillait tolaitta puruvattāḷ vētkai uṟṟu miga virumbumsollait tudikka vallārgaḷ tunbak kadaluḷ tuvaḷārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

villai tolaitta – defeated bow; puruvattāḷ – one who had eyebrows; villi puduvai nagar vittu cittan viyan kōdai – āṇḍāḷ, who has amazing qualities and is the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, who is the leader of śrīvilliputtūr; allal viḷaitta perumānai – creating misciefs (throughout tiruvāyppādi) and being eminent because of that; āyar pādikku aṇi viḷakkai – kaṇṇapirān who is splendorous, as the auspicious lamp for tiruvāyppādi; vētkaiyuṟṟu – desiring; miga virumbum sollai – mercifully composed pāsurams which manifest unsatiated love; tudikka vallārgaḷ – those who are capable of praising and reciting; tunbam kadaluḷ – in the ocean of samsāram (materialistic realm); tuvaḷār – will not have to quiver in pain

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ, who has eyebrows which defeat a bow \[in their shape\], who has amazing qualities and who is the daughter of periyāḻvār, the leader of śrīvilliputtūr, desired kaṇṇapirān, who has the eminence of creating misciefs in tiruvāyppādi and is splendorous as the auspicious lamp for tiruvāyppādi. Those who worship and recite this decad, composed mercifully, with desire and with unsatiated love by āṇḍāḷ, will not quiver in pain in the ocean of samsāram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she brings the decad to an end by mentioning the benefit for those who have learnt this decad, saying that they will not suffer like she did, but will only experience happiness.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**allal viḷaitta perumānai** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 2-26 “*tē tamūcur mahātmānam paurajānapatais saha* l *bahavō nrupa*! *kalyāṇa guṇā putrasya santi tē* ll” (Those \[chieftains\] along with people in urban and rural areas, told the eminent daśarata as follows: Oh king! Your son śrī rāma has many auspicious qualities), those in the divine city of ayōdyā were fully involved with the auspicious qualities of śrī rāma and did not know anything else. In the same way, those in tiruvāyppādi were fully engaged with the misciefs of kaṇṇan. Just as śrī rāma was dear to one’s mind on account of his auspicious qualities, did not kaṇṇan become eminent on account of roaming everywhere, stealing butter and girls, creating suffering, as mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 10-7-2 “*pālum padinkudam kaṇḍilēn*” (I am not able to find the ten vessels which contained milk)! He shined like an auspicious lamp since he was the supreme entity creating all these misciefs and sufferings. It appears that when he was not carrying out any mischief, the entire place would be in darkness!

**aṇi viḷakkai** – since his misciefs were sweet like sugarcane to them, he was like a decoration for the entire tiruvāyppādi. The auspicious qualities of śrī rāma were bitter, in tiruvāyppādi. kaṇṇan roamed around the entire place, with his misciefs giving bright radiance to tiruvāyppādi.

**villi puduvai nagar vittu cittan viyan kōdai … vētkaiyuṟṟu miga virumbum sollai** – the firmness in her desire \[for kaṇṇan\] alone translated into this decad and is coming to an end now. She says that the reason for this desire is because of her birth as the daughter of periyāḻvār.

**villait tolaitta puruvattāḷ** – her eyebrows were such that the bow could not stand in comparison. The radiance of her limbs was such that all the materials which desired to be allegories to her limbs got destroyed.

**vētkaiyuṟṟu miga virumbiya sol** – her love towards kaṇṇapirān multiplied manifold, just like bhūtattāḻvār had said in [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 100](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/02/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-100/) “*endan aḷavanṟāl yānudaiya anbu*” (my affection for you has grown so much that it has become much bigger than I). It outgrew her ātmā too, manifesting like a huge love, making her sing pāsurams, which were composed with that love, to be said as “words which were overflowing with experience about emperumān”.

**sollait tudikka vallārgaḷ** – however much a *samsāri jīvan* (an entity dwelling in this world) tries, (s)he cannot attain her \[āṇḍāḷ’s\] desire. It is enough if one recites the pāsurams composed by her with such huge love.

**tunbak kadaluḷ tuvaḷārē** – since āṇḍāḷ, as the mother, had suffered for their sake too, these people, who are in samsāram, instead of suffering and quivering like a soft tender leaf, when put in fire, will attain the *bhagavad anubhavam* (experiencing emperumān) which she had desired, by reciting the pāsurams mercifully composed by her.

Next, we will consider the pravēśam of the next decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14.1. Patti meyndor

*patti mēyndōr kārēṟu baladēvaṟkōr kīḻkkanṟāyittīṟittu viḷaiyadi ingē pōdak kaṇḍīrēittamāna pasukkaḷai inidu maṟittu nīr ūttivittukkoṇḍu viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ōr kār ēṟu – a dark bull (kaṇṇa pirān); patti mēyndu – roaming as \[he\] liked, without restraint; baladēvaṟku – for balarāma; ōr kīḻ kanṟu āy – as a unique younger brother; ittīṟittu – crying out loudly as an outlet for his happiness; viḷaiyādi – playing; ingē pōda – to come here; kaṇḍīrē – did you see?; ittam Ana – ones he liked; pasukkaḷai – cattle; inidu – sweetly; maṟittu – calling out their names; nīr utti – making them drink water; vittuk koṇḍu – grazing them (like this); viḷaiyāda – (that emperumān) as he played; virundāvanattē – in brindāvanam; kaṇḍōm – we saw

**Перевод:**

**Did you see kaṇṇa pirān, who was like a dark bull, roaming around here without any restriction, as a unique younger brother of balarāma, crying out loudly as an outlet for his happiness and playing here? We saw that emperumān, coming here with the cattle which he liked, calling out their names, making them drink water, grazing them like this, in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – nityasūris \[permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam\] are like aged ministers, as mentioned in tiruviruttam 2 “viṇṇāttavar mūduvar” (nityasūris came ahead of all others); they made paramapada nātan \[emperumān\] as the prince of śrīvaikuṇam as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-6-3 “vāniḷavarasu” (prince for śrivaikuṇṭam). Being obedient to them, when they were performing divine bath for him, as mentioned in tiruviruttam 21 “sūttu nanmālaigaḷ tūyavēndi” (holding distinguished garlands in order to adorn them on his divine crown as ornaments) appropriate to their śēśatva svarūpam (basic nature of being servitors), he heard that in tiruvāyppādi “Butter is available in plenty in every house” and that “nappinnaip pirātti has reached youthful stage”. Not satisfied with being in parama padam, he incarnated as kaṇṇan in this vibhūti (samsāram, the materialistic realm) and roamed around in śrī brindāvanam, just as he liked, as a bull. This pāsuram describes that.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**patti mēyndōr kārēṟu** – when he was in parama padam, emperumān was like a bull on a leash. After incarnating as kaṇṇan and reaching tiruvāyppādi, he was like a bull, roaming as he pleased, shining with a dark complexion. Just as periyāḻvār had mercifully called him as *vāniḷavarasu* (prince of parama padam), he was “nurtured on the lap of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan), under the cane of sēnai mudaliyār (viśvaksēnar) and under the wings of periya tiruvadi (garudan)”, bhattar would often say. Thus, in parama padam he was a bound bull. Here, after incarnating as kaṇṇan, like an elephant which had entered sugarcane fields, he was indulging in misciefs, roaming around freely. Unlike parama padam where no activity could be conducted against one’s *svarūpam* (basic nature), in this tiruvāyppādi, everywhere people were saying “Butter has been stolen by kaṇṇan”, ”Girls have been abducted by kaṇṇan”. He was roaming, destroying all boundaries. āṇḍāḷ refers to this as *patti mēyndu*.

**ōr kārēṟu** – Sine he was roaming as he desired, he was like a unique dark bull, which was roaming arrogantly. Perhaps, in śrī vaikuṇṭam, he was pale in colour!

**baladēvaṟkōr kīḻkkanṟāy** – as the younger brother of balarāma, he was controlled by his elder brother. Though he had incarnated in tiruvāyppādi to roam around as he pleased, since he was nurtured as a prince in śrī vaikuṇṭam by ādiśēśan who kept him in his lap, here too he was under the control of balarāma, the reincarnation of ādiśēśan. kaṇṇan, who used to carry out misciefs towards girls, was obedient to balarāma. Moreover, when covherd girls, who were dear to him, did not talk to him due to love-quarrel, since this balarāma himself was like a mediator between them, he was obedient to balarāma.

In śrī rāmāvatāram, when perumāḷ and pirātti were sleeping in the forest, lakśmaṇa was awake, with bow and arrow in his hand, protecting them. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 1-31 “*ramyamāvasatam krutvā ramamāṇā vanē traya*: l *dēva gandarva sankāśās tatra tē nyavasan sukham*” (Those three (śrī rāma, sītāp pirātti and lakśmaṇa) who were like dēvas and gandarvas \[celestial entities\] and who were happily roaming in the forest, were living comfortably in a hermitage) lakśmaṇa too had a part when śrī rāma and sītāp pirātti were happily together by nurturing their happiness. Similar to that, this balarāma too, when the girls were hurt due to kaṇṇan’s misciefs, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-24-20  “*sandēśais sāma madurai*: *prēma gharbhair agarvitai*: l *rāmēṇāśvāsitā  gōpyō hariṇā hrutacētasa* ll” (covherd girls, who had their hearts stolen by hari \[kriṣṇa\], were consoled by information which was sweet with grace, full of affection and without any arrogance), through his sweet words, would remove their hurt, and unite them with kaṇṇan. Hence, kaṇṇan was obedient towards balarāma. Though kaṇṇan used to act as per balarāma’s thoughts, since he was the śēśi (lord) and balarāma was his śeśan (servitor), he would also control balarāma, on occasions. Hasn’t periyāḻvār too mercifully said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-7-5 “*baladēvan ennum* *tannambiyōdap pin kūdac celvān*” (as his elder brother balarāma ran in the front, kaṇṇan would go behind him), indicating that kaṇṇan was obedient to balarāma? For balarāman too, was it not a habit to ensure that kaṇṇan did not get into any dangerous situation? On a day when balarāman was not with him, did not kaṇṇan jump into the mouth of \[the poisonous snake\] kāḷiyan?

**ittīṟittu** – carrying out activities such as shouting, which demonstrated his arrogance. When a badda cētanan (an entity who is bound to this samsāram) gets liberated from this and attains parama padam as a muktan \[liberated entity\], manifesting his happiness of enjoying emperumān, he would say as said in taittirīya upaniśat bhruguvalli 10-6 “*ētat sāma gāyannāstē* l *hā vuhā vuhā vu* l *ahamannam ahamannam ahamannam* l *ahamannādō’hamannādō’ham annāda*: ll” (muktātmā (after attaining the supreme being) would sing the following sāma – *hā vuhā vuhā vu*; I am food (to the supreme being). This is repeated twice; I eat the food (of the happiness of that supreme entity); this too is repeated twice). Just as the muktan manifests his happiness through singing sāma vēdam, since emperumān comes here incarnating himself and interacts with the entities here by manifesting his simplicity and easy approacability, he emits loud cries, indulging in such activities. In the first line of the pāsuram, the term *kārēṟu* refers to his activities in line with the clan he was born in. Thus, there is no repetition of verse in this. siṟiyāttān, one of the disciples of kūrattāḻvān would say – The supreme entity with such great eminence, as mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*avākyanādara*:” (paramātmā, due to his majesty, does not speak to any one; he does not support anyone), transforms himself to one who is spoken of as indulging in activities such as *ittīṟittu viḷaiyādum*!

**viḷaiyādi** – this speaks about the activities mentioned earlier, manifesting his arrogance. īsn’t playing like this, the purpose of taking an incarnation!

**ingē pōdak kaṇḍīrē** – are there people here who have seen the activities of such kaṇṇan, which cannot be seen even if one goes to parama padam? Until now, it has been the pāsuram of those who were asking.

Now, the second half is the pāsuram of those who are responding. This feature will be seen in all the pāsurams of this decad.

**ittamāna pasukkaḷai** – aren’t girls dear to him! What is the reason for saying cows are dear to him, here? Those who are in the youthful stage, more than their beloveds, will be affectionate towards those who unite them with their beloveds. kaṇṇan too would desire the cows, more than the covherd girls. Just as it has been said in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-2-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/10/thiruvaimozhi-6-2-2-pogu-nambi/) “*āgaḷ pōgavittu kuḻaḷūdu pōyirundē*” (driving the cows far away, going there and playing the flute, as if to call them back), kaṇṇan will drive the cows to where his beloveds were present and would go there and play the flute as if calling them back, while in reality he would invite his beloveds. Thus, the cows are not only mediators between kaṇṇan and his beloveds; when kaṇṇan returns home late, after playing with the covherd girls, when his parents ask him the reason for returning late, he would say that “Cows had gone over a long distance; I had to go after them, call them back and return” thus providing an alibi for his late arrival. Hence, the cows are dear to him.

**ittamāna pasukkaḷai** – isn’t he one who desires to graze the cows more than being in paramapadam, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 10-3-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/11/thiruvaimozhi-10-3-10-avaththangal-vilaiyum/) “*tivattilum pasunirai mēyppuvatti senganaivāy engaḷ āyar dēvē*” (Oh, enjoyable lord for the herd-people, who has beautiful lips which are like reddish fruit! You desire tending to cows more than remaining in parama padam)! Even when he is in parama padam, it is only the cows which he had tended to in kriṣṇāvatāram that he would keep murmuring about and dream of the places where he had tended to them!

**inidu maṟittu** – calling them sweetly, mentioning their names. When a person and his beloved are speaking to each other, if they call each other by their name, it would be sweet to the one who has been called. In the same manner, when the cows are being grazed, if they are called out by their names, they will feel sweet. When there are thousands of cows, could each of them be called by different names? For one who has given *nāma rūpa vyākaraṇam* (giving distinct name and form) at the time of creation for countless cētanas in leelā vibhūti, this is not at all difficult.

**nīrūtti** – if they \[calves\] feel thirsty and want water, he will lead them to a shallow water body; since they can only drink with their mouth, he will fold his hands behind him, bend down and drink water with his mouth, teaching them the way to drink. Then, they too will drink similarly. Hasn’t tirumangai āḻvār too said in [periya tirumoḻi 2-5-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/10/periya-thirumozhi-2-5-3/) “*tadam parugu karu mugil*” that when he drinks water from the pond, it will be like a dark cloud drinking water from the pond? [bhagavat gītā 3-21](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/3-21/) says “*yad yadācarati śrēśtas tat tadēvētarō jana*: l *sa yat pramāṇam kurutē lōkas tadanuvartatē* ll” (whichever deeds a knowledgeable person follows, other people (who do not have knowledge) too follow the same deeds. The extent to which that knowledgeable person observes those deeds is also followed by the world). As mentioned in this gītā ślōkam, for drinking water, kaṇṇan follows the mode which is followed by the cattle. Calves, which do not know as to how to drink water, could be taught only in this manner and not by any other way. Just as tirumangai āḻvār had mercifully said in [tirunedundāṇḍagam 16](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2021/08/thirunedunthandakam-16/) “*kanṟu mēyttu iniduganda kāḷāy* ” he was very keen on tending to calves which do not know how to drink even water.

**vittukkoṇḍu viḷaiyāda** – Is it possible to play with cattle when they are grazing? When he was in parama padam, since he did not get what he desired, without looking at his eminence \[when he came down to leelā vibhūti as kaṇṇan\], not tending to his divine locks, not washing his divine dress cleanly, he would roam around as he liked. After the cattle have grazed and drunk water, there is nothing for him to do. At that stage, is there anything other than playing with them! The benefit of his incarnation too is this playful activity only.

**virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – Since this could not be seen in tiruvāyppādi too, even this would look like as if it had been carried out in parama padam. īsn’t the experience which he had in śrī brindāvanam similar to the experience of eminent kings who would go with their dear consorts to exclusive places for their enjoyment!

Next, we will consider the 2<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14.2. Anunga ennai

*anunga ennaip pirivu seydu āyarpādi kavarndu uṇṇumkuṇungu nāṟik kuttēṟṟaik kōvarttananaik kaṇḍīrēkaṇangaḷōdu minmēgam kalandāṟpōla vanamālaiminunga ninṟu viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anunga – such that I am distressed; ennai pirivu seydu – separating from me; āyar pādi – tiruvāyppādi; kavarndu – seizing; uṇṇum – as one who enjoys; kuṇungu nāṟi – smelling of butter; kuttēṟṟai – one who is like a young bull; gōvarttananai – kaṇṇapirān, who nurtures cows; kaṇḍīrē – did you see?; min mēgam – lightning and cloud; kalandāṟpōla – as if mixing with each other; vanamālai minunga ninṟu – (like lightning on a dark, divine form) with vanamālai shining; kaṇangaḷōdu – with groups (of his friends); viḷaiyāda – as he played; kaṇḍōmē – we saw

**Перевод:**

**Did you see kaṇṇapirān, who separated from me, leaving me distressed, who seized āyarpādi and enjoyed it, who smells like butter, who is like a young bull and who nurtures cows? We have seen him with his divine garland vaijayanti, on his dark divine form, looking like lightning and cloud mixed together, playing with his groups of friends in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – She says that she had seen kaṇṇapirān, wearing the divine garland called vaijayanti, shining like a cloud with a streak of lightning, playing with his friends in virundāvanam.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**anunga** – this could be construed as either – such that I am destroyed or such that I am distressed

**anunga ennaip pirivu seydu** – separating from me such that I am unable to survive. He separates after saying “I will not separate from you; if I separate, I cannot sustain myself”. Due to this, there will be a desire to live with the hope “Perhaps we can see him once more.” Hence, due to the sorrow of separation, one cannot get destroyed. She calls this torture, which is like destruction, as *anunga*.

**ennai** – did he think that I too would grow up well, just like he does, with separation as a manure?

**āyarpādi kavarndu uṇṇum** – here, she explains the term *patti mēyndu* which she had mentioned in the previous pāsuram. She says that he will torment girls who desire him, just like she did, by separating from them and will abduct those girls in āyarpādi who do not desire him, in his houses and enjoy them desirously. She opines that had she too not desired him, she vouldn’t have had to get distressed.

**kuṇungu nāṟi** – it appears that she had desired to enjoy the smell of butter on him. Are there others who had desired like this? śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 16-23 says “*dīkśitam vrata sampannam varājinadaram śucim* l *kuranga śrungapāṇim ca paśyanti tvām bhajāmyaham*” (I shall be delighted to see you, initiated for the perfect religious act of austerity, wearing excellent deer-skin, being pure and bearing a deer-horn in your hand). Did not sītāp pirātti desire to see śrī rāma who had taken a vow for a religious rite, with butter applied on his divine form, with deer skin donned on his divine form, having the apt guise as the *yajamāna* (one who carries out the ritual), fasting for two days when his ācārya (teacher) vasiśtar tells him to fast for one day, getting rid of his silken dresses and donning deer skin, taking a bath each time that he touches sītāp pirātti when vasiśtar has told him not to touch anyone other than sītāp pirātti? kaṇṇan was just the opposite of this, as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-8-4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/03/thiruvaimozhi-4-8-4-niraivinal/) “*kaṟaiyinār tuvarudukkai kadaiyāvin kaḻikōl kaic caṟaiyinār kavarāda taḷir niṟattāl kuṟaiyilamē*” (There is no use of my sprout like reddish complexion if that is not liked by emperumān whose tree-bark garments are having stains, who is having bamboo container and stick, which is carried by covherd boys, in his hands, and who is wearing the waist string with bells). Did not parānkuśa nāyaki say, desiring the guise of kaṇṇapirān which was devoid of customary principles, that there was no benefit of her complexion? In the same way, āṇḍāḷ too desires only the butter-like smell of kaṇṇa pirān.

**kuttēṟṟai** – one who is like an arrogant, young bull, having obtained what he had desired, after seizing āyarpādi.

**gōvarttananai** – one who nurtures cows. One who nurtures cows making them multiply manifold while discarding girls in āyppādi.

Now, the pāsuram gives the response of those who respond to the query in the first half.

**kaṇangaḷōdu** – just as her father periyāḻvār had mercifully said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-1-1 “*tannērāyiram piḷḷaigaḷōdu taḷar nadai ittu varuvān*”, coming with groups of his friends who match him.

**min mēgam kalandāṟpōla vanamālai minunga ninṟu** – the contrast which the divine *vanamālai* which is resplendent and moving on his divine chest, had with his dark divine form is similar to lightning and cloud mingling together.

**vanamālai minunga ninṟu** – since the *mālākārar* (one who strings garlands) had given him a garland after being swayed by him when he went in search of a garland in *vada madurai* (maturā), that was shining well. She opines that since she is the daughter of periyāḻvār who is also a *mālākārar* like that person in maturā, he will don the garland which she had desirously donned and given him, and play.

**viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – if he dons that garland and comes to tiruvāyppādi, other girls will swoon on seeing him and there will be no time to play. Unlike that, we saw him in śrī brindāvanam, where he was enjoying with his friends who could sustain themselves even if they see him with his divine garland, roaming and playing as he liked.

Next, we will consider the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14.3. Malayp piranda

*mālāyp piṟanda nambiyai mālē seyyum maṇāḷanaiēlāp poygaḷ uraippānai ingē pōdak kaṇḍīrēmelāl paranda veyil kāppān vinadai siṟuvan siṟagu ennummēlāppin kīḻ varuvānai virundāanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

māl āy piṟanda nambiyai – it could be said that he was born with affection (towards girls) as his form; mālē seyyum maṇāḷanai – as a bridegroom, carrying out only affection; ēlā poygaḷ uraippānai – kaṇṇapirān who says absurd lies; ingē pōdak kaṇḍīrē – did you see him here?; mēlāl – above; paranda – widespread; veyil – sunlight; kāppān – to prevent (from falling on emperumān’s divine form); vinadai siṟuvan ennum mēlāppin kīḻ varuvānai – that perumān who mercifully comes; virundāvnattē kaṇḍōmē – we saw him in śrī brindāvanam

**Перевод:**

**Did you see kaṇṇa pirān, who has affection towards girls as his divine form, who has only affection as a bridegroom and who says absurd lies? We saw him in śrī brindāvanam, coming mercifully under the canopy of garudan’s wings, who was protecting him from the widespread sunlight, above.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – even as garudan was protecting emperumān with his wings such that sunlight did not fall on him, she says that they saw him in śrī vrindāvanam.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mālāyp piṟanda** – it appears as if his deep love towards girls had taken a divine form. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 1-1 says “*satyam jñāṇam anantam brahma* l *yō vēda nihitam guhāyām paramē vyōman* l *sōś’nutē sarvān kāmān saha brahmaṇā vipaścitēti*” (one who comprehends that brahmam \[supreme being\] which has eternity and wisdom as its form and which is inestimable, resides inside a cave which is one’s heart, enjoys all the auspicious qualities of brahmam, who is omniscient, in parama padam). vēdam said that emperumān, in his supreme form, has qualities such as *jñānam* (knowledge) as his form. In the same way, in his form during incarnation, she says that he has affection as his form. Did not she address him as *mālē* (one who has affection towards his followers) in [tiruppāvai 26](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-26-male-manivanna/)!

**nambiyai** – while one quality of affection was mentioned for his divine form as an indication, he is full of all the auspicious qualities which have not been mentioned. She says that by manifesting his deep love towards them, he creates deep love in them towards him, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17-16 “*sarvēśām hi sa darmātmā varṇānām kurutē dayām* l *caturṇām hi vayastānām tēna tē tamanuvratā*: ll” (That rāma, who is the epitome of righteousness, shows his mercy on the four varṇas \[brāhmaṇa, kśattriya, vaiśya and śudra\] as well as to people in the four stages of life \[infancy, adolescent, youth and old age\] and to all others. They are also affectionate towards him).

**ēlāp poygaḷ uraippānai** – one who says many absurd lies. Looking at him when he was coming with a streak of turmeric and fine grade of sandalwood paste on his divine form, indicating that he had been together with a girl, if a herd-girl asked him “From which girl did you get these marks?” he would respond “Apart from you, from which girl could I get these marks? Do I know any girl other than you?” If we have to listen to only truths, should we not have to go to śrī rāmāvatāram, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 2-32 “*priyavādī ca bhūtānām satyavādī ca rāghava*:” (śrī rāma will say only what is dear to all creations. He will speak only the truth)? Did not śrī rāma have the eminence of speaking only the truth? īsn’t the indication for truth defined as that which is speaking what has been seen, as said in “*yadādruśtārta vacananca*”? perumāḷ’s (śrī rāma’s) words would reveal what is actual, creating benefit for all. Since he is famous for not speaking two words contradictorily, his words will be accepted immediately as truthful. On the other hand, kaṇṇa pirān’s word, though appearing as false since they would be contradictory, since they create benefit only \[for his followers\], will be truthful in the effect mode. Thus, in śrī rāmāvatāram, truth and in kriṣṇāvatāram, lies, would be the refuge for his followers.

śrī rāma would be syncronised in his faculties of mind, word and action, not speaking in contradiction, protecting his followers. kaṇṇan, even by lowering his eminence, will protect his followers, by saying lies. During the mahābhārata war, he made daylight to appear like night, took to weapons after vowing that he would not take up weapons and indicated the vital parts of enemies such as duryōdana, protecting the pāṇḍavas.

**poygaḷ uraippānai** – kaṇṇan will speak, not one or two, but a hundred thousand lies which would appear like truths.

**ingē pōdak kaṇḍīrē** – it is clear that his lies are very much acceptable.

Now, we come to that part of pāsuram where the respondents give their answer.

**mēlāl paranda veyil kāppān vinadai siṟuvan siṟagennum mēlāppin kīḻ varuvānai** – nampiḷḷai would mercifully say, with total engagement \[with āṇḍāḷ’s words\] “garudan spreads his wings as a canopy in order to protect emperumān from sunshine which has spread wide, above. āṇḍāḷ calls him as *vinatai siṟuvan*, to indicate that he is a *śēśabhūtan* (servitor) to her too. How amazing is this! īsn’t one, who is holding an umbrella to emperumān, subordinated to her as a servitor from birth!” Moreover, there is information in mahābhāratam that kadru, garuda’s step mother, made his \[garuda’s\] mother vinatai as her slave and made her stand in sunlight. At that time, garuda gave her shade by spreading his wings.  He \[nampiḷḷai\] would engage with that incident too, saying that the term *vinatai siṟuvan* would refer to that also.

**mēlāppin kīḻ varuvānai virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – just as jatāyu had spread his wings to provide shade to his elder brother *sampāti*, garuda had provided shade for kaṇṇa pirān too by making his wings as a canopy such that sunlight did not fall on him. We saw kaṇṇa pirān, playing under that shade, in śrī brindāvanam.

Next, we will consider the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14.4. Karttan kamalam

*kārttaṇ kamalak kaṇ ennum nedum kayiṟu padutti ennaiīrttukkoṇḍu viḷaiyādum īsan tannaik kaṇḍīrēpōrtta muttin kuppāyap pugarmāl yānaik kanṟē pōlvērttu ninṟu viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kār – in the dark cloud; taṇ – cool; kamalam – like a blossomed lotus; kaṇ ennum – called as divine eyes; nedu kayiṟu – long noose; ennai padutti – trapping me; īrttuk koṇḍu – pulling (my heart along with him, wherever he goes); viḷaiyādum īsan tannaik kaṇḍīrē – did you see the lord who is playing?; pōrtta muttin kuppāyam – having a jacket made of pearls, as the covering sheet; pugar – having resplendence; māl – being huge; yānai kanṟu pōl – like an elephant calf; vērttu ninṟu viḷaiyāda – sweating and playing; virundāvanattē kaṇḍōm – we saw in śrī brindāvanam

**Перевод:**

**Did you see the lord, who had trapped me with his eyes, which are like blossomed lotus on a dark cloud, which are like a long rope and with which he pulled my heart along to wherever he went? We have seen him with a jacket made of pearls, like a huge elephant calf which is resplendent, sweating and playing in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that they saw kaṇṇan, looking like a decorated elephant, sweating and playing in śri brindāvanam.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kārttaṇ kamalak kaṇ ennum** – the reddish eyes on his dark divine form were like lotus flowers, which remove the ennui of those who look at them, on a dark monsoon cloud.  The divine form itself, which is like a monsoon cloud, attracts the heart and eyes of those who see it. Being the limb on top of that, the beauty of the eyes further engages the beholder. ḍoesn’t the divine form itself attract the beholder, as mentioned in śri rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 83-8? “*mēghaśyāmam mahābāhum stirasatvam drudavrutam* l *kadā drakśyāmahē rāmam jagata*: *śōkanāśanam* ll” (When will we see śrī rāmapirān who is dark like a cloud, who has long hands, who has stable strength, who has firm vow and who removes the sorrows in the world?) Did not śrī bharatāḻvān too say “The boiling sensation in the body resulting from being the son of kaikēyi will be annulled only when I wake up to the divine and beautiful face of śrī rāma”?

**kamalak kaṇ ennum nedum kayiṟu padutti** – trapping in the long noose, which is the divine eyes

**kamalak kaṇ ennum** – divine eyes are like the lotus flower in terms of blossoming nature, beauty, coolness and fragrance, manifesting the compassion inside the heart.

**nedungayiṟu** – these eyes are like a long rope, following me wherever I go, trapping me

**nedungayiṟu padutti ennai īrttukkoṇḍu viḷaiyādum īsan** – with the long net, encircling me such that I could not escape, looking at me with coolness, making me as his servitor, ensuring that my deed is not as per my thought, pulling me towards him, being playful due to these, like a *svatantran* (totally independent person)

**kārttaṇ … īsan** – does he not roam around, manifesting his divine form, looking with his divine eyes, trapping girls! In this way, he made me to be engaged with him totally, through his beauty, making me to babble incoherent words, making these activities as his pastime, remaining as the lord, not bothering about anyone.

**pōrtta muttin muppāyap pugarmāl yānaikkanṟē pōl vērttu ninṟu viḷaiyāda** – when kaṇṇan plays, the appearance of little droplets of sweat on his divine form would make him look like an elephant calf, which is radiant, having a pearl jacket \[sweat drops would look like pearls\].

**vērttu ninṟu viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – unlike parama padam where one cannot sweat at all, unlike tiruvāyppādi, where despite having a garland like a prince, due to fear of his parents, he would not be playing, we saw him in śrī brindāvanam, where he could play as he liked.

Next, we will consider the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 14.5. Madavan en maniyinai

*mādavan – as te consort of śrī mahālakśmien maṇiyinai – being dear to me like a bluiś coloured gemstonevalaiyil piḻaitta panṟī pōl – like a boar vhic has escaped from trap (vit arrogance)ēdum onṟum – any material (vhic he possesses)koḷa tārā – one vho does not give, so tat oters could have(not vitin reac of anyone)īsan tannaik kaṇḍīrē – did you see te lord?pīdaga ādai – divine yellov coloured dressudai – divine dresstāḻa – as it śines, coming dovnperu kār mēgam kanṟu pōl – like a little cloud vhic has grovn huge and is darkvīdi āra varuvānai – perumān, mercifully coming, filling up te entire streetvirundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē – ve sav him in śrī brindāvanam*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Did you see the lord, who is the divine consort of śrī mahāḷakśmi, who is like a dear bluish gemstone for me, who is like a boar which has escaped from the trap, hence being with arrogance and who does not bestow anything which he has, on anyone? We saw perumān, wearing the divine yellow coloured dress such that it was flowing down, like a little cloud which has grown huge and is dark, mercifully coming, filling up the entire street, in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that they saw him in śrī brindāvanam, like a little cloud which is together with lightning, donning yellowish silk dress, filling up the entire street. mādavan en maṇiyinai valaiyil piḻaitta panṟi pōlēdum onṟum koḷattārā īsan tannaik kaṇḍīrēpīdagavādai udai tāḻa perungār mēgak kanṟē pōlvīdiyāra varuvānai virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē Word-by-Word Manings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mādavan en maṇiyinai** – *mādavan* – lord of śrī mahālakśmi. Since he has śrīdēvi, who is the best among women, as his own, it is hinted that he is a connoisseur. Since she says *en maṇiyinai*, she has him under her control, having knotted that bluish gemstone in her dress.

**valaiyil piḻaitta panṟī pōl** – like a boar which had got trapped in a net and had escaped from it. One can trap a boar, which has not got trapped until then. However, it is not possible to trap it again, if it escapes from the trap once. In the same way, just as it has been mentioned in [tiruccanda viruttam 83](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/01/thiruchchandha-viruththam-83/) “*ettinōdu iraṇḍenum kayiṟu*” (with a rope known as devotion), though emperumān too could be trapped in the net of devotion, in order to nurture their *bhakti* (devotion) into *paramabhakti* (supreme devotion, by which the follower could sustain only by being united with emperumān and would perish otherwise) he would escape from that net and would not be within reach subsequently. At that time, it would be impossible to reach him by any means.

**ēdum onṟu koḷattārā īsan** – not bestowing anything which he has, to others, but considering everything which others have as his own.

**pīdaga ādai udai tāḻa** – the divine yellow coloured dress flowing down and swaying from side to side

**perum kār mēgak kanṟu pōl** – like a little cloud, which is huge, dark and fully filled up with water. His yellow-coloured divine dress would be like lightning and his divine form would be like a monsoon cloud. The term *kanṟu* refers to his age.

**vīdi Ara varuvāṇai** – just as that cloud comes filling up the entire sky, kaṇṇan would come, filling up the entire street. Has it not been said in kriṣṇa karṇāmrutam too? “*paścād gavām gōpasutai*: *parītō vēṇusvanānandita viśvacētā*: l *sampūrya vīdi savilāsamāyāt gōpālabāla*: *kuladaivatam na*: ll” (Let the deity of our clan, gōpāla, come beautifully behind the cows, surrounded by the herd-boys, making everyone’s heart to be happy through music from his flute, filling up the entire street)

**virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – instead of in tiruvāyppādi where all the people in the place could see him, we got to see him in śrī brindāvanam where he could play with us alone.

Next, we will consider the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 14.6. Darumam ariya

*darumam aṟiyāk kuṟumbanait tan kaic cārngam aduvēpōlpuruva vattam aḻagiya poruttam iliyaik kaṇḍīrēuruvu karidāy mugam seydāy udaya paruppadattin mēlviriyum kadirē pōlvānai virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

darumam aṟīyā – not knowing about compassion; kuṟumbanai – one who always indulges in misciefs; than kai sārngam aduvē pōl – like the divine bow sārngam on his divine hand; puruva vattam – with the circles of eyebrows; aḻagiya – having beauty; poruttamiliyai – perumān who does not live in harmony (by giving that beauty to his followers for enjoying); kaṇḍīrē – did you see?; uruvu karidu āy – having dark complexion on his divine form; mugam seydu āy – having reddish splendour; udaya paruppadattin mēl viriyum kadir pōlvānai – that perumān who is shining like the spreading sun rising on top of eastern mountain; virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē – we saw in śrībrindāvanam

**Перевод:**

**Did you see perumān who does not know about compassion, who indulges in misciefs all the time, who has eyebrows like the bow śarngam on his divine hand, who has beauty and who does not live in harmony by sharing that beauty with his followers? We saw that perumān, having a dark complexion on his divine form with a reddish splendour, shining like the spreading sun on top of the eastern mountain, in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that they saw him in śrī brindāvanam, coming with a reddish radiance on his dark divine form, just like the rising sun on udayagiri (mountain in the eastern direction).*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**darumam aṟīyā** – here *darumam* refers to compassion. He does not know even the starting point of compassion. Did not sītāp pirātti mercifully say in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38-41 “*ānruśamsyam parō darmas tvatta Eva mayā śruta*:” (It was heard by me from you that compassion is the most eminent *darmam* (righteousness))? The meaning for this is: “These are not the words which I heard from your messenger, hanuman. When we were together, did I not hear from you directly, when you mercifully said these words? When we were together, when you were ready to give whatever I had asked for, I had asked you ‘Among all the qualities, which quality do you consider to be ’the most eminent?’. You had said ‘When others suffer, we will not tolerate it. This is the most eminent *darmam* which we consider’. The inner meaning for the conversation between them is: inner meaning of pirātti’s question is ‘What do we do if there is any separation between us?’ The inner meaning of śrī rāma pirān’s response is ‘I, who consider compassion as the most eminent quality, will not allow you to suffer in separation from me’” The meaning of pirātti’s word *mayā śruta* is: “Will it pass off as something which you said through your mouth and I listened through my ears? Will it not be practised by you as your activity?” sītāp pirātti wants hanuman to ask śrī rāma when he returns to meet him.

**kuṟumbanai** – only cakravartit tirumagan (son of emperor daśarata) would know about compassion, would you \[āṇḍāḷ addressing kaṇṇa pirān\], a mischievous person, know about it?

**than kaic cārngam aduvē pōl puruva vattam aḻagiya** – nothing outside him could match his eyebrow. Only the bow in his hand will match it. If there were a bow in his hand, one would have to say “How did the bow, which is supposed to be on your face, come to your hand?” If the bow were not in his hand, and someone sees his eyebrow, one would have to say “How can the bow be on the face? Should it not be on the hand?” Through its beauty, curvature and quality of tormenting others, the eyebrow matches bow.

**poruttamiliyai** – though on the outside, the eyebrows are beautiful, inside he is not in harmony due to absence of affection! What is the benefit of this beauty with one who is without affection? When I, who desire him, show my affection towards him, he is not manifesting his affection for me.

**kaṇḍīrē** – Though he is not in harmony with us, due to lack of love towards us, our affection towards him has not been withdrawn! Through the use of the term *kaṇḍīrē*, it is clear that she desires to see him without harmony towards her.

Now, it is the turn of the respondents.

**uruvu karidāy mugam seydāy … viriyum kadirē pōlvānai** – rising sun is such that he could be seen with eyes and the eyes of the beholder would be comfortable due to darkness and at the same time, being young \[sun just then rising\], he has reddish rays. kaṇṇapirān too has a great form which is dark; through his youthfulness, limbs such as eyes, hands, legs, mouth etc which are like lotus, and through his divine ornaments, he would look like the reddish sun. Thus, the opinion is that when he comes to śri brindāvanam, he looks like the rising sun.

Did not vālmīki too say that śrī rāma looked like the rising sun, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 3-36 “*tadāsanavaram prāpya vyadīpayata rāghava*: l *svayēva prabhayā mērumudayē vimalō ravi*: ll” (After seating on that eminent seat \[throne\], just as the rising sun makes mēru mountain to be radiant, śrī rāma made it radiant)? vālmīki used the metaphor to refer to the compassionate śrī rāma pirān during the time of his coronation when he had decorated himself with divine ornaments and sat on the throne, talking with a smile to people who came to see him. āṇḍāḷ uses the same metaphor which was used by vālmīki, for kaṇṇa pirān in śrī brindāvanam, when he was speaking to the covherd girls. Just as it has been said in “*tatra gōvindam āsīnam prasannādityavarcasam*” ((they vorśipped) gōvindan \[kaṇṇan\] who had the radiance of a comforting sun and who had mercifully come there), there are people who had referred to kaṇṇan himself as a comforting sun.

**virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – unlike in tiruvāyppādi where the noon sun would dazzle the eyes, we saw him in śrī brindāvanam where he was mercifully manifesting himself to the girls.

Next, we will consider the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 14.7. Poruttam udaiya

*poruttam udaiya nambiyai – as te lord in harmonypuṟam pōl uḷḷum kariyānai – being dark inside, in his mind, just like outsidekaruttai piḻaittu ninṟa – acting contradictory to my tinkingak karumā mugilai – tat kaṇṇa pirān, vho is like a dark, huge cloudkaṇḍīrē – did you see?arutti – desired (by tose vho desire oter benefits)tārā gaṇangaḷāl – by constellation of starsārap perugu – being fullvānam pōl – like skyviruttam peridu āy – along vit a huge crovdvaruvānai – tat perumān vho mercifully comesvirundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē – ve sav in śrī brindāvanam*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Did you see kaṇṇa pirān, who is the lord in harmony, who is dark in his mind, just like his divine form outside and who is like a dark, huge cloud? We have seen that perumān who mercifully comes along with his friends, in śrī brindāvanam, just like a constellation of stars which are desired by those who desire other benefits, being full in the sky.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that they saw kaṇṇan in śrī brindāvanam, amidst his friends, just like the constellation of stars in the expansive sky. poruttam udaiya nambiyaip puṟam pōl uḷḷum kariyānaikaruttaip piḻaittu ninṟa akkarumā mugilaik kaṇḍīrēaruttit tārā kaṇangaḷāl ārap perugu vānam pōlviruttam peridāy varuvānai virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē Word-for-Word Meanings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**poruttam udaiya nambiyai** – she had said of him, in the previous pāsuram, as *poruttamili* (one without harmony). She says “My calling him like that is a mistake. Disharmony will apply only to those who are different on their inside, from their outside. Will it apply to kaṇṇan, who is similar both on the inside and outside?”

**nambiyai** – she says that he is complete in all qualities, even in harmony. In which quality does he have complete harmony?

**puṟam pōḷ uḷḷum kariyānai** – Apart from him, is there anyone who has complete harmony in being dark both on the inside and outside? Is he like *cakravartit tirumagan* \[divine son of emperor daśarata\] who, due to his quality of compassion is white inside, while being dark outside?

**karuttaip piḻaittu ninṟa** – she explains how he is dark inside, just like he is outside. The meaning for this term is – “Remaining, contrary to my thoughts”. In other words, during the time when he was united with her, he had said “I will not separate from you. If I do, I will not be able to sustain myself”, even while thinking of separation in his dark mind. Even as she was thinking of those words as a firm statement, he will make her thoughts as false and separate from her.

**ak karumāmugilaik kaṇḍīrē** – his divine form, which is beautiful like a dark cloud, is such that he could not be given up, even as she thinks “He has falsified my thoughts”

Now, it is the pāsuram of the respondents.

**aruttit tārā gaṇangaḷāl ārap perugu vānam pōl** – like the sky which is full of constellation of stars and planets which are desired by those who desire the benefits which they like.

**viruttam peridāy varuvānai** – just as periyāḻvār had said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-1-1 “*tannēr āyiram piḷḷaigaḷ*” (thousand friends who have eminence similar to his) when he comes with his friends, it would appear like a sky with effulgent constellation of stars. The word *viruttam* is a modification of *virundam* which indicates a crowd, in the samaskruta word *brundam*. Thus, it refers to kaṇṇan coming with a huge group of his friends. If the text in the pāsuram were *ārap parugu vānam pōl \[aruttittu ārā kaṇangaḷāl ārap parugum vānam pōl*\] the meaning will be – like the sky which is seen by assemblies of men, who have lot of desire. Did not vālmīki too say in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 3-30 “*garmābhitaptā*: *parjanyam hlādayantamiva prajā*: l *na tatarpa samāyantām paśyamānō narādipa*: ll” (just like people, tormented by the hot summer months, look at clouds which give them happiness, without satisfaction, daśarata, looking at śrī rāma coming opposite him, was not satisfied) that daśarata, seeing śrī rāma coming, kept looking at him unsatiated, like people looking at clouds during summer?

**virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – when looking at kaṇṇan coming along with his thousand friends in tiruvāyppādi, it would appear to be superficial, like looking at a huge orchard fully. Unlike that, since he comes in śrī brindāvanam, exclusively for girls, we got to see him to the full satisfaction of our eyes.

Next, we will consider the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

## 14.8. Veliya sangu

*veḷiya sangu onṟu udaiyānaip pīdaga ādai udaiyānaiaḷinangudaiya tirumalai āḻiyānaik kaṇḍīrēkaḷi vaṇḍu engum kalandāṟpōl kamaḻ pūnguḻalgaḷ tadam tōḷ mēlmiḷira ninṟu viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veḷiya sangu onṟu udaiyānai – one who has the whitish śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch); pīdaga ādai udaiyānai – one who has yellow coloured dress; nangu aḷi udaiya – having deep compassion; āḻiyānai – one who has the divine disc; tirumālai – kaṇṇan, who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; kaṇḍīrē – did you see?; kaḷi vaṇḍu – (due to drinking honey) beetles which are joyous; engum – on all sides; kalandāṟpōl – spread out; kamaḻ pū kuḻalgaḷ – fragrant, beautiful, divine locks; tadam tōḷ mēl – on top of his huge, divine shoulders; miḷira ninṟu viḷaiyāda – (swaying) playing with splendour; virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē – we saw in śrī brindāvanam

**Перевод:**

**Did you see kaṇṇan, who has the whitish divine conch, who has yellow coloured divine dress, who has deep compassion, who has the divine disc and who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi? We saw him, with his fragrant, divine locks similar to spread-out beetles on all sides, which are joyous, playing and shining on his divine shoulders, in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that they saw kaṇṇan, playing in śrī brindāvanam, with his divine locks, similar to beetles, shining on his divine shoulders.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**veḷiya sangu onṟu udaiyānai** – one who has the unique śrī pāncajanyam, divine conch, which is whitish in colour, contrasting with his divine form which is dark like an ocean. Did not tirumangai āḻvār enjoy this contrast in his periya tirumoḻi 7-10-2 “*kaiyilōr sangudai mainniṟakkadal*” (one who has the unique, divine conch on his divine hand, and who is dark like an ocean)?

**pīdaga ādai udaiyānai** – the divine yellow dress too, due to its colour, would provide a distinct contrast to his dark form.

**aḷi nanguidaiya** – his divine heart would be full of compassion. What is the reason for this compassion?

**tirumālai** – because of his intimacy with periya pirāttiyār \[śrī mahālakśmi\]

**āḻiyānai** – why are the divine conch and divine disc not enjoyed together as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/05/thiruvaimozhi-6-9-1-niray-nilanay/) “*kūrārāḻi veṇsangēndi*” (holding the sharp disc which eliminates enemies and the white, divine conch which is enjoyable for the followers) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 7-2-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/thiruvaimozhi-7-2-1-kangulum-pagalum/) “*sangu cakkarangaḷ*” (divine conch and divine disc), but mentioned separately? She says them separately since she feels that if they are mentioned together, it will invite evil eyes to be cast on them.

Next, it is the respondents’ pāsuram.

**kaḷi vaṇḍu engum kalandāṟpoḷ tadam tōḷ mēl kamaḻum pūnguḻalgaḷ** – the swaying of kaṇṇan’s fragrant divine locks on his huge shoulders appears like a swarm of beetles, bewildered due to imbibing honey, spreading everywhere.

**miḷira ninṟu viḷaiyāda virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – we cannot see this sight in tiruvāyppādi since yaśōdāp pirātti would have pleated his hair and decked it with flower. Hence, she says that they got to see kaṇṇan in śrī brindāvanam, with his locks swaying and playing, radiantly on his shoulders, like beetles.

Next, we will consider the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this decad.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 14.9. Nattaip padaiyenru

*nāttaip padaiyenṟu ayan mudalāt tanda naḷir māmalar undivīttaip paṇṇi viḷaiyādum vimalan tannaik kaṇḍīrēkāttai nādit tēnuganum kaḷiṟum puḷḷum udan madiyavēttai ādi varuvānai virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nāttai padai enṟu – saying ‘create all the worlds’; ayan mudalā – prajāpatis such as brahmā et al; tanda – one who created; naḷir mā malar undi vīttai paṇṇi – creating a house, known as the divine navel, which has a cool, huge flower; viḷaiyādum – enjoying the essence of \[his\] play, like this; vimalan tannai – perumāḷ, who is the most sacred; kaṇḍīrē – did you see?; dēnuganum – the monster dēnukan; kaḷiṟum – elephant, kuvalayāpeedam; puḷḷum – bakāsura \[who came in the form of a stork\]; udan madiya – to be killed immediately; kāttai nādi vēttai ādi varuvānai – that perumān, who went to the forest and hunted them; virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē – we saw in śrī brindāvanam

**Перевод:**

**Did you see perumāḷ, who told brahmā and other prajāpatis \[entities in charge of creation\] to create the world, who created a house which is his divine navel having a cool, huge flower, who enjoys the essence of this play and who is the most sacred? We saw that perumān, who went into the forest for hunting and immediately killed the monsters dēnukan, kuvalayāpeedam, the elephant and bakāsura who came in the form of a stork, in śrī brindāvanam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she says that they saw kaṇṇan, who had hunted animals, who are monsters in disguise, in śrī brindāvanam,*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāttaip padai enṟu ayan mudalāt tanda** – emperumān performed *samaśti sruśti* \[where he created on his own and not through someone\] which included the universe, along with brahmā. Then, he asked brahmā to carry out *vyaśti sruśti* \[creating other entities, both with and without knowledge, through that brahmā\], thus creating all the worlds.

**naḷir mā malar undi vīttaip paṇṇi viḷaiyādum** – his divine navel which removes all the ennui, since it is cool, has expanse such that it is the cause for universe and all the other effective entities, has lotus which had just blossomed and which is the house for brahmā. He enjoys this creation as having been carried out for his benefit, enjoying the essence of his play.

**vimalan** – this speaks about the sacredness which comes with his not considering the importance attached to their positions by brahmā and others whom he had created, and who do not have desire for him and the desire to attain him. In the term *ayan mudalāt tanda*, appropriate to the plural number \[ayan and others\] should the text not have been *padaiyungaḷ* (you people create) instead of *padai* (create, which is used with singular number)? Since *prājāpatis* start creating based on brahmā’s impelling, the one who is appointed by emperumān to create is brahmā alone; hence, the term *padai* has been used, in line with the singlular number.

If the text had been *ayan tannait tanda* \[instead of *ayan mudalāt tanda*\], nampiḷḷai, one of our preceptors would mercifully explain saying “You create all the effective entities within the universe”. He would say that this is more apt since it agrees with the singular term *padai*, seen earlier.

Now, the pāsuram of the respondents:

The rest of the pāsuram speaks about how, after he had created the universe and brahmā and through him created all the other entities, instead of leaving it to them to protect themselves by taking care of their enemies, he had come on his own, as the owner of all those worlds and entities, and annihilated the enemies.

**kāttai nādi dēnuganum kaḷiṟum puḷḷum udan madiya vēttaiyādi varuvānai** – just as one searches for bushes etc inside a forest while hunting for animals, in the forests at tiruvāyppādi and śrī brindāvanam, he had searched for places where the monstrous entities had hidden, and instantaneously killed monsters such as dēnukan, kuvalayāpeedam, the elephant, and bakāsuran, who came in the form of a stork, by hunting them.

**virundāvanattē kaṇḍōmē** – instead of in tiruvāyppādi where there will be many people, we saw him  in śrī brindāvanam, where we, who desire him, could embrace him in privacy, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 94-18 “*tam druśtvā śatruhantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham* l *babhūva hruśtā vaidēhī bhartāram pariśasvajē* ll” (Looking at śrī rāma, who annihilated his enemies and made the great sages joyous, sītai, the daughter of vidēha rāja, became happy; she embraced her husband strongly).

Next, we will consider the last pāsuram of this decad as well as this prabandam.

## 14.10. Parundal kalirrukku

*parundāḷ kaḷiṟṟukku aruḷ seyda paraman tannai pārin mēlvirundāvanattē kaṇḍamai vittucittan kōdai solmarundām enṟu tam manattē vaittukkoṇḍu vāḻvārgaḷperundāḷ udaiya pirān adikkīḻ piriyādu enṟum iruppārē*

**Пословный перевод:**

paru tāl kaḷiṟṟukku – to gajēndārḻvān \[elephant\] who had stout legs; aruḷ seyda – one who bestowed mercy; paraman tannai – tirumāl \[emperumān, consort of śrī mahālakśmi\]; pārin mēl – on this land; virundāvanattē kaṇḍamai – regarding seeing him in śrī brindāvanam; vittucittan kōdai sol – these pāsurams, mercifully composed by āṇḍāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār; marundu Am enṟu – considering it as the medicine (for the disease of taking birth repeatedly); tam manattē – in their minds; vaittukkoṇḍu – meditating on it; vāḻvārgaḷ – those who live; peru tāl udaiya pirān adikkīḻ – under the eminent, divine feet of emperumān; enṟum – all the time; piriyādu irupāar – they will remain (having permanent experience) without ever separating

**Перевод:**

**āṇḍāḷ, the divine daughter of periyāḻvār, had mercifully composed these pāsurams on how she had seen tirumāl in śrī brindāvanam, on this world, who had bestowed his mercy on gajēndrāḻvān who had stout legs. Those who meditate on these pāsurams, treating them in their minds as the medicine for the disease of repeated births, will have permanent enjoyment under the divine, eminent feet of emperumān, forever, without separating from him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) – she brings the prabandam to an end by stating the benefit to those who meditate on this decad, which is that they will constantly carry out servitude to emperumān.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**parundāḷ kaḷiṟṟukku aruḷ seyda** – this speaks about the mercy which emperumān showered on the elephant which had stout legs. Since it had a huge form, its pain also would have been intolerable for the elephant.

**paraman tannai** – such an elephant entered a pond which was not its own place, got caught by the crocodile and in pain, cried out “*ādimūlamē*” (Oh, one who is the root of all entities!). As soon as emperumān heard that cry, he came running, with his ornaments and weapons in a disarray, with his dress and locks diśevelled, to that pond. He stroked that elephant such that its injury got healed, such that it could be said that in terms of protecting all entities, there was none anywhere near him. It is pertinent to recall the ślōkam in śrī viṣṇu darmam 69-47 “*paramāpadamāpannō manasā’cintayat harim* l *sa tu nāgavara*: *śrīmān nārāyaṇa parāyaṇa*: ll” (that gajēndrāḻvān, who was in severe danger, who had the wealth of servitude and who had taken refuge under śrīman nārāyaṇan, thought of hari (emperumān) in his mind). What is the reason for āṇḍāḷ to think of this narrative here? She recalls this in order to highlight that she too had distress due to separation from emperumān, just like the danger which the elephant had and just as emperumān had removed the distress of that elephant, he removed her distress too, by manifesting himself to her.

**pārin mēl virundāvanattē kaṇḍamai** – seeing him, not in śrī vaikuṇṭam, not in a common place in this world such as tiruvāyppādi, but in śrī brindāvanam, where he gave himself to those who desired him.

**vittucittan kōdai sol** – the means which she had for seeing such a sight was that she was the daughter of periyāḻvār. ḍidn’t she already say in [nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2024/10/nachchiyar-thirumozhi-10-10/) “*vittu cittar tangaḷ dēvarai valla parisu varuvipparēl adu kāṇḍumē*”!

**marundām enṟu tam manattē vaittukkoṇḍu vāḻvārgaḷ** – keeping firmly in their minds that this is the medicine for samsāram \[living repeatedly in materialistic realm\], without having fear, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 9 “*ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān na bibhēti kutaścana*” (one who knows the bliss of brahmam \[emperumān\] will not fear anything)

**perundāḷudaiya pirān adikkīḻ** – carrying out servitude to emperumān’s divine feet which are the best entity among all entities to be attained. Did she not mercifully say in [tiruppāvai 29](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/) “*unakkē nām ātseyvōm*” (we shall be subservient to you only)!

**piriyādu enṟum iruppārē** – without the need for asking some people “Did you see him?”, like I did, having the fortune of seeing him all the time, and enjoying him all the time as their pastime.

This brings us to the end of this divya prabandam.

*periya perumāḷ periya pirāttiyār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*  
*āṇḍāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*  
*periyavāccān piḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*
